# Architecture implementation evidence archive

This preserves the dated implementation and validation notes that were formerly embedded in the status ledger. It is supporting evidence, not a current status source. Current status and next gates live in [implementation-status.md](implementation-status.md). Credential/DSN values, email addresses, and long opaque identifiers are redacted; technical descriptions and test outcomes are retained.

---

**G10 lifecycle script selection transaction-boundary correction (2026-10-16):** `script.Store.LoadActiveScriptsTx` now reads selected lifecycle script rows through the caller's SQL transaction, and `orm.TxManager.activeHookScripts` uses it whenever a kernel transaction is active. This makes the script snapshot share the source mutation's database view; durable delivery is still not implemented by this change. Added sqlmock coverage for transaction use and missing-transaction rejection. `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-g10-async-hook-tmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-g10-async-hook-tmp go test -p 1 ./script ./orm ./kernel -count=1` passed. After adding the missing config-revision schema/seed to `TestScriptHookSnapshotRefreshesForEachCommand`'s MySQL fixture, `GOTMPDIR=/tmp/kora-api-hook-test-tmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/tmp/kora-api-hook-test-tmp go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./api -run '^TestScriptHookSnapshotRefreshesForEachCommand$' -count=1 -timeout=180s` passed on the disposable MySQL fixture. The race-enabled version failed at Go link time with `no space left on device` before executing the test; `/tmp` and `/dev/shm` were full. `git diff --check` passed. No schema or wire contract changed. This narrows the selection race but does not close the async lifecycle queue, provider-effect receipt, worker-recovery, or uncertain external-outcome gates.

**G10 async-hook handoff recheck (2026-10-16):** Re-traced `kernel/command_exec.go` and `orm/query.go`: document lifecycle completion currently runs only after the source transaction commits, then routes `after_*` hooks to the bounded process-local sink. The integration path is functional but not durable across failure between commit and enqueue. The script provider already supports a transaction-bound `MutationExecutor`, but post-commit completion deliberately clears it; a durable executor can bind local mutations and the invocation receipt to one worker transaction. The same script provider also exposes outbound HTTP, so a durable invocation must isolate/reconcile external effects rather than hold a local transaction open around network calls. Passed `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/engine ./api -run '^(TestAsyncHookWorkflowActionCanKeepProviderDisabled|TestAsyncHookQueueReportsFullAndClosedWithoutBlocking|TestAsyncHookQueueCloseSerializesWithConcurrentEnqueue|TestAsyncHookQueueBoundsPayloadAndAggregateBytes|TestAsyncHookAdmissionRetriesWithoutDroppingActiveItem|TestAsyncHookAdmissionTreatsTypedNilAsUnconfigured|TestAsyncHookAdmissionWaitStopsOnCancellation|TestWorkflowActionAsyncScriptUsesBoundedSinkWithoutProvider)$' -count=1 -timeout=3m` (all selected Engine tests passed; API package had no selected non-integration tests) and, against task-owned disposable MySQL with a uniquely named database created/dropped by the fixture, `go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./api -run '^TestWorkflowActionAsyncScriptUsesBoundedSinkWithoutProvider$' -count=1 -timeout=3m -v`. Both commands passed; the integration test proves successful enqueue and visible queue-full rejection, not restart recovery or durable invocation semantics. No code/schema/contract change. Next action: record immutable invocation and identity with the source transaction; execute local provider mutations transactionally with the completion receipt; stage HTTP side effects separately with stable idempotency or explicit uncertainty; then add restart/replay tests before retiring the compatibility queue.

## Current checkpoint — 2026-10-16

- **G9 Cloud ingress cache revision fencing (2026-10-16):** Fixed the alias-fallback cache key path: tenant aliases that pass the existing opaque-ID parser now retain the original route selector key while the cache entry also carries the directory-verified site/alias snapshot, preventing repeated directory lookups and preserving ownership checks on cache hits. Cloud ingress cache writes compare canonical decimal directory revisions across cached keys for a tenant, keep a newer snapshot when an older request finishes late, reject equal-revision disagreement, and remove superseded alias keys when accepting a newer snapshot. Added deterministic out-of-order directory response coverage proving the older response cannot route to the prior Engine, same-revision conflict coverage proving the prior route stays intact, removed-alias coverage, and cache-index cleanup through the common expiry/eviction path. Updated target-spec §11.5 to record the monotonic cache rule. Passed Cloud `go test ./internal/ingress ./cmd/kora-cloud -count=1 -timeout=90s`, `go vet ./internal/ingress ./cmd/kora-cloud`, `go test -race ./internal/ingress -count=1 -timeout=120s`, and 100 repeated runs of the out-of-order/conflicting-revision tests; Engine `make check-cross-repo-contracts`; and scoped formatting/whitespace checks. No public contract or schema change. Files: Cloud `internal/ingress/gateway.go`, `internal/ingress/gateway_test.go`; Engine `docs/architecture/target-state-spec.md`, this ledger. G9 production assignment, external consumer/origin inventory, traffic cutover and cell-path purge gates remain open.

- **G9 revision-fenced assignment writes (2026-10-16):** The deployment assignment CLI previously read a descriptor then called an unconditional placement update, allowing a concurrent stale move to overwrite a newer one. Added `SQLSiteRegistry.SetRuntimeCellIDAtRevisionContext`, which performs a `config_revision` compare-and-update in the same transaction as the directory outbox event; stale or missing rows fail without writing. The CLI now passes the descriptor revision it inspected and honors cancellation. The source-compatible `SetRuntimeCellID` wrapper also uses the revision-fenced path. Updated target-spec section 11.5 to make the CAS and transactional outbox requirement explicit. Added SQLmock success/stale-writer cases and real-dialect stale-writer checks to the aggregate assignment acceptances. Passed `go test ./site ./cli -count=1`, `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./site ./cli`, `go vet -p 1 ./site ./cli`, and disposable live assignment-summary/integration checks on MySQL 8.4.11, PostgreSQL 16, and actual LibSQL HTTP 0.24.33. In each dialect the first write advanced placement/revision and a second write using the old revision returned `ErrSiteRevisionConflict` while preserving the winner. The three real DB runs used synthetic rows and disposable databases; no tenant DBs or persistent assignments were touched. `GOCACHE=/home/asena/.cache/go-build GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm go test -race -p 1 ./site -run '^TestSQLSiteRegistrySetRuntimeCellIDAtRevision' -count=1 -timeout=120s`, cross-repository contract checks, and source/spec whitespace checks passed. A race build of the much larger `./cli` package remains unrun after `/dev/shm` exhaustion; normal CLI tests and vet passed. No API or schema change. Files: `site/directory_registry.go`, `site/registry_test.go`, `cli/site_directory_assign_cell.go`, assignment tests, `cli/site_directory_summary_dialects_integration_test.go`, `docs/architecture/target-state-spec.md`, this ledger.

Compose/model and focused unit tests do not prove production process shutdown under load. Files: `deploy/selfhost/compose.yaml`, `deploy/selfhost/README.md`, this ledger. No API/schema change.

# Current implementation stage (2026-10-16)

**Domain-boundary audit (2026-10-16):** Traced current Engine production imports for Digitax/eTIMS, inventory/stock and Purchase Request behavior. Digitax handlers/provider implementation and Digitax starter fields are already retired (see dated entries below); no stock/inventory mutation policy is implemented by the generic workflow runtime. `script/inventory.go`, `configstore/workflow_action_inventory.go`, and `cli/workflow_legacy_inventory.go` are bounded technical scanners of tenant-defined scripts/workflows and persisted formats, not a stock domain. One built-in business adapter remains: `internal/application/purchasing` is directly composed by `internal/engine/tenant_services.go` and `internal/engine/workflow_runtime.go` to authorize governed Purchase Request approvals and apply their document transitions. The adapter is not part of generic workflow execution, but removing it before migrating existing governed requests/instances would strand persisted work, contrary to sections 1 and 15; no tenant migration or compatibility proof has been performed. Optional vertical packs under `config/` remain example/configuration content, not Engine runtime rules. **Open gate:** define and test the adapter migration/retirement path before removing that compatibility behavior; do not describe all business-domain content as removed. No source/schema change in this audit. Evidence: production-import search, direct-composition trace, and current migration/purge requirements in the target specification.

**G7 Cloud-outage isolation correction (2026-10-16):** The required active-tenant outage proof exposed that `auth/session.go` sent first-login telemetry synchronously; the new process acceptance hung on a test Cloud endpoint before returning the login response. Doctype activation also sent first-doctype telemetry inline. Both now enqueue onto the tenant-owned `analytics.CloudRelay` worker through the pinned runtime service snapshot. The event queue is bounded to 32; only the three fixed event shapes are accepted, string lengths are checked before encoding, payloads are capped at 16 KiB, and overflow/oversize/unknown payloads are counted. Engine resource metrics export buffered items, aggregate capacity and drops. The worker HTTP timeout is 10 seconds. Added queue-bound and resource-metric regressions and extended the real CLI/MySQL process-restart approval acceptance: the Cloud endpoint holds the first-login event open, the authenticated approval still returns 200 and commits the domain transition, then the endpoint returns 503; the workflow still resumes after Engine restart. Passed `go test ./analytics ./auth ./api ./internal/tenantruntime -count=1`, `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./cli`, `go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./cli -run '^TestEngineProcessRestartResumesGovernedHumanApproval$' -count=1 -timeout=480s -v` twice after the fix (real disposable MySQL 8.4.11; 15.06s and final 15.23s), `go test -race ./analytics ./auth ./api ./internal/tenantruntime -count=1`, final `go test -race ./analytics -count=1`, `go test ./analytics ./internal/engine -run '^(TestCloudRelayProductEventQueueIsBounded|TestResourceMetricsRegisterAndObserveProcessResourceGauges)$' -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./analytics ./auth ./api ./internal/tenantruntime`, final `go vet -p 1 ./analytics ./internal/engine`, and `git diff --check`. The first acceptance attempt timed out on the synchronous login telemetry and supplied the regression evidence; the next passed after the fix. The first integration compile with a fresh `/dev/shm` Go cache failed `ENOSPC`; after removing only that task-created cache and using the existing Go build cache with `/dev/shm` as `GOTMPDIR`, the integration compile and real process test passed. Cloud product telemetry remains best effort/in-memory and is not evidence of Cloud management outage recovery or durable telemetry. Updated target-spec sections 13.2 and 16.1. No wire or database schema change.

**G10 local image-build resource recheck (2026-10-16):** Rechecked before retrying the full Engine+Studio Docker build. Root filesystem has 246 MB free and `/dev/shm` 834 MB free; Docker reports 4.216 GB of reclaimable images but 0 B reclaimable volumes, and no Kora Engine/workspace images are present. No image/volume/cache cleanup or build was attempted; the prior cleanup authorization was specifically for unused volumes, and Docker reports none removable. Full image build remains unverified. Continue with source-level and disposable-service checks that fit available resources; retry the image build after disk capacity changes or with a separate build host.

### G0 Baseline and contracts

**Status:** verified.


**Changes and contracts:** This ledger; complete first-party import graph artifact; `cmd/contractgen/main.go`, tests, generated `contract/schema/engine-v1.json`, `contract/generated/engine-v1.ts`, `contract/schema/engine-v2.json` and `contract/generated/engine-v2.ts`; `cmd/routeinventory/main.go`; `docs/architecture/g0-inventory.md`; `docs/architecture/route-inventory.json`; and `docs/architecture/contract-fixture-manifest.json`. `scripts/check-contract-fixture-manifest.py` is wired into `make check-contracts` for Engine-local copies and `make check-cross-repo-contracts` for shared fixture copies plus the embedded UI, standalone UI and SDK generated v2 artifacts. The route inventory invokes actual API/auth/public/request Gin registration functions and records CLI-conditional endpoints separately. Go wire roots and enums are declared at their type definitions using `contract:wire` and `contract:enum`; open `json.RawMessage` fields require an explicit `contract:json` annotation. Field rules cover numeric bounds (`contract:min`/`max`), Unicode character lengths, array item counts, UTF-8 byte limits, JSON payload byte limits, regex patterns (`contract:pattern`/`itemPattern`) and fixed numeric values (`contract:const`). The TypeScript runtime validator applies regex patterns and the generator rejects invalid regex and misuse on non-string arrays. Generated TypeScript definitions and runtime guards/decoders are derived from the same JSON Schema; named scalar ID/revision types are inlined as their base schema instead of dangling references. The TypeScript generator now emits literal discriminated `oneOf` unions and runtime exact-one checks, and enum-keyed maps produce finite key validation; regression tests cover the workflow step union and routing maps. The SDK vendors this generated v2 artifact with a digest and explicitly marks it as sourced from the uncommitted Engine working tree; it is not a release pin. A collision with the frozen `contract.Cursor` struct is resolved by introducing `PageCursor` for new pagination APIs and documenting the correction in the spec. Added the generic Go `contract.Page[T]` helper, shared marketplace artifact wire roots (`MarketplacePackageManifest`, `PackManifest`, `PackFile`, and validation DTOs), and pure canonical manifest/artifact digest algorithms in `contract/package_digest.go`; legacy `site` names remain type aliases. The Cloud marketplace fixture now decodes these shared contract types and verifies existing v1 digest values. Added the required machine-readable error codes `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`, `STALE_SUBJECT`, `LEASE_LOST`, `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`, and `UNSUPPORTED_VERSION`, plus normalization and HTTP status mappings. The v1 schema/error enum artifact was regenerated; Engine v2 keeps its generated error code field open-string, so the SDK’s v2 bytes remained unchanged.

**G0 inventory and remaining later gate:** route method/path registrations, CLI-conditional endpoints, Go imports (including nested SDK), consumer DTO locations, shared fixture copies, database matrix, persisted workflow/durable-work formats, runtime ownership, working-tree snapshot, and Cloud's direct Engine imports are recorded in `g0-inventory.md` and its linked artifacts. **Status:** verified.

**Changes and contracts:** Engine `api/realtime_auth.go` denies record events without authenticated read roles, preserves owner restrictions, and applies the same authorization helper to replay. Owner-scoped events now load the current row through the owner-filtered document read instead of trusting the event snapshot's historical `owner`; local and NATS parity tests cover an ownership change while a delayed event still names the old owner. Long-lived SSE/WebSocket streams now refresh session roles or delegated permissions at heartbeat and close when the credential is revoked or cannot be revalidated. `analytics/bus.go` now returns WAL persistence failures to publishers instead of logging and falsely returning success; append data is synced before success, new WAL files and rotation/acknowledgment directory changes are synced, write/sync failures poison publication until restart to avoid appending behind a partial record, and close errors surface. WAL scans now report directory/read/corrupt-record failures, continue accounting for valid records around corruption, and retain corrupt bytes after acknowledgment; a regression uses a directory name containing glob syntax and verifies valid records drain while the corrupt line survives. NATS outbox decoding now preserves the envelope event ID when bridging into the analytics WAL, with a regression asserting identity preservation. Added optional `analytics.WALBatchDrainer`; the local bus rotates a stable WAL snapshot and returns bounded batches, and the worker treats channel events as wakeups while scanning the WAL on wake and timer. An in-memory pending-ID set prevents duplicate accumulation while a batch awaits acknowledgment. The base channel now has one `MultiBus` subscriber before analytics worker startup on cold load and hot reload, preserving fanout for webhook/realtime/relay consumers while worker-listener overflow recovers from WAL. `MultiBus.Close` closes listeners, closes the inner bus, and joins its fanout goroutine; startup rollback and generation retirement therefore wait for this owned worker. `analytics/worker.go` serializes flushes; `analytics/backfill.go` uses PostgreSQL-safe text casts and LibSQL `||` concatenation for grouped dimensions. `outbox/outbox.go` fences publisher claims and reports durable transition failures; added `Dispatcher.RegisterTransactional`, which inserts a unique consumer receipt before invoking local effects in the same SQL transaction. Duplicate deliveries skip the handler; handler/commit errors roll back receipt and effects. The legacy non-transactional handler is explicitly at-least-once for external/idempotent effects. The target spec records the receipt crash-window correction and the separate publication/consumer completion boundary. `Dispatcher` selects events missing applicable local receipts without changing publication state; only the external `Publisher` marks rows published after provider acknowledgment. This prevents local completion from suppressing provider delivery, excludes irrelevant event types, and prevents receipted old events from starving later work. Focused outbox replay and claim/ack tests pass. `api/console.go` records onboarding active before runtime publication and unwinds prepared resources on persistence failure. `site/onboarding_store.go` atomically replaces file-backed provisioning state. `storage/local.go` now persists a same-directory recovery journal and fsynced copies of the prior blob and metadata before replacing either file. Every read, write, and delete recovers an interrupted operation under a per-key lock; rollback is repeatable across crashes, successful publication keeps the flat-file and FileMeta sidecar format, and persistence errors are surfaced. Existing PostgreSQL bootstrap fixes remain. `site/bootstrap.go` now fails and leaves the bootstrap marker unadvanced if default Administrator seeding fails; `site/bootstrap_role_test.go` verifies the seed error propagates. Standalone and embedded generated-page stores now commit the current revision and bounded history in one localStorage envelope, retain legacy split-key reads, and preserve the previous committed value on write failure. Standalone and embedded Studio catch save failures, keep the previous persisted/editor revision, and display an accessible recovery message instead of claiming success. Standalone crystallization progress now guards storage reads, bounds restored step indexes, and updates UI state only after its storage write succeeds. Theme storage reads and writes now fail safely in both UIs, preserving the active-session theme when browser storage is denied; tenant default-theme selection also tolerates denied storage. Standalone recent-doctype and favorite preferences now fall back to bounded session-memory state on read/write errors instead of throwing during navigation. The still-supported legacy workflow runtime now rejects malformed or non-object persisted payload JSON before invoking a step handler and releases its acquired lease after decode failure, handler failure, or cancellation using bounded cleanup contexts. Legacy due-timer recovery now preserves timer rows on actor-load errors and competing live owners, deduplicates duplicate deliveries without deleting persisted rows, and propagates stale/consumed timer deletion errors; regression tests prove fail-closed behavior and exercise the real handler-failure release path, and due-timer tests verify transient load failure and live-owner recovery retain the durable timer.

This is process-crash evidence, not filesystem/device power-loss validation. Existing storage checks include interrupted publish/delete recovery, same-key concurrent replacement pairing, idempotent deletion, and legacy flat-file metadata compatibility. **Latest outbox verification:** `go test ./outbox -count=1`, `go test -race ./outbox -count=1`, `go test -tags=integration ./outbox -run '^$' -count=1`, `go test -race -tags=integration ./outbox -run '^TestMySQLTransactionalConsumerReceiptAndLocalEffect$' -count=1 -timeout=120s`, full Engine `go test ./... -count=1`, `go vet ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, and `git diff --check` passed after separating local consumer completion from publication acknowledgment. Disposable MySQL `go test -tags=integration ./outbox -run '^TestMySQLTransactionalConsumerReceiptAndLocalEffect$' -count=1 -timeout=120s` passed: failed local effect rolled back its receipt, retry committed once, local completion left publication pending, the publisher then delivered and marked it published, and dispatcher replay skipped the completed local consumer. The same MySQL scenario now holds a provider call after its claim while local dispatch commits; the publisher subsequently acknowledges and both local/provider outcomes remain exactly once.

**Analytics rebuild verification (2026-10-14):** Added `analytics/backfill_mysql_integration_test.go`, gated on `KORA_ANALYTICS_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN` and refusing non-loopback endpoints. A task-created disposable MySQL 8.4 container with a tmpfs data directory ran `KORA_ANALYTICS_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' go test -tags=integration ./analytics -run '^TestMySQLBackfillFailurePreservesPreviouslyCommittedRollups$' -count=1 -timeout=120s`; it passed and the container was stopped. The test triggers a source-column failure after rebuild deletes and earlier metric inserts, then confirms prior daily and monthly values remain committed. It does not prove concurrent flush/rebuild cutoff coordination.

**Cross-dialect rebuild rollback verification (2026-10-14):** Extended the isolated PostgreSQL rebuild integration to fail after rebuild work begins and assert prior daily/monthly values and the committed checkpoint survive; its existing flush/rebuild serialization assertion still passes. Extended the actual HTTP LibSQL rebuild integration to temporarily make the source table unavailable, forcing failure after the transaction has removed old rollups, then assert the prior daily/monthly values and checkpoint remain. Exact disposable runs passed: `KORA_ANALYTICS_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN='<loopback disposable DSN>' go test -tags=integration ./analytics -run '^TestPostgresRebuildCutoffSerializesWithFlush$' -count=1 -timeout=120s` and `KORA_ANALYTICS_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' go test -tags=integration ./analytics -run '^TestLibSQLRebuildCutoffAndLaterEvents$' -count=1 -timeout=120s`. Both services used tmpfs-only data and were stopped/removed. `go test ./analytics -count=1`, `go test -race ./analytics -count=1`, `go vet ./analytics`, integration compile check `go test -tags=integration ./analytics -run '^$' -count=1`, and `git diff --check` passed. This adds rollback evidence; the complete supported-database rebuild/flush matrix remains open.

**Cross-dialect analytics flush retry and portability (2026-10-14):** Added `analytics/flush_failure_integration_test.go` and wired it into the disposable MySQL, isolated-schema PostgreSQL, and actual HTTP LibSQL analytics integrations. Each injects a real daily-rollup write failure after receipt insertion, proves the transaction rolls back the receipt and rollup, confirms the event remains in WAL, retries successfully, and verifies exactly one rollup/receipt before WAL acknowledgment. The same helper exercises an existing-row monthly increment. This uncovered PostgreSQL's ambiguous unqualified target value and MySQL's timezone conversion of local `time.Time` parameters into a previous-month SQL `DATE`. Preserved the public `QueryDialect.UpsertIncrement` signature and added optional `TableQualifiedIncrementDialect.UpsertIncrementForTable`; analytics uses the table-qualified method where supported. Monthly rollup now binds canonical ISO calendar-date strings. Exact disposable runs passed: `KORA_ANALYTICS_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='<loopback disposable DSN>' go test -tags=integration ./analytics -run '^TestMySQLBackfillFailurePreservesPreviouslyCommittedRollups$' -count=1 -timeout=120s`, `KORA_ANALYTICS_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN='<loopback disposable DSN>' go test -tags=integration ./analytics -run '^TestPostgresRebuildCutoffSerializesWithFlush$' -count=1 -timeout=120s`, and `KORA_ANALYTICS_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' go test -tags=integration ./analytics -run '^TestLibSQLRebuildCutoffAndLaterEvents$' -count=1 -timeout=120s`. All used tmpfs-only databases and their containers were stopped/removed. Final checks passed: `go test ./... -count=1`, `go test -race ./analytics ./db ./natsprovider ./cli ./outbox -count=1`, `go vet ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `go test -tags=integration ./analytics -run '^$' -count=1`, `make check-contracts`, and `git diff --check`. Rebuild-lease migrations, concurrent claims, takeover and stale fencing now have real MySQL, PostgreSQL and LibSQL HTTP coverage. The wider supported-database matrix still needs coverage for remaining concurrency, migration, rebuild and pooler boundaries.

**Analytics lease parity on LibSQL HTTP (2026-10-14):** Added `analytics/rebuild_lease_libsql_integration_test.go`, including the v3→v4 additive lease bootstrap on an empty disposable database, two concurrent claimers, expiration/takeover with a higher fencing token, rejection of stale completion, and successful current-owner completion/readback. The first actual HTTP run exposed that the LibSQL driver returns SQL `DATE` as a string while MySQL/PostgreSQL return driver-native date values; `ClaimNextRebuildJob` and `GetRebuildJob` now parse `time.Time`, string, and byte values into UTC calendar dates. The new integration passed against a fresh tmpfs-only `sqld` service. Updated MySQL 8.4 and PostgreSQL 16 lease-fencing integrations also passed against fresh tmpfs-only services; all three were stopped and removed. Exact focused runs: `KORA_ANALYTICS_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' go test -tags=integration ./analytics -run '^TestLibSQLRebuildLeaseFencesExpiredOwner$' -count=1 -timeout=120s`, and matching MySQL/PostgreSQL DSNs with `TestMySQLRebuildLeaseFencesExpiredOwner` / `TestPostgresRebuildLeaseFencesExpiredOwner`. Unit coverage accepts each driver value form and rejects malformed dates.

**Tenant event source sequence (2026-10-14):** `contract.EventEnvelope.sequence` is an optional canonical decimal string. `outbox.SQLWriter.Append` allocates it from `_kora_event_sequence` and inserts it on `_kora_outbox.source_sequence` in the same business transaction; bootstrap adds the table/column/index for existing tenant databases. Local analytics and NATS outbox decoding retain the sequence. `go run ./cmd/contractgen -write` and `make check-contracts` passed. Focused `go test ./outbox ./contract ./analytics ./site ./cli`, request/workflow SQL-store suites and `go test -tags=integration ./outbox -run '^$'` passed. A disposable tmpfs MySQL 8.4 service ran `KORA_OUTBOX_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' go test -tags=integration ./outbox -run '^TestMySQLSourceSequenceCommitsWithOutboxTransaction$' -count=1 -timeout=120s`; it passed and the service was stopped. The test verifies committed sequences `1,2` and reuse of sequence `2` after an event transaction rollback. Rebuild cutoff and flush serialization are still open.

**Verification after sequence integration (2026-10-14):** Full Engine `go test ./... -count=1`, focused `go test -race ./outbox ./requests/sqlstore ./workflow/sqlstore ./analytics -count=1`, `go vet ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-contracts`, `go test -tags=integration ./kernel -run '^$' -count=1` (compile-only), and `git diff --check` passed. The tagged kernel delivery integration was compile-checked only; it was not run because its existing helper targets the shared default MySQL DSN. No disposable PostgreSQL or LibSQL source-sequence integration was run.

**Browser draft persistence (2026-10-14):** Engine embedded UI and standalone Studio now reject failed document-draft writes with `DraftPersistenceError`, distinguish a persisted draft from a failed metadata-index update, wait for IndexedDB transaction completion/abort before reporting success, and show autosave failures to the user. Draft cleanup errors after a successful server create/update are surfaced separately and do not turn the successful business save into a failure. Added quota-failure, partial-index-write, and failed-clear tests in both UIs. Exact checks: `bun run test` (embedded UI: 31 files/153 tests; standalone Studio: 43 files/192 tests), `bun run build` in both, and `bunx eslint src/lib/draft-storage.ts src/lib/draft-storage.test.ts` in both passed. Linting the touched route files remains red on pre-existing hook-order and explicit-`any` errors; no new lint issue was reported in the storage helper/test paths. Other browser storage features still require an inventory before claiming complete persistence-error handling.

**Other browser persistence paths (2026-10-14):** Page Manifest editor local drafts now propagate localStorage read/write/delete errors, show the draft error in the editor, and report cleanup failure separately after a successful server save. Console auth now guards storage access at module initialization and API token reads; if server login succeeds but its token/email cannot be persisted, the store stays unauthenticated and reports that the session was not saved. Logout and invalid-session cleanup always clear in-memory credentials even when storage deletion fails. Added quota/security failure tests for Page Manifest drafts and console login in both UI repositories. Current full UI checks: Engine embedded UI `bun run test` (32 files/157 tests), Studio `bun run test` (44 files/196 tests), both `bun run build`, targeted ESLint on console auth/API and manifest draft helpers, and Engine `make check-contracts` passed. The first targeted lint run found two existing explicit-`any` casts in the manifest-helper test; replaced them with `unknown` casts and reran targeted ESLint cleanly in both clients. Existing Vite chunk and dynamic-import warnings remain. Remaining browser persistence paths outside drafts, onboarding, generated-page state, auth, theme, recent/favorite preferences, setup nudge, dashboard onboarding indicator and POS shift context still need review.

**Onboarding persistence regression (2026-10-14):** Rechecked `ConsoleHandler.persistOnboarding`: it returns errors from both the durable job-state write and its checkpoint write, and active provisioning state is persisted before router publication. Added API-level unit coverage proving neither failure is returned as success. `go test ./api -run '^TestPersistOnboardingPropagatesDurableStateWriteFailures$' -count=1`, the same command with `-race`, and `git diff --check` passed. This tests persistence boundary failures, not crash recovery of a real provisioning operation. Standalone Studio onboarding now routes every localStorage read/write/delete through an error-reporting helper; storage denial leaves the server-backed current conversation usable and shows a recovery warning instead of aborting the event handler. Embedded onboarding sessionStorage failures no longer abort conversation loading, and failure to persist the conversation pointer is shown while the server conversation is loaded. POS shift context reads/writes now fail safely; if shift creation succeeds but browser persistence fails, the UI warns the operator to keep the register open because refresh may require a new shift. Setup-nudge and dashboard preference reads are also guarded. Added denied-storage tests. Current checks: standalone Studio `bun run test` passed (48 files/200 tests), embedded UI `bun run test` passed (34 files/159 tests), `bun run build` passed in both with existing chunk/dynamic-import warnings, helper-focused ESLint passed, and `git diff --check` passed in Engine and standalone Studio.

**Embedded POS session persistence follow-up (2026-10-14):** Embedded PaymentPanel now guards `sessionStorage` reads and writes and keeps newly created shift/till IDs in component state when browser persistence is denied. It warns the operator that a refresh can lose the browser-side pointer, while continuing the server-backed setup. Action result handling now narrows unknown values instead of using `any`. Added blocked-storage and quota tests. Embedded `bun run test` passed (35 files/162 tests), `bun run build` passed with the existing ineffective dynamic-import warning, ESLint passed for PaymentPanel and the new helper/tests, and `git diff --check` passed.

**Transactional analytics replay receipts (2026-10-14):** Replaced the worker's in-memory-only crash window with per-event `(site,event_id)` receipts in `_kora_analytics_event_receipt`. Event contributions remain associated with their WAL IDs until flush; receipt reservation, accepted rollup deltas, and workflow transitions commit together. Receipt duplicates skip only their associated contribution, and WAL acknowledgment remains after commit. Analytics bootstrap moves from v1 to v2 and installs the table on existing databases. `analytics/bus.go` now documents this recovery behavior. Updated the executable spec to use stable event identity because bounded WAL batch boundaries may change after restart. Added restart-style receipt-skip and receipt-write-failure retention unit tests, dialect DDL assertions and v1-to-v2 bootstrap regression; the disposable MySQL 8.4 integration also asserts the persisted rollup remains 1 after replay. Checks passed: Engine `go test ./... -count=1`, `go test -race ./analytics -count=1`, `go vet ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-contracts`, and `git diff --check`; disposable MySQL 8.4 tests passed for `TestMySQLAnalyticsReceiptDeduplicatesReplayAfterCommit` and `TestMySQLBackfillFailurePreservesPreviouslyCommittedRollups` using a temporary tmpfs container and isolated databases, then the container was stopped and removed. Exact gated invocation: `KORA_ANALYTICS_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='<loopback disposable DSN>' go test -tags integration ./analytics -run '^(TestMySQLAnalyticsReceiptDeduplicatesReplayAfterCommit|TestMySQLBackfillFailurePreservesPreviouslyCommittedRollups)$' -count=1`.

**Analytics rebuild/flush coordination (2026-10-14):** Rebuild and every rollup flush now serialize through a tenant-scoped SQL lock row. `BackfillContext` holds that transaction lock through a repeatable source snapshot, range replacement and checkpoint commit; it records source `last_sequence` by DocType/from-date scope. The worker reserves event receipts under the same lock and omits only deltas covered by the committed snapshot, preserving out-of-range changes and workflow transitions. Historical outbox envelopes recover their sequence from `_kora_outbox.source_sequence` before local/NATS delivery. CLI backfill's duplicate, non-atomic SQL loops were removed in favor of this shared path; CLI and NATS handlers propagate cancellation. Updated the spec to define why a transaction-held row lock is sufficient and to document range filtering. Added scope/sequence unit tests, schema migration coverage, and disposable MySQL and PostgreSQL integration tests. MySQL 8.4 proves v2→v4 bootstrap migration, replay deduplication, rollback preservation and blocked-flush/unlock handoff. PostgreSQL 16 proves rebuild cutoff filtering and a concurrent flush blocked until rebuild commit. PostgreSQL testing exposed and fixed dialect handling for grouped dimension concatenation and source table identifier quoting. `go test ./analytics ./outbox ./cli -count=1`, `go test -race ./analytics ./outbox ./cli -count=1`, `go vet ./...`, full Engine `go test ./... -count=1`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-contracts`, import graph check, `git diff --check`, and disposable MySQL/PostgreSQL analytics integrations passed. The LibSQL cutoff test is present but skipped because neither a local SQLite driver nor a disposable remote endpoint is configured; this is not LibSQL verification. Analytics receipt/checkpoint retention and real LibSQL verification remain open gates.

**Tenant-owned fenced analytics rebuild runner (2026-10-14):** The HTTP route persists the job and signals a single-worker `analytics.RebuildRunner` stored in the tenant's immutable service snapshot and launched through `runtimeContextRegistry.Go`; runtime retirement cancels and joins it before closing the tenant pool. The tenant runner is active with or without NATS. NATS rebuild deliveries now only wake the durable SQL-backed runner, and the notification consumer also has a registered join handle. Analytics bootstrap advances to v4 and adds `_kora_analytics_rebuild_lease` without rewriting existing job IDs. Claims use a DB-clock expiry, unique owner and monotonic fence; a 10-second heartbeat renews the 45-second lease, and final state transitions/requeue require a matching live fence. The runner polls persisted jobs after missed notifications, acquires the tenant's bounded active-work admission, returns admission-rejected/canceled work to the queue, and recovers crashed jobs through expired lease takeover. Added SQL-mock coverage plus real concurrent-claim/takeover/stale-completion tests. The full `go test -tags integration ./analytics -count=1 -timeout=180s` suite passed against disposable MySQL 8.4 and PostgreSQL 16, including v2→v4 migration; both services were removed. Focused and race analytics/API/CLI tests and the full Engine suite, vet/build, contracts, import graph and diff checks passed. Strict global active-work limits across replicas remain a G3 gate; the lease behavior is not verified on actual LibSQL transport.

**Configuration activation recovery (2026-10-14):** Added the tenant `_kora_config_operation` journal to system bootstrap v17. Activation stores the immutable target snapshot, derives a fresh DDL plan from live schema on every attempt, quarantines still-present removed fields, records applying/validated phases, and commits the activated journal phase with config/version state. Conditional phase updates prevent concurrent attempts from moving a terminal journal backward. A retry after the metadata commit repairs runtime registry/cache/event publication and finalizes the journal. PostgreSQL integration simulates interrupted finalization and failed-transaction retry. Disposable MySQL 8.4 integration bootstraps a real tenant schema and runs an isolated child test process whose wrapped driver exits with code 86 immediately after the first of two target-column DDL statements succeeds. The parent confirms the durable operation remains applying and the schema is partially upgraded, then retries through the live-schema planner, injects a history-write failure, and verifies another retry completes without duplicate DDL. `KORA_CONFIG_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='<loopback disposable DSN>' go test -tags=integration ./api -run '^TestMySQLConfigActivationJournalRetry$' -count=1 -timeout=120s` passed on a tmpfs-only MySQL 8.4.11 container, which was stopped and removed. Focused `go test ./configstore ./api -count=1`, `go test -race ./configstore ./api -count=1`, `go vet ./configstore ./api`, disposable PostgreSQL 16 `TestLivePostgresConfigVersionRoutes`, and the tagged MySQL test passed. Full Engine `go test ./... -count=1`, `go vet ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-contracts`, and `git diff --check` passed before the child-process harness; the harness itself compiled and passed in the tagged integration run.

**Configuration rollback recovery (2026-10-14):** Rollback now accepts/reuses `Idempotency-Key`, records the expected active version and result version in the same operation journal, replans from live schema, commits the activated phase with config/version writes, and finalizes the runtime registry/cache/journal after commit. The MySQL and PostgreSQL tenant routes lock and recheck the expected active version before commit; stale concurrent restores return conflict. A restore planner retries field/table quarantine renames idempotently, restores uniquely matching quarantined fields/tables (preserving their values), and refuses ambiguous candidates for operator selection. Engine and standalone UI rollback clients use operation envelopes and retain the idempotency key in session storage for retries, with in-tab fallback if storage is denied. Planner unit tests cover partial field rename, no repeated DDL, table restoration, ambiguity rejection and operation identity. Disposable MySQL 8.4 integration injects failure after a non-transactional field rename, retries with the same key, verifies data remains quarantined, then restores the field and confirms the original value. Disposable PostgreSQL 16 config-version route integration passed. `go test ./api ./doctype ./configstore -count=1`, focused race tests, vet, SDK-consumer route helper tests (7 each), ESLint and builds passed. This does not cover forced process death inside a multi-statement DDL plan or actual LibSQL transport.

**Recoverable CLI config import (2026-10-14):** The CLI now stores the parsed immutable config snapshot in `_kora_config_operation` before tenant-config writes, using a tenant-scoped snapshot digest as its stable operation ID. An interrupted pre-version import can be rerun with the same pack to repeat idempotent upserts; the version row and transition to `validated` commit in the same transaction, and the version ID is retained as the recovery reference. Schema migration is rerun against the live schema and `completed` is recorded only after it succeeds. Failures preserve their phase and error in the journal. Unit and transaction phase/stale-phase SQL-mock tests pass. A disposable MySQL 8.4 integration installs a trigger that fails version creation after config upserts, verifies the applying phase/error is durable, removes the trigger, retries the same pack, reruns the completed operation, and proves one operation and one active version remain. Exact command: `KORA_CONFIG_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='<loopback disposable DSN>' go test -tags=integration ./cli -run '^TestMySQLConfigImportRetriesJournaledPackWithoutDuplicateVersion$' -count=1 -timeout=120s`. The container used tmpfs-only storage and was stopped/removed after the test.

**Actual remote LibSQL transport (2026-10-14):** Added opt-in remote-endpoint support to `analytics/rebuild_libsql_integration_test.go` and an integration for config import. The official disposable `sqld` HTTP service ran with only a tmpfs database. `KORA_CONFIG_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' go test -tags=integration ./cli -run '^TestLibSQLConfigImportUsesRemoteTransportAndReusesOperation$' -count=1 -timeout=120s` passed through initial import and completed-operation replay. `KORA_ANALYTICS_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' go test -tags=integration ./analytics -run '^TestLibSQLRebuildCutoffAndLaterEvents$' -count=1 -timeout=120s` passed cutoff, deduplication and post-cutoff event checks. Both server containers were stopped and removed. This proves these import and analytics paths on remote HTTP transport, not every LibSQL API, authorization and outbox scenario.

**IndexedDB queue failure handling (2026-10-14):** Both browser clients now resolve IndexedDB reads/writes/deletes only on transaction completion, reject blocked upgrades and transaction aborts, and retain in-memory fallback data when a persistent clear fails. Regression tests simulate request success followed by transaction abort, blocked upgrade and preservation of the memory queue. Focused offline-queue tests passed in embedded and standalone UIs (6 tests each); both production builds passed. Changed-file ESLint passed. Embedded full-tree lint was attempted and remains red with 390 errors/14 warnings across the already-dirty UI tree; this was not caused by the touched offline-queue files.

**Browser-engine IndexedDB recovery (2026-10-14):** Tested the embedded UI queue module in isolated headless Google Chrome 152.0.7977.82 using the skill's local CDP driver and a temporary Vite page. The page used real IndexedDB, allowed a `put` request to succeed, then aborted its native transaction from the request success event. The queue API rejected the write, preserved the prior committed item, successfully updated it in a later transaction, and cleared it. At test time the standalone `offline-queue.ts` was byte-identical to the embedded source; standalone still has its own unit failure-path tests. The temporary harness, dev server and browser profile were removed. This closes the browser transaction recovery gate for the shared queue implementation; it is not a browser support matrix or browser storage power-loss test.

**Analytics replay-record retention review (2026-10-14):** The specification now explicitly gates pruning analytics event receipts and rebuild checkpoints on a coordinated replay horizon. Inspection confirmed local WAL recovery files, durable outbox history, broker redelivery and restored backups currently have no shared enforced maximum age; arbitrary age-based receipt deletion could duplicate rollup effects, and checkpoint deletion could remove rebuild filtering. No unsafe cleanup was added. This coordinated source-retention contract belongs to G3 and remains open. After this review, `go test ./analytics ./outbox ./api -count=1`, `go test -race ./analytics ./outbox ./api -count=1`, `go vet ./analytics ./outbox ./api`, and `git diff --check` passed.

**Realtime replay connection release (2026-10-14):** A real MySQL replay test using a one-connection pool exposed nested connection acquisition: replay held the outbox result rows open while owner authorization queried the current document, deadlocking the tenant pool. `replayRealtime` now reads the bounded 501-row page, checks iteration errors, closes the result set, then performs authorization and delivery. The new disposable MySQL regression verifies owner replay before transfer, denies the former owner afterward despite historical event data, agrees with the NATS sanitizer for the new owner, and filters hidden/historical fields. The test passed with a tmpfs-only MySQL 8.4.11 container, which was stopped after the run. Exact command: `KORA_CONFIG_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='<loopback disposable DSN>' go test -tags=integration ./api -run '^TestMySQLRealtimeReplayAndNATSUseCurrentOwner$' -count=1 -timeout=120s`. Focused replay/auth tests, full `go test -race ./api -count=1`, `go vet ./api`, and `git diff --check` passed.

**PostgreSQL realtime authorization parity (2026-10-14):** Added `api/realtime_auth_postgres_integration_test.go` using an isolated per-test schema. Against disposable PostgreSQL 16, the one-connection replay path, live owner transfer, delayed NATS envelope, and hidden/historical field filtering all passed. Exact command: `KORA_API_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN='<disposable DSN>' go test -tags=integration ./api -run '^TestPostgresRealtimeReplayAndNATSUseCurrentOwner$' -count=1 -timeout=120s`. **Transactional outbox dialect coverage (2026-10-14):** Added disposable PostgreSQL and actual-HTTP LibSQL integrations for local consumer receipt/effect transactions and external publication acknowledgment ordering. Both prove handler failure rolls back receipt plus local effect, retry commits both once, local completion leaves provider publication pending, provider acknowledgment marks it published, and redelivery skips the completed effect. PostgreSQL uses an isolated schema; LibSQL used a fresh tmpfs-only `sqld` endpoint. Exact commands: `KORA_OUTBOX_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN='<disposable DSN>' go test -tags=integration ./outbox -run '^TestPostgresTransactionalConsumerReceiptAndPublication$' -count=1 -timeout=120s` and `KORA_OUTBOX_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' go test -tags=integration ./outbox -run '^TestLibSQLTransactionalConsumerReceiptAndPublication$' -count=1 -timeout=120s`. An initial PostgreSQL run caught non-rebound placeholders in test-only assertion queries; after correcting them both integrations passed. API-independent outbox unit, race, vet and diff checks passed; tmpfs DB services were stopped and removed.

**NATS consumer acknowledgment recovery (2026-10-14):** `natsprovider.Consumer.Run` now joins its per-delivery lease-extension goroutine before settlement, returns lease-extension errors, uses synchronous JetStream ACK confirmation after handler success, leaves delivery unsettled when shutdown cancels the handler, and surfaces NAK, dead-letter publication and TERM errors. Dead-letter publication uses the source delivery/message sequence as a stable JetStream message ID to reduce duplicates when termination fails after publication. Embedded-JetStream tests cover lease renewal during a handler longer than AckWait, ACK/NAK errors after connection loss, dead-letter publish failure, and shutdown retaining one pending acknowledgment without publishing a dead letter. Reconnection tests prove messages remain durable and redeliver to a new consumer after ACK/NAK/dead-letter publish failures. `go test ./natsprovider ./outbox -count=1`, `go test -race ./natsprovider ./outbox -count=1`, `go vet ./natsprovider ./outbox`, integration compile checks for API/outbox, and `git diff --check` passed; the focused failure/recovery matrix passed 5 consecutive runs. The final full Engine `go test ./... -count=1`, NATS/outbox race tests, `go vet ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-contracts`, and diff check passed after the shutdown change. TERM failure injection is covered by the subsequent durable-redelivery test; automatic owner restart after `Run` returns an infrastructure error remains unverified.
**Runtime-owned NATS consumer retry (2026-10-14):** CLI now supervises tenant analytics-rebuild and NATS-side-effect consumers with a 250 ms exponential retry delay capped at 10 seconds. Generation cancellation interrupts the retry timer; the outer runtime join registry still owns and joins the supervisor. `natsprovider.Consumer.Run` serializes runs on one consumer and unsubscribes its pull subscription before returning, allowing the same durable consumer instance to reconnect safely. A real embedded-JetStream test deletes the durable while a handler is active, forces synchronous ACK failure, observes the supervisor recreate the durable, verifies the same event is redelivered and acknowledged, and confirms cancellation stops the supervisor. CLI supervisor tests passed five consecutive runs. After this addition, full Engine `go test ./... -count=1`, race tests for CLI/NATS/outbox, `go vet ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-contracts`, and `git diff --check` passed. TERM failure injection is covered by the subsequent durable-redelivery test.

**NATS TERM failure recovery (2026-10-14):** Added `TestConsumerTermFailureAfterDeadLetterLeavesSourceRedeliverable`, which closes the consumer connection only after the dead-letter publish has been confirmed, then invokes the real `nats.Msg.Term` and verifies the error is returned. A reconnecting consumer receives and successfully acknowledges the original source event at delivery attempt 2 or higher; the stream contains only the original event and its one dead-letter record. The test excludes the dead-letter subject from the consumer's filter, matching the intended event-consumer routing. A private termination function defaults to the real `Msg.Term` and permits deterministic failure injection. The targeted test passed five consecutive race runs; `go test ./natsprovider ./cli -count=1`, `go test -race ./natsprovider ./cli -count=1`, `go vet ./natsprovider ./cli`, and `git diff --check` passed. This proves TERM failure leaves source work recoverable; it does not independently prove process-level supervisor restart under an external NATS outage.

**Safe AI analytics query bounds refreshed (2026-10-15):** Revalidated the AI read path while answering whether AI can query data outside preset reports. It supports governed semantic models, measures, dimensions and filters; it accepts no raw SQL/table/join/executable query text. Engine validation now bounds result rows and date range even outside the model-provider schema, applies filters before SQL limits, limits SQL result rows, uses dialect-specific time-bucket expressions, avoids monthly rollups when date filters require daily precision, rejects ambiguous multi-value scalar operators, and keys analytics cache entries on every query input (including filters, dimensions and limits). Catalog visibility denies hidden/password/layout fields and owner-only aggregates. Updated `target-state-spec.md` with this executable read-query boundary. Exact checks passed: `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test ./analytics ./api/ai ./api -run 'Test(AnalyticsQuery|SemanticRollup|QueryEngine|RelatedAnalytics|AnalyticsModels|AnalyticsInsight|Analytics.*Request|Analytics.*Query|Analytics.*Permission)' -count=1`; same command with `-race`; `go vet ./analytics ./api/ai ./api`; and `git diff --check`. These are focused unit/race checks; real-dialect analytics query grouping/filter execution was not run in this pass. No schema or public contract change. G7 remains the active group.

### G2 Process and tenant lifecycle

**Status:** verified.

**Changes and contracts:** Engine `net.LoadedSite` owns typed service snapshots, tenant session managers, and a generation object in `internal/tenantruntime`. Each generation combines request handles (release-once), cancellation/join ownership for workers, and retirement admission fencing. `SiteRouter` acquires these handles for all host, cookie, and path-routed requests; replacing/removing a site retires that exact generation, and request drain waits for handles before resources close. Runtime identity fields distinguish tenant config revision, global directory-feed revision, runtime generation, and opaque stable-resource identity. The global directory revision is initialized from the startup snapshot cursor, advanced on feed and resync application independently of config reloads, and injected into request context. The target specification now includes this field in activation and states explicitly that durable worker fencing tokens remain claim/store state, not runtime identity. The old CLI `runtimeContextRegistry` keyed by opaque site/revision strings is removed; worker cancellation and joining now act on the runtime object itself. The process rate limiter no longer starts an unowned cleanup goroutine; it removes stale entries opportunistically at request-time intervals. `auth.SystemGuard` now cancels and joins its expiring-session cleanup loop through `Close`, and CLI `runServe` owns its shutdown on both normal and startup-error returns. CLI no longer creates per-site service maps or starts tenant service bundles. The startup loader attaches storage and work-admission references; typed `internal/engine.TenantServiceInstaller` builds the request, storage, analytics, script/secret, realtime, webhook, outbox, relay, and rebuild services and starts tenant-owned workers for both initial activation and hot reload. Routed tenant API and public-file handlers receive no per-site fallback maps. Cleanup and shutdown stop resources from the tenant snapshot; async hooks resolve the active loaded snapshot. Failed startup assembly cancels tenant worker contexts, stops analytics/webhook/relay services, joins session managers and closes DB pools; partial startup collection also joins every successfully loaded tenant session manager when a sibling tenant load fails. Compatible directory reloads compare an opaque hash of the fully resolved database target and pool limits; matching generations reuse the DB pool and tenant session manager. On successful swap, the retired generation relinquishes those stable handles before its drain closes generation-owned workers; failed publication leaves reused resources available to the active generation. Retired-generation drain goroutines are now joined during process shutdown before the process-owned script runner closes. Webhook workers receive the tenant generation context, cancel in-flight SQL/HTTP calls on retirement, and `Stop` joins their listener worker before pools close. The process scheduler is retained by `runServe`, stopped and joined before tenant pools close, and deferred for early exits. Each tenant generation registers a join handle before launching recovery, rebuild, realtime bridge, outbox publisher, and NATS side-effect workers; retiring one generation cancels and joins those workers without affecting a replacement generation. Scheduler ORM reads carry worker cancellation to `QueryContext`. Added `/readyz`, which checks the canonical platform directory with a two-second context deadline and is bypassed by tenant-host routing; Docker liveness now uses this readiness path. Cold-load and hot-reload analytics pipelines construct one `MultiBus` before starting the analytics worker; the worker subscribes to a fanout listener instead of competing with the fanout reader on the base channel. `MultiBus.Close` now joins its forwarding goroutine, and startup rollback/generation retirement use that close path. Durable WAL batch scanning handles worker-listener overflow.

**Checks and results:** This turn: `go test ./site ./cli ./net ./scheduler ./orm -count=1` and `go test -race ./site ./cli ./net ./scheduler ./orm -count=1` passed, including opaque connection-identity, resource transfer, scheduler cancel/join and canceled DB-read tests. After readiness changes, `go test ./cli ./net -count=1`, full Engine `go test ./...`, `go test -race ./cli ./net ./kernel ./orm ./requests/... ./outbox ./notifications ./site -count=1`, `go vet ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, and `git diff --check` passed. The fanout join addition passed the current full `go test ./... -count=1` and `go test -race ./analytics ./cli -count=1` checks. Compose config check passed. The disposable image build compiled the standalone and embedded UI plus Engine binary but failed during final BuildKit metadata output because the filesystem was full; the two task-created tagged images were removed and 6.287 GB of reclaimable BuildKit cache was cleared to recover 3.5 GB of disk; Docker volumes, containers and other images were left intact. The local stack was not started.

**Completion evidence and later gates:** `internal/tenantruntime.Manager` owns stable-ID acquire, atomic activation, retirement, bounded retired generations, idle fencing and retryable close; `SiteRouter` delegates its active index and routing acquisition to it. Typed `Engine.New(ctx, Config)` owns process assembly and rollback; `cli.runServe` parses configuration, runs Engine, and returns errors. `ProcessResources` registers worker starters, `Engine.Run` starts them before listener readiness, and registrations for a live tenant activation start immediately. Worker start/stop joins cover process-owned workers, tenant-generation workers, request-scoped streams and bounded/joined startup helpers. Startup failure injection covers process, shared-provider, tenant and HTTP assembly boundaries, with lower-level acquisition, SessionManager, bind-failure, reload and shutdown regressions. The exported compatibility `auth.NewSiteGuard(db)` remains explicitly closable; Engine production assembly uses request or explicitly owned session managers. Exact final checks are recorded in the Config-driven Engine and worker-start checkpoints below. Eager tenant loading and residency eviction are G3 work; `api.SiteRuntimeServices` compatibility wrappers remain until external callers migrate; action/event workers and bounded-queue readiness are G6/G3 work. These later items do not block G2.

**Engine-owned HTTP application composition (2026-10-14):** Added typed `ApplicationConfig` and `BuildApplication` in `internal/engine`. Engine now constructs the Gin router, trusted proxies, security/request/tenant/work-admission/rate-limit middleware, fallback registry, transaction manager, and auth/API/workspace routes. It returns the router and owned dependencies together so CLI can register only platform/reload/console routes and build the listener. A regression verifies the builder returns all owned dependencies and expected auth/versioned request routes. Checks passed: `go test -race ./internal/engine ./cli ./api -count=1`, full Engine `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, import graph regeneration/check (456 internal edges), `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check`. `runServe` still owns most platform/process acquisition and composition; the specification's `Engine.New(ctx, Config)` and full startup-stage failure injection remain open G2 work.

**Tenant-manager Add retirement join (2026-10-14):** Audited runtime replacement paths and confirmed `SiteRouter.AddSite` retires its prior generation in a manager-owned goroutine rather than the CLI `RetiredRuntimeDrains` group. `Manager.Close` is the join owner for this path. Added a regression that blocks the retired analytics worker, proves `Manager.Close` returns an incomplete timeout without closing the old tenant DB, then releases the worker and proves a retried close joins retirement and closes both tenant generations. Checks passed: `go test -race ./internal/tenantruntime ./internal/engine ./net ./cli ./api -count=1`, full Engine `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, import graph regeneration/check (456 internal edges), `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check`; the new manager test also passed 20 race-enabled repetitions. G2 remains open for whole-startup stage injection, `Engine.New(ctx, Config)`, and remaining process assembly ownership.

**Engine-owned tenant service installation (2026-10-14):** Added typed `TenantServiceConfig` and `NewTenantServiceInstaller` in `internal/engine`. Initial tenant activation and hot reload now share one installer for direct-task request services, storage, script/secret stores, analytics tables and `MultiBus`, analytics/webhook/relay workers, rebuild runner, optional NATS consumers, outbox publisher and human-wait recovery. The installer publishes the candidate service snapshot before fallible setup so normal `LoadedSite.Close` owns partial rollback. Startup loading retains its already-created storage backend and endpoint work admission; Engine reuses that backend rather than replacing/leaking it while installing the full bundle. Startup loading no longer constructs analytics services. Moved direct-task request composition out of CLI and removed the duplicate startup worker loops. Regressions verify failed-candidate close ownership and reuse of startup storage without invoking the resolver twice. Checks passed after the storage reuse correction: `go test -race ./internal/engine ./cli ./api -count=1`, full Engine `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, import graph regeneration/check (457 internal edges), `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check`; the preceding full-scope race run over Engine/CLI/API/NATS/workflow packages also passed before that narrow storage correction. G2 remains open: most process dependency construction and server assembly remain in CLI; full-stage `runServe` injection, SessionManager rollback, and the final process/tenant worker ownership audit are outstanding.

**Engine-owned deletion recovery worker (2026-10-14):** Moved the pending tenant-deletion retry loop from `cli/serve.go` into typed `internal/engine.PendingDeletionWorker`. Engine now owns its readiness gate, retry timeout/cadence, cancellation and join through `ProcessResources`; CLI starts it before listener startup and releases the gate from the ready callback. Tests cover join before dependent script-runner close, retention and retryable shutdown when a worker does not honor cancellation before the deadline, and stopping before readiness without starting recovery. `go test -race ./internal/engine ./cli -count=1`, full Engine `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, full Engine `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check` passed. G2 remains open for full-stage startup injection, remaining process assembly in CLI, and the final worker-ownership audit.

**Engine-owned cold tenant loading (2026-10-14):** Moved startup tenant concurrency, per-tenant storage/admission/runtime acquisition, startup result assembly, and sibling rollback from `cli/site_loader.go` into typed `internal/engine.LoadStartupTenants`. CLI now supplies its validated directory snapshot, binary version, platform DSN identity and storage resolver; Engine returns one coherent runtime/domain/primary-DB result or closes every successful sibling and joins close errors. Moved rollback and trace-sanitization tests alongside the Engine owner and added injected failures at storage resolution, work admission and runtime activation, each proving acquired storage closes. `go test -race ./internal/engine ./cli -count=1`, full Engine `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, full Engine `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-contracts`, regenerated graph (457 internal edges), `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check` passed. Cross-repository check first detected the expected stale graph and passed after regeneration. G2 still has broad process assembly in CLI and no whole-`runServe` startup failure matrix; this change closes only cold tenant acquisition ownership.

**Webhook worker follow-up (2026-10-14):** Added `StartContext`; the old `Start` remains as a compatibility wrapper. Stop removes the subscription, cancels the worker context, and joins its goroutine; HTTP requests and extension lookups use that context, while outcome bookkeeping gets a bounded cleanup context. `TestWorkerStopCancelsAndJoinsInFlightDelivery` holds a webhook request open and verifies stop cancels and joins the listener. `go test ./webhook ./cli -count=1`, `go test -race ./webhook ./cli -count=1`, `go vet ./webhook ./cli`, full Engine `go test ./... -count=1`, `go vet ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, and `make check-contracts` passed. **HTTP and session ownership follow-up (2026-10-14):** Auto-TLS redirect HTTP serving is now created only after the main listener binds; shutdown and forced close stop and join both listeners. The CLI constructs its auth manager as an explicitly owned process resource, shares it with `SiteGuard`, and joins it before closing tenant/platform pools (deferred close covers early startup returns). **Process context, scheduler, and startup rollback (2026-10-14):** `runServe` now creates one process context for startup tracing, platform/tenant startup work, NATS bootstrap, directory consumers, async hooks, deletion retry, and scheduled jobs; the owning cleanup cancels it, and platform-pool closure cancels on early returns. The process scheduler now has synchronized one-shot start/stop transitions: repeated/concurrent starts cannot duplicate workers, repeated/concurrent stops cancel and join, late job registration is ignored, and the CLI passes its process context instead of `context.Background()`. Telemetry setup now delays publishing global providers until all exporters initialize and shuts down already-created providers in reverse order if a later exporter fails; rollback errors remain joined with the startup cause. Exact checks after the scheduler/process-context change: `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, `go test -race ./scheduler ./cli ./net ./auth ./site -count=1`, `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, and `git diff --check` passed; focused scheduler/CLI race and vet checks passed after the late-registration assertion. Telemetry exporter tests and rollback reverse-order/error aggregation tests passed under ordinary and race runs, with `go vet ./cli` and `git diff --check`. Whole-process injected startup failures and reload/shutdown integration remain unproven.

**HTTP startup fault boundary (2026-10-14):** The listener/signal lifecycle is now a single `serveHTTPAndShutdown` path; it always unregisters and joins the signal watcher and runs assembled-runtime cleanup even when the HTTP bind fails. An occupied-port test proves readiness is withheld and cleanup runs once on this late startup failure. Exact checks: `go test ./cli -run 'Test(HTTPBindFailureRunsRuntimeShutdown|ShutdownSignalWatcherStopsAndJoins)$' -count=10`, the same under `-race -count=3`, `go vet ./cli`, and `git diff --check` passed. This test exercises the actual HTTP stage helper, but full staged `runServe` injection and runtime reload/shutdown integration remain open.

**Tenant publication/reload regressions (2026-10-14):** `SiteRouter` now has explicit acceptance coverage for the coherent-snapshot guarantees used by reload: an alias-conflict replacement returns before draining/removing the current runtime, and a replacement with the same config revision still gets a new runtime generation while only the old generation drains. Exact checks: `go test ./net -run 'Test(FailedReplaceSitePreservesActiveRuntime|ReplaceSiteSameRevisionStillPublishesNewGeneration|ReplaceSiteDrainsRequestsAgainstRetiredRuntime)$' -count=20`, the same under `-race -count=5`, `go vet ./net`, and `git diff --check` passed. Router publication is verified in isolation; end-to-end directory-consumer reload plus runtime worker drain/shutdown remains open.

**Current G2 verification (2026-10-14):** After the HTTP lifecycle helper and router reload regressions, full Engine `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, and `git diff --check` passed. Focused HTTP fault tests passed 10 repetitions and 3 race repetitions; focused router replacement tests passed 20 repetitions and 5 race repetitions. This raises confidence in the shutdown and publication components but does not satisfy the missing `internal/engine` owner or end-to-end reload/process fault-injection gates.

**Shutdown error propagation (2026-10-14):** The Engine cleanup callback now returns an error, and `Engine.Shutdown`/`Run` propagate it after the once-only cleanup. CLI shutdown aggregates session-manager, script-runner, and tenant-manager close failures while continuing later cleanup steps. A regression verifies the caller observes a cleanup failure and repeated shutdown does not repeat cleanup. Exact checks: `go test ./internal/engine ./cli -count=1`, `go test -race ./internal/engine ./cli -count=1`, `go vet ./internal/engine ./cli`, full Engine `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check`, `make check-contracts`, and `git diff --check` passed. G2 remains open: CLI still composes most process dependencies; stage-injected `runServe` failures, full worker ownership audit, and process-level reload/shutdown fault tests remain unverified.

**Typed process owner and bounded joins (2026-10-14):** Moved the shutdown sequence into `internal/engine.ProcessResources`, which `Engine` now owns through a narrow `ShutdownOwner` contract. CLI supplies concrete process and tenant resources rather than a local cleanup closure. Shutdown cancels producers and joins the scheduler, deletion and directory-feed workers, stops tenant sidecars and generation workers, then closes/drains the async-hook queue and joins its independent worker while tenant pools and script execution remain available. A timed-out async/sidecar/tenant join retains dependent resources; a tenant-manager drain failure leaves NATS open. Retired-generation drains remain tracked while handles are live, and the Engine owner bounds its join. `Workers.StopContext` and `Runtime.Close(ctx)` now honor shutdown deadlines and allow retry after the worker exits. Scheduler gained `StopContext`, a shared join channel, and retry-after-timeout behavior; Engine retains dependent resources if a bounded join expires. Tests cover error propagation, once-only Engine cleanup, scheduler and tenant-worker stop timeout/retry, dependency retention on async/deletion/tenant-drain timeout, and successful worker join before script-runner closure. Startup rollback cancels and joins the async worker; if that join exceeds its deadline, it retains shared resources rather than closing pools under the worker. Exact checks after these changes: `go test ./internal/tenantruntime ./internal/engine ./cli ./scheduler -count=1`, the same under `-race`, full Engine `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-contracts`, import graph regeneration and `--check` (434 internal edges), and `git diff --check` passed. The owner is now explicit, but Engine construction and most startup composition remain in `cli/serve.go`; failure injection across all acquisitions, platform DB/telemetry ordering, and end-to-end process reload remain open.

**Async-hook admission during drain (2026-10-14):** The process queue is now wrapped by a close-safe bounded sink: concurrent enqueue/close cannot panic, accepted items remain available for draining, closed submissions return an error, and full queues return a counted enqueue failure instead of blocking the single consumer. Added full/closed and concurrent close/enqueue tests. This is a count-bounded in-memory queue; it is not durable and has no byte budget, so it does not satisfy G3 or durable-effect requirements. Covered by the exact focused and full test commands in the preceding lifecycle entry.

**Worker audit and incomplete-shutdown retention (2026-10-14):** A full production `go`-statement scan found console onboarding as a process goroutine outside the earlier worker registry. `ConsoleHandler` now prevents new onboarding job admission after shutdown starts, cancels and joins in-flight jobs with the Engine deadline, and the process owner retains platform-dependent resources if that join times out. Added a close timeout/retry test. The process owner now marks incomplete shutdown explicitly; after that marker, CLI defers do not issue unbounded joins or close platform/session/scheduler dependencies beneath live workers. Tenant `Workers.StopContext` and `Runtime.Close(ctx)` likewise bound joins and leave pools available for retry. The audit also rechecked signal watcher, startup loader, directory monitor/feed, deletion retry, async hook, scheduler, tenant sidecars/workers, retired runtime drains, session sweepers, and telemetry cleanup paths. Exact checks: `go test -race ./api ./internal/engine ./cli ./internal/tenantruntime ./scheduler -count=1`, scoped `go vet`, full Engine `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, full Engine `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-contracts`, import graph regeneration/`--check` (434 internal edges), and `git diff --check` passed. Full process composition and acquisition-by-acquisition failure injection remain open; this source scan does not prove subsystem goroutine ownership outside the inspected lifecycle paths.

**Cleanup-error reporting (2026-10-14):** `runServe` now returns a joined error when OpenTelemetry shutdown, startup resource rollback, process session cleanup, system-session cleanup, or platform DB close fails. Normal shutdown continues to leave telemetry last. Process transfer and `ErrShutdownIncomplete` handling prevent later CLI defers from undoing the resource-retention safety on a timed-out Engine shutdown. Exact verification: the scoped race suite and full Engine tests, vet, build, contract check, import-graph check (434 edges), and diff check all passed. Engine `New(ctx, Config)` and startup construction are not implemented yet; G2 remains in progress.

**Rollback aggregation regression (2026-10-14):** Added a test that injects both tenant DB close and script-runner close failures into `closeStartupSharedResources` and asserts the aggregate reports both. `go test ./cli -run 'TestStartupRollback' -count=1` and `go test -race ./cli -run 'TestStartupRollback' -count=3` passed; `go vet ./cli` and `git diff --check` passed.

**Platform and telemetry ownership transfer (2026-10-14):** `ProcessResources` now owns the platform DB and telemetry shutdown callback after startup transfer. Engine closes the broker, then the platform pool, then telemetry last; startup failures before transfer retain the CLI fallback cleanup, while incomplete Engine shutdown skips those fallbacks and keeps dependencies available. Added a regression proving platform close precedes telemetry shutdown. Exact checks: `go test -race ./internal/engine ./cli -count=1`, `go vet ./internal/engine ./cli`, full Engine `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-contracts`, import graph `--check` (434 edges), and `git diff --check` passed. This moves two process resources under Engine ownership but does not yet move startup assembly; G2 remains in progress.

**Generation-owned worker follow-up (2026-10-14):** Added `internal/tenantruntime.Workers` with cancellation, admission fencing, concurrent-safe join, and one owner per `LoadedSite` runtime generation. CLI worker starts and retirement now call the generation directly; the CLI's string-keyed `runtimeContextRegistry` and its tests were removed/moved to the owner package. Same-config-revision router replacement now runs actual workers on both generations, retires the old one, and proves the replacement context stays live. Exact checks: `go test ./internal/tenantruntime ./net ./cli -count=1`, `go test -race ./internal/tenantruntime ./net ./cli -count=1`, scoped vet, and `git diff --check` passed. The full Engine `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, and `git diff --check` also passed. This closes the CLI registry ownership defect, while the complete tenant manager handle/snapshot API and process-level owner/failure tests remain required.

**Independent directory revision follow-up (2026-10-14):** `LoadedSite` now carries the applied directory-feed cursor independently of tenant config revision and runtime generation.
