Startup uses the captured directory snapshot boundary; feed no-ops, descriptor-only changes, resync, and runtime replacement update the cursor separately; request context exposes both config and directory revisions. The authenticated reload route now uses the coherent replacement path, retaining resource reuse and installing runtime-owned services instead of `AddSite`-replacing a live runtime. The spec's `Activation` example was corrected to include `DirectoryRevision` and clarify that worker fencing tokens belong to durable claims. New router tests prove directory-only revision advancement, stale directory rejection, and context projection. Exact checks: `go test ./net ./cli -count=1`, `go test -race ./net ./cli -count=1`, `go vet ./net ./cli`, then full Engine `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, and `git diff --check` passed. End-to-end directory reload under concurrent requests and shutdown remains open.

**Generation handles and request-drain follow-up (2026-10-14):** `internal/tenantruntime.Generation` now unifies request pinning, background worker admission/cancellation/join, and retirement. `SiteRouter` host, cookie, and path routes now acquire a release-once handle; retiring a site prevents new request and worker admission, cancels its worker context, and lets existing requests drain before the retired runtime closes. Tests cover handle retention across replacement, post-retirement rejection, release-once behavior, and worker join. Exact checks after this integration: `go test ./internal/tenantruntime ./net ./cli -count=1`, `go test -race ./internal/tenantruntime ./net ./cli -count=1`, scoped vet, full Engine `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-contracts`, import graph `--check`, and `git diff --check` passed. The global tenant manager and process owner, plus end-to-end reload/shutdown integration, remain open.

**Process listener owner follow-up (2026-10-14):** Added `internal/engine.Engine` and moved production signal handling, listener serving, readiness callback timing, signal-watcher joining, and one-time runtime shutdown behind it; `cli.runServe` now delegates the final listener stage. The owner accepts a root context, publishes and clears readiness around the listener lifetime, and rejects a second `Run`; bind failure withholds readiness and runs cleanup. A timed-out HTTP drain no longer invokes runtime cleanup while an admitted handler may still use tenant pools; `Run` retries within a bounded shutdown window and surfaces a timeout instead of closing resources under live requests. Tests cover bind-failure cleanup, root cancellation, readiness, repeated Run, concurrent idempotent shutdown, and active-request drain timeout/recovery. Exact checks: `go test -race ./internal/engine ./internal/tenantruntime ./cli ./net -count=1`, `go vet ./internal/engine ./internal/tenantruntime ./cli ./net`, and `git diff --check` passed. This is the process run boundary, not yet the complete Engine assembly owner: startup construction and acquired-resource rollback still live in `cli.runServe`; the typed tenant manager/snapshot API and integrated reload/shutdown failure tests remain open.

**Bounded retired-generation admission follow-up (2026-10-14):** Added `tenantruntime.Retirements` as a context-aware capacity gate. `KORA_MAX_RETIRING_GENERATIONS` defaults to 32; replacement reserves before publication (even if a concurrent reload made the earlier lookup stale), and deletion reserves before unpublishing. Capacity returns only after the old runtime drain closes its resources, so excess directory churn waits or returns a retryable apply error while the active snapshot remains published. Unit tests prove the bound, cancellation and release. Exact checks: `go test -race ./internal/engine ./internal/tenantruntime ./cli ./net -count=1`, scoped vet, and `git diff --check` passed. The full tenant manager, snapshot API, readiness reasons/tenant health, and process assembly/rollback owner are still open.

**Startup rollback coverage follow-up (2026-10-14):** Rechecked partial tenant-load rollback. It now cancels/joins each loaded runtime's generation workers, stops analytics and installed tenant service workers, closes the session manager and optional `io.Closer` storage backend, then closes the tenant DB. The sibling-load-failure test now exercises and verifies cancellation/join of a real generation worker in addition to analytics worker, bus and DB cleanup. Exact checks: `go test -race ./cli ./internal/engine ./internal/tenantruntime ./net -count=1`, scoped vet, and `git diff --check` passed. Whole-process stage injection and rollback for shared resources acquired before/after tenant loading remain open.

**Retired-runtime timeout safety follow-up (2026-10-14):** A timed-out request drain now keeps the old tenant session manager, services and DB pool alive until all generation handles release. Since the retirement slot is released only after drain returns, stuck requests also apply bounded backpressure to future changes. Added a regression test proving the DB stays open across a drain timeout and closes after handle release. Exact checks: `go test -race ./cli ./internal/engine ./internal/tenantruntime ./net -count=1`, scoped vet, and `git diff --check` passed. Process shutdown still needs an integrated failure/timeout test covering this behavior with a real listener and tenant runtime.

**G2 integrated validation refresh (2026-10-14):** Full Engine `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, and `make check-contracts` passed. The first import graph check correctly detected new lifecycle package/test edges; regenerated `docs/architecture/import-graph.json`, after which `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check` and `git diff --check` passed. No Cloud, standalone UI, or nested SDK tests were rerun for this Engine-only slice. G2 remains in progress; startup composition/rollback ownership, manager snapshots, and integrated process reload/shutdown test gates remain open.

**Tenant runtime ownership/snapshot follow-up (2026-10-14):** Moved concrete tenant runtime resource ownership and routing config from `net` into `internal/tenantruntime`; `net.LoadedSite` and `net.SiteRouterConfig` remain source-compatible aliases. Runtime handles now carry a typed immutable per-activation snapshot (tenant/config/directory/resource revisions, generation, config, DB, registry, session manager, analytics worker and typed service bundle). Host, cookie and path-based routing inject that acquired snapshot into request context, including redispatch, rather than rereading mutable runtime fields mid-request. Focused tests and race tests passed: `go test -race ./internal/tenantruntime ./net ./cli -count=1`, scoped vet, and `git diff --check`. A tenant-keyed manager with atomic activation/reload/retirement remains to be implemented; SiteRouter still owns the active tenant index and process startup still eagerly loads tenants.

**Tenant runtime type migration (2026-10-14):** Moved `LoadedSite`'s runtime fields and lifecycle methods into `internal/tenantruntime.Runtime`; retained `net` aliases to preserve current imports. Added typed `Activation` and immutable `Snapshot`; acquired handles pin that snapshot, and all routed request contexts (including nested path-route dispatch) now project from the pinned snapshot. Existing string-valued `site_id` and absent-service compatibility semantics remain intact. Exact checks: `go test -race ./internal/tenantruntime ./net ./cli -count=1`, `go vet ./internal/tenantruntime ./net ./cli`, import graph regeneration plus `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. The tenant-keyed manager still needs to own active publication, acquire, retirement and close semantics; this migration alone does not close the manager requirement.

**Tenant manager and startup rollback hardening (2026-10-14):** Tightened activation to reject malformed numeric config revisions, stable tenant-ID changes during same-name replacement, and duplicate checks for empty legacy IDs. Missing-tenant retirement now returns before waiting for bounded retirement capacity. Startup rollback uses `Runtime.Close` as the single reverse-owner cleanup path, including optional closable storage. Added manager regressions for all three activation/retirement edge cases. Exact checks: `go test ./internal/tenantruntime ./cli -count=1`, `go test -race ./internal/tenantruntime ./cli -count=1`, `go vet ./internal/tenantruntime ./cli`, `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-contracts`, `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py`, its `--check` mode (428 edges), and `git diff --check` passed. These changes close the manager API gate but do not verify process-composition rollback or integrated reload/shutdown; G2 remains in progress.

**Async-hook tenant pinning (2026-10-14):** The process async-hook worker previously looked up a mutable runtime pointer and used its DB/registry/services without a request handle, allowing a concurrent reload or deletion to close the tenant pool during script execution. It now acquires by tenant name, reads the pinned immutable snapshot, and releases only after execution and logging. A regression blocks the script runner, retires the tenant, starts runtime close, and proves the pool remains open until the hook finishes; it passed 20 normal repetitions and 5 race repetitions. Exact checks: focused hook tests, `go test -p 2 ./cli ./internal/tenantruntime ./net ./internal/engine -count=1`, matching scoped race tests, full `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-contracts`, regenerated and checked import graph (429 edges), and `git diff --check` passed. The initial graph check detected the new test-only import and was regenerated; the subsequent check is current. Whole-process startup-stage injection and coverage of all CLI-owned resources in integrated shutdown remain open.

**Integrated Engine and tenant drain test (2026-10-14):** Added an HTTP-level test that pins the old tenant generation in a live request, publishes a same-tenant replacement, forces a shutdown timeout, then releases the request and verifies Engine shutdown joins and closes both old and active tenant DB pools. It passed 20 repetitions and 5 race repetitions. Exact checks: the targeted repetitions, the earlier full Engine suite and vet/build/contracts, plus current `go test -race ./cli ./internal/tenantruntime ./net ./internal/engine -count=1` and `git diff --check`. This closes the narrow manager/server reload-and-drain test gate; the test does not exercise all of the CLI’s process-owned dependencies or its startup failure stages. G2 remains in progress.

**Shared startup resource rollback (2026-10-14):** Engine’s script runner and optional NATS provider are now closed on errors after their construction but before listener startup. The rollback first closes and joins tenant runtimes, then closes process-shared providers they may use. Added a test whose tenant worker exits only after cancellation and whose fake script runner rejects closure until that worker has joined. It passed three normal repetitions and two race repetitions after the final helper change. Exact checks: targeted startup rollback tests, `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-contracts`, import-graph regeneration/check (429 internal edges), scoped vet, and `git diff --check` passed. Failures across the full `runServe` acquisition sequence and platform/session/telemetry teardown remain untested. G2 remains in progress.

**Retryable Engine shutdown (2026-10-14):** `Engine.Shutdown` previously consumed `sync.Once` even when the resource owner returned an incomplete-shutdown error, so a worker that later drained could not be joined and its retained dependencies could not be released through Engine. Shutdown attempts are now serialized, incomplete cleanup can be retried, and the Engine marks itself stopped only after a successful owner shutdown. Successful cleanup still runs once. A regression now holds an admitted tenant request through the first timed-out Engine shutdown, then releases it and proves retry joins the runtime and closes its shared provider exactly once. `Run` also rejects an Engine whose shutdown has already begun instead of resetting its lifecycle state and serving with closed resources. Exact checks: full `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `make check-contracts`, `git diff --check`, `go test -race ./cli ./internal/engine ./net ./internal/tenantruntime -count=1`, and ten repetitions of `go test -race ./internal/engine -run 'TestShutdown(RetriesIncompleteCleanup|RetryFinishesTenantDrainAndClosesSharedProviders|ConcurrentCallersJoinSingleCleanup)' -count=10` passed before the final Run-after-shutdown guard; afterward `go test -race ./internal/engine -count=1`, `go vet ./internal/engine`, and `git diff --check` passed. This closes retry after incomplete cleanup; it does not close the full process-startup fault-injection or CLI composition gates.

**Engine-owned scheduler startup (2026-10-14):** Moved scheduler configuration loading and worker construction from `cli/serve.go` to `internal/engine.StartScheduler`. CLI passes explicit typed dependencies, then immediately registers the worker with the existing process rollback/shutdown owner. Missing `scheduler.yaml` still disables the optional scheduler; unreadable or malformed configuration now aborts startup rather than silently dropping configured jobs. The legacy Fieldwork config location remains ignored. Exact checks: `go test ./internal/engine ./cli ./scheduler -count=1`, matching race tests, scoped `go vet`, regenerated and checked import graph (445 internal edges), `make check-cross-repo-contracts` (11 fixture/schema artifacts and route/import graph), and `git diff --check` passed. This moves scheduler composition and joins into Engine but leaves the wider route/service composition and full startup-stage injection gates open.

**Engine-owned platform readiness handler (2026-10-14):** Moved the bounded canonical-directory DB probe from `cli/readiness.go` into `internal/engine.PlatformReadinessHandler`; CLI only registers the Engine-owned handler. The response remains stable and excludes raw driver errors. Existing nil/unhealthy/healthy behavior is covered in `internal/engine/readiness_http_test.go`. Checks passed: Engine/CLI/scheduler package tests and race tests, scoped vet, import graph check (445 edges), cross-repository contract/route/graph checks, and `git diff --check`. The HTTP endpoint still reports the platform-directory probe separately from listener `Engine.Readiness`; full route and process composition extraction remains open.

**Engine-owned async script-hook worker (2026-10-14):** Moved the bounded enqueue sink, consumer goroutine and runtime-pinned script execution from `cli/serve.go` to `internal/engine.AsyncHookWorker`. Its API exposes only the enqueue interface, queue close, cancellation and join signal; process shutdown preserves drain-before-cancel ordering. CLI no longer owns the hook worker implementation or queue type. The existing regression now starts the Engine worker and proves a retired tenant's DB stays open until the hook completes; queue-full, close/enqueue races and shutdown tests also pass. Exact checks: focused Engine/CLI/ORM tests and matching race tests; full `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`; full `go vet -p 2 ./...`; `go build -o /dev/null .`; scoped vet; regenerated and checked import graph (447 internal edges); `make check-cross-repo-contracts`; and `git diff --check` passed. Worker startup is now Engine-owned, while the rest of route/service composition and whole-startup fault injection remain open G2 gates.

**Engine-owned application route composition (2026-10-14):** Moved auth/session setup, global API configuration, authenticated v1 and compatibility groups, request v2, public routes and SPA/HTMX workspace selection into typed `internal/engine.ApplicationRouteConfig` and `RegisterApplicationRoutes`. The returned session manager is handed immediately to `ProcessResources` for startup rollback and shutdown. CLI retains platform middleware plus management/reload, console and health endpoints. A regression verifies auth, canonical/compatibility OpenAPI and request-v2 routes remain registered and that the session manager is returned for ownership. Exact checks: full `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, focused race tests for Engine/CLI/API/auth/workspace, `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, regenerated/checked import graph (449 internal edges), and `git diff --check` passed. This moves core route groups and session setup out of CLI; custom console/reload/health registration, overall server/dependency assembly, and full startup fault injection remain open G2 gates.

**Engine-owned console route registration (2026-10-14):** Moved the `/api/console/*` endpoint registrations into `internal/engine.RegisterConsoleRoutes`; CLI still creates/configures the `api.ConsoleHandler`, then delegates route binding. Tests verify onboarding/list/reset route bindings and that the site-list route rejects an unauthenticated request. Exact checks: `go test -race ./internal/engine ./cli ./api -count=1`, scoped vet, `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, regenerated/checked import graph (450 internal edges), and `git diff --check` passed. Console handler construction, admin reload route, health endpoints and overall server/dependency assembly remain in CLI; G2 is still in progress.

**Engine-owned tenant reload controller (2026-10-14):** Moved `/_kora/admin/reload-site` authorization, decoding, existing-tenant refresh, unloaded-tenant discovery/activation, publication and status mapping into `internal/engine.TenantReloadController` and `RegisterTenantReloadRoute`. CLI now supplies the assembled tenant installer/replacer and dedicated reload token. Bearer comparison uses fixed-size constant-time digest comparison. Failed candidate cleanup errors are joined with the activation/publication error, and candidates that cannot close are returned to the process owner for shutdown retry. Tests cover missing/bad credentials, existing-runtime reload identity/revision, candidate-close failure retention, and process-owner close deduplication. Validation: after the initial full-suite linker failure caused by disk exhaustion, cleared only Go build/test caches (`go clean -cache -testcache`); then `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, `go test -race ./internal/engine ./cli ./api -count=1`, `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, `make check-contracts`, import graph check (450 internal edges), and `git diff --check` all passed. An earlier broader race command that included `site` failed to link while only 229 MB was free; the successful rerun covered the changed Engine/CLI/API packages. No database integration test for the admin activation path was run. Remaining directory feed/resync service composition and full startup stage injection keep G2 open.

**Console tenant-resource ordering (2026-10-14):** Deletion now fences new requests durably, waits for admitted request handles, then discards request-visible session/event services before data cleanup. After the fence it re-resolves the current runtime by stable tenant ID, avoiding cleanup of a stale generation captured before a concurrent reload. Site metadata update reads its pinned configuration snapshot; password reset holds a tenant handle for the entire password/session transaction. Tests hold an active handle during deletion and verify runtime discard is deferred, and delay a password-reset SQL statement while concurrently retiring/closing the tenant to prove pool closure waits for the transaction. The first password-reset mock expectation failed because its PostgreSQL placeholder was wrong; after correcting the fixture to actual rebound SQL, the test passed. Exact checks: `go test ./api ./cli ./internal/tenantruntime ./net -count=1`, matching race tests, `go vet ./api ./cli ./internal/tenantruntime ./net`, import-graph `--check`, and `git diff --check` passed; after the final stable-ID re-resolution, `go test -race ./api -count=1`, `go vet ./api`, and `git diff --check` passed. The first race build ran out of disk while linking; only generated Go build/test caches were cleared, then the same race command passed. No contract/schema changed. Raw compatibility lookups and broader runServe startup/composition gates remain open.

**Pinned runtime resource handoff and metadata snapshots (2026-10-14):** Added `Manager.PinExisting` and a SiteRouter wrapper for control-plane replacement assembly. A reload pins the previous generation across DB/session reuse, candidate construction, publication and ownership transfer; a process-level reload mutex serializes these steps so concurrent feed/manual reloads cannot both reuse or clear the same stable handles. Added atomic metadata snapshot enumeration and ID/name lookup; directory revision consumers and console site listing/create/update/delete identity checks no longer read mutable manager-owned fields through raw runtime pointers. Console DB health probes acquire the current tenant handle for just the ping. Request acquisition still checks traffic status under the manager lock; the handoff pin is explicitly for a fenced existing generation. Regressions prove deleting tenants reject request admission while remaining pinnable for handoff, copied metadata cannot mutate manager state, and console listing pings through the runtime manager. Exact checks: affected package tests and race tests (`api`, `cli`, `internal/tenantruntime`, `net`), scoped vet, full `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-contracts`, import-graph regeneration/check (430 edges), and `git diff --check` passed; after the final console create snapshot change, API tests and import-graph/diff checks passed. Full G2 process composition and stage-injection gates remain open.

**Engine-owned feed and listener workers (2026-10-14):** Moved durable directory poller, optional NATS revision relay, wakeup subscription consumer, cancellation and goroutine joining into `internal/engine.DirectoryFeedWorker`; `ProcessResources` now owns the typed worker and joins it before closing tenants/providers. Cancellation drains the subscription channel until its provider-owned fetch loop closes it, and a bounded join failure keeps dependent resources open. `cli/serve.go` now supplies the existing tenant-specific apply/resync consumer and registers the returned worker. Added worker tests for poller/relay/wakeup joining and SQL-poll fallback when NATS subscription is unavailable. Also moved instrumented HTTP listener construction into `BuildHTTPServer`, with required-handler/address and timeout tests. Exact checks passed: `go test ./internal/engine ./cli -count=1`, `go test -race ./internal/engine ./cli -count=1`, full Engine `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, import-graph regeneration/check (450 internal edges), `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check`. G2 remains in progress: tenant service assembly, full-stage startup failure injection, and complete process-worker ownership review are not closed by this change.

**Engine-owned platform routes (2026-10-14):** Moved optional management-inspection, liveness, health and readiness route registration into typed `PlatformRouteConfig` and `RegisterPlatformRoutes`. Self-hosted mode remains Cloud-optional when no management token is configured; configured management still requires a valid directory and deployment ID. Regression tests assert route registration, ping payload/version, degraded health without a DB, and readiness failure without the platform directory. Exact checks passed: `go test -race ./internal/engine ./cli ./api -count=1`, full Engine `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, import-graph regeneration/check (450 internal edges), `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check`. This trims route assembly from CLI but does not close G2's tenant-service composition or staged startup rollback gates.

**NATS realtime subscription join (2026-10-14):** The realtime `Provider.Subscribe` context watcher now has an explicit stop path, and the returned drain function unsubscribes, closes the channel and joins that watcher. The API handler already defers the drain callback, so a completed/aborted stream now joins its provider goroutine before request teardown completes. Nil subscription contexts are rejected. `go test ./natsprovider ./api -run 'TestSubscribeDrainClosesChannel|Realtime' -count=3`, the matching race run, scoped vet and `git diff --check` passed. This closes the audited watcher gap but is not the complete cross-package worker inventory; G2 remains in progress.

**Engine-owned human-wait recovery and bounded NATS drain (2026-10-14):** Moved durable request/workflow recovery-store construction and per-tenant worker registration from CLI to `internal/engine.StartHumanWaitRecoveryWorker`; the tenant generation remains the cancellation/join owner. A regression uses a canceled process context to prove the worker registers and joins without touching the database, and rejects runtimes without stable tenant identity. Fixed `natsprovider.Consumer.Drain` so its timeout path force-unsubscribes and joins the in-flight `sub.Drain` goroutine before returning; it no longer leaves a provider user behind when the caller can proceed to teardown. Added deadline, join and error tests. Exact checks passed: `go test -race ./internal/engine ./cli ./api ./natsprovider ./workflow -count=1`, full Engine `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, import-graph regeneration/check (453 internal edges), `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check`. Tenant service-bundle construction and whole-startup stage injection remain open G2 work.

**Engine-owned tenant directory policy and candidate cleanup (2026-10-14):** Moved durable change application and authoritative resync from `cli/serve.go` into `internal/engine.NewTenantDirectoryConsumer`; the typed configuration preserves the stable per-replica consumer ID and initial directory cursor, delegates only process-specific service installation/replacement/retirement, and keeps optional ingress placement projection at the Engine boundary. Stale config events still advance directory revision without replacing an equal/newer runtime. Candidate installation/publication failures now close via `LoadedSite.Close(ctx)`; failed closes are retained by the process owner. Hot-reload candidate rollback uses the same close owner, joins DB/session/storage close errors, and preserves borrowed stable DB/session handles on a failed compatible replacement. Runtime-service snapshots are attached before sidecars start so startup rollback can see partial installation. Regression tests cover stable cursor identity, same-config revision directory advancement, ingress snapshot replacement, retirement of missing local runtimes, candidate close failure retention, and borrowed pool preservation. Exact checks passed: `go test -race ./internal/engine ./cli ./api ./natsprovider -count=1`, full Engine `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, import-graph regeneration/check (452 internal edges), `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check`; the latest tenant-directory policy regression also passed 10 normal and 3 race repetitions. Tenant service composition and failure injection across every startup acquisition stage remain open G2 gates.

**Config-driven Engine owner and startup rollback matrix (2026-10-14):** Added `internal/engine.New(ctx, Config)` as the production constructor; the previous preassembled-server helper is now explicitly named `NewFromListener`. `cli.runServe` now validates/parses process configuration, calls `engine.New`, runs it, and returns errors; Engine composes telemetry/platform discovery, tenant loading, optional providers, tenant services, route groups, listener, readiness metrics and shutdown ownership. Engine preserves discovered tenant domains in listener TLS configuration. Injected constructor tests fail at process, shared-provider, tenant and HTTP assembly boundaries and assert reverse cleanup of telemetry/script resources; the late HTTP route regression covers SessionManager rollback, and a constructor regression protects autocert domain propagation. Startup spans end and record listener errors from the Engine run lifecycle. Full-suite verification passed before the worker activation correction; the final G2 checks are in the worker-start checkpoint below.

**Run-owned worker activation (2026-10-14):** Added a process worker-start registry. `Engine.New` prepares process and tenant workers without launching them; `Engine.Run` starts registered workers before binding/publishing readiness. Later tenant activations start immediately through the same process owner. Deferred startup now covers tenant session sweepers and service workers, analytics fanout, system-session cleanup, directory lag/feed consumers, scheduler, async hooks and deletion recovery. Shutdown cancels and joins any in-progress worker startup before dependency cleanup; workers never started before rollback are discarded and their prepared owners close safely. Added Engine-level proof that a registered worker is idle after `New` and has started by readiness, plus tests for live activation, worker-start cancellation/join, paused session managers and system guards, and deferred analytics fanout. Exact checks passed: `go test -race ./auth ./analytics ./internal/engine ./cli -count=1`; `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`; `go vet -p 2 ./...`; `go build -o /dev/null .`; `make check-contracts`; import-graph regeneration and check (454 edges); `make check-cross-repo-contracts` (11 fixture/schema artifacts and current route inventory); and `git diff --check`. No public contract or persisted schema changed. G2 is verified.

**Single-Engine tenant activation regression (2026-10-15):** The new Cloud→Engine process integration exposed that `startup.Ingress` was a nil `*ingress.Gateway` converted to the non-nil `TenantIngressDirectory` interface, causing `MarkLocalReady` to panic when the real management worker activated a tenant without cell routing. `engine.New` now passes a true nil interface when no gateway exists; the target spec explicitly preserves ingress optionality for single-Engine deployments, and `TestNewUsesPlatformDatabaseForApplicationFallbacks` asserts the nil boundary. Verified with Engine `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/engine -count=1` and `go vet -p 1 ./internal/engine`, plus the full Cloud→Engine MySQL process test recorded under G7. G2 remains verified with this late regression fixed; no schema or public contract changed.

### G3 Database and memory budgets

**Status:** verified.


**Analytics WAL stable namespace (2026-10-15):** Resolved the name-keyed cold-WAL discovery gap with stable tenant-ID paths, startup migration for canonical names/current aliases, fail-closed handling for unassigned or conflicting pending WAL, and replay of interrupted `.wal-ack-*` files. See the detailed evidence entry below. Historical route names no longer present in directory aliases still require operator recovery; no data is automatically merged or discarded. The separate replay-retention/pruning protocol remains unimplemented; it is not a G3 section-7 exit gate because the specification explicitly requires retaining records until that protocol is proven.

**Tenant source-sequence exhaustion guard (2026-10-15):** `SQLWriter` allocates source positions in signed SQL `BIGINT`/Go `int64` fields, while the specification previously described the value as unsigned and the allocator relied on a post-increment read to detect wraparound. The allocator now computes `max(current singleton, legacy route-keyed maximum)`, rejects negative persisted values and returns `ErrSourceSequenceExhausted` at `9,223,372,036,854,775,807` before writing. It writes the next value once under the existing transaction/row lock, eliminating the overflow-prone SQL increment/read sequence. The spec now states the actual nonnegative signed-64-bit range and fail-closed behavior; this is the smallest correction consistent with existing persisted schema. Added regressions for current and legacy sequence exhaustion, legacy seeding, and exact rollback/no-write behavior at exhaustion. No schema or wire shape changed. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./outbox -count=1`; matching `go test -race -p 1 ./outbox -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./outbox`; and, against fresh loopback-only MySQL 8.4.11, PostgreSQL 16 and actual LibSQL HTTP services with disposable storage, `KORA_OUTBOX_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN=[REDACTED] KORA_OUTBOX_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN=[REDACTED] KORA_OUTBOX_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN=[REDACTED] go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./outbox -run '^(TestMySQLSourceSequenceCommitsWithOutboxTransaction|TestPostgresTransactionalConsumerReceiptAndPublication|TestLibSQLTransactionalConsumerReceiptAndPublication)$' -count=1 -timeout=180s`. All passed; the three task-owned containers were stopped and removed. `make check-contracts`, `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check`, and `git diff --check` also passed. No live/user database was used. G3 remains open for the coordinated replay-retention protocol and remaining group-wide acceptance gates.

**Self-host resource budget wiring (2026-10-15):** The single-Engine Compose profile previously forwarded only pool and active-work limits; values in `.env` for other documented process budgets were silently unavailable inside Engine. Compose and `.env.example` now pass the static replica count, connection/work waiter deadlines, heavy-work allocation, resident-tenant and activation limits, separate per-tenant analytics queue and process-wide queued-byte budgets, subscriber/payload limits, async-hook queue bounds, script concurrency/wait/timeout caps, realtime stream cap, session-cache bytes, and upload count/bytes. `KORA_ANALYTICS_PROCESS_MAX_QUEUED_BYTES` controls process admission independently of the per-tenant `KORA_ANALYTICS_MAX_QUEUED_BYTES`; existing deployments that set only the older variable retain its historical process-budget fallback. The self-host README states these are process-local and must be recalculated before scaling. Validation: `docker compose -f deploy/selfhost/compose.yaml --env-file deploy/selfhost/.env.example config --quiet`; PyYAML parse of Compose YAML; resolved Compose JSON assertion for separate 512 MiB process and 64 MiB per-tenant analytics limits plus replica, resident, subscriber, script, realtime, cache and upload values; CLI analytics queue env tests for precedence, compatibility fallback and invalid ranges; `go test -p 1 ./analytics ./cli -count=1`; matching `-race` tests; `go vet -p 1 ./analytics ./cli`; full Engine `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; and `git diff --check`. No services were started; no database/schema/wire change. Changed files: `cli/{serve.go,serve_test.go}`, `deploy/selfhost/{compose.yaml,.env.example,README.md}`, `docs/architecture/{resource-budgets.md,target-state-spec.md}` and this ledger. G3 remains in progress for coordinated replay retention and remaining acceptance evidence.

**Budgeted database lock contention in CI (2026-10-15):** Engine CI's disposable MySQL integration job now includes `internal/dbaccess` and supplies a dedicated `kora_dbaccess_test` DSN for the real lock-contention metric test. This closes a coverage gap where the integration test existed and had previously been run manually, but routine CI did not execute it. Verified the workflow parses as YAML and contains the integration step; `KORA_DBACCESS_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/dbaccess -run '^TestDatabaseLockContentionMetricsAgainstSupportedDialects$' -count=1 -timeout=180s` passed against a fresh loopback-only MySQL 8.4.11 container with tmpfs data. The exact container was stopped and removed after the test. No schema/wire change. Changed files: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `docs/architecture/database-support-matrix.md`, and this ledger. G3 remains in progress; this does not close the coordinated replay-retention protocol or full resource/compatibility acceptance.

**Process-wide analytics listener cap (2026-10-15):** Analytics subscriber queues already reserved queued payload bytes process-wide, but empty listener channels were limited only per tenant, allowing registrations to scale with resident-tenant count. `AnalyticsQueueAdmission` now also reserves a process-wide listener slot (default 4,096; `KORA_ANALYTICS_MAX_SUBSCRIBERS` supports 1–65,536). MultiBus returns capacity errors when the process limit is full and releases slots on listener removal, realtime overflow eviction, and tenant bus close. Engine exports process active/capacity/rejected listener metrics. The target spec and resource guide now distinguish listener-count bounds from queued-payload byte accounting. Changed files: `analytics/{queue_admission.go,queue_admission_test.go,multibus.go,multibus_test.go}`, `cli/{serve.go,serve_test.go}`, `internal/engine/{config.go,resource_metrics.go,resource_metrics_test.go}`, `docs/architecture/{target-state-spec.md,resource-budgets.md}`, and this ledger. No schema or wire contract changed. Verified focused admission/environment/metric tests; concurrent registration contention test repeated 20 times under `-race`; `go test -race -p 1 ./analytics ./cli ./internal/engine -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./analytics ./cli ./internal/engine`; full Engine `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; `make check-contracts`; `make check-cross-repo-contracts`; and `git diff --check`. Tests prove concurrent registrations across multiple tenant buses cannot exceed a shared cap and capacity returns after removal and shutdown. G3 remains in progress: coordinated replay retention and other database/version/deployment gates remain open.

**MySQL integration CI coverage (2026-10-15):** Engine CI now runs the integration-tagged `internal/dbaccess`, `api`, `analytics`, `outbox`, `site`, `requests/sqlstore`, `workflow/sqlstore`, `notifications/sqlstore`, and `management/sqlstore` packages against its MySQL 8.4.11 service, supplying a dedicated lock-contention database plus generic, analytics-specific, outbox-specific, and config-activation/realtime disposable DSNs. The full nine-package run passed locally against a fresh MySQL 8.4.11 container bound only to loopback, with `/var/lib/mysql` on tmpfs; fixtures use isolated test databases. Exact command: `KORA_TEST_DSN='[REDACTED]' KORA_ANALYTICS_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' KORA_OUTBOX_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' KORA_CONFIG_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' KORA_DBACCESS_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/dbaccess ./api ./analytics ./outbox ./site ./requests/sqlstore ./workflow/sqlstore ./notifications/sqlstore ./management/sqlstore -count=1 -timeout=15m`; all nine packages passed. Adding API coverage exposed a stale 500 assertion for kernel `DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`; the route correctly maps this retryable dependency failure to 503, so `api/kernel_resource_integration_test.go` now asserts 503. The isolated regression and full eight-package run passed after the correction. API channel-handler and live site-directory integrations use other opt-in DSNs and remain outside this CI step; PostgreSQL and LibSQL also remain opt-in. Changed Engine files: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `api/kernel_resource_integration_test.go`, `docs/architecture/database-support-matrix.md`, and this ledger. No schema or wire contract changed. CI covers the listed package paths, not the complete G1–G10 acceptance matrix. G3 remains open for coordinated replay retention and broader resource-budget evidence.

**Backup-only replay floor inventory (2026-10-15):** Extended `scripts/selfhost-source-manifest.py` with `backup-floor` and `validate-backup`. Floor computation requires at least one manifest and the same database set in every supplied backup; it records per-database minimum source sequence and canonical manifest digests. Updates use an atomic mode-0600 replacement under an exclusive lock, reject database-set changes and sequence regressions, and restore validation requires the selected manifest digest to remain in the retained catalog and meet its floor. Persisted floor validation now rejects empty/malformed database maps, mismatched backup counts, duplicate labels, invalid digests, and noncanonical sequence values. Updated self-host backup/restore instructions with the full-inventory requirement, remove-before-advance ordering, and explicit limits. This component cannot discover omitted remote copies or prove deletion, and it does not cover WAL, outbox, receipts/checkpoints or broker acknowledgments; pruning remains disabled. No DB or wire schema changed. Verified `make check-selfhost-backup` (14 tests), Python compilation, CLI help for both new commands, and `git diff --check`.

**Analytics receipt source-sequence migration (2026-10-15):** Analytics bootstrap schema v5 adds nullable `source_sequence` to `_kora_analytics_event_receipt`. The worker records a canonical event sequence in the same transaction as the receipt and effects when present; sequence state is restored with the batch after rollback. A duplicate event ID arriving without sequence and later with one fills the missing value; conflicting duplicate sequences remain explicitly unknown. Duplicate receipt inserts remain `DO NOTHING`/no-op upserts so source metadata cannot cause a replayed effect to be applied twice. The additive migration detects the existing column separately for MySQL, PostgreSQL, and LibSQL; concurrent duplicate ALTER is accepted only after a fresh schema check confirms the column exists. Older receipts remain NULL because their source sequence cannot be inferred safely. Changed Engine files: `analytics/{schema.go,schema_test.go,worker.go,worker_test.go,receipt_mysql_integration_test.go,rebuild_postgres_integration_test.go,rebuild_libsql_integration_test.go}` and architecture resource/support docs. Verified exact commands: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./analytics -count=1`; matching `go test -race -p 1 ./analytics -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./analytics`; `go test -p 1 ./internal/engine -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./internal/engine ./analytics`; `git diff --check`; and `go test -tags integration -p 1 ./analytics -run 'Test(MySQLAnalyticsReceiptDeduplicatesReplayAfterCommit|PostgresRebuildCutoffSerializesWithFlush|LibSQLRebuildCutoffAndLaterEvents)$' -count=1 -timeout=180s` (with three disposable loopback DSN variables set) against MySQL 8.4.11, PostgreSQL 16, and LibSQL HTTP image digest `sha256:[REDACTED_HASH_OR_TOKEN]`. Final integration run used tmpfs-only MySQL/PostgreSQL data directories and a fresh no-volume LibSQL container; it passed and proved old receipt preservation plus new sequence persistence on all three dialects. Earlier disposable runs surfaced a reused LibSQL namespace, a missing tenant-sequence table in the MySQL fixture, and an extra migration sentinel in its receipt count; those fixture issues were corrected. One rerun also hit the host's full root filesystem before tmpfs was used. All temporary containers were stopped and removed; the host root volume still has approximately 239 MiB free, so unrelated caches and user data were left untouched. This supports later floor computation but does not provide contiguous acknowledgments or authorize pruning; G3 remains in progress.

**Outbox consumer receipt source-sequence migration (2026-10-15):** Tenant bootstrap v19 adds nullable `source_sequence` to `_kora_outbox_receipt`, preserving existing event-ID receipts with unknown sequence. Both legacy external consumers and transactional local consumers now persist the delivered canonical tenant sequence when available; dispatch loads the authoritative value from the outbox column and replaces stale envelope metadata, while sequence-zero migrated rows retain only a validated payload fallback. Transactional receipt and local effect remain atomic, and duplicate-ID handling still cannot rerun the effect. The MySQL, PostgreSQL, and actual LibSQL HTTP upgrade fixtures verify old receipt retention with `NULL`; dialect integration scenarios verify sequence values on new committed receipts. A conflict regression proves the database column wins over a stale payload sequence. The LibSQL migration and dispatcher integrations were run against separate fresh HTTP endpoints because the migration fixture intentionally retains a pending event. Changed Engine files: `db/tables_outbox.go`, `db/tables_outbox_test.go`, `site/bootstrap.go`, `site/{bootstrap_kernel_result_integration_test.go,directory_postgres_integration_test.go,bootstrap_outbox_libsql_integration_test.go}`, and `outbox/{outbox.go,dispatcher_test.go,dispatcher_mysql_integration_test.go,dispatcher_dialects_integration_test.go}`; documentation `database-support-matrix.md` now records the exercised migration and receipt behavior. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./db ./site ./outbox -count=1`; integration-tag compile for `./site ./outbox`; the selected MySQL/PostgreSQL/LibSQL HTTP bootstrap migration tests; the selected three-dialect transactional receipt tests; `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -race -p 1 ./db ./site ./outbox -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./db ./site ./outbox`; `git diff --check`; focused sequence-precedence tests; and full Engine `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`. All real-dialect checks used fresh disposable services, MySQL/PostgreSQL data on tmpfs, and were stopped afterward. This adds source evidence for a future retention protocol; it does not provide contiguous acknowledgments or authorize pruning. G3 remains in progress.

**JetStream legacy-stream publish gate (2026-10-15):** Follow-up inspection found that preserving a legacy `MemoryStorage` stream still allowed a JetStream acknowledgment to move an outbox row to `published`, despite the provider diagnostics identifying the stream as non-restart-durable. Bootstrap now records the actual stream mode, and `Provider.Publish` returns `ErrNonDurableEventStream` for memory-backed streams. This preserves the stream and its consumers while leaving new durable events in the SQL outbox for retry/recovery; the regression verifies no message is accepted. The target specification and resource guide now state this behavior and the availability consequence. Changed Engine files: `natsprovider/{provider.go,provider_test.go}`; docs `docs/architecture/{target-state-spec.md,resource-budgets.md}`. Verified: focused `go test -p 1 ./natsprovider ./cli -run 'Test(BootstrapIsIdempotentAndRequestReplyWorks|FileBackedEventStreamSurvivesServerRestart|BootstrapPreservesLegacyMemoryStorageAndReportsIt|NATSConsumerSupervisorRecreatesDurableAndRedelivers)$' -count=1 -timeout=180s`; matching `go test -race -p 1 ./natsprovider -run 'Test(BootstrapIsIdempotentAndRequestReplyWorks|FileBackedEventStreamSurvivesServerRestart|BootstrapPreservesLegacyMemoryStorageAndReportsIt)$' -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./natsprovider`; and `git diff --check` passed. G3 remains open for legacy stream migration, coordinated cross-source replay retention, and remaining compatibility evidence.

**Analytics batch hard ceiling (2026-10-15):** The environment setting and direct worker constructor previously accepted any positive batch count, allowing a large `KORA_ANALYTICS_BATCH_SIZE` to expand one receipt transaction and WAL replay batch without a hard cap. Added a 500-event ceiling in `LoadConfig` and `NewWorker` (default remains 100), with coverage for both configuration paths. Updated the resource guide to distinguish this per-transaction bound from durable WAL/receipt retention. Changed Engine files: `analytics/{config.go,config_test.go,worker.go,worker_test.go}` and `docs/architecture/resource-budgets.md`. Verified focused configuration/worker tests with race, full analytics package tests and race tests, `go vet -p 1 ./analytics`, and `git diff --check`. After this change, full Engine `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go vet -p 1 ./...`, `make check-contracts`, `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check`, and `git diff --check` all passed. G3 remains open for coordinated replay retention and broader compatibility evidence.

**Cold tenant WAL byte telemetry (2026-10-15):** Added `kora.analytics.wal.cold_bytes` and `kora.analytics.wal.cold_bytes_unknown`. The durable-work scanner accumulates WAL directory sizes while walking its persisted keyset and publishes only after a full sweep. A resumed mid-sweep cursor or failed/cancelled tenant probe marks the aggregate unknown rather than reporting a partial sum; resident and cold metrics remain separate because activation can move tenants between samples. No retention behavior, schema, or wire contract changed. Verified: focused scanner aggregation/metric tests, including an exact byte count from a real temporary WAL file; full `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./internal/engine -count=1`; focused matching `go test -race`; `go vet -p 1 ./internal/engine`; `git diff --check`; and trailing-whitespace scan of edited source/docs. G3 remains open; this improves observability only and does not cap WAL disk growth or close replay-retention gates.

**Console and background provisioning admission (2026-10-15):** Engine HTTP assembly now provides the console handler a tenant-provisioning admission callback. It compares opaque physical endpoint keys and reuses the already-held platform admission for a shared endpoint; a distinct tenant endpoint acquires its maintenance lane. Capacity exhaustion is a retryable 503 for synchronous console creation, while background onboarding waits and retries under its owned context instead of marking the durable job failed. Tests cover shared-endpoint deduplication under saturation, separate-endpoint permit accounting/release, capacity error classification, and the handler gate. Changed Engine files: `site/site.go`, `api/console.go`, `api/console_work_admission_test.go`, `internal/engine/console_admission.go`, `internal/engine/console_admission_test.go`, `internal/engine/http_process.go`, and `docs/architecture/worker-admission-inventory.md`. Verified: focused race tests; full `go test -p 1 ./api ./internal/engine ./cli ./site -count=1` and matching `-race`; `go vet -p 1 ./api ./internal/engine ./cli ./site`; `git diff --check`. No contract or schema changed.

**Initial setup provisioning and schema-import admission (2026-10-15):** The one-command `kora setup` path now acquires maintenance admission for platform and tenant endpoints while `site.CreateSite` provisions the site, deduplicating the reservation when both databases share a physical endpoint. It then separately admits config import/schema migration on the tenant endpoint after releasing creation reservations. Added regressions that saturate shared and separate endpoint budgets, prove provisioning/import do not run before capacity is available, and verify permits release afterward. Changed Engine files: `site/create.go`, `cli/setup.go`, `cli/work_admission.go`, `cli/setup_admission_test.go`, `cli/setup_creation_admission_test.go`, and `docs/architecture/worker-admission-inventory.md`. Verified: `go test -race -p 1 ./cli ./site -run 'Test(SetupConfigImportUsesBoundedMaintenanceAdmission|SetupSiteCreationAdmitsAndDeduplicatesDatabaseEndpoints|CreateSite|CreateSiteRequiresDurableDirectoryBeforeProvisioning|CreateSiteDirectoryWriteFailurePrecedesTenantProvisioning)$' -count=1`; full `go test -p 1 ./cli ./site -count=1` and matching `-race`; `go vet -p 1 ./cli ./site`; `git diff --check`. No contract or schema changed.

**Analytics WAL disk-footprint telemetry (2026-10-15):** Resident WAL snapshots measure bytes in the current and flushing files, retaining exact counts through restart, partial acknowledgment, and committed cleanup. Inspection and write failures expose a separate `bytes_unknown` gauge. The byte gauge covers resident tenants; cold tenants continue to expose pending state and age because summing a keyset scan across failed/retried pages would risk double-counting without durable per-tenant measurement state. New OTLP gauges are `kora.analytics.wal.bytes` and `kora.analytics.wal.bytes_unknown`. This is monitoring, not a disk cap or retention permission; durable WAL growth remains unbounded pending the coordinated replay floor. Changed Engine files: `analytics/bus.go`, `analytics/bus_test.go`, `internal/engine/resource_metrics.go`, `internal/engine/resource_metrics_test.go`, and `docs/architecture/resource-budgets.md`. Verified: focused resident WAL and resource-metric tests; full `go test -p 1 ./analytics ./internal/engine -count=1`; matching `go test -race -p 1 ./analytics ./internal/engine -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./analytics ./internal/engine`; and `git diff --check` passed. No contract or schema changed.

**Process-wide analytics wake-up and subscriber queue admission (2026-10-15):** One Engine-owned `AnalyticsQueueAdmission` is shared by each resident tenant's `ChannelBus` wake-up channel and `MultiBus` subscriber queues. Each retained queue item reserves that bus's maximum accepted event payload; source reservations release when the owning `MultiBus` consumes wake-ups, while subscriber reservations release on consumption, listener removal, realtime overflow, or shutdown. `MultiBus.Publish` now fans out after successful WAL persistence and suppresses its matching source-channel copy, so a full process wake-up budget cannot hide an event from realtime or other resident listeners; when the wake-up hint itself is skipped, the durable WAL and periodic worker scan remain authoritative. Realtime queue rejection closes/removes the stream for reconnect and authorized refetch. Default process limit is 512 MiB, configurable from 1 byte through 4 GiB with `KORA_ANALYTICS_MAX_QUEUED_BYTES`. OTLP exports queued reservation bytes, capacity and rejected queue reservations. This bounds conservative payload reservations, not channel/object metadata or the event temporarily held by the fan-out goroutine; replicas do not coordinate. A two-tenant regression proves aggregate wake-up admission, WAL replay after hint rejection and capacity release; another proves `MultiBus.Publish` avoids duplicate fan-out when source hints are consumed, and shutdown-before-start releases source and listener reservations. The full CLI test run also exposed a typed-nil DB admission panic during async-hook tenant retirement; `acquireAsyncHookWork` now treats a nil `*dbaccess.Admission` as unconfigured. No public wire or persisted schema changed. Changed files: Engine `analytics/{bus.go,multibus.go,multibus_test.go,queue_admission.go,queue_admission_test.go}`, `internal/engine/{config.go,resources.go,tenant_services.go,resource_metrics.go,resource_metrics_test.go,async_hooks.go,async_hooks_test.go}`, `cli/{serve.go,serve_test.go}`, and architecture resource/status docs. Verified on the final code: `TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./analytics ./internal/engine ./cli -count=1`; same with `-race`; full Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./...`, and `go build -o /dev/null .`; targeted `go test -race -p 1 ./analytics -run '^TestChannelBusWakeupQueuesShareProcessWideByteBudget$' -count=5`; `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py` and `--check` (471 internal edges); `make check-contracts`; `make check-cross-repo-contracts`; and `git diff --check` passed. G3 remains in progress for remaining worker/admin admission audit, replay-retention contract, supported-operation/version coverage and pooler compatibility.

**Existing-outbox tenant-sequence bootstrap migration across dialects (2026-10-15):** Strengthened the MySQL, PostgreSQL, and actual HTTP LibSQL bootstrap upgrade fixtures to assert `_kora_tenant_event_sequence` is created while the existing outbox event and sequence-zero migration state remain intact. This closes the migration-schema test gap for the new route-independent allocator; it does not prove coordinated replay retention. Changed Engine integration tests: `site/bootstrap_kernel_result_integration_test.go`, `site/directory_postgres_integration_test.go`, and `site/bootstrap_outbox_libsql_integration_test.go`. Verified: `go test -p 1 ./site -count=1`, integration-tag compile, and `go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./site -run '^(TestBootstrapMigratesExistingSystemAndKernelReceiptSchema|TestLivePostgresSiteBootstrapWrites|TestBootstrapMigratesExistingLibSQLV17OutboxWithoutLosingRows)$' -count=1 -timeout=240s` against fresh tmpfs MySQL 8.4.11, PostgreSQL 16, and actual LibSQL HTTP services; all passed. The services and their temporary data were removed after the run. `git diff --check` passed. No public contract changed; the singleton is created by existing tenant bootstrap DDL. G3 remains in progress for global backup/replay floor, resumable pruning, broader operation/version coverage, and pooler evidence.

**Self-host backup sequence checkpoint and restore validation (2026-10-15):** Added `scripts/selfhost-source-manifest.py`. Backup captures a versioned JSON checkpoint of every non-system database and the maximum sequence found across the tenant singleton, legacy route-keyed allocator and outbox source column; restore checks that every recorded database exists and its sequence is at least the checkpoint before Engine starts. Writes use a mode-0600 temporary file, fsync and atomic rename. Updated self-host backup/restore instructions, the target-spec correction, and resource-budget caveats. Added `make check-selfhost-backup` with five unit tests for high-water selection, identifier quoting, missing/behind restore state, invalid/duplicate entries and atomic permissions. Verified `make check-selfhost-backup`; a fresh disposable MySQL 8.4.11 Compose service captured a manifest with sequence 10, accepted the matching restore, and rejected a modified checkpoint of 11. The disposable service used tmpfs and Compose teardown; no existing database was touched. This checkpoint detects incomplete/stale restoration of the selected backup only; it is not the oldest-restorable-backup floor and does not govern WAL, broker, receipts, or outbox pruning. No database/wire schema changed; G3 remains in progress.

**Tenant-stable source-sequence migration (2026-10-15):** Added `_kora_tenant_event_sequence` to MySQL, PostgreSQL and LibSQL outbox bootstrap DDL. `SQLWriter.Append` now locks the tenant-database singleton, seeds it from the maximum legacy `_kora_event_sequence.last_sequence`, increments it in the same business/outbox transaction, and leaves route names and historical outbox rows unchanged. Analytics rebuild cutoff reads use the singleton, falling back to the legacy maximum before the first post-upgrade append. Schema bootstrap creates the singleton table before normal runtime operations. The release procedure must drain old event writers before new writers start because old allocators do not lock the singleton. No wire contract changed. Changed: Engine `db/tables_outbox.go`, `outbox/outbox.go`, `analytics/{rebuild_state.go,backfill.go}`, tests in those packages, and target specification. Verified: full Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; focused `go test -race -p 1 ./outbox ./analytics ./db -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./outbox ./analytics ./db`; integration-tag compile; fresh disposable MySQL 8.4.11 source-sequence test, PostgreSQL 16 and actual HTTP LibSQL outbox sequence migration tests, plus MySQL/PostgreSQL/LibSQL analytics cutoff tests; `make check-contracts`; `make check-cross-repo-contracts`; and `git diff --check`. The first disposable integration attempt found shared test-state and fixture setup issues; tests were isolated to fresh endpoints and the final run passed. Replay-floor manifest/restore validation and resumable cross-store pruning remain open; no records were pruned. G3 remains in progress.

**Outbox payload and batch bounds (2026-10-15):** Outbox publication and local dispatch now default to batches of 100 and reject requested batch sizes above 500. New envelopes are limited to 16 MiB serialized using the longest valid uint64 sequence as preflight headroom, so rejection occurs before tenant sequence allocation; the assigned sequence is then validated and serialized for storage. Existing rows are not rewritten or deleted and remain readable by consumers during compatibility migration. Regressions verify oversize and near-limit rejection occur without sequence SQL, and oversized publisher/dispatcher limits are rejected without DB work. No schema or wire contract changed. Changed files: Engine `outbox/{outbox.go,append_test.go,budget_test.go}` and architecture resource/status docs. Verified: `TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./outbox -count=1`; same with `-race`; full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; full `go vet -p 1 ./...`; contract and cross-repository/import-graph checks; and `git diff --check` passed. G3 remains in progress; this does not close the broader worker/admin audit, replay-retention contract, SQL operation/version matrix, or pooler compatibility.

**Bounded JetStream retention (2026-10-15):** The optional NATS event stream now defaults to a 1 GiB/1,000,000-message cap, supports validated `KORA_NATS_STREAM_MAX_BYTES` (1 byte–64 GiB) and `KORA_NATS_STREAM_MAX_MESSAGES` (1–100 million), and uses `DiscardNew` with no time-based expiry. A full broker rejects new events, leaving the SQL outbox unacknowledged; bounded retries eventually mark the row failed for visible operator recovery. Bootstrap upgrades an existing stream only when subjects and retention mode match, the current backlog fits, and no age expiry could already have removed events; it preserves unrelated settings such as replica count. It fails for an unsafe shrink rather than trimming events. The SQL-backed directory wake-up stream has fixed 64 MiB/100,000-message limits and 24-hour age retention; dropping hints is safe because consumers resume from the SQL cursor. `natsprovider.Diagnostics` reports event-stream bytes/messages and capacities. No DB schema or event wire shape changed; NATS remains optional, and the main stream remains memory storage, so the cap is not a broker restart/backup durability guarantee. Changed files: Engine `natsprovider/{config.go,provider.go,provider_test.go}`, `README.md`, and architecture resource/status docs. Verified: `TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./natsprovider -count=1`; same with `-race`; full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./...`, and `go build -o /dev/null .`; `make check-contracts`; `make check-cross-repo-contracts` (including route inventory and 471-edge graph); and `git diff --check` all passed. The embedded-JetStream tests cover count and byte overflow, safe config upgrade, preservation of existing settings and messages, refusal to shrink below retained event state, diagnostics, and directory replay. The statement that the main stream remained memory-backed describes this checkpoint and is superseded by the later restart-durability correction below for newly created streams; existing memory streams remain a migration gate. G3 remains in progress for coordinated replay retention and compatibility evidence.

**Platform deletion-worker pressure metrics (2026-10-15):** The process-owned pending tenant-deletion recovery worker now counts admitted cleanup attempts, retry failures, and work-admission deferrals. Engine exports these as low-cardinality monotonic resource counters; no tenant or deletion IDs are attached, and no DB schema or public wire contract changed. Worker tests prove capacity rejection is counted without running cleanup and transient retry failures are observable; the manual OTel reader verifies all three counters. Changed files: Engine `internal/engine/{deletion_worker.go,deletion_worker_test.go,resource_metrics.go,resource_metrics_test.go,config.go}` and architecture resource/status docs. Verified: `TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./internal/engine -run 'Test(PendingDeletionWorker|ResourceMetricsRegisterAndObserveProcessResourceGauges)$' -count=1`; same command with `-race`; full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./...`, and `go build -o /dev/null .`; `make check-contracts`; `make check-cross-repo-contracts` (route inventory and 471-edge graph); and `git diff --check` all passed. This improves observability for one administrative worker; the full worker/admission audit and coordinated replay-retention contract remain open G3 gates.

**Tenant activation and onboarding background-work admission (2026-10-15):** Analytics bootstrap and durable scheduler DDL acquire the tenant endpoint-shared active-work permit before issuing SQL. Analytics bootstrap gained `BootstrapTablesContext`; all DDL, marker reads and marker writes use the supplied context, while the existing context-free API remains source-compatible. In Engine's created-tenant path, session cleanup now stays paused until the tenant runtime installer attaches admission and starts the worker; standalone `ConsoleHandler` behavior remains self-starting. Site creation releases platform admission before runtime installation; the created-site directory lookup and post-install provisioning-state writes acquire their own platform work unit, avoiding nested acquisition when platform and tenant pools share an endpoint. Admission rejection prevents scheduler schema SQL, and canceled analytics bootstrap makes no DB call. No schema or wire format changed. Verified: gofmt on changed Go files; focused bootstrap/scheduler admission tests; full `go test -p 1 ./analytics ./scheduler ./scheduler/sqlstore ./internal/engine -count=1`; `go test -race -p 1 ./analytics ./scheduler ./internal/engine -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./analytics ./scheduler ./scheduler/sqlstore ./internal/engine`; focused `go test -p 1 ./api ./internal/engine ./auth -run 'Test(RequirePlatformWork|PrepareCreatedRuntime|Console|TenantService|TenantScheduler|SessionDatabaseCleanup|PausedOwnedSessionManager)' -count=1`; `go test -race -p 1 ./api ./internal/engine ./auth -run 'Test(RequirePlatformWork|PrepareCreatedRuntime|Console|TenantService|TenantScheduler|SessionDatabaseCleanup|PausedOwnedSessionManager|AssembleHTTPProcess)' -count=1`; scoped vet; and `git diff --check` passed. Tests used `/dev/shm` Go temp/cache paths because the root filesystem has only 1.1 GiB free. Remaining G3 work includes the exhaustive background/admin admission audit, replay-retention coordination, broader database-version matrix and pooler compatibility.

**Shared-endpoint admission handoff verification (2026-10-15):** The created-site path now releases its platform permit before tenant installation, then separately admits the platform directory lookup and each durable onboarding state write. The request middleware uses an idempotent release hook, so handler handoff does not double-release; the worker path has the same explicit release boundary. A capacity-one regression executes directory lookup and tenant installation successively against one shared admission and proves there is no nested acquisition. Rechecked the retention gate against current durable sources: WAL and outbox history can remain replayable, NATS redelivery is independent of SQL publication, and no enforced backup-age/restore cutoff exists; therefore analytics receipts/checkpoints remain retained, with no speculative purge. Verified: `go test -p 1 ./api -count=1`, `go test -race -p 1 ./api -run 'Test(RequirePlatformWork|PrepareCreatedRuntime|Console|ScheduleOnboard)' -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./api ./internal/engine`, and `git diff --check` passed. G3 remains in progress; retention cutoff and full worker/dialect audits are open.

**Bounded analytics subscriber queues (2026-10-15, follow-up verified):** `MultiBus` rejects caller-supplied listener channels larger than 64 events; default generic listeners and CloudRelay use the same bounded capacity, while realtime has its own smaller item limit and slow listeners disconnect. Follow-up byte-budget work derives each subscriber channel capacity from the durable bus's maximum event size and a 16 MiB serialized-payload ceiling; listener snapshots expose per-tenant listener count, queued items, conservative queued/capacity bytes, and oldest-age upper bound. Engine OTLP aggregates those snapshots across resident tenant buses and exposes overflow/registration rejection counters. Thus the earlier item-count-only caveat is superseded. The cap is per listener and the gauges are observational; no independent process-wide weighted listener admission exists. No wire or persisted schema changed. The earlier `go test ./analytics ./api -run 'Test(MultiBus|Realtime|CloudRelay)' -count=1` and `go test -race ./analytics -count=1` passed. Current verification passed: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./analytics ./internal/engine -run 'Test(MultiBusListenerQueueCapacityUsesEventByteLimit|ResourceMetricsRegisterAndObserveProcessResourceGauges)' -count=1`; `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -race -p 1 ./analytics -run '^TestMultiBusListenerQueueCapacityUsesEventByteLimit$' -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./analytics ./internal/engine`; and `git diff --check`. A process-wide listener-byte admission budget remains an open G3 decision if per-tenant count, per-listener bytes, and resident-tenant limits prove too large for target deployment memory.

**Webhook compatibility with listener cap (2026-10-15):** Follow-up inspection found the webhook worker still requested a 1,000-event channel, which the new `MultiBus` bound would reject and close. It now allocates 64 events; a test uses the real `MultiBus` to verify registration and removal. Verified: `go test -race ./analytics ./webhook -count=1` and `git diff --check` passed. This prevents a bounded-queue change from silently disabling the webhook listener; durable webhook recovery and byte accounting remain open G3 work.

**Durable webhook source gap confirmed (2026-10-15):** Tracing `webhook.Worker` showed it created `_kora_webhook_delivery` rows only after consuming an in-memory `MultiBus` event; `RetryDeadLettersContext` retries HTTP delivery records but cannot recover a source event dropped by overflow or missed while the tenant worker is stopped. `orm.TxManager.writeOutbox` already emits `kora.<doctype>.after_*` envelopes with both current and historical field data in the same business transaction; command-level events use separate event names and should not be turned into record webhooks. This gave the durable adapter a bounded filter and the payload needed by current subscriptions. The outbox row itself is the durable source, so the implemented design dispatches the external effect from the outbox worker and records a consumer receipt only after delivery; a second SQL queue was not needed for this first adapter. Network delivery is outside the local DB transaction, remains at-least-once, and uses a stable provider idempotency key. This closes source-event recovery while the tenant worker is resident. It does not enable runtime eviction: a process-owned sweep across unloaded tenant IDs is still required. This was the verified dependency that led to the outbox-backed worker implementation below.

**Outbox-backed webhook delivery (2026-10-15):** For outbox-enabled tenants, Engine now runs a bounded-poll `outbox.Dispatcher` worker for only the five canonical `kora.<doctype>.after_*` record event families. The dispatcher supports parameterized SQL LIKE event-family filters, escaped with an explicit escape character, and checks the same filter again before invoking each worker. Webhook delivery decodes the existing current/old document payload, queries subscribed extensions, and only records the event receipt after processing finishes. Failed HTTP requests, retryable HTTP statuses, or delivery-log write failures leave the outbox receipt absent for retry. Finalized extension attempts are checked before replay so an earlier successful extension is skipped when another extension failed; outbound requests include stable `Idempotency-Key: <event-id>.<extension-name>` and delivery IDs use the source event ID. This follows at-least-once external delivery and does not promise exactly-once behavior if the receiver ignores idempotency or a crash occurs after the external effect and before the local attempt log. Outbox-disabled deployments retain the legacy bounded event-bus listener. No schema or wire contract changed. Verified: full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1` and `go build -o /dev/null .` passed; after the final SQL-pattern escape refinement, `go test -race ./outbox ./webhook ./internal/engine -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./outbox ./webhook ./internal/engine`, `git diff --check`, `make check-contracts`, and `make check-cross-repo-contracts` passed. The import graph regenerated and checked at 461 edges. Query-pattern regression proves unrelated event families are excluded, replay regression proves finalized recipients are skipped, and invalid source envelopes remain retryable. Full multi-dialect end-to-end delivery and dormant-tenant scanning remain open.

**Transient startup tenant credentials (2026-10-15):** Process startup now clears `PlatformSiteDirectory.Sites` immediately after the eager startup loader completes, so resolved tenant DB passwords and coordinates are not retained inside `ProcessStartupAssembly` for the process lifetime. `DiscoveredTenants` preserves the startup count independently for readiness metrics. A startup-assembly regression supplies a credential-bearing fixture, proves the loader sees it, verifies the retained platform snapshot is empty, and shuts down the acquired platform DB through the owner. No contract or schema changed. Verified: `go test -race ./internal/engine ./cli -run 'TestAssembleProcessStartup|TestCollectEngineMetrics' -count=1` and `git diff --check` passed. Startup remains eager; credentials are still transiently loaded for all local tenants and full lazy activation/residency remains open.

**Lazy-start composition dependency audit (2026-10-15, updated):** Engine no longer passes `StartupTenants.PrimaryDB` (the first tenant pool) into application fallback or platform health routes. The application fallback/session manager and health check now use the platform DB; tenant requests continue to use their pinned `site_db`/`site_session_manager`. A constructor regression supplies distinct platform and tenant pools, proves the fallback uses only the platform pool, and verifies rollback closes its owned platform DB. Scheduler jobs are loaded as definitions and instantiated against each tenant generation's own DB, registry, transaction manager, webhook worker and script store; the prior first-tenant scheduler binding is removed. Configured scheduled work still needs durable process-level discovery/wakeup before tenant eviction. Cold startup still eagerly activates tenants; replace it with credential-free directory descriptors and bounded on-demand activation before claiming resident-pool bounds.

**Bounded lazy tenant residency (2026-10-15):** Added `MaxResidentTenants` to Engine runtime composition, default 256 and configurable through `KORA_MAX_RESIDENT_TENANTS`. The runtime manager rejects an initial set above the cap and atomically rejects activation when full; serialized coordinator activation checks active plus retiring generations before credential lookup/runtime load, while the manager remains the atomic publication guard. Host/cookie/path routing maps the capacity error to HTTP 503 with `Retry-After: 1` and `resident_capacity`. OTLP exports configured resident capacity; resource observations now use metric-name keys so adding a gauge cannot shift existing cache/queue values. Tests prove manager cap and initial validation, cold third-tenant rejection without loading, same-tenant activation coalescing, and metric registration. No wire/persisted schema change. Verified: `go test ./internal/tenantruntime ./internal/engine ./net -count=1`; `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/tenantruntime ./internal/engine ./net ./ingress -count=1`; full `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`; `go vet -p 2 ./...`; `go build -o /dev/null .`; `make check-contracts`; `make check-cross-repo-contracts`; `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check` (regenerated to 463 edges); and `git diff --check`. A final `go test ./internal/engine ./internal/tenantruntime ./net -count=1` and race-enabled resident/resource metric subset also passed after the metric fixture was tightened to assert capacity 2. Vet/build completed without diagnostics; the full scoped race run passed. A first clean-cache race attempt hit disk exhaustion; only the Go build cache was cleared, and reruns passed. The cap intentionally does not evict: scheduled jobs and durable outbox/workflow/webhook work still depend on resident tenant workers. Documentation/spec now state this capacity-versus-eviction behavior explicitly.

**Credential-free lazy tenant activation (2026-10-15):** Platform startup now reads only credential-free `SiteDescriptor` aliases/status/revisions, leaves tenant runtime and pool sets empty, and retains no credential-bearing `DBSiteInfo` list. The runtime manager indexes active and inactive descriptors separately from loaded generations; host, site-cookie/name, and `/s/:site` routing invokes a coordinator that resolves one tenant's descriptor and credentials, loads its DB/services, and publishes one complete generation. Concurrent first requests serialize before credential lookup, preventing duplicate tenant pool construction. Directory feed/resync refreshes alias/status metadata without loading credentials for cold active tenants; loaded same-config runtimes apply a newer directory projection without rebuilding the config registry. The platform DB now supplies app fallback/session and health checks; cell ingress treats locally assigned active descriptors as locally activatable while preserving remote forwarding.
