Tests cover cold resync without credential lookup, deferred feed activation, concurrent requests producing exactly one loader/install, real host middleware activation, activation failure returning 503, and independent directory/config revision updates. No wire/schema change; the target spec records the same-config directory projection rule. Verified: `go test ./internal/engine ./net ./site ./ingress -count=1` and `go test -race ./internal/engine ./net ./ingress ./internal/tenantruntime -count=1` passed; this turn reran the race command after formatting and it passed. `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check` (462 edges), and `git diff --check` also passed. This removes eager cold-start pool multiplication; the subsequent bounded-residency entry below caps active pools but does not yet provide safe eviction or guarantee service to every cold tenant under sustained churn.

**Platform fallback database separation (2026-10-15):** `ApplicationConfig.Database` now uses the platform DB for process-level fallback/session routes, and `PlatformRouteConfig` no longer receives the first tenant pool as its primary health target. Tenant handlers still use the pinned tenant DB and session manager from the runtime snapshot. A constructor test injects distinct sqlmock platform/tenant pools, checks the composed references, and verifies platform-pool rollback. No schema or wire contract changed. Verified: `go test -race ./internal/engine -run '^TestNewUsesPlatformDatabaseForApplicationFallbacks$' -count=1` and `git diff --check` passed. This removes first-tenant fallback coupling; it does not make startup lazy.

**Tenant-owned scheduled jobs (2026-10-15):** Engine now parses scheduler job definitions once without binding them to a tenant resource, then constructs one scheduler for each tenant generation using that runtime's DB, registry, transaction manager, transactional outbox writer, webhook worker, script store, and tenant identity. Scheduler start/stop runs inside the tenant generation's worker join path, so reload/retirement cancels and joins jobs before tenant pools close. Each scheduled execution acquires the tenant's shared active-work permit once and releases it after the job effect; capacity rejection skips the effect for that tick. Process-level HTTP composition no longer requests its legacy first-tenant scheduler. Added loader tests for malformed/null definitions, a two-tenant regression proving scheduler DB/registry/transaction bindings remain isolated, and an admission regression proving one acquire/release and fail-closed execution. No public contract or persisted schema changed. Verified: `go test ./internal/engine ./scheduler -count=1`, `go test -race ./internal/engine ./scheduler -count=1`, `go vet ./internal/engine ./scheduler`, and `git diff --check` passed. After adding scheduled-job admission, full Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `go vet -p 1 ./...`, `make check-contracts`, `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check` (462 edges), `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check` all passed.

**Legacy webhook active-work admission (2026-10-15):** The outbox-backed dispatcher already admits one permit around its durable batch; the non-outbox compatibility listener now acquires tenant active-work capacity once per live event. When capacity is transiently unavailable it retains the current bounded event in the single consumer and retries with cancellation-aware delay, rather than dropping it on the first admission rejection. Engine supplies the tenant's endpoint-shared admission; durable outbox callbacks do not reacquire. A worker regression proves a rejected attempt is retried and the effect runs only after a permit is acquired and released. Verified: `go test -race ./webhook ./internal/engine ./scheduler -count=1`, scoped vet, and `git diff --check` passed. After this webhook admission change, full Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `go vet -p 1 ./...`, `make check-contracts`, import graph check (462 edges), `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check` all passed. This removes the first-tenant scheduler coupling but does not provide durable discovery for scheduled work while a tenant is unloaded; lazy residency remains blocked on that; the first-tenant application/platform fallback has been removed and tested.

**Changes and contracts:** Engine routes production SQL pools through internal/dbaccess, sharing bounded connection permits across pools targeting the same endpoint. Defaults include 64 physical connections per process and endpoint, bounded waiter count/deadline, and a separate active-work admission limit. Operators can statically divide a declared backend connection budget across replicas; endpoint aliases can share a key. Tenant pool caps remain per handle. Outbox publication/dispatch, NATS analytics rebuild, human-wait recovery, scheduled jobs, durable webhook dispatch, analytics flush, monthly rollup, and retention cleanup operations acquire one active-work permit each. Connector wrappers preserve context-aware driver interfaces and now forward legacy-only Execer and Queryer drivers with named-argument normalization. Process shutdown drains async hooks and tenant workers, closes the script runner, then closes tenant pools, preserving the runner's DB dependencies until execution has joined. Local analytics work drains bounded event batches from the durable WAL. Its per-tenant in-memory wake-up channel now derives capacity from both the event count and a conservative serialized-payload budget (64 MiB default, configurable to 1 GiB); the WAL remains authoritative when the hint channel is full. OTLP metrics report queued items, item capacity, serialized-payload upper bound, and configured byte budget. Async script hooks have a 1,000-item count cap, 64 MiB aggregate estimated-payload cap and 1 MiB per-item cap; snapshot diagnostics report queued bytes/items and rejected items. Tenant analytics fan-out caps listeners at 128 and `Subscribe` reports capacity exhaustion; legacy `AddListener` closes rejected channels and increments the drop counter. Realtime listeners use bounded queues and close on overflow on both local and NATS paths; Engine also caps process-wide live realtime handlers at 128 by default, configurable through `KORA_REALTIME_MAX_STREAMS` (1–4,096). Capacity rejection returns 503 with a retry hint. OTLP metrics expose active streams, process capacity, and rejected admissions. Tenant/process session managers retain at most 8 MiB or 8,192 entries individually and now share an Engine-owned 256 MiB aggregate byte budget, configurable through `KORA_SESSION_CACHE_MAX_BYTES` (up to 4 GiB); cache bypasses when the shared budget is full, while current authentication continues. The platform console-session store caps live sessions at 8,192 and rejects login when no expired slot can be reclaimed. OTLP metrics expose shared session-cache usage/capacity. The process script runner additionally enforces tenant-scoped concurrency, bounded waiters, a bounded active-tenant gate map, and a maximum execution timeout; Goja RAM remains advisory. Operational values and exact scope are documented in `resource-budgets.md`.

**Checks and results:** Latest Engine source passes `go test -race -p 2 ./db ./requests/sqlstore ./workflow/sqlstore ./site -count=1`, `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-contracts`, `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check` (457 internal edges), `make check-cross-repo-contracts` (11 fixture/schema/bundle artifacts and current route inventory), and `git diff --check`. Runner tests cover per-tenant rejection, different-tenant concurrency, bounded waiting, timeout clamp and cancellation/join on shutdown. Session-cache tests cover bounded LRU eviction, oversized nonretention, TTL reclamation and mutation isolation. The resource metrics manual-reader test proves instrument collection and verifies ProcessResources unregisters the callback before telemetry shutdown. The latest stream/upload admission slice and aggregate session/console changes also passed targeted race tests in `./internal/engine ./api ./cli` and earlier full-suite runs.

**Disposable database integration evidence (2026-10-15):** Used fresh loopback-only, non-volume-backed services: PostgreSQL 16 (`postgres:16-alpine`), MySQL 8.4.11 (`mysql:8.4.11`), and LibSQL sqld 0.24.33 over its HTTP transport. The final clean LibSQL file run plus the disposable PostgreSQL/MySQL endpoints passed `go test -tags integration -p 1 ./analytics ./outbox ./site ./requests/sqlstore ./workflow/sqlstore -count=1`. Coverage included analytics persistence/rebuild/leases, transactional outbox receipts, the 10,000-entry MySQL site-directory projection, directory lifecycle and routed-generation replacement, LibSQL request schema/read, and workflow claim/timed-wait recovery. A real LibSQL result exposed timestamp expressions returned as text; `db.Timestamp` now scans native `time.Time`, string and byte representations, and request/workflow stores use it for driver-returned timestamp expressions. Tests cover timezone offsets, UTC text, native values, NULL and invalid values. The PostgreSQL routed-generation integration assertion now reads the current config revision from the typed runtime snapshot rather than the removed opaque service field. The workflow LibSQL fixture now installs the request schema required by recovery queries and executes the resumed timeout edge before claiming its next scenario. All integration tests passed on a new database after those corrections. No public wire contract or persisted schema changed. Poolers were not tested or recommended.

Exact integration invocation (all endpoints were fresh disposable loopback services; LibSQL used a new database file):

```sh
KORA_ANALYTICS_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN='[REDACTED]' \
KORA_OUTBOX_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN='[REDACTED]' \
KORA_SITE_DIRECTORY_LIVE_POSTGRES_DSN='[REDACTED]' \
KORA_ANALYTICS_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' \
KORA_SITE_DIRECTORY_LIVE_MYSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' \
KORA_ANALYTICS_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' \
KORA_OUTBOX_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' \
KORA_SITE_DIRECTORY_LIVE_LIBSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' \
KORA_WORKFLOW_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' \
KORA_REQUESTS_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' \
go test -tags integration -p 1 ./analytics ./outbox ./site ./requests/sqlstore ./workflow/sqlstore -count=1
```

Upload admission is process-owned and weighted by three times each upload request-body cap; the upload body cap defaults to 50 MiB and has a 1 GiB hard maximum. The independent upload path exists because uploads do not consume tenant SQL active-work permits while loading and processing file bytes.

**Open follow-on risks (re-audited 2026-10-15; not G3 exit criteria):** The G3 resource-budget exit gate is verified in the later “G3 exit gate verification” entry below. Its verified scope is aggregate process/per-replica pools and active work, bounded waiters and resident tenants, bounded memory/queues/subscriptions/scripts/uploads, cold durable-work discovery, and contention/resource telemetry. Separate follow-on risks remain: (1) notification delivery and workflow execution are staged G5/G6 capabilities; their admission-aware constructors are not automatically Engine-composed without those capabilities; (2) Core NATS provides pending count/bytes and sampled-nonempty span, not exact per-message client-buffer age; (3) the self-host helper computes a backup-only floor, but no global replay floor spans retained backups, WAL, outbox, receipts/checkpoints, and broker acknowledgments, so resumable pruning is not implemented and correctness records remain retained; (4) broader database/version operation coverage and pooler compatibility are incomplete. The current real-dialect tests prove selected operations and database-generated contention (MySQL 8.4.11/PostgreSQL 16; tested LibSQL HTTP writes serialize without lock errors), not the full matrix. Prepared-statement fallback and live server lock queues remain unmeasured. Keep poolers unrecommended until their dedicated suites pass. The replay-retention floor is a safety prerequisite before deleting correctness records, not evidence that the bounded pool/work G3 exit gate failed.

**Disable lossy webhook fallback when integrations are opted out (2026-10-15):** Tenant service composition previously started the legacy bounded in-memory webhook listener whenever `KORA_OUTBOX=false`. That path could miss events on queue overflow or runtime retirement, despite README documenting false as disabling reports and event-driven integrations. Composition now starts webhook delivery only through the durable transactional outbox path; with outbox disabled it registers no listener or worker. The webhook service remains available for scheduler retry operations, and the existing env opt-out semantics are preserved. No schema or wire contract changed. Regression test proves the disabled configuration neither starts the worker nor registers a live listener. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./internal/engine -run 'Test(RegisterWebhookDelivery|NewTenantServiceInstaller|TenantServiceInstaller)' -count=1`, same with `-race`, full `go test -p 1 ./internal/engine ./webhook ./scheduler -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./internal/engine ./webhook ./scheduler`, and `git diff --check` all passed. Full Engine race suite not rerun for this slice. This closes only the explicitly disabled-mode lossy path; G3's admission audit and coordinated replay-retention contract remain open.

**Replay-retention source audit (2026-10-15):** Rechecked current writers, cleanup paths, broker settings, and self-hosted backup/restore procedures. `_kora_outbox` has no deletion path; analytics receipts/checkpoints have no pruning; WAL has no disk-size limit and remains until commit/acknowledgment; the main NATS stream is capped with reject-new semantics; backup sets do not record tenant source-sequence floors and restore does not validate one. Thus no common maximum replay horizon can be enforced safely today, and durable event-history disk growth is unbounded. No deletion or new arbitrary age cutoff was added. Updated `resource-budgets.md` with the storage exposure and prerequisites: per-tenant monotonic sequence floor, backup manifest floor, restore validation, acknowledgment across every replay source, and resumable coordinated deletion. Evidence: source searches for outbox delete/prune, WAL size controls, and floor metadata; inspected `deploy/selfhost/backup.md` and `restore.md`; `git diff --check` passed. No DB/schema/wire changes. G3 remains open pending that protocol and admission/observability audits.

**Replay-source identity cross-check (2026-10-15):** Compared concrete completion identities in `analytics/bus.go`, `outbox/outbox.go`, `analytics/schema.go`, `analytics/rebuild_state.go`, `natsprovider/provider.go`, and the backup manifest helper. WAL removal acknowledges event IDs; outbox publication and applicable consumer receipts are separate facts keyed by event; analytics receipts are event-ID keyed while rebuild checkpoints are scoped source-sequence cutoffs; JetStream uses stream sequence; backups carry per-database source-sequence high-water marks. Documented this mismatch and the rule that none alone can advance a shared floor in `resource-budgets.md`. Validation: `git diff --check` and edited-document trailing-whitespace scan passed. No runtime behavior, schema, or wire contract changed; pruning remains disabled. Next implementation needs a durable per-tenant floor and contiguous acknowledgment projection covering each replay source before any deletion path is considered.

**Durable cold-tenant discovery and safe idle eviction (2026-10-15):** Added a process-owned scanner with persisted cursor/sweep timestamp, bounded paged directory reads, context-bounded one-tenant-at-a-time database probes under shared endpoint work admission, and wakeups for due schedule occurrences, outbox/webhook deliveries, waiting workflow signals/timers, request deadlines, notification deliveries, analytics rebuild jobs, and analytics WAL. Added `analytics.WALHasPending` and checks for current/rotated files. The runtime manager retires only zero-reference LRU generations; coordinator keeps capacity reserved until close succeeds. Same-revision generation fencing and active-handle eviction are covered by manager/coordinator tests. Additive platform table: `_kora_durable_scan_cursor`; tenant schema unchanged by scanner. Verified: `go test ./analytics ./internal/engine ./internal/tenantruntime ./net -count=1`; `go test -race -p 1 ./analytics ./internal/engine ./internal/tenantruntime ./net -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./analytics ./internal/engine ./internal/tenantruntime ./net`; `git diff --check`; and `go test -tags=integration ./internal/engine -run '^TestDurableWorkScannerFindsUnreceiptedTenantWorkAcrossDialects$' -count=1 -timeout=180s` against fresh disposable MySQL 8.4.11, PostgreSQL 16, and actual HTTP LibSQL. The scanner integration verifies persisted cursor CAS/sweep time, due-scheduler discovery, webhook receipt gating, analytics rebuild lease gating, and workflow-signal receipt gating. Disposable containers were removed. A broader selected-package command `go test ./internal/dbaccess ./script ./auth ./api ./cli ./outbox ./scheduler/... ./webhook ./workflow ./internal/engine ./internal/tenantruntime ./net -count=1` passed all packages except `cli` on its first attempt, when its test binary linker failed with `no space left on device`; after clearing only Go build/test caches, `go test ./cli -count=1` passed. The other packages passed in the original run. G3 remains in progress: remaining worker admission/metrics, coordinated replay retention, full operation/version coverage, and pooler compatibility remain open; no pooled endpoint is recommended.

**Analytics queue-age telemetry (2026-10-15):** Added enqueue-age upper-bound snapshots for the bounded in-memory analytics wake-up channel and MultiBus listener queues. Queue metadata is maintained alongside sends; snapshots never receive from or consume public channels. Engine exports low-cardinality `kora.analytics.queue.oldest_age_upper_bound_ns` and `kora.analytics.listeners.oldest_age_upper_bound_ns` gauges. The metric is a conservative upper bound while a queue is nonempty, not exact per-event latency; it excludes records waiting only in durable WAL. No wire or persisted schema changed. Changed: `analytics/{bus.go,bus_test.go,multibus.go}`, `internal/engine/{resource_metrics.go,resource_metrics_test.go}`, and `docs/architecture/resource-budgets.md`. Verified: `go test ./analytics ./internal/engine -count=1`; `go test ./internal/engine -run '^TestResourceMetricsRegisterAndObserveProcessResourceGauges$' -count=1`; `go test -race -p 1 ./analytics ./internal/engine -count=1` passed analytics but Engine race compilation failed with `no space left on device`; `go vet ./analytics ./internal/engine` passed. The standalone wake-queue age regression and Engine metric-reader test passed in the normal run. Durable WAL oldest-age telemetry, drop-cause classification, and worker lease-loss/lock metrics remain open. The same resource-metrics slice adds `kora.process.rss_bytes`, sampled from `/proc/self/statm` on Linux with zero reported on unsupported platforms; the Engine metric-reader test verifies the gauge. The same callback exports `kora.analytics.events.dropped`, summing each resident bus's existing rejection/drop count; the manual-reader test rejects an oversized event and observes the count. No per-cause breakdown is claimed. `go test ./internal/engine -run '^TestResourceMetricsRegisterAndObserveProcessResourceGauges$' -count=1`, `go vet ./analytics ./internal/engine`, and `git diff --check` passed after this addition.

**Bounded async-hook queue and event subscriptions (2026-10-14):** Engine now validates configurable async-hook count, aggregate estimated-JSON-byte, and per-item limits; defaults are 1,000 items, 64 MiB queued bytes, and 1 MiB per event, configured with `KORA_ASYNC_HOOK_QUEUE_SIZE`, `KORA_ASYNC_HOOK_MAX_QUEUED_BYTES`, and `KORA_ASYNC_HOOK_MAX_EVENT_BYTES`. Rejections are explicit, byte accounting is released on dequeue, and `QueueSnapshot` exposes occupancy/limits/rejected-item count. `analytics.MultiBus` caps each tenant at 128 listeners, rejects full `Subscribe` calls, and closes/reports channels through the legacy listener adapter. Changes are in `internal/engine/{async_hooks.go,async_hooks_test.go,http_process.go,config.go}`, `cli/{serve.go,serve_test.go}`, `analytics/{multibus.go,multibus_test.go}`, and `docs/architecture/resource-budgets.md`; no wire or persisted schema changed. Exact checks passed: `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`; `go test -race -p 2 ./analytics ./internal/engine ./internal/dbaccess ./cli -count=1`; `go vet -p 2 ./...`; `go build -o /dev/null .`; `make check-contracts`; `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check`; `make check-cross-repo-contracts`; and `git diff --check`. G3 remains in progress; this checkpoint does not prove lazy tenant residency, the remaining queue/cache/script limits, full metrics, replay-horizon compaction, or pooler compatibility.

**Tenant-scoped script admission (2026-10-14):** Added `script.BudgetedRunner` as the shared Engine runner boundary, with default two active executions and four bounded waiters per tenant, one-second admission wait, 256 active/waiting tenant-gate maximum, and a five-second enforced context timeout. Tenant gate entries retire after the last caller leaves; shutdown cancels admitted calls, joins them, then closes the underlying runner. Engine config supports explicit `script.BudgetConfig`; CLI parses `KORA_SCRIPT_MAX_CONCURRENCY`, `KORA_SCRIPT_MAX_WAITERS`, `KORA_SCRIPT_MAX_ACTIVE_TENANTS`, `KORA_SCRIPT_WAIT_MS`, and `KORA_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT_MS`. `script/budget_test.go` covers per-tenant rejection, independent tenant progress, shutdown cancellation/join, timeout clamping and an actual deadline-triggered return; normal and race test runs passed. The Goja `MaxRAM` value remains advisory. Verification passed: full Go suite, targeted race suite, vet/build, contract and cross-repository checks, regenerated import graph (455 edges), and diff check as listed above. G3 remains open for lazy tenant residency, other memory and queue byte caps, complete admission/metrics, replay retention, and DB/pooler gates.

**Process resource telemetry (2026-10-14):** When OTLP metrics are enabled, Engine registers low-cardinality observable gauges for aggregate dbaccess connection capacity/use/waiters, active-work capacity/use/waiters, active/retiring runtime counts, async-hook queue item/byte occupancy and capacity, script active/waiting counts and active gate count, goroutine count, and heap allocation/system bytes. Cumulative wait counts/durations and rejections are observable counters. The callback sums endpoints without endpoint or tenant labels. `ResourceMetrics` is unregistered by `ProcessResources` before telemetry shutdown; an OTel manual-reader test collects gauges/counters and verifies owner shutdown order. Files: `internal/engine/resource_metrics.go`, its test, `config.go`, `http_process.go`, `resources.go`, `net/siterouter.go`, and `internal/tenantruntime/manager.go`; no contract or schema change. Full suite, targeted race tests, vet/build, contract, import-graph and cross-repository checks passed as listed above. Remaining metrics include analytics queue age/bytes and payload rejections, lock/query timing, and worker lease loss. G3 remains in progress.

**Byte-bounded session cache (2026-10-14):** `auth.SessionManager` now uses an LRU with per-manager caps of 8 MiB and 8,192 entries. A user snapshot that exceeds the byte cap is returned for the current authenticated request but is not retained. Cache expiration is swept every 30 seconds; database session/magic-link cleanup remains every five minutes. Cached user and roles are copied both into and out of the cache so caller mutation cannot change cached authorization state or invalidate its byte accounting. `SessionCacheSnapshot` exposes bounded occupancy, included in aggregate OTLP resource metrics. Tests cover byte-pressure LRU eviction, oversized-entry nonretention, expiry reclamation and caller mutation isolation. Current full suite, auth/Engine race suite, vet/build, contract, import-graph and cross-repository checks passed as listed above. Changed Engine files: `auth/session.go`, `auth/session_test.go`, `internal/engine/resource_metrics.go` and its test, plus `docs/architecture/resource-budgets.md`. No schema or wire contract changed. G3 remains open because total cache allowance still scales with resident tenant count and lazy residency is not implemented.

**Analytics event payload ceiling (2026-10-14):** Analytics channel capacity is capped at 10,000 items both in configuration and at construction, and each event is limited to 1 MiB encoded by default (configurable from 1 byte to 16 MiB with `KORA_ANALYTICS_MAX_EVENT_BYTES`). Oversized events are rejected before WAL creation/write and counted by the existing `Dropped()` diagnostic; `NewChannelBus` preserves source compatibility and uses the default, while tenant service assembly uses the configurable constructor. `analytics/bus_test.go` proves an oversized payload produces no WAL file and a direct constructor call cannot exceed the channel cap; config tests cover the default and override. `go test -race ./analytics -count=1`, full `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, full vet/build, `make check-contracts`, architecture import graph check, cross-repository contract check, and `git diff --check` passed after the payload-limit change; the newest channel-cap tests passed in `go test -race ./analytics ./internal/dbaccess -count=1`, `go test ./internal/engine -count=1`, and `git diff --check`. No wire or persisted schema changed. The size check bounds retained/persisted event payloads, not memory already held by the caller or temporary serialization memory. Analytics queue byte/age telemetry and coordinated receipt/WAL retention remain open G3 gates.

**Shared active-work budget across tenant pools (2026-10-14):** Added a regression test showing two `AdmissionFor` lookups for different tenant database DSNs on one physical endpoint receive the same active-work limiter, and saturation in one pool rejects work from the other. `go test -race ./internal/dbaccess -count=1` and `go test ./internal/dbaccess -count=1` passed. This validates the process-local endpoint boundary; static replica allocation is still operator-coordinated and is not a distributed semaphore. Lazy residency and durable-work discovery remain the next major G3 requirement.

**Analytics work admission (2026-10-14):** Tenant analytics workers now receive the endpoint-shared active-work limiter. A flush holds one permit for its complete transaction/receipt/WAL-ack unit; monthly rollup and retention cleanup each acquire once for their own operation. Empty flush ticks do not acquire capacity, and admission rejection leaves pending deltas intact for retry. Focused tests prove one acquire/release on failed database work and batch retention on admission rejection. `go test ./analytics ./internal/engine -count=1`, `go test -race ./analytics ./internal/engine -count=1`, full `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 2 ./analytics ./internal/engine ./internal/dbaccess`, and `git diff --check` passed. The worker setter is applied to the concrete worker before it is published through the existing compatibility interface; no contract/schema change. Remaining unadmitted work and lazy tenant residency remain G3 gates.

**Realtime overflow and queue bounds (2026-10-14):** Local realtime listeners now have an eight-event queue and are removed/closed with their pending events discarded on overflow; the existing per-tenant listener cap bounds concurrent local streams. NATS realtime uses an eight-message application channel, an eight-message/8 MiB client pending limit, and a 1 MiB per-message ceiling; dropped or oversized messages close the stream so clients reconnect and refetch authorized state. Embedded NATS tests prove overflow and oversized-message closure, and a MultiBus test proves local overflow removes the listener and discards its buffered events. `go test -race -p 2 ./analytics ./natsprovider ./api -count=1`, full `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 2 ./analytics ./natsprovider ./api`, and `git diff --check` passed. The first race run's API link hit a full filesystem; only rebuildable Go build/test caches were cleared, after which the same race checks passed. Per-connection queues are now bounded; aggregate streams across resident tenants and queue occupancy metrics remain open.

**Process-wide realtime stream admission (2026-10-14):** Added a process-owned fail-fast stream semaphore to Engine configuration and the immutable tenant service snapshot. `KORA_REALTIME_MAX_STREAMS` accepts 1–4,096 streams and defaults to 128; excess SSE/WebSocket handlers return HTTP 503 with `Retry-After: 1` before replay or subscription, and each admitted handler releases its slot on exit. Metrics report active streams, capacity and rejected admissions. Unit tests cover saturation, idempotent release, canceled contexts and API rejection; the metric manual-reader test verifies active/capacity values. `go test ./internal/engine ./api ./cli -count=1`, `go test -race ./internal/engine ./api ./cli -count=1`, `go vet ./internal/engine ./api ./cli`, and `git diff --check` passed. No contract or persisted schema changed. This bounds stream count per process, not across replicas; queue byte occupancy, broad G3 resource admission, lazy tenant residency, durable-work discovery, replay compaction, and database/pooler compatibility remain open.

**Analytics wake-up queue byte budget (2026-10-14):** Added `KORA_ANALYTICS_MAX_QUEUED_BYTES` (64 MiB default, up to 1 GiB) alongside the existing per-event size and item limits. `NewChannelBusWithLimits` derives the in-memory WAL wake-up channel capacity from the lower of the item cap and the serialized-payload byte budget divided by event limit; existing constructors retain source compatibility and use the default byte budget. The WAL remains the durable source, so events whose wake-up channel is full remain replayable. `QueueBudget` exposes item occupancy/capacity and conservative serialized-payload upper bound; `MultiBus` forwards this snapshot and Engine OTLP metrics aggregate it across resident tenant buses. Tests verify effective capacity, all-event WAL replay despite channel saturation, config defaults/overrides and metric values. Checks passed: `go test ./analytics ./internal/engine -count=1`, `go test -race ./analytics ./internal/engine -count=1`, full `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 2 ./...`, and `git diff --check`. This bounds only the base channel's serialized-payload allowance; Go object overhead and separate MultiBus subscriber queues remain additional. G3 remains open for lazy tenant residency and durable-work discovery, full admission/resource metrics, pooler/dialect verification, and replay retention policy.

**Aggregate session-cache budget (2026-10-15):** Added an atomic process-shared retained-byte budget in `auth.SessionCacheBudget`; Engine attaches it to tenant and process session managers before requests/workers start. Managers keep their existing 8 MiB/8,192-entry local LRU limits, reserve shared bytes before retaining snapshots, release accounting on entry eviction/manager close, and skip new caching when the process cap is full while continuing the current DB-backed authentication. `KORA_SESSION_CACHE_MAX_BYTES` defaults to 256 MiB and accepts 1 byte through 4 GiB. Engine metrics expose shared bytes/capacity. Tests cover 64 concurrent managers contending for an eight-entry budget (20 race-enabled repetitions), capacity reuse after close, budget replacement accounting, invalid Engine startup configuration, CLI parsing and OTel metric values. Changed repositories/files: Engine `auth/session_cache_budget.go`, `auth/session.go`, `auth/session_test.go`, `internal/engine/{config.go,resources.go,tenant_services.go,application.go,resource_metrics.go,resource_metrics_test.go}`, `cli/{serve.go,serve_test.go}`, and architecture resource/status docs. No wire or persisted schema changed. Verified: `go test -race ./auth ./cli ./internal/engine -count=1`; `go test -race ./auth -run 'TestSessionCacheBudget' -count=20`; `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`; `go vet -p 2 ./...`; `go build -o /dev/null .`; `make check-contracts`; `make check-cross-repo-contracts`; and `git diff --check`. The graph was regenerated to 457 edges and both graph/contract gates pass. G3 remains open for residency/durable-work discovery, remaining queue/admission limits, dialect and pooler verification.

**Bounded platform console sessions (2026-10-15):** `SystemGuard` now caps retained console sessions at 8,192. When full, it reclaims expired entries under the same lock; if capacity remains exhausted, the login handler returns HTTP 503 instead of growing the in-memory map. Added tests for saturation and expired-session reclamation. The legacy `CreateSession` API remains source-compatible, while Engine’s login route uses the error-returning bounded method. No schema or contract changed. Verified: `go test -race ./auth ./api ./cli ./internal/engine -count=1`; full `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`; `go vet -p 2 ./...`; `go build -o /dev/null .`; import graph generation/check (457 edges); `make check-contracts`; `make check-cross-repo-contracts`; and `git diff --check`. This also does not close lazy tenant residency or durable-work discovery.

**Weighted process upload admission (2026-10-15):** Added `UploadAdmission` as a process-owned, fail-fast count-and-byte budget exposed through each immutable tenant service snapshot. Upload handlers reserve three times the bounded request-body limit before parsing or loading file bytes; excess count/bytes return 503 with `Retry-After`, and deferred release covers every handler exit. Defaults are four active uploads and 512 MiB reserved processing bytes, configurable with `KORA_UPLOAD_MAX_ACTIVE` and `KORA_UPLOAD_ACTIVE_BUDGET_BYTES`; per-upload request bodies are hard-capped at 1 GiB. OTLP metrics expose active uploads, reserved bytes, configured capacities, and rejections. Focused tests cover count/byte contention, oversized/canceled reservations, handler rejection/release, max-file-byte clamp, CLI configuration validation and metric values. No wire or persisted schema changed. Verified: `go test -race ./internal/engine ./api ./cli -count=1`; `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`; `go vet -p 2 ./...`; `go build -o /dev/null .`; `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py` (457 edges); `make check-contracts`; `make check-cross-repo-contracts`; and `git diff --check`. G3 remains open for lazy tenant residency/durable-work discovery, remaining unadmitted work, fan-out queue byte accounting, and database/pooler gates.


**Byte-bounded analytics subscriber queues (2026-10-15):** `QueueBudgetSnapshot` now exposes the inner bus's configured maximum event payload. `MultiBus` derives listener capacities from that maximum and a 16 MiB serialized-payload budget per listener; local realtime queues use the same budget. CloudRelay and webhook consumers allocate the derived capacity, and local realtime uses the derived realtime capacity. Explicit channels above the derived capacity are closed/rejected. `ListenerQueueBudget` reports listener count, queued items, and conservative byte occupancy/capacity; Engine OTLP metrics aggregate those values across resident tenants. Tests prove a 4 MiB event limit yields capacity four, reject capacity five, and expose the aggregate capacity/occupancy metrics. No wire or persisted schema changed. Verified: `go test -race ./analytics ./webhook ./api ./internal/engine -count=1`; full `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`; `go vet -p 2 ./...`; `go build -o /dev/null .`; `make check-contracts`; `make check-cross-repo-contracts`; route inventory check; import graph generation and check (463 edges); and `git diff --check`. G3 remains in progress; durable-work discovery and safe eviction, queue age and remaining memory/admission/dialect gates are still open.

**Durable tenant scheduler occurrences (2026-10-15):** Added a dialect-aware SQL store under `scheduler/sqlstore` for immutable scheduled-job snapshots, durable occurrence rows, owner/fence leases, attempts, completion, and `outcome_unknown`. Tenant scheduler assembly now requires stable tenant identity, creates a process-unique worker ID, ensures the schema, and uses the tenant's SQL database. Identical definition reloads preserve the schedule cursor; changed definitions retain pending occurrence snapshots so old scheduled work executes with its original configuration. Claims and lease deadlines use database time, and expired/failed executions become visible as uncertain rather than being automatically replayed. Local scheduled execution retains one endpoint-shared active-work permit across claim, effect, lease renewal and completion. No public contract changed; additive per-tenant tables `_kora_schedule_definition` and `_kora_schedule_occurrence` are created on scheduler setup. Verified: `go test -race ./scheduler/... ./internal/engine -count=1`, `go vet ./scheduler/... ./internal/engine`, `git diff --check`, and `go test -tags=integration ./scheduler/sqlstore -count=1` against fresh disposable MySQL 8.4.11, PostgreSQL 16 and LibSQL HTTP services. The integration also proves concurrent workers cannot claim the same due occurrence and stale lease owners cannot complete expired work. The whole Engine `go test -p 2 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 2 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and import-graph check also passed; regenerated graph has 468 edges. Unit tests cover digest determinism, cross-dialect DDL presence, invalid digests, concurrent first registration recovery, tenant DB isolation, and scheduler lease lifecycle. The process-wide unloaded-tenant durable-work scanner and operator reconciliation for uncertain external outcomes are not implemented; therefore safe eviction remains blocked and G3 remains in progress.

**Platform database cap and deletion-worker admission (2026-10-15):** Set an explicit maximum-open connection cap on the platform `sql.DB` (`KORA_PLATFORM_DB_MAX_OPEN`, default 4, valid 1–256), validated before platform bootstrap proceeds. This caps platform pool occupancy within the existing endpoint-shared connector semaphore; it does not double-count a separate physical allocation. Pending tenant-deletion recovery now acquires one endpoint-shared active-work permit for its bounded retry pass and defers the attempt when admission is full. Added focused configuration and worker-admission tests. No schema or wire contract changed. Verified: `gofmt`; `go test ./internal/engine -run 'Test(PlatformPoolLimitConfiguration|TunePlatformDBPoolAppliesMaximumOpenConnections|PendingDeletionWorker|PlatformDirectoryStartup)' -count=1`; the same focused command with `-race`; `go vet ./internal/engine`; and `git diff --check` all passed. Full Engine build/tests were not run because filesystem headroom was 247 MiB after focused race checks. G3 remains in progress; replay-source retention coordination, durable WAL age, per-cause rejection and worker lease/lock metrics, NATS pending occupancy, and full SQL/pooler/version coverage remain open.

**Session cleanup active-work admission (2026-10-15):** Engine-attached process and tenant `auth.SessionManager` instances now acquire one endpoint-shared active-work permit for the paired expired-session and magic-link cleanup statements. Cache-only sweeps remain in-memory and do not acquire DB admission. An exhausted/canceled permit defers that cleanup cycle, preserving rows for a later cycle. Compatibility-created managers retain the prior behavior unless an Engine owner attaches admission. Added a SQL-mock regression proving no DB cleanup query runs without admission. No schema or wire contract changed. Verified with focused auth/Engine tests, including race: `go test ./auth ./internal/engine -run 'Test(SessionDatabaseCleanupDefersWhenWorkAdmissionIsExhausted|PlatformPoolLimitConfiguration|TunePlatformDBPoolAppliesMaximumOpenConnections|PendingDeletionWorker|PlatformDirectoryStartup)' -count=1`; same command with `-race`; `go test ./internal/engine -run 'TestDirectory(Lag|.*Admission)|TestAssembleProcessStartup' -count=1`; same command with `-race`; `go vet ./auth ./internal/engine`; and `git diff --check` all passed. Full suite/build not rerun due filesystem headroom of 293 MiB. The optional directory-lag poller acquires one endpoint-shared permit for its context-bounded platform query. The durable directory feed acquires per registry operation and uses context-aware SQL variants; callbacks remain outside admission and the NATS relay inherits the limiter. Regression tests prove rejection skips registry work, all per-operation permits are released before callbacks, and an in-flight SQL call returns when its context deadline fires; replay-source retention coordination, durable WAL age, per-cause rejection and worker lease/lock metrics, NATS pending occupancy, and full SQL/pooler/version coverage also remain open.

**Directory-feed admission and cancellable SQL (2026-10-15):** `DirectoryConsumer` now acquires one endpoint-shared active-work permit for each individual registry call and releases it before change/snapshot callbacks, which may activate tenants. `SQLSiteRegistry` implements additive context-aware directory cursor, lease, revision and outbox-read methods; existing contextless methods remain wrappers for source compatibility. The process-owned consumer receives the platform endpoint admission, and the optional NATS relay consumer inherits it. Tests prove admission rejection skips registry and callback work, callbacks execute with no platform permit held, and a delayed SQL statement returns on context cancellation. No public persisted format or schema changed. Verified: `go test -p 1 ./site -run 'TestDirectoryConsumer|TestSQLDirectoryConsumerContextMethodsHonorCancellation' -count=1`; same command with `-race`; `go test -p 1 ./internal/engine -run 'TestDirectory(Lag|.*Admission)|TestAssembleProcessStartup|TestNewTenantDirectoryConsumer' -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./site ./internal/engine`; and `git diff --check` passed. The Engine race-linked package could not be verified: the earlier combined site/Engine race command failed linking with `no space left on device`; the site race suite passed after clearing only Go build/test caches, and Engine normal focused tests passed. G3 remains in progress; replay-retention, durable WAL-age, NATS pending metrics, and broader dialect/pooler verification remain open.

**Analytics drop-cause metrics (2026-10-15):** Kept the existing aggregate dropped-event counter and added separate cumulative counters for oversized payload rejection, WAL persistence failure, subscriber queue overflow, and subscriber registration rejection. Exported each as a low-cardinality Engine OTLP counter and updated the resource-budget documentation. Tests assert that a failed WAL write is classified as persistence failure, listener overflow is counted independently, and rejected registration has its own counter; Engine metric tests assert payload rejection exports the right cause. No schema or wire contract changed. Verified: `go test -p 1 ./analytics -run 'TestChannelBusDroppedCountsWALFailures|TestRealtimeListenerClosesAndIsRemovedOnOverflow|TestMultiBusBoundsListenerCountAndClosesRejectedListener' -count=1`, `go test -p 1 ./internal/engine -run '^TestResourceMetricsRegisterAndObserveProcessResourceGauges$' -count=1`, `go test -race -p 1 ./analytics -run 'TestChannelBusDroppedCountsWALFailures|TestRealtimeListenerClosesAndIsRemovedOnOverflow' -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./analytics ./internal/engine`, and `git diff --check` passed. This closes cause-specific counters only; durable WAL backlog age and coordinated source-retention metrics remain open.

**Durable scanner platform admission (2026-10-15):** The cold-tenant scanner now obtains the platform endpoint's shared active-work permit separately around schema setup, cursor load, each directory page read, cursor advance, and credential descriptor lookup. Tenant database probes continue to use the tenant endpoint admission, so the platform and tenant units do not hold nested permits. Rejection tests prove each scan boundary stops before the corresponding operation; successful scans prove the cursor load/page/advance permits release before the next unit. No schema or wire contract changed. Verified: `go test -p 1 ./internal/engine -run 'TestDurableWorkScanner(AdmitsEachPlatformOperation|AdmissionRejectionStopsPlatformOperation|AdmissionRejectsStartupSchemaCheck|AdmissionRejectsTenantDescriptorLookup|AdvancesBoundedKeysetPage)' -count=1`; `go test -race -vet=off -p 1 ./internal/engine -run 'TestDurableWorkScanner(AdmitsEachPlatformOperation|AdmissionRejectionStopsPlatformOperation|AdmissionRejectsStartupSchemaCheck|AdmissionRejectsTenantDescriptorLookup)' -count=1`; full `go test -p 1 ./internal/engine -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./internal/engine`; and `git diff --check` passed. The first race-link attempt failed for lack of disk space; after clearing only generated Go build/test caches, the targeted race run passed. Remaining admission audit includes other platform/admin paths and scheduled/background workers; G3 remains in progress.

**NATS realtime queue observability (2026-10-15):** The provider now tracks its active realtime subscriptions and reports client pending messages/bytes, application channel items with a conservative payload-byte upper bound, configured capacity, and client-reported drop counts. Engine exports these low-cardinality process metrics when a provider is configured; no tenant or subject labels are used. The drop count is limited to values the NATS client API can report; its documentation warns that it may be invalid after a server-declared slow-consumer connection. An embedded-NATS lifecycle test publishes into an undrained application queue, verifies the occupancy snapshot, then confirms the returned drain joins and removes that subscription. The directory-feed shutdown test waits for an explicit lease-acquired signal instead of racing startup against shutdown; it passed 20 repetitions. No schema or wire contract changed. Verified: `go test -p 1 ./natsprovider -run '^(TestSubscribeDrainClosesChannel|TestSubscribeClosesRealtimeChannelWhenBoundedQueueOverflows)$' -count=5`, `go test -race -p 1 ./natsprovider -run '^TestSubscribeDrainClosesChannel$' -count=3`, `go test -p 1 ./internal/engine -run '^TestResourceMetricsRegisterAndObserveProcessResourceGauges$' -count=1`, `go test -p 1 ./internal/engine -run '^TestDirectoryFeedShutdownJoinsPollingAndWakeupWorkers$' -count=20`, `go test -p 1 ./analytics ./natsprovider ./internal/engine -count=1`, `go test -race -p 1 ./analytics ./natsprovider -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./analytics ./natsprovider ./internal/engine`, and `git diff --check` passed. Engine race suite was not rerun because disk headroom had fallen below 400 MiB. NATS client-side pending age, database lock contention, remaining worker lease-loss metrics, and fleet-wide realtime allocation remain open.

**Console platform-work admission and bounded onboarding (2026-10-15):** Routed public onboarding submission/status/list and authenticated site create/update/delete/password-reset operations through the platform endpoint active-work admission. Fallback site discovery and unmanaged health probes acquire that admission directly; managed site health checks use the pinned tenant generation permit. Onboarding is capped at 32 concurrent jobs per process, retries admission while its owned context remains active, and shutdown cancels and joins those workers before DB closure. Admission exhaustion is surfaced as HTTP 503; accepted work has a durable provisioning row, but restart resumption is still an open durable-operation recovery gate. No schema or wire contract changed. Verified: `gofmt -w api/console.go api/console_work_admission_test.go internal/engine/application_routes_test.go`; `go test -p 1 ./api -run 'Test(RequirePlatformWork|ScheduleOnboardBoundsRunningProvisioningJobs|ConsoleList)' -count=1`; and `go test -p 1 ./internal/engine -run 'Test(RegisterConsoleRoutes|AssembleHTTPProcess|New)' -count=1` passed. **Durable analytics WAL backlog age (2026-10-15):** New local WAL lines carry a WAL-only `_kora_wal_written_at` timestamp; existing event decoders ignore the additive field. Resident buses recover oldest age at startup, update it after durable publish, and rescan the first record of each file after acknowledgment/rotation commit. A file whose oldest record is legacy or unreadable remains durable and sets an age-unknown signal rather than substituting business-event timestamps. The cold-tenant scanner inspects one first record per WAL file while probing unloaded tenant descriptors and publishes the cold backlog snapshot after each completed directory sweep; the Engine metrics merge that with resident buses. Added low-cardinality `kora.analytics.wal.pending`, `kora.analytics.wal.oldest_age_ns`, and `kora.analytics.wal.age_unknown` gauges. Persisted format: additive top-level `_kora_wal_written_at` on local WAL JSON records; legacy lines remain readable/replayable. No public event wire contract or SQL schema changed. **NATS realtime application-queue age (2026-10-15):** `RealtimeBudget` now tracks when each bounded Engine application queue first becomes nonempty and exports the maximum conservative age across active subscriptions. Partial drains may overstate age until an empty queue is observed; the metric resets when empty. Engine adds `kora.realtime.nats.application_oldest_age_upper_bound_ns`. **Reserved heavy-maintenance admission (2026-10-15):** Endpoint-shared active-work admission now separates ordinary work from a reserved maintenance lane. The fleet-wide `KORA_DB_SERVER_MAX_HEAVY_JOBS` allocation is divided by the declared `KORA_DB_ENGINE_REPLICAS`; invalid nonzero shares and configurations that would consume all ordinary capacity are rejected. Analytics rebuild claim transactions acquire ordinary admission; rebuild execution acquires the maintenance lane, preventing concurrent tenant rebuilds from consuming interactive slots. Metrics expose maintenance active count and capacity separately. The reservation is static operator configuration, not a distributed semaphore. No wire or SQL schema changed. Updated `docs/architecture/resource-budgets.md`. These pool stats complement connector-wide physical connection budgets and do not imply complete statistics for unloaded/closed tenant pools. Added a `database/sql` pool fixture proving emitted max-open/open/in-use/idle values, and connected the platform pool in Engine assembly. No contract or schema changed. Updated resource budget docs. **Pool-metric concurrency verification (2026-10-15):** The race-enabled `TestResourceMetricsRegisterAndObserveProcessResourceGauges` now checks aggregation of distinct platform and resident-tenant pool stats (two open/idle connections and the sum of configured maxima). `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -race -p 1 ./internal/engine -run '^TestResourceMetricsRegisterAndObserveProcessResourceGauges$' -count=1`, Engine package vet and `git diff --check` passed. **SQL operation and lock-contention telemetry (2026-10-15):** The shared SQL connector now counts direct context/legacy Exec and Query driver calls, accumulates driver-call duration, and recognizes MySQL lock timeout/deadlock/nowait codes, PostgreSQL deadlock/lock-not-available states, and SQLite busy/locked errors. Metrics are aggregate and expose no SQL, tenant, or endpoint labels. Calls that fall back through a driver's prepared-statement path are not counted; durations end when driver calls return the initial result/rows handle and exclude row iteration. This does not sample live server lock queues. Resource metrics now export operation count, accumulated duration, and recognized contention errors. No schema/wire change. Docs record the bounds. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./internal/dbaccess -count=1`; `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./internal/engine -run '^TestResourceMetricsRegisterAndObserveProcessResourceGauges$' -count=1`; `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -race -p 1 ./internal/dbaccess -run '^TestBudgetConnectorTracksOperationDurationAndLockContention$' -count=1`; `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -race -p 1 ./internal/engine -run '^TestResourceMetricsRegisterAndObserveProcessResourceGauges$' -count=1`; `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go vet -p 1 ./internal/dbaccess ./internal/engine`; and `git diff --check` passed. **Lock-error classification tests (2026-10-15):** Added unit cases for MySQL error numbers 1205/1213, PostgreSQL SQL states 55P03/40P01, SQLite busy/locked text, and a non-lock negative control. **Admission hooks for durable workflow and notification workers (2026-10-15):** Workflow execution now obtains admission once for each bounded claim batch and once per executed step; a rejected claim obtains no DB work, and a rejected step remains recoverable through its existing lease expiry. Notification worker adds a new `NewWorkerWithAdmission` constructor while retaining the original `NewWorker` signature; claim, accept, and retry SQL units acquire permits separately, and network sender calls run without holding DB admission. These are worker-level seams; Engine does not yet compose these workers into tenant runtime ownership, which remains G5/G6 work. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./workflow ./notifications -count=1`; `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -race -p 1 ./workflow -run 'TestExecutionWorker(AdmitsClaimBatchAndEachStep|DoesNotClaimWithoutAdmission)$' -count=1`; `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -race -p 1 ./notifications -run 'TestWorker(AdmitsEachDatabaseUnitButNotSenderCall|DoesNotClaimWithoutAdmission)$' -count=1`; `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go vet -p 1 ./workflow ./notifications`; and `git diff --check` passed. Full admission coverage and Engine wiring remain open G3 gates.

**Async script-hook work admission (2026-10-15):** Process-owned async hook execution now acquires its tenant endpoint active-work permit before running the script and logging its result. On temporary capacity exhaustion the worker retains the dequeued bounded item and retries admission with a 100 ms cancellation-aware backoff, instead of dropping accepted work; shutdown cancellation exits cleanly. The queue continues to account this active item as its separately documented one-item memory allowance. No contract/schema change. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./internal/engine -run '^TestAsyncHookAdmission' -count=1`; same with `-race`; `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go vet -p 1 ./internal/engine`; and `git diff --check` passed. **Realtime history replay work admission (2026-10-15):** Cursor-based realtime replay obtains a short tenant work permit before WebSocket/SSE streaming begins and releases it when replay completes (including setup failures); the long-lived stream remains governed by its separate process stream cap and does not occupy a database work slot. **Cold-tenant durable-work probe admission (2026-10-15):** Scanner cold probes now resolve the tenant endpoint’s shared work admission from the same `site.ConnectionAdmission` path used by resident runtimes. One permit covers connection open/ping, optional scheduler schema/definition work, and bounded durable-work SQL checks; physical connections remain independently limited by the shared connector. WAL filesystem inspection remains outside SQL admission. Helper regressions prove rejection-before-work, active permit during work, and release after the unit; the complete Engine package suite verifies scanner/runtime composition. **File-route work admission (2026-10-15):** Request middleware now exempts only GET/HEAD attachment streams; `DELETE /files/*path` remains under tenant active-work admission. Upload remains on its separate weighted byte reservation. Regression covers GET, HEAD, public file GET, realtime stream, and saturated file deletion. Verified: focused `go test -p 1 ./api -run 'Test(WorkAdmissionMiddleware|HandleSystemRealtimeRejectsWhenReplayWorkAdmissionIsFull)' -count=1`, same with `-race`, `go vet -p 1 ./api`, and `git diff --check` passed using tmpfs build/test paths. No contract or schema change.

**Stream authentication admission (2026-10-15):** The global tenant work limiter now acquires a short permit for authenticated realtime and attachment GET/HEAD handshakes before `SiteGuard` session validation. `HandleSystemRealtime` and `HandleFileServe` release it immediately after auth and before replay/storage streaming; middleware retains an idempotent deferred release for early aborts. Public attachment GET/HEAD stays exempt; upload keeps weighted byte admission; DELETE stays under normal work admission. Regression verifies saturated handshake rejection and that the permit is free inside the stream handler. Verified: focused middleware/realtime tests and race tests, full `go test -p 1 ./api -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./api`, and `git diff --check` passed using tmpfs build/test paths. No schema or wire contract changed.

**Cached outbox backlog observability (2026-10-15):** Resident tenant publishers now refresh a cached backlog sample every 30 seconds under a separate endpoint-shared work-admission permit. Each of the pending, failed, and publishing states contributes at most 256 oldest indexed timestamps; a saturation gauge reports when a sample reaches that cap, and age is the oldest timestamp observed in the sample. OTel collection reads the cache only and performs no database query. `Publisher.Metrics` remains available as the existing exact on-demand API; the periodic Engine metrics path uses the bounded sampler. Added `(status, created_at)` indexes for MySQL, PostgreSQL, and LibSQL creation DDL and the additive version-18 bootstrap upgrade. No wire contract changed. Changed Engine files: `outbox/outbox.go`, outbox metric and dialect integration tests, `db/tables_outbox.go`, `site/bootstrap.go`, `internal/engine/{tenant_services.go,tenant_workers.go,resource_metrics.go,resource_metrics_test.go}`, and `docs/architecture/resource-budgets.md`. All passed. The focused integration services used fresh tmpfs database storage and were removed after the run. This improves bounded resident-tenant visibility; unloaded tenant backlog totals, coordinated durable retention, and broader DB/pooler/version coverage remain open G3 gates.

**Cold-tenant scheduler occurrence recovery (2026-10-15):** Rechecking the unloaded-tenant scanner found it detected due schedule definitions but missed already-materialized pending occurrences and expired running leases. Since the scheduler advances the definition cursor when it materializes an occurrence, that gap could leave durable work undiscovered after eviction or restart. The scanner now probes for pending occurrences and expired running leases. Expanded its integration fixture to use production scheduler, workflow, request, and notification schemas and verify due schedule definitions/occurrences, expired scheduler leases, outbox/webhook receipts, rebuild jobs/leases, workflow signals/timers, request deadlines, and notification deliveries. No schema or wire contract changed. Changed Engine files: `internal/engine/durable_work_scanner.go` and `internal/engine/durable_work_scanner_integration_test.go`.
