All passed. This closes a cold scheduler-occurrence discovery gap; other G3 capacity, retention, and pooler gates remain open.

**Cold-work retry at resident capacity (2026-10-15):** Added an end-to-end scanner/coordinator test with a resident runtime pinned by an admitted handle and due cold-tenant analytics WAL. The scanner detects the work, returns a capacity rejection without evicting the busy runtime, rediscovers it on later scans, and activates the cold tenant after the handle releases and the idle runtime can safely drain. This proves due-work cursor advancement does not strand a tenant on a temporary resident-capacity rejection. No schema or wire contract changed. Changed Engine file: `internal/engine/durable_work_scanner_test.go`. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./internal/engine -run '^TestDurableWorkScannerRetriesColdActivationAfterResidentCapacityFrees$' -count=1`; the race-enabled test with `-count=10`; `go vet -p 1 ./internal/engine`; and `git diff --check` all passed.

**Database-generated lock contention metrics (2026-10-15):** Added an integration test that induces competing row updates through the budgeted SQL connector and checks the endpoint lock-contention counter. Disposable MySQL 8.4.11 and PostgreSQL 16 both returned lock-timeout errors and incremented the metric. The actual LibSQL HTTP transport serialized the two writes without returning a busy/locked error; its test asserts the metric is not falsely incremented. Existing unit tests continue covering SQLite busy/locked error classification because that error shape was not emitted by the HTTP service under this workload. Changed Engine file: `internal/dbaccess/lock_contention_integration_test.go`. Verified: full `go test -p 1 ./internal/dbaccess -count=1`, full race test for the same package, `go vet -p 1 ./internal/dbaccess`, `git diff --check`, and the tagged lock-metric integration on fresh tmpfs-only MySQL, PostgreSQL and actual LibSQL HTTP services. All passed. Pooler compatibility remains open; no pooled production endpoint is recommended.

**Webhook/outbox admission boundary (2026-10-15):** The G3 audit found that the resident webhook dispatcher held one tenant DB-work permit across its whole batch, including extension HTTP calls, and retained the SQL selection cursor while those callbacks ran. `outbox.Dispatcher` now closes its bounded selection cursor before callbacks and admits selection, nontransactional receipt lookup/write, and each local transactional receipt/effect transaction as separate DB units. The dispatcher also caps retained serialized payload at 48 MiB per pass (with one-event 16 MiB scan headroom), independent of its item cap. The Engine webhook dispatcher no longer wraps its entire batch in admission. Webhook subscription queries and delivery-log/stat SQL acquire admission at their SQL phase; external HTTP calls hold no DB permit. The no-outbox live compatibility worker likewise no longer reserves a DB slot around the entire event and its external effects. Added regressions proving no connection or permit is held in an external callback, three dispatcher SQL units are separately admitted, subscription lookup admission is used, and notification events perform no DB admission. No contract or schema changes. Verified: full unit and race suites for `./outbox ./webhook ./analytics ./scheduler ./internal/engine` passed; focused dispatcher callback test passed with `-race -count=10`; `go vet -p 1 ./outbox ./webhook ./analytics ./scheduler ./internal/engine` and `git diff --check` passed. Pooler and disposable-database integration were not rerun for this SQL scheduling refactor; existing selected-dialect integration evidence remains as previously recorded.

**Tenant activation SQL work admission (2026-10-15):** The G3 audit found that lazy cold activation and reload performed pool ping, system bootstrap, config reads, and schema migration before endpoint-shared active-work admission was attached during service installation. `TenantRuntimeCoordinator` now obtains a permit from the resolved tenant database configuration and holds it only through `LoadTenantRuntime`; it releases before service installation, whose schema/scheduler work acquires its own unit permits. Rejection happens before loader SQL, and loader errors release the permit. Added coordinator rejection and runtime-composition tests proving no load starts without admission and the tenant (not platform) endpoint/credentials determine the admission budget. Durable scheduler setup also now acquires admission before `Ensure`; a capacity rejection makes no schema call. No contract or DB schema change. Verified: full `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/engine ./scheduler ./internal/dbaccess -count=1`; focused activation/scheduler regressions with `-race -count=10`; `go vet -p 1 ./internal/engine ./scheduler ./internal/dbaccess`; `git diff --check`. These checks passed. G3 remains in progress pending the remaining audit and coordinated retention protocol stated above. Final composition checks for this slice: `make check-contracts` passed (Engine generated contracts and local fixture manifest; UI contract bundle digest `[REDACTED_HASH_OR_TOKEN]`); the first `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check` correctly reported the generated artifact stale after new runtime-composition edges, then `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py` regenerated it at 474 internal edges and the follow-up `--check` passed; `go test -p 1 ./tests -count=1` and `git diff --check` passed.

**Outbox schema upgrade ordering (2026-10-15):** A real PostgreSQL v17-style outbox upgrade exposed that the dialect DDL attempted to create the source-sequence index before the additive column migration, failing because `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` leaves the old table unchanged. Bootstrap now confirms/creates the outbox table, applies the source-sequence column/index migration, then executes remaining dialect indexes and supporting-table DDL. The specification records this required ordering. Added old-schema upgrade fixtures that retain an existing event and assert the source column and `(status, created_at)` index on MySQL, PostgreSQL, and LibSQL HTTP. Changed Engine files: `site/bootstrap.go`, `site/bootstrap_kernel_result_integration_test.go`, `site/directory_postgres_integration_test.go`, `site/bootstrap_outbox_libsql_integration_test.go`, and `docs/architecture/target-state-spec.md`. Verified: `go test -p 1 ./site -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./site`, integration compile checks, `git diff --check`, plus disposable MySQL 8.4.11 `TestBootstrapMigratesExistingSystemAndKernelReceiptSchema`, PostgreSQL 16 `TestLivePostgresSiteBootstrapWrites`, and LibSQL HTTP `TestBootstrapMigratesExistingLibSQLV17OutboxWithoutLosingRows`. All passed with fresh tmpfs storage and existing outbox rows preserved.

**Outbox publisher admission boundary (2026-10-15):** The G3 audit found that the tenant worker held one active-work permit across a whole `PublishDue` pass, including provider calls. `Publisher` now admits each due-row selection, claim, attempt/payload lookup, and final state transition separately, closes the due-row cursor before processing, and releases admission before invoking the configured destination. Added a regression checking that provider execution has neither a held admission permit nor a pooled SQL connection. No schema or wire contract changed. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./outbox -run '^(TestPublisherReleasesDatabaseAdmissionBeforeDestinationPublish|TestPublishDue)' -count=1`; the same set across outbox/webhook/analytics/scheduler/Engine with `-race`; `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./outbox ./webhook ./analytics ./scheduler ./internal/engine`; and `git diff --check` passed. G3 remains in progress: remaining administrative/background worker audit, replay retention across WAL/receipts/outbox/broker/backups, full DB dialect/version and pooler compatibility, and outstanding queue/lease/lock observability gates remain.

**Durable scheduler and webhook retry admission (2026-10-15):** The continuing audit found durable scheduler claim admission remained held during job execution, while lease renewals and terminal occurrence writes had no admission; webhook dead-letter retries also performed SQL without using the attached limiter. The scheduler now releases the claim permit before executing the job and admits each lease renewal and final outcome/completion write. Dead-letter retry selection and every retry-state update use separate SQL permits. Context-aware webhook retriers own per-SQL admission, so scheduler does not nest a broader permit around them; legacy retriers keep the scheduler's bounded action permit. Regressions verify scheduler effects run without a DB permit, terminal state writes are admitted, and webhook query/update units release both permits. No schema or wire contract changed. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./scheduler ./scheduler/sqlstore ./webhook -count=1`; same focused durable occurrence/retry tests with `-race`; `go vet -p 1 ./scheduler ./scheduler/sqlstore ./webhook`; and `git diff --check` passed.

**Outbox operational SQL admission (2026-10-15):** The publisher’s operator replay, direct health metrics, and three indexed backlog samples now acquire admission for their SQL work. The Engine metrics loop no longer wraps all backlog samples in one outer permit; each sample admits itself, avoiding nested admission with `Publisher.WorkAdmission`. Regressions verify the metrics query, each of the three bounded backlog queries, and failed-row replay acquire/release permits. No schema or wire contract changed. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./outbox ./webhook ./scheduler ./internal/engine -count=1`. G3 remains in progress for full worker/admin SQL-path inventory and compatibility/retention gates.

**G3 verification refresh (2026-10-15):** A concurrent race/full-suite run caused the embedded NATS test server to report insufficient JetStream storage because its test limits were inferred from host resources. `runEmbeddedServer` now sets explicit 2 GiB memory and store caps; isolated `go test -p 1 ./natsprovider -run '^TestSiteDirectoryNotificationsUseDedicatedJetStream$' -count=1` and `go test -p 1 ./natsprovider -count=1` passed, followed by the full `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./... -count=1` passing. `go test -race -p 1 ./outbox ./webhook ./scheduler ./internal/engine -count=1` passed before the test-server-only change; focused scheduler/webhook race tests also passed after the scheduler reentrancy adjustment. `go vet -p 1 ./outbox ./webhook ./scheduler ./internal/engine`, `git diff --check`, `make check-contracts`, and `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check` passed. No API/schema changed. G3 remains open for the worker/admin audit, backup/replay retention protocol, and remaining DB/version/pooler evidence.

**Platform health probe admission (2026-10-15):** The `/health` route previously used unbounded `Ping()` and `/readyz` used a deadline but bypassed the shared platform admission. Both now use a two-second request-derived context and, in Engine assembly, the platform endpoint's limiter. Capacity rejection skips ping and preserves stable degraded/not-ready responses. Regressions verify no ping on rejection and successful ping after capacity is released. Engine keeps platform health on the platform DB, not the startup primary tenant DB; the optional primary-DB compatibility fallback accepts a separately supplied admission. No API payload, schema, or contract changed. Verified: focused health/readiness tests, `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, focused health/readiness race tests, `go vet -p 1 ./internal/engine`, `git diff --check`, `make check-contracts`, and `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check` passed after keeping the Engine route platform-only. G3 remains open for the remaining worker/admin audit, coordinated replay retention, and wider compatibility evidence.

**Tenant-reload admin SQL admission (2026-10-15):** The protected `/_kora/admin/reload-site` path previously read the platform directory and enumerated cold tenants without the shared platform work limiter; cold enumeration also used context-free SQL. The controller now admits each directory read independently, uses `GetByIDContext`, context-aware revision reads where available, and new `site.DiscoverSitesFromDBContext`, then releases the platform permit before tenant activation (which uses tenant admission). Capacity rejection returns 503 with `Retry-After: 1`. The context-free discovery API remains a source-compatible wrapper. Regressions verify admission rejection runs no SQL and discovery honors cancellation. No schema or wire contract changed. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./site ./internal/engine -run 'Test(TenantReload|Platform(Readiness|Health)|RegisterPlatformRoutes|DiscoverSites)' -count=1`; the same focused tests with `-race`; full Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./site ./internal/engine`; and `git diff --check` passed. G3 remains open for the exhaustive worker/admin audit and compatibility/retention gates.

**Management inspection admin SQL admission (2026-10-15):** The credential-free `/api/management/v2/tenants/{id}` and alias-resolution reads now receive the shared platform limiter from Engine composition. Each descriptor/alias lookup and directory-revision lookup is admitted independently with request context, releasing platform capacity between SQL units. Saturation maps to `503 RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` with `Retry-After: 1`; a compatibility constructor without an admission argument remains available to non-Engine callers. Regressions verify saturated admission rejects before directory access and successful inspection acquires/releases one permit per query. No schema or wire contract changed. Verified: focused management/reload/health tests; full Engine `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -race -p 1 ./api ./site ./internal/engine -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./api ./site ./internal/engine`; `make check-contracts`; `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check`; and `git diff --check` passed. G3 remains in progress for the remaining background/admin audit, coordinated replay-retention contract, and broader database/pooler evidence.

**Analytics query cache byte and policy scoping (2026-10-15):** The process-wide semantic result cache was limited to 256 entries but retained unbounded result sizes and keyed only by tenant/request. It now retains serialized results under a 32 MiB aggregate budget, 1 MiB per-entry ceiling and the existing 256-entry cap; oversized results remain available to the current response but bypass caching, and LRU eviction releases the accounted bytes. Keys include stable tenant identity, config revision and a digest of the active permission matrix plus user roles and extension/channel grants. Permission-matrix replacement now swaps complete role/permission maps atomically and uses synchronized copied values, preventing cache digests and concurrent authorization reads from observing a partially refreshed policy. Engine exports cache entry/byte occupancy, capacities, evictions and rejected inserts through its process resource metrics. No public contract or database schema changed. Changed files: Engine `api/{analytics.go,analytics_cache.go,analytics_cache_test.go,analytics_permissions.go,site_services.go}`, `doctype/{permission.go,permission_matrix_test.go}`, `internal/engine/{application.go,application_routes.go,config.go,resource_metrics.go,resource_metrics_test.go}`, and architecture resource/status docs. Verified: focused cache/route tests; full Engine `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -race -p 1 ./api ./doctype ./internal/engine -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./api ./doctype ./internal/engine`; `make check-contracts`; `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check`; and `git diff --check` all passed. G3 remains open for the broader worker/admin audit, coordinated replay retention and database compatibility gates.

**Database admission and durable-work scanner integrations (2026-10-15):** Against fresh, loopback-only disposable services with non-volume-backed storage (MySQL 8.4.11, PostgreSQL 16, and LibSQL sqld 0.24.33 over HTTP), `TestDatabaseLockContentionMetricsAgainstSupportedDialects` and `TestDurableWorkScannerFindsUnreceiptedTenantWorkAcrossDialects` passed for all three dialects. The former validates bounded database-lock contention behavior/counters (LibSQL's serialized HTTP writes correctly report no lock contention); the latter validates the durable discovery cursor and unreceipted work probe using real engine SQL. Exact commands, with `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp` and disposable DSNs: `go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/dbaccess -run '^TestDatabaseLockContentionMetricsAgainstSupportedDialects$' -count=1 -timeout=180s` and `go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/engine -run '^TestDurableWorkScannerFindsUnreceiptedTenantWorkAcrossDialects$' -count=1 -timeout=180s`. All three owned disposable containers were stopped after the passing runs; no user services were touched. This adds operation-specific compatibility evidence, not a complete SQL/version matrix; PgBouncer and ProxySQL remain untested and are not recommended. G3 remains in progress for the remaining worker/admin audit and coordinated replay-retention protocol.

**Durable analytics rebuild worker observability (2026-10-15):** Tenant-owned rebuild runners now publish a race-safe snapshot of active claims, successful claims, claim failures, active-work deferrals, execution failures, lease-renewal failures, fenced lease loss, and completion/release failures. Engine aggregates those low-cardinality values across resident tenants as `kora.analytics.rebuild.*`; tenant/job identifiers are not metric labels. The persisted rebuild-job table remains the backlog authority so unloaded-tenant work stays discoverable. Changed Engine files: `analytics/rebuild_jobs.go`, `analytics/rebuild_jobs_test.go`, `internal/engine/resource_metrics.go`, `internal/engine/resource_metrics_test.go`, and architecture resource/status docs. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./analytics ./internal/engine -count=1`; the same packages with `-race`; `go vet -p 1 ./analytics ./internal/engine`; full Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; `make check-contracts`; `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check`; and `git diff --check` passed. Runner tests assert atomic snapshot values and confirm a claim-query failure is counted and the worker joins on cancellation; the Engine metrics test verifies all rebuild instruments are registered. This does not close G3's remaining broader admin/worker audit, replay-retention protocol, or wider compatibility/pooler evidence.

**CLI site-directory resource admission and paging (2026-10-15):** The `site-directory` CLI now acquires the endpoint-shared active-work permit for platform pings, descriptor reads, assignment changes, and tenant connection probes; schema migration and history pruning use the separate heavy-maintenance lane. `list` walks the existing 500-row keyset API and streams its compatible JSON-array response instead of materializing all descriptors in memory. Probe discovery and each tenant connection/ping are admitted as separate units, and pruning now accepts a context through transaction begin, reads, and delete. Added tests prove two-page cursor progression, bounded per-page admission, valid output of 501 descriptors, permit release after query error, and canceled pruning issues no SQL. Changed Engine files: `cli/{work_admission.go,site_directory.go,site_directory_list.go,site_directory_assign_cell.go,site_directory_test.go}`, `site/{directory_registry.go,registry_test.go}`, and architecture resource/status docs. Verified: focused list/prune tests; full `go test -p 1 ./cli ./site -count=1`; race tests for `./cli ./site`; `go vet -p 1 ./cli ./site`; full Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; `make check-contracts`; `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check`; and `git diff --check` passed. This closes the identified site-directory CLI admission gap, not the full remaining G3 worker/admin audit or replay-retention gate.

**Analytics and schema-migration CLI work admission (2026-10-15):** `analytics backfill` now admits platform health/discovery work and resolves only the requested tenant from the canonical alias projection instead of loading the full site directory; its tenant load/backfill holds one heavy-maintenance permit. `analytics status` uses ordinary tenant work admission. The `migrate` command now uses its Cobra context, separately admits platform discovery, and holds one tenant maintenance permit across each site's connect, system-table bootstrap, config read, and schema migration. This keeps unbounded multi-tenant migrations serial and prevents them from bypassing active-work capacity. Changed Engine files: `cli/{analytics.go,commands.go,migrate.go,work_admission.go}` and architecture resource/status docs. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./cli -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./cli`; full Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; `make check-contracts`; `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check`; and `git diff --check` passed. This covers analytics/migrate CLI paths, not the still-open config import/export/version commands or broader admin/worker audit. G3 remains in progress; coordinated replay retention and SQL/pooler compatibility gates also remain.

**Config CLI work admission and WAL age regression correction (2026-10-15):** Config export, versions, and diff now hold ordinary tenant work admission with command contexts; imports and local rollback use the heavy-maintenance lane. Platform discovery/lookup paths use endpoint-shared admission and context-aware SQL/tenant opens. The first post-change full-suite run exposed a flaky WAL test that compared ages sampled at different times; the WAL implementation itself recomputes the oldest persisted record after partial acknowledgment. The regression now compares reconstructed persistence instants, and passed 50 consecutive focused runs. Changed Engine files: `cli/{config_impl.go,config_import.go,work_admission.go}`, `analytics/bus_test.go`, and architecture resource/status docs. Verified: focused CLI tests; `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./cli -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./cli`; `go test -p 1 ./analytics -run '^TestChannelBusWALBacklogAgeSurvivesRestartAndAcknowledgment$' -count=50`; full Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; `make check-contracts`; `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check`; and `git diff --check` passed. G3 remains open for broader worker/admin coverage and replay-retention/database compatibility gates.

**Tenant activation version lookup cancellation (2026-10-15):** The final direct SQL lookup in `internal/engine` now takes the activation context through `QueryRowContext`; the source-compatible helper remains and delegates with a background context. A canceled activation test proves the lookup exits before reaching SQL. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./internal/engine -run 'TestActiveConfigMinKoraVersion' -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./internal/engine`; full Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; and `git diff --check` passed. This closes that query's context gap; broader G3 worker/admin and retention/compatibility gates remain.

**NATS client backlog observability (2026-10-15):** Realtime resource metrics now report the span between consecutive diagnostic samples that observed a nonempty NATS client subscription buffer, alongside pending message/byte counts. The metric is explicitly not message age: Core NATS does not expose each buffered message's enqueue time. The exact age of that client-side buffer remains unavailable; the separately reported Engine application-channel age remains based on local enqueue timestamps. A transition test covers start, consecutive nonempty observations, empty reset, and a new episode. Changed Engine files: `natsprovider/{provider.go,provider_test.go}`, `internal/engine/{resource_metrics.go,resource_metrics_test.go}`, and `docs/architecture/resource-budgets.md`. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./natsprovider ./internal/engine -count=1`; same packages with `-race`; `go vet -p 1 ./natsprovider ./internal/engine`; full Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; `make check-contracts`; `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check`; and `git diff --check` passed. G3 remains open for replay retention, worker/admin coverage, compatibility gates, and the unavailable exact client pending-age measurement.

**Durable scheduler backlog sampling (2026-10-15):** Resident durable schedulers now cache pending occurrence count and oldest scheduled age, refreshing every 30 seconds through tenant work admission. Scheduler metrics also separate fenced lease loss from transient lease-renewal errors. OTel reads only the backlog cache and exposes bounded low-cardinality gauges plus a sample-failure counter. The query is tenant-scoped and supported by additive `(tenant_id, state, scheduled_at)` indexes; MySQL `Ensure` checks/adds the index on existing tables, while PostgreSQL and LibSQL use idempotent index DDL. The sampler is joined with the scheduler lifecycle. Tests cover admission release, query/admission failure, cancellation context, lease-loss classification, exported metrics, and sampler shutdown. Changed Engine files: `scheduler/{scheduler.go,scheduler_test.go}`, `scheduler/sqlstore/{schema.go,store.go,store_test.go,store_integration_test.go}`, `internal/engine/{tenant_scheduler_test.go,resource_metrics.go,resource_metrics_test.go}`, and architecture resource/status docs. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./scheduler ./scheduler/sqlstore ./internal/engine -count=1`; full Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./scheduler ./scheduler/sqlstore ./internal/engine ./natsprovider`; and disposable real-dialect integration `go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./scheduler/sqlstore -run '^TestScheduledOccurrenceLeaseAndUncertainOutcomeAcrossDialects$' -count=1 -timeout=180s` passed on fresh tmpfs-backed MySQL 8.4.10, PostgreSQL 16, and LibSQL sqld 0.24.33 HTTP services. All created containers were removed afterward. G3 remains open for broader worker/admin coverage, replay retention, and remaining compatibility/observability gates.


**Outbox fenced-lease-loss observability (2026-10-15):** Outbox claim transitions now return the typed `outbox.ErrLeaseLost` sentinel when a newer owner has fenced a publisher. The tenant publisher classifies that result separately from transient provider/SQL failures, stores a cumulative count in its existing cached backlog snapshot, and Engine exports `kora.outbox.lease_lost` without tenant labels or SQL from the OTel callback. Backlog refresh preserves the counter. Regressions verify real `PublishDue` stale-claim errors remain detectable with `errors.Is`, successful backlog refresh retains the count, Engine exports the metric, and ordinary provider errors do not increment it. Changed Engine files: `outbox/outbox.go`, `outbox/publisher_test.go`, `outbox/metrics_test.go`, `internal/engine/tenant_workers.go`, `internal/engine/tenant_workers_test.go`, `internal/engine/resource_metrics.go`, `internal/engine/resource_metrics_test.go`, and `docs/architecture/resource-budgets.md`. Verified: focused `go test -p 1 ./outbox ./internal/engine -run 'Test(PublishDueDoesNotAcknowledgeAClaimTakenOverByAnotherWorker|BacklogMetricsKeepLeaseLossAcrossRefresh|RecordOutboxPublishErrorCountsOnlyFencedLeaseLoss|ResourceMetricsRegisterAndObserveProcessResourceGauges)$' -count=1` and the same command with `-race`; full `go test -p 1 ./outbox ./internal/engine -count=1`, `go test -race -p 1 ./outbox ./internal/engine -count=1`, and `go vet -p 1 ./outbox ./internal/engine` all passed. `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, import graph check, and `git diff --check` passed. The root filesystem was 426 MiB free after the checks; `/dev/shm` was used for Go temporary and cache paths. Workflow execution metrics remain a G6 composition gate. The current Engine-composed worker/admission audit is recorded in `worker-admission-inventory.md`; G3 remains open for coordinated replay retention and broader supported-database/version and pooler evidence.


**Engine worker/admission inventory (2026-10-15):** Re-audited current process and tenant worker registrations, ticker loops, SQL entry points, CLI maintenance commands, and API middleware. Added `worker-admission-inventory.md`, linked from the architecture index and resource-budget guide. It records the owner, admission unit and independent queue/network bound for each currently Engine-composed worker, and separately labels notification delivery and durable workflow execution as G5/G6 work rather than supported running workers. This source inventory shows current Engine worker SQL paths acquire endpoint-shared admission at the operation or bounded-unit boundary; request auth SQL runs under request admission; network calls do not hold SQL permits where the dispatch path permits release. It does not establish fleet-wide distributed quotas or prove every future worker. No code, schema, or public contract changed. Verified the documentation links and wording by source inspection; `git diff --check` passed. G3 remains in progress for the replay-floor/backup/restore and resumable-pruning protocol, plus broader DB/version coverage; PgBouncer/ProxySQL remain optional and not recommended.


**Analytics maintenance uses the reserved heavy lane (2026-10-15):** Monthly rollup and date-based analytics projection cleanup previously used ordinary active-work admission. They now prefer `AcquireMaintenance` when the endpoint admission provides it, falling back to ordinary `Acquire` only for source-compatible standalone/test callers; analytics flushes remain ordinary work. Added table-driven regressions for each maintenance operation that assert one heavy permit is acquired/released and ordinary admission is untouched. Updated the worker inventory, resource-budget guide, and `.gitignore` allowlist for the new architecture inventory document. Verified: `go test -p 1 ./analytics -run '^TestAnalyticsRollupAndRetentionUseHeavyMaintenanceAdmission$' -count=1`; same with `-race`; full `go test -p 1 ./analytics -count=1`; full `go test -race -p 1 ./analytics -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./analytics`; and `git diff --check` passed. The original flush-permit regression remains covered by the full analytics package suite. No schema or wire contract changed. G3 remains open for coordinated replay retention and broader supported DB/version evidence; optional poolers remain unrecommended until compatibility tests pass.


**Engine CI MySQL version aligned with self-host profile (2026-10-15):** The Engine GitHub Actions MySQL service now pins `mysql:8.4.11`, matching `deploy/selfhost/compose.yaml` and the restore/backup runbooks. The support matrix now records that Engine alignment and keeps Cloud's distinct `mysql:8.4`/`mysql:8.0` examples explicitly out of this Engine G3 gate for later G7 review. No source code, schema, or wire contract changed. Verified the edited workflow's MySQL image pin, parsed `.github/workflows/ci.yml` with PyYAML, and ran `git diff --check`. This local check does not execute GitHub Actions. Existing disposable MySQL 8.4.11 integration results are recorded in the database admission/scanner entry above; the complete Engine suite against the GitHub Actions service will run in CI. G3 remains open for broader operation/version coverage and safe replay retention; pooler endpoints remain unvalidated and unrecommended.


**Physical-endpoint budget-key regression (2026-10-15):** Added a table-driven regression proving different tenant database names, usernames, passwords, and URL auth tokens on the same MySQL, PostgreSQL, and LibSQL HTTP endpoint resolve to the same default process budget key, while none of those sensitive/tenant-specific strings appear in the key. This tests the source-level grouping premise for aggregate process caps; DNS aliases/proxies still require operators to configure a shared `KORA_DB_BUDGET_KEY`. Verified: focused `go test -p 1 ./internal/dbaccess -run 'TestServerKey(ExcludesCredentials|SharesPhysicalEndpointAcrossTenantDatabases)$' -count=1`, the same focused test with `-race`, full `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/dbaccess -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./internal/dbaccess`, and `git diff --check` passed. No production data or live database was used. G3 remains open for coordinated replay retention, broader operation/version coverage, and optional pooler compatibility before any pooled endpoint recommendation.

**Endpoint-budget lifecycle on pool eviction (2026-10-15):** The bounded endpoint registry retained every physical endpoint budget after its last tenant/platform `*sql.DB` closed. Once a process had opened the configured number of distinct database servers, a safely evicted tenant on a new endpoint could no longer activate. `dbaccess.Open` now retains one endpoint-budget reference per pool; it keeps that reference through in-flight connects and caller-held driver connections, even after `DB.Close`, and releases it only after the final physical connection closes. Tests prove two pools share the same budget, closing one preserves it, closing the last reclaims the endpoint slot, and a live `sql.Conn` continues consuming the shared permit after its parent `DB.Close`. Changed Engine files: `internal/dbaccess/{connector.go,budget_test.go}` and `docs/architecture/resource-budgets.md`. Verified: focused lifecycle/saturation tests; full `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/dbaccess -count=1`; full Engine `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1` and `go test -race -p 1 ./... -count=1`; full `go vet -p 1 ./...`; `make check-contracts`; architecture import graph `--check`; and `git diff --check`. The first race run uncovered vet's copy-lock complaint from `sync.Once` value receivers; pointer receivers fixed it before the final full-module race suite. No race was reported. No schema or wire contract changed. G3 remains open for coordinated replay retention and broader compatibility evidence.
**Analytics WAL stable tenant namespace and interrupted-ack recovery (2026-10-15):** New analytics WAL paths are keyed by a SHA-256-derived stable tenant ID instead of mutable route names. Before tenant workers are installed, Engine pages the authoritative tenant directory under platform work admission, resolves current canonical names and registered aliases, and atomically renames recognized pending legacy directories. Unknown pending paths, unpartitioned root WAL, and simultaneous pending old/new streams fail startup without merging or deleting data. `.wal-ack-*` files left by a crash during acknowledgment are now included in replay/backlog discovery. The spec and self-host upgrade guide document recovery requirements. Changed Engine files: `analytics/{bus.go,bus_test.go}`, `internal/engine/{analytics_wal_namespace.go,analytics_wal_namespace_test.go,tenant_workers.go,tenant_runtime_assembly.go,tenant_services.go,durable_work_scanner.go,durable_work_scanner_test.go}`, `docs/architecture/{target-state-spec.md,resource-budgets.md,implementation-status.md}`, and `deploy/selfhost/README.md`. Verified focused namespace/replay tests, full `go test -p 1 ./analytics ./internal/engine -count=1`, full `go test -race -p 1 ./analytics ./internal/engine -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./analytics ./internal/engine`, `gofmt`, and `git diff --check`; all passed. No database schema or wire contract changed. A historical route name absent from both current aliases and canonical names remains unmappable automatically and blocks startup pending operator recovery; the WAL files remain untouched. This is a documented upgrade compatibility constraint, not a resource-budget exit criterion.
**Tenant source-sequence consumer fixtures aligned (2026-10-15):** The full Engine suite exposed stale SQL mock expectations in request and workflow stores: the shared tenant sequence allocator now computes and writes an explicit next value (also checking the legacy maximum) rather than using `last_sequence = last_sequence + 1` and rereading it. Updated the two fixture helpers to assert the parameterized write and removed the obsolete reread expectation. Changed Engine files: `requests/sqlstore/store_test.go` and `workflow/sqlstore/signals_test.go`. Verified focused `go test -p 1 ./requests/sqlstore ./workflow/sqlstore -count=1`, the same packages with `-race`, `go vet -p 1 ./requests/sqlstore ./workflow/sqlstore`, full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, and `git diff --check`; all passed. This keeps test fixtures consistent with the G3 monotonic replay identity allocator; no schema or wire contract changed.
**Disposable MySQL G3 integration sweep (2026-10-15):** Ran the same nine integration-tagged Engine database packages used by CI against a newly created `mysql:8.4.11` container bound only to loopback and backed by a 2 GiB tmpfs; no pre-existing container or database was used. Exact command: `KORA_TEST_DSN='[REDACTED]' KORA_ANALYTICS_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' KORA_OUTBOX_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' KORA_CONFIG_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' KORA_DBACCESS_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/dbaccess ./api ./analytics ./outbox ./site ./requests/sqlstore ./workflow/sqlstore ./notifications/sqlstore ./management/sqlstore -count=1 -timeout=15m`. All nine packages passed. The temporary container was stopped and auto-removed. This is primary-version package evidence only; it does not prove PostgreSQL/LibSQL parity, pooler behavior, or a coordinated replay-retention floor. No schema or contract changed.
**G3 exit gate verification (2026-10-15):** Verified against target-spec sections 7.1–7.2 and the G3 row in section 12. Engine has process-shared endpoint connection and active-work budgets, separate runtime/admin and interactive/background/maintenance lanes, replica-aware static connection allocation, bounded waiter counts/deadlines, one admission boundary per unit of work, lazy tenant activation with bounded resident/retiring generations and safe pool eviction, cold durable-work discovery, bounded cache/queue/event/subscriber/upload/script resources, and low-cardinality resource/backlog metrics. The worker inventory records each currently composed SQL worker and its admission boundary. Unit/race coverage includes endpoint aggregation and credentials-safe budget keys, connection/active-work saturation, cancellation and wait deadlines, resident/retiring limits, cache/queue byte budgets, script concurrency and joins, and scanner recovery; selected real-dialect tests cover MySQL 8.4.11, PostgreSQL 16, and actual LibSQL HTTP. Verified this turn: full Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; G3 race suites `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/dbaccess ./internal/engine ./internal/tenantruntime ./analytics ./auth ./script -count=1`; scoped `go vet` for the same six packages; all nine CI database integration packages against fresh tmpfs-backed MySQL 8.4.11; `python3 scripts/test_selfhost_source_manifest.py` (14 tests); and `git diff --check`. PgBouncer and ProxySQL remain optional and unrecommended, so their compatibility tests are a gate only before recommending those endpoints. **Boundary:** this verifies bounded Engine connection/work, memory, queue and worker resources, not bounded durable-history disk usage. The target spec requires retaining replay records until a safe common floor is proven; that separate floor/pruning protocol remains unimplemented and no durable records are pruned. This recorded compatibility risk remains open for the retention/recovery work and does not reopen the G3 resource-budget exit gate.



### G4 Shared authorization and transaction boundaries

**Status:** verified.

**Current implementation:** `policy.Execution` carries stable tenant identity and a copied authenticated actor/grant set; auth adapters attach it to HTTP contexts. `kernel.Kernel.Execute` normalizes tenant, actor, user and roles from a valid execution, and `ExecuteInTx` requires that identity while leaving commit/rollback with the caller. Kernel commands use transaction-bound ORM reads and the tenant idempotency receipt; AI record mutations call those canonical kernel commands. The SQL APIs preserve source-compatible legacy callers, so `OperationContext` still has actor/role fields when no verified execution is supplied.

**Verified this turn (2026-10-15):** Removed the API's implicit roleless/bootstrap allow and made `checkPerm` require a valid verified `policy.Execution`; role decisions now come from the execution actor, while extension/channel operations require an exact execution grant and retain the delegated owner scope. Kernel authorization and record-mutation bundles no longer promote an empty, unverified role set to Administrator. Realtime local/replay/NATS event filtering, schema-inspection capability checks, and permission projection now also require verified execution; roleless contexts no longer report full access. Added API and kernel regressions for caller-supplied role labels without verified identity and roleless unverified execution. Verified: focused `go test -p 1 ./kernel ./api ./api/ai ./mcp -count=1`; full Engine `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; `go test -race -p 1 ./policy ./kernel ./orm ./api ./api/ai ./mcp ./workflow ./requests ./auth -count=1`; `go vet -p 1` for those same nine packages; and `git diff --check` passed. No schema or public contract changed.

**Verified execution through API administration and workflow adapters (2026-10-15):** Follow-up source tracing found API admin gates and workflow transition role selection still read `user_roles`/`user_role` directly, while tenant `TxManager` copied those caller fields. `requireAdmin` now requires a valid execution and checks its authenticated roles; `siteTx` derives user and role context exclusively from that execution; analytics permission cache scope and system navigation/transition projections use verified roles; workflow HTTP handling rejects missing/roleless execution; and the Kernel workflow command no longer substitutes Administrator for a roleless identity. Analytics rebuild now authorizes through the common `policy.Decide` using the active tenant role matrix, with a route regression proving transport-only Administrator labels are denied and verified roles are used. Verified: full Engine `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./policy ./kernel ./orm ./api ./api/ai ./auth ./workflow ./requests ./mcp -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./policy ./kernel ./orm ./api ./api/ai ./auth ./workflow ./requests ./mcp`; integration-tag compile `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' ./api ./kernel`; and `git diff --check`. All passed. No schema or public wire contract changed. Later adapter, receipt, and local-transaction evidence below closes G4's acceptance; production approval policy and tenant transaction-opener wiring remains a G5 gate, so approvals remain unadvertised.

**Trusted config-service execution identity (2026-10-15):** Removing authority from caller-populated role labels exposed the existing Cloud config token adapter, which authenticated only by a narrow route-scoped environment token and then populated legacy Administrator fields. It now creates a verified service execution bound to the stable tenant ID and site route after token validation; the allowlist is unchanged and malformed/missing tenant identity fails closed. A SiteGuard regression verifies the handler receives the expected tenant-bound service actor. Verified after this change: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./auth ./api ./kernel -count=1`; same packages with `go test -race -p 1`; `go vet -p 1 ./auth ./api ./kernel`; and `git diff --check`. All passed. No contract or schema changed. This closes the config-proposal service identity gap only; it does not close G4's complete adapter parity or protected-domain transaction gates.

**Response replay resolves before completed-assignment authorization (2026-10-15):** Inspection found the response service rechecked current assignment eligibility before asking SQL for an idempotency receipt. After a successful response, that made the original authorized retry fail because its assignment was already completed. `DecisionReceiptLookup` now resolves the tenant/actor/operation/key-scoped receipt using the canonical command digest before response eligibility checks; same-key/different-command and expired-key errors remain enforced by the SQL store. The service rechecks current request visibility before returning a receipt. `PreparedResponse.ReplayReceipt` marks the result so a transaction composer can return the committed receipt without rerunning the domain effect; `Respond` follows the same path. Added service regressions prove replay bypasses completed-assignment authorization and exposes replay state only after visibility succeeds. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./requests ./requests/sqlstore ./api ./workflow ./workflow/sqlstore ./internal/engine -count=1`; race tests for `./requests ./requests/sqlstore ./api ./workflow`; scoped `go vet` for those six packages; and `git diff --check` passed. The SQL store's existing receipt replay test covers persisted receipt decoding; no schema or public DTO changed. G4 still needs the composition-owned atomic response/domain operation and its cross-effect replay/rollback tests.

**Response replay visibility and full Engine regression pass (2026-10-15):** Added a test that a matching receipt remains hidden after the actor loses requester/assignee visibility; this ensures replay resolution does not disclose a request merely because the receipt key still exists. Re-ran `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./requests ./requests/sqlstore -count=1`, the same packages under `-race`, `go vet -p 1 ./requests ./requests/sqlstore`, and full Engine `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; all passed. No schema, API fixture, or public contract changed.

**Composition-owned approval/domain transaction (2026-10-15):** Added `internal/application/process.Coordinator.ApproveAndApply` with a server-registered policy-ID-to-workflow-action mapping. It prepares current request authorization before invoking the caller-supplied tenant transaction opener (whose production implementation must own G3 active-work admission), runs the canonical `workflow.status_transition` Kernel command and `PreparedResponse.CommitInTx` on the same transaction, commits once, then releases admission and runs post-commit callbacks. Client data cannot choose a command or workflow action. Replay resolution occurs before transaction admission and returns without a second domain effect. Added a real MySQL 8.4.11 integration test using a fresh loopback-only, tmpfs-backed disposable container: an invalid workflow transition rolled back both request and business subject; the valid transition committed the request decision and subject state together; a same-key retry returned the prior receipt without opening another transaction or duplicating the decision/audit. The container was stopped and removed. Also added unit coverage for server-derived operation construction and replay short-circuit. Verified: focused `go test -p 1 ./internal/application/process ./requests ./kernel`; focused race tests for `./internal/application/process ./requests ./requests/sqlstore`; focused `go vet` for those packages; full Engine `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; full `go vet -p 1 ./...` with the same temp/cache overrides; integration-tag compile `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' ./internal/application/process`; real DB `KORA_PROCESS_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN=[REDACTED] go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/application/process -run '^TestApproveAndApplyCommitsDecisionAndDomainTransitionAtomically$' -count=1 -timeout=180s`; `make check-contracts`; regenerated and checked architecture import graph (488 internal edges); and `git diff --check`. All final checks passed. The initial vet attempt using root `/tmp` failed due to filesystem exhaustion; rerunning with `/dev/shm` passed. This is an internal composition capability only: no production business approval policy provider or tenant runtime opener is wired, so the approvals capability remains unadvertised.

**MCP execution boundary (2026-10-15):** Removed placeholder tenant tool handlers that returned “Would call…” or validation-only text as if they were executable. The CLI now starts validation-only MCP and states that tenant data tools remain disabled until the runtime supplies an authenticated, policy-aware executor. Executable mode refuses to run without that adapter; `New` defaults to validation-only. Tests assert the refusal and default, and executable tool projection tests inject a test executor. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./mcp ./cli -count=1`, the same packages with `go test -race -p 1`, `go vet -p 1 ./mcp ./cli`, full Engine `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, `make check-contracts`, architecture import graph `--check`, and `git diff --check`; all passed. No public contract or schema changed. MCP does not yet have a production verified executor, so its executable parity gate remains open.

**Verified script identity projection (2026-10-15):** API-method and payment scripts previously received `user` and role labels from the request context. Those script-visible values now come from the verified execution already bound to their provider, and invocation fails closed when identity is missing or its tenant route differs. Roles are copied for presentation only; writes still pass through the provider's verified Kernel boundary. Added tests for verified subject/roles, route mismatch, missing execution, and role-slice isolation. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./api ./mcp ./cli -count=1`, the same packages under `-race`, `go vet -p 1 ./api ./mcp ./cli`, full Engine `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, integration compile for `./api ./internal/application/process`, `make check-contracts`, import graph `--check`, and `git diff --check`; all passed. No schema or public contract changed.

**Approval quorum gates the domain effect (2026-10-15):** Review of the coordinator against the G5/G6 acceptance criteria found that it ran a protected transition before recording the prepared decision, so a first approval could advance a subject while an `all` or `sequential` request remained pending. It now records the decision within the caller transaction first and runs the Kernel transition only when the resulting transactional receipt is resolved with `approved` outcome. Partial approval commits its request decision without a domain effect; a failing final action rolls back that final decision while preserving earlier decisions. Strengthened real MySQL 8.4.11 disposable integration coverage with a two-assignee `all` request: first response leaves the subject Draft, bad final action rolls back, valid final response commits subject and request together, and final same-key retry does not duplicate effects. Fresh loopback-only tmpfs-backed container was stopped and removed. Verified the real DB test `KORA_PROCESS_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN=[REDACTED] go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/application/process -run '^TestApproveAndApplyCommitsDecisionAndDomainTransitionAtomically$' -count=1 -timeout=180s`, full Engine `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, process/request SQL store race tests, scoped vet, integration-tag compile, `make check-contracts`, import graph `--check`, and `git diff --check`; all passed. No schema or public wire contract changed. The process approval API is still an internal composition capability, not enabled as a production business-domain provider; no requests.approvals.v2 capability is advertised.

**Owner-scoped AI execution approvals (2026-10-15):** Replaced the HTTP grant handler's unrestricted read/modify/upsert with one conditional update constrained by tenant, verified owner principal, and `pending_approval` state, followed by a persisted-row verification. A caller-supplied `granted_by` body field and legacy Gin user label cannot determine attribution; an owner mismatch, stale approval, or duplicate concurrent grant affects zero rows and returns not found. The approval inbox now requires verified identity and filters by the same owner. Added regressions for spoofed attribution, a different owner being unable to grant a guessed approval ID, and rejecting unverified list access; the disposable PostgreSQL fixture includes another owner's row and asserts it is not visible (live test not executed without its DSN). No schema or public wire contract changed. Verified: full `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./api -count=1`; focused `go test -race -p 1 ./api -run 'TestHandleAI(ListApprovals|GrantApproval)' -count=1`; integration-tag compile `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' ./api`; `go vet -p 1 ./api`; and `git diff --check` passed. `TestLivePostgresAIApprovalListBootstrap` was compiled but not run because `KORA_API_LIVE_POSTGRES_DSN` is unset.

**Generic updates cannot bypass active document workflows (2026-10-15):** Kernel `record.update` and `record.mutate_bundle` now reject writes to an active workflow state field. They also compare pre/post validation-hook state, preventing hooks from changing workflow state through a generic mutation. New unit tests cover active/default/custom state fields, inactive workflows, malformed payload handling, and post-hook state changes. A disposable MySQL integration test verifies both update commands leave the state unchanged. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./kernel -count=1`; `go test -race -p 1 ./kernel -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./kernel`; `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' ./kernel`; and the real focused disposable MySQL test `go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./kernel -run '^TestGenericRecordWritesCannotBypassActiveWorkflow$' -count=1 -timeout=180s`; all passed. No persisted schema or wire contract changed.

**AI related-record analytics uses shared policy (2026-10-15):** Related report-model source reads used `PermissionMatrix.UserCan` directly, so they could not honor exact resource-operation grants from verified service executions. They now use `policy.Decide` and reject missing execution and owner-scoped source permissions. Tests cover role permissions, owner-only rejection, missing identity, and exact service grants. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./api/ai ./api -count=1`, the same packages under `-race`, `go vet -p 1 ./api/ai ./api`, and `git diff --check`; all passed. No schema or wire contract changed.

**Analytics projections require source readability (2026-10-15):** The API analytics path checked permission for relationship edges but skipped the ordinary model's `SourceDoctype`; insight routes and catalog/report/metric listings could reveal aggregates or metadata for unreadable sources. API queries, individual insights and metrics now check verified unscoped source-read policy; model/report/catalog and metric projections omit unreadable sources. AI insights, analytics models and reports apply the same verified policy, including relationship and linked dimension sources. Regression tests cover ordinary source permissions, owner-scope rejection, exact service grants, route checks before database access, and hiding reports with any unreadable or unknown model. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./api ./api/ai -count=1`, same under `-race`, `go vet -p 1 ./api ./api`, `git diff --check`, and `go run ./cmd/routeinventory -check`; all passed. No schema, route, or wire shape changed.

**Generated AI analytics models authorize their source (2026-10-15):** A second pass found AI query authorization enumerated models only from saved reports. Automatically generated semantic models could validate and run without reaching `policy.Decide`. The authorization helper now builds the complete validated catalog, checks every queried model's source, relation and linked-dimension DocTypes, and rejects unknown model names. A regression covers the report-free generated-model case with and without source read access. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./api/ai ./api -count=1`, same under `-race`, `go vet -p 1 ./api/ai ./api`, and `git diff --check`; all passed. No schema or public contract changed.

**AI reads omit hidden/password fields (2026-10-15):** Read review found AI summaries, `_get`, find filters, generated tool schemas/hints, and semantic analytics models could expose fields marked `hidden` or of type `Password`. AI document projections now allow only visible non-password fields; query/filter/order validation and tool schemas apply the same rule, and semantic catalog generation omits those fields. Tests cover the response projection, summaries, tool descriptions/hints/schemas, mutation/filter rejection, and analytics model metadata. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./api ./api/ai ./analytics -count=1`; race tests for `./api/ai ./analytics`; `go vet -p 1 ./api ./api/ai ./analytics`; and `git diff --check`; all passed. No route, persisted schema, or wire contract changed. This implements existing hidden/password visibility constraints; it does not add field-specific role policy, which the current permission model does not represent.

**Script provider reads use visible-field projection (2026-10-15):** Cross-adapter review found authorized `kora.getDoc`/`kora.getList` calls returned `Document.ToMap()` directly, including hidden/password fields and nested child-table values. Script reads now preserve standard document metadata, filter hidden/password fields, and recursively apply the same projection to child rows. A regression covers parent and child documents; script authorization still derives from verified execution and owner scope. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./api ./api/ai ./analytics -count=1`, same under `-race`, `go vet -p 1 ./api ./api/ai ./analytics`, and `git diff --check`; all passed. No wire contract or persisted format changed. Role-specific field grants remain unsupported by the existing permission model.

**HTTP document projections omit hidden/password fields (2026-10-15):** The standard resource and Kernel result mappers returned hidden/password fields, including nested child values and explicitly requested hidden fields. Resource, view, Kernel response, and realtime projections now omit those fields; tests verify top-level, child, and explicit-field-list behavior. Verified: focused `go test -p 1 ./api ./api/ai ./analytics -count=1`, same packages under `-race`, `go vet -p 1 ./api ./api/ai ./analytics`, and `git diff --check`; all passed. No DTO/schema migration occurred, but dynamic response keys are narrowed on existing resource/view routes. This is a compatibility gate: inspect consumers that intentionally read hidden fields and deliver the compatible UI/API release before claiming the v1 consumer gate closed. No production rollout has occurred.

**Idempotent replay respects current authorization (2026-10-15):** Added a kernel regression that creates a record with a verified Creator execution, removes that role's Create permission from the active tenant matrix, and retries the same key. The retry is denied before receipt replay and does not create a second record. This demonstrates that durable receipts do not outlive current command authorization for the shared kernel boundary used by HTTP, AI, and other record-mutation adapters. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./kernel -count=1`, same under `-race`, `go vet -p 1 ./kernel`, and `git diff --check`; all passed. This does not close the adapter parity matrix or replace adapter-level tests for external effects.

**Tenant-bound executions at adapter boundaries (2026-10-15):** AI chat no longer passes Gin `user_roles` into its tool executor; roles come from verified `policy.Execution`. The `/api/v2/requests` and channel tool adapters now use the shared `requestExecution` resolver, which rejects an execution whose stable tenant or site route differs from the routed request. Added a route-mismatch regression. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./api ./api/ai -count=1`, the same packages under `-race`, `go vet -p 1 ./api ./api/ai`, `go run ./cmd/routeinventory -check`, and `git diff --check`; all passed. No contract or schema change. Remaining Gin `user` reads in source are limited to rate-limit keying and server-rendered presentation fields; they do not make authorization decisions.

**Script-created record ownership follows verified execution (2026-10-15):** Rechecking the script CRUD adapter found `CreateDoc` forwarded its `owner` argument into the Kernel even when it differed from the verified invocation actor. **Bounded AI reads and authenticated read-only MCP (2026-10-15):** The AI's existing DocType `find`/`list` query surface now enforces integral limits (maximum 100 rows), bounded offset, at most 20 filters, at most 100 values per set filter, bounded filter strings, and rejects unsupported arguments. This enables callers to combine authorized field filters to retrieve data beyond predefined reports while preserving tenant read grants, owner scope, and visible-field restrictions; it does not permit generated SQL or arbitrary joins. MCP now has a read-only mode exposing only `find`, `list`, and `get`, plus an opt-in CLI tenant executor configured with `KORA_MCP_EXTENSION_TOKEN` and stable `--tenant-id`. It reloads the tenant extension credential and grants per request, caps concurrency at four and execution time at five seconds, and returns sanitized authentication/query failures. Extension auth now uses dialect rebinding, including SiteGuard's HTTP path. Tests prove read-only tool publication, mutation rejection before auth, revoked-token denial, cancellation propagation, and extension query placeholder/identity behavior for MySQL, PostgreSQL, and LibSQL; a SiteGuard PostgreSQL regression verifies that actual adapter path. Verified: `gofmt -w auth/site_test.go mcp/server_test.go mcp/extension_reader.go api/ai/tools.go`; `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./auth ./api/ai ./mcp ./cli -count=1`; same with `-race`; `go vet -p 1 ./auth ./api/ai ./mcp ./cli`; and `git diff --check`, all passed. ### G6 Durable workflow orchestration

**Status:** verified against the specified group exit gate. Definition/instance persistence, worker fencing, human waits, durable recovery, APIs, and the CLI-composed governed approval recovery path are implemented and tested. Generic Engine workflow composition has no business grants/actions; the CLI explicitly registers the current Purchase Request owning-domain adapter. Additional action/event capabilities remain unavailable until their owning domains register authorization and transactional-effect proof; this is capability gating, not an unmet G6 exit requirement.

**G6 Engine/domain composition boundary (2026-10-16):** Removed direct purchasing imports and policy registration from generic `internal/engine` tenant/workflow composition. Added `RequestAuthorizerFactory` and workflow-approval bindings to Engine config, with `internal/application/purchasing/engineadapter` registering the domain authorizer, exact grants and transition names. The CLI is the explicit application composition root and registers this adapter, preserving existing Purchase Request request/workflow behavior and persisted-instance compatibility. Generic `SelfHostedWorkflowConfig` now has no business grants or action registrations. Updated the executable spec to clarify domain integration placement. Added unit coverage that generic workflow config has no domain grants and adapter registration owns the purchasing bindings. This removes domain-specific coupling from Engine internals; it intentionally retains the purchasing domain implementation because the target architecture assigns invariants to their owning domain and persisted work must not be stranded. Changed Engine `internal/engine/{config.go,tenant_services.go,workflow_runtime.go,workflow_runtime_config_test.go,workflow_runtime_mysql_integration_test.go}`, new `internal/application/purchasing/engineadapter/{configure.go,configure_test.go}`, `cli/serve.go`, target spec and this ledger. Passed `go test ./internal/application/purchasing/... ./internal/engine ./cli -count=1`, focused race `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/application/purchasing/engineadapter ./internal/engine -run 'TestConfigure|TestSelfHostedWorkflowConfig' -count=1`, `go vet ./internal/application/purchasing/engineadapter ./internal/engine ./cli`, and `git diff --check`. The real disposable MySQL acceptance passed: `KORA_ENGINE_WORKFLOW_PROCESS_RESTART=1 GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-g6-adapter-tmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-g6-adapter-tmp go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./cli -run '^TestEngineProcessRestartResumesGovernedHumanApproval$' -count=1 -timeout=480s -v` (Engine restarted, authorized decision recovered, subject reached Approved). `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check` passed; regenerated complete graph records 585 internal edges. No schema or wire contract changed. This closes the documented G6 exit gate; optional unregistered domain actions/events remain explicitly unavailable. G7 is next.

**G6 asynchronous hook actor-role preservation (2026-10-16):** Rechecked the process-local async hook path and found that it reduced a verified actor's role set to one `UserRole` before running the script. `AsyncHookRequest` now carries `UserRoles`; enqueue derives them from the verified execution actor when available, and the worker preserves the full role list while retaining the legacy single-role fallback only for requests without verified execution. Added multi-role regressions at both ORM enqueue and Engine worker execution. `go test ./orm ./internal/engine ./api`, `go test -race -p 1 ./orm ./internal/engine ./api -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./orm ./internal/engine ./api`, and `git diff --check` passed. This fixes role context for process-local async work; it does not make the hook queue durable or close the required transactional invocation/outbox gate.

**Implemented:** immutable versioned definitions; typed validation and generated step unions; durable instances, attempts, timers, event inbox and history; bounded per-tenant execution worker under tenant lifecycle ownership; claim lease renewal and fencing; registered action schemas; transaction-bound local domain action executor; conservative external outcome classification, bounded retries and operator reconciliation; durable human request creation, quorum-aware terminal recovery; timed/event wait recovery; cancellation; authenticated v2 workflow APIs; and SQL-backed recovery without Cloud, broker or external workflow service. Engine assembles SQL stores, catalog, API services, execution handler, bounded worker and recovery workers from trusted tenant workflow configuration. The supported CLI now supplies a constrained baseline for Purchase Request conditions, human approvals and timer waits; its service identity has exact `Purchase Request:read` and approval-submission grants, with no generic action schemas/builders or event correlation fields. Existing document workflow editing remains a separate state-machine feature.

**Compatibility/design correction:** workflow cancellation does not implicitly cancel a linked pending business request. The request owner must authorize and persist request cancellation first; terminal recovery then advances the waiting workflow through its declared cancellation route. Running and outcome-unknown steps cannot be cancelled directly. This preserves request authorization and history ownership. `target-state-spec.md` records this correction.

**Changed Engine files:** `workflow/` contracts, SQL stores, worker, retries and tests; `internal/application/process/workflow_local_action.go` and real-dialect integration tests; `internal/engine/workflow_runtime.go`, tenant composition/configuration; `api/workflow_api.go` and route registration/tests; workflow fields in `internal/tenantruntime/services.go`. Persistence adds `_kora_workflow_cancel_receipt` for idempotent cancel history/receipt. Public HTTP adds `/api/v2/workflow-definitions/{validate,publish}`, `/api/v2/workflow-instances` and `/{id}/{reconcile,cancel}` routes; no technical package or module paths changed.

**Composition boundary correction (2026-10-16):** The CLI now composes the domain-neutral `engine.SelfHostedWorkflowConfig` and separately registers Purchase Request behavior through `internal/application/purchasing/engineadapter`. The generic config has no business grants; adapter-specific grants and workflow transition mappings are covered by adapter tests. Purchase Request conditions still read the pinned subject; the authorizer recomputes subject revision/reviewer assignments; the human response coordinator applies the transition under the verified assignee. Real dialect workflow/request integrations remain MySQL, PostgreSQL and actual LibSQL HTTP; CLI process-restart acceptances verify timed-wait and human-approval recovery. Local-action transaction tests across all three dialects still use generic record creation, while the roleless worker-restart regression rejects document transitions without the required human role. G6 remains in progress: additional domain action capabilities require owning-domain registrations with authorization and transactional-effect proof; generic action/schema/event features remain unavailable without those registrations. Existing document state-machine editing and AI approval semantics remain separate; no persisted-instance migration or legacy executor deletion is authorized until its own gate passes.

**G6 exact action grant enforcement (2026-10-16):** Corrected the executable design and implemented the first enforcement boundary. `policy.Grant.Valid` validates an exact resource/operation; `workflow.ActionRegistration.RequiredGrant` is trusted assembly metadata; `ExecutionIdentityProvider.RequireActionGrants` checks all registered grants against the tenant service identity during workflow assembly; the definition claim handler, external executor, and local transaction executor recheck the grant before dispatch. Action contexts carry the required grant. No workflow JSON can add a grant, and no synthetic human role is created. Tests now require explicit grants; a regression proves a missing external grant creates neither a durable attempt nor a provider call. Exact checks passed: `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./workflow/... ./internal/application/process ./internal/engine -count=1`, matching scoped `go vet -p 1 ./workflow/... ./internal/application/process ./internal/engine`, integration-tag compile `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./internal/application/process ./workflow/sqlstore ./internal/engine`, and `git diff --check`. No wire DTO or persisted schema changed. `workflow.ActionRegistration` is a Go API behavior change; external extension compatibility must be checked before release. The request service now has an optional, fail-closed `WorkflowSubmissionAuthorizer` entry point separate from user submission; the purchasing domain implements it only for Purchase Request approvals and requires exact `requests.purchase_request_approval:submit`, validates workflow source IDs, recomputes current subject state/revision and eligible reviewer assignments, and retains human-only public submission/response authorization. Unit tests cover service-grant absence, stale subject, current reviewer resolution and no fallback when a workflow policy is absent. This does not close G6: a complete composed Engine path with that policy, workflow authorizers, authorized condition scopes, registered domain provider and supported CLI wiring remains open; the workflow-originated approval submission itself was then exercised through the production purchasing authorizer and request SQL store on all supported HTTP/database paths. Exact disposable integrations passed under race: `KORA_PROCESS_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN=[REDACTED] go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/application/process -run '^TestPurchaseApprovalPolicyAndDomainTransitionCommitTogether$' -count=1 -timeout=240s` against MySQL 8.4.11; the corresponding `TestPostgresPurchaseApprovalPolicyAndDomainTransitionCommitTogether` against PostgreSQL 16; and `KORA_PROCESS_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN=[REDACTED]` with `TestLibSQLRemotePurchaseApprovalPolicyAndDomainTransitionCommitTogether` against the actual sqld HTTP transport. The shared acceptance setup verifies durable source identity, service requester attribution and current reviewer assignments. The graph was regenerated at 562 internal edges; `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check` and `make check-cross-repo-contracts` passed, covering current contract copies, 15 fixture artifacts, route inventory and Cloud import allowlist. `git diff --check` passed.
