Orchestration is still not advertised.

**G6 roleless local action and LibSQL single-connection fix (2026-10-16):** Replaced synthetic `Manager` workflow service roles in the MySQL, PostgreSQL and LibSQL local-action tests with exact roleless `doctype:create` grants and a registered record-create action. The tests prove local authorized effects, receipt, outbox event and workflow progress share one transaction; the rollback/fencing cases remain covered. These tests intentionally do not claim a protected state transition: the separate roleless restart test rejects that operation because a service identity cannot synthesize a human transition role. The real LibSQL HTTP test initially timed out during tenant schema migration; the diagnostic stack showed `LibSQLDialect.LoadSchema` held table/index result cursors while opening nested PRAGMA queries, deadlocking with the test's one-connection pool. Updated `db/dialect_libsql.go` to close outer cursors before nested introspection and check cursor errors. After the fix, both `TestLibSQLWorkflowLocalActionCommitsDomainAndProgressAtomically` and `TestLibSQLRemotePurchaseApprovalPolicyAndDomainTransitionCommitTogether` passed together under `-race` against disposable sqld HTTP. Also passed `go test -race -p 1 ./db ./schema ./internal/application/process -count=1`, integration-tag compile for `./db ./schema ./internal/application/process ./workflow/sqlstore`, scoped `go vet -p 1 ./db ./schema ./internal/application/process`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check`. The disposable LibSQL service was removed. No public wire or database schema changed.

**G6 supported CLI baseline wired (2026-10-16):** Added `engine.SelfHostedWorkflowConfig` and passed it from `cli/serve.go` into Engine assembly. It supplies exact `Purchase Request:read` and `requests.purchase_request_approval:submit` grants, the deterministic expression evaluator, and fail-closed empty payload/event registries; the built-in purchasing authorizer still rechecks current subject revision and eligible human reviewers, while the response coordinator applies the domain transition under the verified assignee identity. It registers no generic record mutation, external adapter, arbitrary information schema, or event correlation field. Added `TestSelfHostedWorkflowConfigIsLimitedToRegisteredHumanApprovalPath` and integration-tagged `TestSelfHostedWorkflowConfigAssemblesTenantRuntimeOnMySQL`; the latter passed under `-race` twice against fresh tmpfs-backed MySQL 8.4.11 databases, composing request/workflow stores and services, creating the tenant execution worker, and running its empty recovery pass. Both disposable DBs/services were removed. `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/engine ./cli ./api -count=1`, scoped `go vet -p 1 ./internal/engine ./cli ./api`, integration-tag compile for Engine/process/workflow SQL store, and `go build -o /dev/shm/kora-engine-g6-check .` passed. `make check-cross-repo-contracts` first detected the untracked import graph artifact was stale after adding the integration test; regenerated with `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py`, then the cross-repository contract check passed: 15 fixture artifacts, current route inventory, complete import graph and the reviewed 3-edge Cloud implementation-import allowlist. `git diff --check` passed. No wire or persistence schema changed. The production human-wait path is separately verified on MySQL/PostgreSQL/LibSQL with service reconstruction, but a true Engine process restart through this CLI composition is still open; action, information-schema and event-wait adapters remain intentionally unavailable pending registered domain providers.

**G6 CLI process-restart timed-wait acceptance (2026-10-16):** Added `cli/serve_workflow_restart_integration_test.go`. The opt-in test starts a fresh tmpfs-backed MySQL 8.4.11 container, creates a separate tenant database and canonical directory entry, bootstraps tenant tables, publishes and starts a valid wait→end workflow, builds the current Engine binary, and starts the supported `serve` CLI with the domain-neutral `engine.SelfHostedWorkflowConfig`. It waits until the persisted instance is `waiting`, stops the first Engine, waits past the database-persisted wake deadline, starts a second Engine against the same directory/database, routes a tenant request to activate the runtime, and verifies that instance reaches `completed`. The separately registered purchasing adapter has since been verified by the human-approval restart acceptance recorded above. Exact checks passed: integration-tag compilation `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./cli`; the disposable run with `KORA_ENGINE_WORKFLOW_PROCESS_RESTART=1 go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./cli -run '^TestEngineProcessRestartResumesTimedWorkflow$' -count=1 -timeout=360s -v` passed in 21.34s; the same scenario under `-race` passed in 21.04s; integration-tag `go vet -p 1 ./cli`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts` (15 artifacts, route inventory, complete import graph and reviewed Cloud allowlist), and `git diff --check` passed. The MySQL driver logged transient `unexpected EOF` messages during database startup probing; the test completed successfully. Its tenant schema and container were removed. No production service/database was used and no contract/schema changed. Remaining G6 gate: a registered local workflow action must exercise a protected domain mutation plus request receipt and workflow progress in the specified atomic/fenced transaction; current cross-dialect local-action tests cover generic record creation and human-approval restart covers domain transition/recovery separately.

**G6 CLI process-restart governed approval acceptance (2026-10-16):** Extended `cli/serve_workflow_restart_integration_test.go` with a second real-process test. It starts a tmpfs-backed MySQL 8.4.11 Engine, imports an isolated Purchase Request doctype/workflow/permission snapshot, seeds a submitted request and verified tenant users, then uses authenticated Engine APIs to publish/start a human approval workflow. An assigned Purchase Manager responds through the authorized `/api/v2/requests/{id}/responses` route; the test confirms the request and protected Purchase Request transition are committed while workflow state is still waiting. It stops the first Engine before the 15-second human-recovery poll, restarts against the same tenant DB, and verifies the second process resumes the terminal request and completes the workflow. Both branches are exercised under real session cookies, CSRF, workflow policies, assignment eligibility and transaction-bound approval coordination. Passed normal run: `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp KORA_ENGINE_WORKFLOW_PROCESS_RESTART=1 go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./cli -run '^TestEngineProcessRestartResumesGovernedHumanApproval$' -count=1 -timeout=420s -v` (10.08s); and race run: `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp KORA_ENGINE_WORKFLOW_PROCESS_RESTART=1 go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./cli -run '^TestEngineProcessRestartResumes(TimedWorkflow|GovernedHumanApproval)$' -count=1 -timeout=480s -v` (31.27s). Integration-tag `go vet -p 1 ./cli` passed; regenerated and checked the complete import graph (577 edges); `make check-cross-repo-contracts` passed (15 artifacts, current route inventory, reviewed Cloud import allowlist); and `git diff --check` passed. An initial run correctly failed with HTTP 403 because `/api/v1/ping` does not issue a CSRF cookie; the test was fixed to use an authenticated GET before writes, and the reruns passed. Transient MySQL driver `unexpected EOF` probe logs also appeared on passing runs. The disposable Engine processes and MySQL container were removed. This verifies the default Purchase Request human-workflow path across process restart, but not any registered workflow action; no wire or persisted schema changed. G6 remains open for a concrete domain-owned action adapter and its authorized/atomic/fenced execution proof.

**G6 condition subject-read boundary (2026-10-16):** Closed a policy gap in the worker before invoking its condition-scope provider. A condition now requires an exact service `read` grant for the instance subject kind; owner-scoped grants fail closed because the workflow service has no authenticated human identity against which to evaluate ownership. The provider contract and target specification require current-revision reads and projection of only visible scalar fields, excluding hidden/password fields, unqualified names, owner metadata, tables and aggregates. Added an Engine-owned default provider using the current tenant registry/ORM; callers may still inject a stricter provider, but a missing `Conditions` factory no longer blocks Engine assembly. The provider rejects stale subject revisions and has SQL-mock coverage for numeric/string conversion, hidden-field exclusion, missing/owner-scoped grants and stale revision. Deterministic authorization, identity, validation, missing-subject, stale-revision and invalid-result failures now commit a terminal failed instance with a bounded reason/event; cancellation and transient persistence/provider failures remain retryable. Tests prove stale and unauthorized condition failures cannot remain in an endless claim/retry loop. Verified: `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./workflow/... ./internal/engine -count=1`; `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go vet -p 1 ./workflow/... ./internal/engine`; `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py` and `--check` (563 internal edges); and `git diff --check` all passed. The condition-scope boundary is implemented; supported CLI composition, production action identity/registrations and composed restart recovery remain open, so orchestration stays unadvertised.

**G6 live tenant authorization and Engine defaults (2026-10-16):** Added `internal/application/workflow.Authorizer`, which uses current `_kora_user` membership rather than cached session roles. Publishing requires `Workflow Definition:write`; starts require `Workflow Definition:read`, `Workflow Instance:create`, current subject read permission and an unchanged subject revision; current owner is checked for owner-only access; reads recheck current subject visibility; starters may cancel readable instances and operators may cancel/reconcile under `Workflow Instance` permissions. `workflow/sqlstore.AuthorizationStore` reads current roles, subject owner/revision in a single transaction, and the persisted starter history row. `configureTenantWorkflow` uses this authorizer for either missing policy override; condition scopes also have an Engine-owned default. The target spec now records the resource-operation policy and persistence semantics. A disposable real-dialect test initially exposed that LibSQL HTTP rejects `sql.TxOptions{ReadOnly:true}`; the store now uses a default transaction containing only `SELECT`s, preserving the snapshot boundary on that transport. Verified: `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./internal/application/workflow ./workflow/... ./internal/engine`; matching scoped `go vet`; integration-tag compile `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./workflow/sqlstore ./internal/application/workflow ./internal/engine`; and `KORA_WORKFLOW_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN=[REDACTED] KORA_WORKFLOW_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN=[REDACTED] KORA_WORKFLOW_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN=[REDACTED] go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./workflow/sqlstore -run '^TestAuthorizationStoreLiveReadsAcrossSupportedDialects$' -count=1 -timeout=180s`. The live test passed on disposable MySQL 8.4.11, PostgreSQL 16 and pinned LibSQL sqld HTTP; all three tmpfs-backed loopback containers were removed. `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check` (575 internal edges) and `git diff --check` passed. No wire DTO or persisted schema changed; workflow Go authorization interfaces were added. The defaults are available to Engine composition but do not activate the feature while CLI leaves `engine.Config.Workflow` nil. Full composed business action and human-wait recovery remain open.

**G6 Engine-minted workflow identity (2026-10-16):** Engine now constructs the workflow's verified service identity from trusted registration metadata and explicit `ServiceGrants`, using a stable tenant-scoped `kora-workflow-runtime` principal with no user, delegation, or roles. The prior injected identity callback remains source-compatible during migration, but Engine validates its tenant/site/service binding and strips grants beyond the configured trusted set. Assembly checks registered action grants, and dispatch still checks the exact required grant. Added race-tested coverage for tenant/site binding, unsupported actors, excess-grant stripping and missing action grants. Updated the target spec to state the actual minted identity and compatibility rule; no public wire contract or persisted schema changed. Passed `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./internal/engine ./workflow/... ./internal/application/workflow -count=1`. The supported CLI still does not configure domain workflow actions, so this does not activate or advertise orchestration; production action composition, a true process restart under the production policy, and a registered protected domain action remain open.

**G6 production governed human wait (2026-10-16):** Extended `internal/application/process/purchasing_approval_mysql_integration_test.go` with a combined MySQL/PostgreSQL/LibSQL acceptance using the production `purchasing.RequestAuthorizer` and `internal/application/workflow.Authorizer`. It publishes and starts a human approval workflow through current role/subject checks, creates a source-bound Purchase Request approval with the Engine-style roleless service identity, lets an eligible Purchase Manager decide through `Coordinator.ApproveAndApply`, closes the database pool after the domain commit, reopens the same disposable database, rebuilds request/workflow services, recovers the durable terminal request, and completes the pinned workflow. This verifies the governed human wait and approved domain transition on MySQL 8.4, PostgreSQL 16, and the actual LibSQL sqld HTTP transport while the CLI composition remains gated. First execution correctly rejected a subject already transitioned earlier in that test; the scenario now uses a fresh submitted subject. Passed `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./internal/application/process`; and three race runs, each against a fresh tmpfs-backed disposable service and with the same `go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/application/process -run <test-name> -count=1 -timeout=240s` command: `TestPurchaseApprovalPolicyAndDomainTransitionCommitTogether` with `KORA_PROCESS_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN=[REDACTED]` (MySQL 8.4.11), `TestPostgresPurchaseApprovalPolicyAndDomainTransitionCommitTogether` with `KORA_PROCESS_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN=[REDACTED]` (PostgreSQL 16), and `TestLibSQLRemotePurchaseApprovalPolicyAndDomainTransitionCommitTogether` with `KORA_PROCESS_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN=[REDACTED]` (LibSQL sqld HTTP). Also passed `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go vet -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/application/process`, `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check`; all disposable database containers were stopped and removed. No production database was used. A true process restart under this production policy, CLI action composition and a registered domain-action test remain open.

**G6 roleless workflow transition guard (2026-10-16):** Re-audited `workflow_human_restart_mysql_integration_test.go` against the target rule forbidding synthetic human roles and found its workflow service identity carried `Roles: ["Manager"]` to pass `CommandWorkflowStatusTransition`. Renamed and corrected the integration to create the subject as a verified human manager, run the workflow as a roleless service with only an exact doctype `submit` grant, and assert the restarted worker records a failed workflow while the document remains `Draft`. The harness request authorizer now implements the explicit workflow-submission interface; its absence caused the first run to fail before exercising the role check. Passed integration-tag compile `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./internal/application/process` and race run `KORA_PROCESS_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN=[REDACTED] GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/application/process -run '^TestRolelessWorkflowServiceCannotSynthesizeTransitionRoleAfterWorkerRestart$' -count=1 -timeout=240s` on fresh tmpfs-backed disposable MySQL 8.4.11. The container was stopped and removed. This regression preserves configured domain transition role checks; enabling a workflow action that mutates documents still needs a domain-owned authorization adapter and CLI registration.

**G6 injected-harness evidence (2026-10-15; not the group completion gate):** The legacy MySQL human-wait restart harness injects a task authorizer and now verifies that its roleless workflow service cannot synthesize a human role to complete the protected document transition. Separate local-action integration tests cover transactional action execution, while the production purchasing approval integration above covers a human decision applying the domain transition and recovering the workflow after database-pool reopen. Other transaction, fencing, cancellation and durable SQL recovery boundaries pass on MySQL, PostgreSQL and actual LibSQL HTTP. These tests do not verify supported CLI composition.

**AI read-query capability boundary (2026-10-15):** AI can answer queries outside preset reports when authorized fields are exposed through governed semantic models. It cannot compose arbitrary SQL, discover arbitrary tables/joins, or read fields absent from that catalog. The server applies tenant/field/read/owner checks and row/date/result bounds; user prompts are interpreted as bounded model queries. This is the current safe read-only route; exposing broader data requires explicit schema registration and authorization, not model-authored SQL. Verified by the focused analytics and AI tests recorded in the G3/G4 evidence above; real-dialect semantic query coverage remains a separate verification gate.

**G6 exit-gate verification (2026-10-16):** The target exit gate is one complete request→decision→domain action flow recovering across worker/process failure. The supported CLI integration exercises that path with the Purchase Request domain: the assigned Purchase Manager's authenticated, CSRF-protected response is reauthorized, the purchasing coordinator applies the protected document transition, the process stops while workflow continuation is pending, and a second Engine process resumes and completes the durable instance. This is a domain action executed as part of the human decision; it is not evidence that arbitrary separate `StepAction` adapters or correlated-event waits are available. Re-ran the two real-process MySQL 8.4.11 acceptances under race on 2026-10-16: `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp KORA_ENGINE_WORKFLOW_PROCESS_RESTART=1 go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./cli -run '^TestEngineProcessRestartResumes(TimedWorkflow|GovernedHumanApproval)$' -count=1 -timeout=480s -v` — both passed (30.932s total). MySQL startup probing emitted transient `unexpected EOF` logs; the tests passed and their isolated tmpfs-backed containers/processes were cleaned up. The implementation's action catalog, exact grant checks, fenced claims, local transactional executor, external uncertainty/reconciliation, human terminal recovery, and three-dialect durable-store tests are recorded above. No wire or persisted schema changed. **G6 is verified for the specified group exit gate; generic action/event features remain unavailable until a domain registers and verifies them.**

### G7 Cloud management boundary

**G7 checkpoint after G6 completion (2026-10-16):** Re-audited current Cloud composition and its actual Engine imports. `platform.Build` opens the Engine platform database only when `KORA_CLOUD_ALLOW_LEGACY_ENGINE_LIFECYCLE=true`; direct `site.CreateSite`, tenant DB config import, and legacy template catalog paths are behind that same compatibility setting. Default composition uses the typed management client when configured and otherwise fails closed for provisioning/package operations. The managed provisioning and immutable package paths have real Engine-process restart coverage recorded below; managed tenant deletion remains unadvertised without verified checkpoint and organization providers. The three remaining Engine implementation import edges are the reviewed compatibility allowlist, and their retirement still requires deployment config/consumer release evidence. No deletion provider, default flag, or compatibility path was changed. Reverified with Cloud race tests: `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./internal/config ./internal/platform ./internal/provisioning -run 'Test(LoadDisablesLegacyEngineLifecycleByDefault|KoraSiteLifecycleLegacyMutationsFailClosed|KoraSiteLifecycleProvisioningAvailabilityRequiresConfiguredPath|TemplateApplierFailsClosedWithoutLegacyLifecycle|RequestSiteDeletionDoesNotPersistWhenOnlyDrainProviderIsConfigured|DeleteWithCheckpointRequiresPersistedOperationID)$' -count=1` — passed. Engine `make check-cross-repo-contracts` passed: 15 shared artifacts, current route inventory/import graph, and the 3 reviewed Cloud compatibility edges; scoped ledger `git diff --check` passed. **G7 is externally blocked** on trusted provider/model composition and production compatibility-window evidence. Next action: obtain/verify those provider and deployment facts; continue local management-path work without enabling deletion or removing the compatibility adapter.

**G7 Cloud Compose management-only DB boundary (2026-10-16):** Rechecked the checked-in `docker-compose.yml` against `.env.example` and found Compose supplied a hard-coded Engine platform DB DSN even when the legacy lifecycle switch was false. Changed the Compose default to empty, preserving explicit operator DSN pass-through for the legacy compatibility path. No API, schema or credential changed; no service was started. Verified rendered Compose configuration for both default and `--profile ingress` using an ephemeral `.env` copied from `.env.example` (the repository had no `.env`; the temporary file was removed): management-only configuration keeps the Engine DSN empty, and a separate render confirmed an explicit placeholder legacy DSN passes through. Cloud config race tests and `go vet -p 1 ./internal/config` passed: `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./internal/config -run 'Test(LoadDisablesLegacyEngineLifecycleByDefault|ValidateAllowsManagementOnlySharedDeploymentWithoutEngineDatabaseCredentials)$' -count=1`. Engine `make check-cross-repo-contracts` and `git diff --check -- docker-compose.yml` passed. An initial Compose-render attempt correctly reported the missing local `.env`; the retry used only the checked-in example and left no `.env`. This removes the unintended DB coordinate from default Cloud container configuration but does not prove deployed environment values or close the external import-retirement gate.

**Status:** externally blocked; local managed provisioning, immutable package application, operation recovery, shared-tenant routing, credential revocation and drain/join checks are implemented and verified across Engine/Cloud. A trusted deployment checkpoint provider, production deletion wiring, and coordinated legacy-path release/retirement gates remain open.

**Cloud implementation-import isolation (2026-10-16):** Moved all three remaining Cloud-to-Engine implementation imports (`db`, `doctype`, `site`) out of the default `internal/platform` package source into `legacy_engine_compat.go`, selected only by `-tags=kora_legacy_engine`. The no-tag file provides explicit unavailable errors; `Build` rejects `KORA_CLOUD_ALLOW_LEGACY_ENGINE_LIFECYCLE=true` before starting services if the compatibility implementation was not compiled. The old direct platform registry/site creation, alias inspection/deletion, and tenant-database template importer remain available in the tagged binary, and the existing runtime opt-in is still required. No API, schema, or package/module path changed. Updated Cloud CI to test the tagged adapter and build the tagged migration binary; updated Cloud boundary/go-live/README, G0 inventory snapshot note, target spec and boundary checker. Exact verification: Cloud full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 1 ./...`, focused race tests `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/platform ./internal/provisioning -count=1` and `go test -tags=kora_legacy_engine -race -p 1 ./internal/platform -count=1`; default and tagged Cloud binary builds; Engine `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py` (575 edges), `make check-cross-repo-contracts` (15 fixture artifacts, route inventory, complete import graph, zero normal-build Engine implementation imports), Cloud boundary checker unit tests (3 passed), and scoped `git diff --check`. The legacy build's SQL alias-inspection tests passed under the tag. Provider/deployment deletion gates and retirement of the tagged binary remain externally blocked; the adapter is not purged.

**Latest G7 checkpoint (2026-10-15):** Engine composes `tenant.provision` and recovery only when deployment and opaque allocation settings are configured; its fenced worker uses context-aware canonical tenant creation and verifies identity/allocation on replay. Cloud persists the exact command and operation ID, retries lost acceptance with the stable key, and resumes after restart without sending a password. The Cloud typed-client integration registers Engine's actual Gin management routes over the real SQL operation service/store; its worker effect is fake. Separate Engine live tests run the actual route, composed durable worker, SQL store and tenant creator on MySQL/PostgreSQL, with a stub runtime coordinator. Durable invitation delivery is now implemented as a tenant-scoped typed Engine management operation, capability-gated on configured SMTP, with a token-hash/delivery journal, worker fencing, uncertain-outcome recovery state, Cloud operation-ID checkpointing, and resume polling. Unit tests exercise the Engine management route, journal transactions/reclaim fence, client route, and Cloud checkpoint/poll behavior. These tests do not yet form one cross-repository test with the real invitation executor, tenant runtime supervisor, SMTP provider, and Cloud lifecycle. Cloud and Engine package application, production deletion checkpoint configuration, Cloud direct database/template path retirement, invitation operator reconciliation, and release gates remain open. `KORA_CLOUD_ENGINE_MANAGEMENT_PROVISIONING` stays opt-in and legacy paths stay available. No production-readiness claim is made.

**G7 composed Engine provisioning route verification (2026-10-15):** Added `TestLiveManagementProvisionRouteAndWorker`, which registers the production Engine management routes around the composed management runtime, SQL operation store and worker, then provisions through HTTP into the real context-aware tenant creator. A test proxy accepts the first request durably and drops its accepted response; retrying the same idempotency key returns the same operation. The real worker claims and finishes it, and the test verifies the canonical active directory entry and exactly one bootstrap administrator. Passed under `-race` against fresh loopback/tmpfs MySQL 8.4.11 and PostgreSQL 16 services; both disposable containers were removed. The runtime coordinator is a test stub, so this does not prove tenant runtime supervisor replacement. Commands and outcomes are recorded here from the rerun after atomic synchronization: integration-tag compile, `go test -p 1 ./internal/engine -count=1`, `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/engine -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./internal/engine`, and `git diff --check` all passed. This closes the Engine route/real-creator gate, not Cloud lifecycle or production runtime convergence. Cloud's typed-client HTTP integration is now separately exercised against the actual Engine Gin routes and SQL store (below). Remaining: real runtime coordinator behavior, durable invitation delivery, immutable package resolver/application, production deletion checkpoint provider, Cloud direct DB/template path migration/removal after compatibility, and release/deployment gates; G7 remains in progress.

**G7 Cloud typed client through registered Engine API (2026-10-15):** Replaced the custom HTTP wire adapter in `internal/platform/engine_management_provision_integration_test.go` with Engine `api.NewManagementInspectHandler` and `RegisterOperations`, backed by the real `management/sqlstore` and `management.Service`. The Cloud `KoraSiteLifecycle` and typed client now perform lost-accepted-response retry and restart replay against the actual registered Engine HTTP handlers. A test worker effect executes once; the test does not compose Engine's real tenant creator (covered by the previous MySQL/PostgreSQL Engine integration) or runtime supervisor. Passed under `-race` against disposable tmpfs MySQL 8.4.11 after waiting for service readiness: `KORA_CLOUD_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='<loopback disposable MySQL DSN>' GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/platform -run '^TestCloudEngineManagementProvisionLostResponseReplay$' -count=1 -timeout=180s`. The first attempt before MySQL readiness failed at `Ping` without writes; the rerun passed and the container was removed. Cloud full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full race `go test -race -p 1 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./...`, and `git diff --check` passed. API imports required adding Gin/API transitive modules to Cloud's existing graph; prior checksum entries were retained. G7 remains open for a one-harness real Cloud lifecycle + Engine real worker/creator/runtime test, provider configuration and remaining package/deletion/legacy migration gates.

**Provisioning contract fidelity (2026-10-15):** Corrected the `ProvisionTenant` source comment and target-spec prose: the current Engine creates a passwordless bootstrap account, but does not send an enrollment link. Invitation delivery remains unsupported until a durable trigger and delivery outcome exist. This is a documentation-only wire-compatible correction; `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check` passed. The management `tenant.provision` capability remains unadvertised because the operation executor and trusted database-allocation resolver are not yet composed.

**Context-owned site provisioning primitives (2026-10-15):** Added `CreateSiteContext` and context-aware tenant database creation, system bootstrap, config activation, admin insertion, platform-registry registration, directory status activation, alias indexing, and PostgreSQL/MySQL bootstrap lock operations. Existing `CreateSite`, `CreateDatabase`, and bootstrap APIs delegate with a background context for source compatibility. Platform bootstrap's process-local wait now honors the caller deadline, and advisory-lock release is bounded; alias-index dirty recovery gets a two-second detached cleanup deadline. Added regressions for canceled create/bootstrap and canceled local bootstrap-lock admission. Passed Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, `go test -race -p 1 ./site ./cli ./internal/engine -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./...`, `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./...`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check`. Live compatibility passed under `-race`: `TestLivePostgresCreateSiteRegistersCanonicalDirectoryRecord` on a disposable PostgreSQL 16 container, and `TestLiveMySQLEnsureConfigVersionRecoversInterruptedActivation` on disposable MySQL 8.4; both containers were removed. No wire or persistence schema changed. Changed files: `site/create.go`, `site/site.go`, `site/bootstrap.go`, `site/alias.go`, `site/platform_bootstrap_lock.go`, `site/platform_bootstrap_lock_test.go`, `site/registry.go`, `site/directory_registry.go`, `site/create_directory_test.go`, and this ledger. Still open: the management operation worker does not yet pass its fencing/cancellation context into a composed provision executor; trusted allocation resolution and package application remain unimplemented, so provisioning/package routes stay disabled.

**Provisioning database identifier safety (2026-10-15):** `CreateSiteContext` now validates the resolved tenant database name before opening/bootstraping the platform directory; `CreateDatabaseContext` also validates at its own boundary. Names are bounded to each dialect's identifier limit and ASCII letters/digits/underscore/hyphen, preventing SQL quoting and DSN path/query metacharacters from reaching MySQL/PostgreSQL create/connect operations. Added unit cases for dialect limits and injection characters plus a SQL-mock assertion that invalid names cause no directory access. Added `site/create_mysql_integration_test.go`; live MySQL 8.4 passed a full create/retry, stable tenant ID, one admin, one active config version, and additional-alias resolution under `-race`. The disposable database server was removed. No schema or public contract changed. **Existing boundary reviewed:** Public management DTOs, the durable operation journal/service/worker, a supported typed Go HTTP client, and credential-free tenant inspection routes exist. Engine now composes `GET /api/management/v2/tenants/{id}`, alias resolution, durable `set_tenant_state`, and operation reads. `tenant.delete` is composed only when a trusted checkpoint verifier is injected; this workspace has no configured provider, so delete is not advertised. Provision and package executors remain unavailable. Cloud has an opt-in management client but retains direct Engine provisioning/deletion, tenant DB template imports, platform registry reads, Engine package validation, and some implementation imports in messaging/event transport. The inspection handler previously violated the specified wire envelope; it now returns the required `{data,meta}` and `{error,meta}` forms with explicit assertions.

**Provisioning identity compatibility (2026-10-15):** `site.CreateSiteInput` now accepts an optional validated canonical tenant ID from a trusted management command. Platform registration locks and reads the existing hostname identity before upsert, reuses it on retries, and rejects attempts to replace it; self-hosted callers with no supplied ID retain generated IDs. This closes a concrete contract/code mismatch because `ProvisionTenant.Tenant` is the stable cross-service ID. Added MySQL/PostgreSQL/LibSQL lock-query unit coverage, mismatch rejection and early invalid-ID checks, and extended the PostgreSQL `TestLivePostgresCreateSiteRegistersCanonicalDirectoryRecord` flow to verify requested identity, retry retention, and rejection of replacement before creating tenant data. That test passed with `-race` against fresh PostgreSQL 16 on a tmpfs-backed disposable container, which was stopped and removed afterward. Passed: `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/tmp TMPDIR=/tmp go test ./site -count=1`; `go test -race ./site -count=1`; `go test -tags=integration ./site -run '^$' -count=1`; `go vet ./site`; and `git diff --check`. The isolated PostgreSQL run used a fresh database and dropped its per-test tenant database during cleanup; no pre-existing database was touched. Changed files: `site/create.go`, `site/registry.go`, `site/create_directory_test.go`, `site/registry_test.go`, `site/directory_postgres_integration_test.go`, and this ledger. No public wire or DB schema change. Operation execution and Cloud migration remain open.

**Contract correction:** The specified `ProvisionTenant` omitted the administrator bootstrap identity needed by current Cloud provisioning. Removing Cloud's direct `site.CreateSite` path with no Engine-owned bootstrap identity would create tenants nobody can enter. Added required `bootstrap_admin_email`; no password crosses this boundary. Rechecking auth found the Engine has passwordless bootstrap accounts and single-use magic-link sign-in, but no invitation trigger/durable delivery flow. The target specification now requires provisioning to initiate that existing sign-in flow and forbids claiming invitation delivery until its outcome is durable; this remains an open implementation gate.

**Changed Engine files:** `contract/management.go`, `contract/management_test.go`, `contract/schema/engine-v2.json`, `contract/generated/engine-v2.ts`, `api/management_inspect.go`, `api/management_inspect_test.go`, and new `api/management_operations.go` plus tests; management service tests and nested `sdk/management` client tests; generated v2 copies and artifact manifests in embedded UI, Studio and SDK; contract fixture manifest; `target-state-spec.md`. Public DTO `ProvisionTenant` now includes validated `bootstrap_admin_email`; no persistence schema change yet. Inspection now matches the typed client's envelope. The new strict operation route adapter validates exact JSON fields, a 1 MiB cap, matching route/body tenant, optional matching idempotency header, verified configured caller, complete operation-specific grants, durable service interface, and safe public operation projection. Registration requires an explicit worker-ready composition signal, but Engine does not yet register it; `management/sqlstore` remains unwired to endpoints and an executor.

**Cloud-owned JetStream adapter (2026-10-15):** Replaced Cloud `internal/platform/event_bus.go` direct dependency on Engine `natsprovider` with a Cloud-owned adapter using public `contract.EventEnvelope` plus the pinned `nats.go` transport. The adapter validates event subject tokens/envelopes and a 1 MiB payload cap, uses JetStream message-ID deduplication, bootstraps a file-backed limits-retention stream with explicit 1 GiB/1,000,000-message caps and discard-new behavior, upgrades compatible bounded streams, and rejects incompatible, age-expiring, or memory-only streams. Added unit tests and disposable NATS integration tests for stream bounds, duplicate suppression and memory-stream fail-closed behavior. Passed `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/tmp TMPDIR=/tmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 1 ./...`, `go test -tags=integration ./internal/platform -run '^$' -count=1`, and `go mod tidy -diff`. The first integration attempt used a test server with 192 MiB maximum storage and correctly failed to create the adapter's 1 GiB bounded stream; rerunning with a 4 GiB tmpfs-backed test server passed. `rg -n 'natsprovider' internal --glob '*.go'` now returns no Cloud production imports. **Engine durable package application and activation recovery (2026-10-15):** Added the Engine executor behind a deployment-injected immutable artifact resolver; it validates the trusted artifact digest and package, merges package records additively over the tenant's current configuration, pins the accepted tenant configuration revision, journals schema activation, validates a fenced operation claim in the same transaction as config revision/version commit, refreshes or retires the tenant runtime according to its current directory state, and marks activation complete only after runtime convergence. Retries resume the stored target snapshot and replan schema changes from the live database; runtime replacement failure remains `recovery_required` and a newer claim resumes the same operation without duplicating its config version. API capabilities for apply/reconcile are exposed only when the resolver and executor are composed; package-bearing provisioning remains explicitly rejected until its combined provisioning lifecycle is implemented. A real cross-dialect test first upgrades a tenant from bootstrap version 21, then fails runtime replacement after durable activation and proves a newer fenced execution recovers the same activation on MySQL, PostgreSQL and LibSQL HTTP. That exposed `_kora_analytics_metric` missing from fresh/upgrade bootstrap despite `CollectSnapshot` requiring it; added dialect DDL and bumped system bootstrap to version 22. Changed Engine files: `internal/engine/management_package_executor.go`, `management_package_executor_integration_test.go`, `management_package_merge.go` and tests, `management_runtime.go`, `http_process.go`, `management_delete_executor.go`, `api/management_operations.go` and tests, `configstore/store.go`, `configstore/activation_journal.go`, `configstore/management_activation_fence.go`, its integration test, `db/tables_extensibility.go`, and `site/bootstrap.go`. No public contract or persisted workflow format changed; tenant bootstrap schema gained `_kora_analytics_metric`, and the management activation journal/fence tables and operation recovery contract are part of the current dirty G7 worktree. Passed full Engine `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, matching full `go test -race -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 1 ./...`, `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./...`, `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check`; the final disposable cross-dialect activation/recovery command was `KORA_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='<loopback tmpfs MySQL 8.0>' KORA_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN='<loopback tmpfs PostgreSQL 16>' KORA_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/engine -run '^TestLiveManagementPackageExecutorAcrossDialects$' -count=1 -timeout=240s` and passed. All disposable databases/containers were removed. The normal, race, vet, integration-compile, and drift checks were run before the final strengthening of the integration test; only that final live race integration was rerun afterward. No Cloud production migration or deployment gate closed here.

**Provisioning executor live replay (2026-10-15):** Rechecked the current Engine provision executor and its composition instead of trusting the earlier inventory note: it is enabled only when a trusted deployment ID and fixed database-allocation reference are configured; the executor validates both against the accepted command, invokes context-owned `CreateSiteContext`, verifies returned tenant identity/aliases/allocation, and activates the runtime. Its package-bearing command path remains fail-closed. `TestLiveManagementProvisionReplay` passed under `-race` against disposable tmpfs-backed MySQL 8.4.11 and PostgreSQL 16 services, proving canonical tenant identity, alias set, active directory entry, exactly one bootstrap administrator, stable config revision on replay, and runtime activation. Temporary tenant DBs, directory rows, and containers were removed. The older ledger statements that the provisioning executor is unavailable are superseded by this entry. The executor still uses one configured static allocation rather than a managed per-command allocation resolver, does not deliver a durable bootstrap invitation, and has not yet passed Cloud-to-Engine HTTP interruption/retry compatibility tests.

**Opt-in Cloud managed package application recovery (2026-10-15):** Cloud can now apply immutable marketplace package releases through Engine's typed management API when `KORA_CLOUD_ENGINE_MANAGEMENT_PACKAGES=true`, while persisting the immutable artifact reference, digest, expected tenant config revision and accepted Engine operation ID in the provisioning job before/after submission. Restart recovery re-resolves the same marketplace selection, verifies the immutable digest and tenant identity, resumes the recorded operation, and reconciles one `recovery_required` result. The legacy direct DB importer is not treated as durably recoverable. Added HTTP-level applier tests for initial apply, reconciliation and persisted-operation resume, and provisioning-manager recovery tests. Cloud full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full `go test -race -p 1 ./... -count=1`, and full `go vet -p 1 ./...` passed; Engine `make check-cross-repo-contracts` passed after the Cloud import changes. `git diff --check` passed in Cloud and Engine. Changed Cloud files include `internal/config/config.go` and tests, `internal/platform/dependencies.go`, `internal/platform/template_applier.go` and tests, `internal/provisioning/manager.go` and recovery tests, `internal/domain/models.go`, and the Cloud boundary/go-live/README docs. No shared contract or SQL schema changed; Cloud's persisted JSON checkpoint fields are additive. Still open: this opt-in package path requires Cloud and Engine to use the same deployment-owned immutable artifact resolver, and it lacks a cross-service test with a real shared resolver; base tenant creation remains on the legacy lifecycle and package-bearing Engine provisioning remains rejected.

**Opt-in Cloud managed tenant provisioning (2026-10-15):** Added `KORA_CLOUD_ENGINE_MANAGEMENT_PROVISIONING` plus required deployment ID and opaque database-allocation reference settings. The opt-in lifecycle sends Cloud's persisted site ID as Engine's canonical tenant ID, derives a stable idempotency key, sends no password, and persists the exact validated Engine command before submission. After acceptance it persists the operation ID before polling; after Cloud restart it either resumes that operation or replays the persisted command with the same key when acceptance preceded the operation-ID checkpoint. It performs one provision reconciliation on `recovery_required`, then verifies the active tenant and canonical alias through the typed API. Existing Cloud logic recognizes the passwordless bootstrap and requests the magic-link flow after runtime deployment. Legacy direct lifecycle remains a separate explicit path. Added typed-HTTP tests for command identity/allocation, raw request assertion that no password crosses the API, persist-before-submit, checkpoint-before-poll, polling, exact-operation resume, lost-response command replay, and tenant verification; config validation/loading tests; provisioning-manager checkpoint/restart recovery tests; and control-plane JSON payload round-trip/backward-compatibility plus public redaction checks. Final Cloud verification passed: `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, same with `-race`, `go vet -p 1 ./...`, and `git diff --check`. The disposable MySQL G7 fault-injection integration also passed under race: `KORA_CLOUD_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='<loopback-only disposable MySQL 8.4.11 DSN>' GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/platform -run '^TestCloudEngineManagementProvisionLostResponseReplay$' -count=1 -timeout=120s`; integration-tag vet and Engine `make check-cross-repo-contracts` passed. The test uses Cloud's real typed HTTP client, Engine's `management.Service` and durable `management/sqlstore` on MySQL; it drops the first accepted response, observes stable-key replay and restart-style replay, and verifies one worker execution. Its HTTP wire adapter and fake operation executor do not exercise Engine's registered Gin routes or real tenant site-provision executor. The tmpfs-backed MySQL container was removed. Changed Cloud files include `.env.example`, `README.md`, `docs/go-live.md`, `docs/cloud-boundary.md`, `internal/config/config.go` and tests, `internal/platform/dependencies.go`, `kora_site.go` and tests, `engine_management_provision_integration_test.go`, `internal/provisioning/manager.go` and tests, `internal/domain/models.go`, `internal/controlplane/mysql.go`, `memory.go`, and `provisioning_checkpoint_test.go`. The existing `steps_json` payload gains additive fields for command/operation checkpoint; legacy step-array payloads remain readable. These fields are redacted from public Cloud job JSON. No shared API contract or SQL schema changed. Engine accepts only its deployment's statically configured allocation reference, which Cloud must match exactly.

**Cloud management consumer recheck (2026-10-15):** The typed Cloud lifecycle already uses Engine management operations for state changes and checkpointed deletion, with operation polling, transient request retry, and a single explicit reconcile attempt for deletion; alias/ID inspection and adoption/reconciliation reads also use the typed API. Direct site creation and template imports still use the legacy Engine `site` and tenant-database paths when `AllowLegacyEngineLifecycle` is on, and management-only Cloud cannot provision marketplace templates because no configured immutable artifact publisher/resolver connects Cloud releases to the injected Engine resolver. Current Cloud consumer checks passed: from `kora-cloud`, `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./internal/platform ./internal/provisioning ./internal/app ./internal/sitereconcile -count=1` and the same package scope with `go vet -p 1`. These checks validate current compatibility but do not close the provisioning/template migration gate.

**Opt-in Cloud template application through Engine (2026-10-15):** Added `KORA_CLOUD_ENGINE_MANAGEMENT_PACKAGES` (false by default and requires the typed management URL/token pair). When explicitly enabled, Cloud verifies the resolved marketplace manifest and bytes against the immutable bundle digest, asks Engine to validate the pack, verifies active tenant identity/alias, then applies by opaque release ID and digest through the durable Engine package operation with a stable tenant/release/digest idempotency key. The Cloud provisioning job now persists the selection, artifact reference, expected tenant config revision before submission, and accepted Engine operation ID before polling; startup/operator recovery resolves the exact persisted release, resumes the same operation or idempotent request, and reconciles an uncertain Engine activation once. The old tenant DB importer remains available only behind the separate legacy switch and is not selected by default. Changed Cloud files: `internal/config/config.go` and tests, `internal/domain/models.go`, `internal/provisioning/manager.go` and recovery tests, `internal/platform/dependencies.go`, `template_applier.go` and management tests, `README.md`, `docs/go-live.md`, `docs/cloud-boundary.md`, and this ledger. Cloud persistence receives additive optional JSON fields on the existing `ProvisioningPackageSnapshot`; no Engine public contract or DB schema changed. Verification passed: Cloud full `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full `go test -race -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 1 ./...`, and `git diff --check`; focused HTTP tests cover normal submission, recovery-required reconciliation, and resuming a persisted operation ID, while a provisioning recovery test proves Cloud re-resolves the same immutable release and persists the updated revision/operation checkpoint. This does not yet test the full Cloud→Engine flow against a shared real artifact provider or remove the tenant creation/direct-import compatibility gates.

**Cloud directory reads moved to management API (2026-10-15):** Cloud site adoption and directory reconciliation no longer read Engine's platform registry or request `DBSiteInfo` containing database credentials. Both use the supported typed management client for per-tenant ID/alias inspection; adoption's contract test uses the real nested `sdk/management.Client` over HTTP and verifies the management bearer token is distinct from the provisioning credential. Reconciliation reports `scope: cloud_references`, resolves only tenants referenced by Cloud, classifies Engine 404s as `cloud_only`, and fails closed on malformed descriptors, identity mismatch, or management API errors. It cannot discover Engine-only tenants because the current contract exposes no global tenant listing; the report and Cloud boundary documentation state this limitation. Changed Cloud files: `internal/platform/dependencies.go`, `internal/app/site_adoption.go`, `site_reconciliation.go`, their tests, `management_test_helpers_test.go`, `internal/sitereconcile/reconcile.go`, and `docs/cloud-boundary.md`. **Managed channel permission contract (2026-10-15):** Added versioned `contract.PermissionGrant` as the stable JSON projection for managed channel-session permissions. Engine converts it into internal `doctype.Permission` only at the HTTP boundary; Cloud messaging and tenant-client code no longer import Engine authorization implementation types. `make sync-contracts` now regenerates Engine v2 validator copies for embedded UI, standalone UI and TS SDK, with checked manifests; `make check-cross-repo-contracts` validates generated-copy drift, fixtures, route inventory and the full Go import graph.
