**Compatibility/deployment gates:** Engine provisioning, state, and package routes are conditionally composed with a recovery worker; package routes require an injected trusted artifact resolver, provisioning uses its statically configured allocation, and deletion still requires a verified checkpoint provider. Cloud still imports Engine `site`, `db`, `doctype`, and `script` implementations and still opens tenant DBs for package application; no G7 migration is complete. Cross-service provisioning/apply/delete migration, notification/invitation delivery, artifact allowlisting evidence, delete checkpoint semantics, Cloud outage behavior, and management HTTP/client interruption-recovery compatibility tests remain open. No production data or deployment was changed.

**Next concrete action:** Replace the integration test's in-process HTTP wire adapter and fake executor with Engine's registered management HTTP routes and composed provision executor, or a disposable Engine process harness, and verify one tenant/admin/config activation under the same dropped-response and restart sequence on MySQL and PostgreSQL. Then test package application through a shared immutable resolver and deletion with verified checkpoints. Keep legacy lifecycle/template paths until these compatibility gates pass. Deletion still requires persisted Cloud workflows and a configured verified checkpoint provider.


**Current implementation:** Versioned request contracts, tenant SQL persistence, an authorization-driven service, direct user-to-user task policy, quorum/decision/reassignment/cancel/read/history mechanics, HTTP endpoints and a local atomic approval coordinator exist. Engine tenant composition consumes committed direct-task request outbox events into durable notification deliveries and owns the bounded notification worker. Recipient resolution is current-membership and assignment scoped; realtime inbox events carry only recipient, request, and event identifiers. A server-owned Purchase Request approval policy derives eligible reviewers from the active workflow, current memberships, and live read/submit permissions; it exposes only role-authorized approve/reject choices and atomically applies the corresponding workflow transition. Unsupported governed policies fail closed; request workflows remain a G5 Engine capability, while generated consumer/UI integration is a later G8 gate.

**G5 notification verification (2026-10-15):** Added focused recipient filtering and realtime payload tests, MySQL integration coverage for active requester/assignee resolution and governed-request exclusion, and an end-to-end disposable MySQL test from committed outbox event through durable delivery claim, recipient-scoped event-bus notification, acceptance, and duplicate suppression. Both new MySQL tests passed under `-race` against fresh loopback-only MySQL 8.4.11; the disposable services were removed. Scoped request/notification/Engine unit tests, race tests, vet, and integration-tag compile passed. Full Engine verification also passed: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; same with `-race`; `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' ./...`; `go vet -p 1 ./...`; and `git diff --check`. The integration-tag compile does not run the full DB matrix. No public request contract or schema changed in this notification wiring.

**G5 stale-subject response and reconciliation retry (2026-10-15):** Response-time subject reference or content-digest drift now returns the defined `STALE_SUBJECT` contract code and still creates no decision. `Store.MarkStale` treats a repeat against the same stale request revision, subject identity, and changed subject revision as successful without another history row or event; other revision/status/subject mismatches continue to conflict. Added service/store regressions and expanded the disposable MySQL decision-race integration to stale the same subject twice and assert exactly one stale history row and outbox event. Verified: `go test -race -p 1 ./requests ./requests/sqlstore -count=1`; scoped `go vet`; and `go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./requests/sqlstore -run '^TestMySQLDecisionRace$' -count=1 -timeout=240s` against a fresh tmpfs MySQL 8.4.11 instance. The full decision race, stale retry, transaction rollback/commit, and idempotent winning response passed; the container was removed. No schema or wire shape changed; the stale response now uses an already-defined error code.

**Post-fix Engine verification (2026-10-15):** After the stale-subject change, the full Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full `go test -race -p 1 ./... -count=1`, repository-wide integration-tag compile `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' ./...`, `go vet -p 1 ./...`, and `git diff --check` all passed with tmpfs build/cache paths. This confirms the change has no Engine compile, unit, race, static-analysis, or whitespace regression; it does not substitute for the still-missing PostgreSQL/LibSQL request lifecycle execution.

**G5 PostgreSQL and LibSQL stale lifecycle (2026-10-15):** Added integration tests which apply the real request/outbox schemas and assert a subject change is persisted as stale on the first call and an exact retry leaves one history entry and one outbox event. `TestPostgresStaleRequestReconciliationIsIdempotent` passed with `-race` on a fresh PostgreSQL 16 database inside a uniquely named disposable schema. `TestLibSQLRemoteStaleRequestReconciliationIsIdempotent` passed with `-race` on the actual LibSQL HTTP transport (sqld 0.24.33) backed by fresh tmpfs storage. The services/schemas were removed after each run. Also passed request package race tests, integration-tag compile, regular and integration-tag vet, and `git diff --check`. Changed Engine file: `requests/sqlstore/terminal_dialects_integration_test.go`; no schema or wire changes. These runs verify stale terminal persistence across the remaining supported transports; they do not yet prove direct-task inbox, decision race, notifications, or approval behavior across all three dialects.

**G5 cross-dialect quorum and receipt lifecycle (2026-10-15):** Extended `terminal_dialects_integration_test.go` so the same PostgreSQL and actual LibSQL HTTP tests seed a two-assignee `all` request, commit one decision and verify it stays pending, commit the second and verify resolution, then replay the final idempotency key and check the same receipt. Database assertions prove two immutable decisions, two response-history entries, one resolved-history entry, two outbox events, and two receipts. Both `TestPostgresStaleRequestReconciliationIsIdempotent` and `TestLibSQLRemoteStaleRequestReconciliationIsIdempotent` pass with `-race` on fresh disposable PostgreSQL 16/schema and LibSQL sqld 0.24.33/tmpfs services; exact command form: `KORA_REQUESTS_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN='<redacted disposable DSN>' go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./requests/sqlstore -run '^TestPostgresStaleRequestReconciliationIsIdempotent$' -count=1 -timeout=180s` and corresponding `KORA_REQUESTS_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN` / `TestLibSQLRemoteStaleRequestReconciliationIsIdempotent`. Services were removed. Request package race tests, integration-tag compile, regular/integration vet, and diff check passed. No contract/schema change. This verifies all-quorum decision persistence/replay for PostgreSQL and LibSQL, but not concurrent races, direct-task HTTP projection, notification delivery or approvals on these dialects.

**G5 cross-dialect notification recipient resolution (2026-10-15):** The PostgreSQL and LibSQL lifecycle tests now also exercise `DirectTaskNotificationRecipients` against real SQL tables. They prove created events select only the active current assignee, response events select the requester and pending active assignee, terminal events omit inactive users, and governed approval requests produce no direct-task recipients. Passed with `-race` as part of `TestPostgresStaleRequestReconciliationIsIdempotent` on a fresh PostgreSQL 16 disposable schema and `TestLibSQLRemoteStaleRequestReconciliationIsIdempotent` on the actual sqld 0.24.33 HTTP transport. Both services were removed. Also passed race tests for `./requests ./requests/sqlstore ./api`, integration-tag compile for request SQL/API packages, regular and integration vet, and `git diff --check`. No schema or contract change. This verifies recipient planning on these dialects; durable delivery worker persistence and HTTP projection remain untested there.

**G5 response-versus-cancel race across dialects (2026-10-15):** The PostgreSQL and LibSQL lifecycle now also races one authorized assignment response against requester cancellation on the same expected request revision. Each service must produce exactly one commit and one `CONFLICT`; persisted request state/revision, decisions, and cancellation history must match the winner with no partial losing effects. Both `TestPostgresStaleRequestReconciliationIsIdempotent` and `TestLibSQLRemoteStaleRequestReconciliationIsIdempotent` passed under `-race` against fresh PostgreSQL 16/schema and actual LibSQL sqld 0.24.33 HTTP/tmpfs services. Services were removed. Follow-up request/API race suites, integration-tag compile, regular/integration vet, and `git diff --check` passed. No schema or wire change. A competing response-versus-response race on these dialects and expiry/cancel/response deadline races remain untested.

**G5 response-versus-expiry race and LibSQL timestamp compatibility (2026-10-15):** Extended the real PostgreSQL/LibSQL lifecycle to race `ExpireDue` against an authorized response for an already-due task. Both runs prove one commit and one conflict, with an expired revision, no decision, and exactly one expiry history/outbox event. The actual LibSQL HTTP reader exposed a space-separated offset timestamp (`YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS.sss+00:00`) that `parseDBTime` rejected; added the offset layout and a focused regression. The expiry outbox assertion uses the request tenant key, matching `terminalEvent`'s persisted route. Verified: `go test -race ./requests/sqlstore -count=1`; real-dialect command `go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./requests/sqlstore -run '^Test(Postgres|LibSQLRemote)StaleRequestReconciliationIsIdempotent$' -count=1 -timeout=180s` against disposable PostgreSQL 16 and actual LibSQL HTTP service; and `git diff --check`. Both temporary services were removed. No schema or wire changes. Cross-dialect concurrent response-versus-response and HTTP projection parity remain open.

**G5 competing response race across dialects (2026-10-15):** The same real PostgreSQL and LibSQL HTTP lifecycle now races two assignees responding to an `all` request at revision 1. Exactly one response commits; the other gets `CONFLICT`; the request remains pending at revision 2, and decision/history/outbox each contain exactly one effect. Both dialect integrations passed under `-race` against fresh disposable PostgreSQL 16 and actual LibSQL HTTP services; both were removed. Also passed `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./requests ./requests/sqlstore ./api -count=1`, `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' ./requests/sqlstore ./api`, scoped `go vet`, and `git diff --check`; the owned test containers were absent after cleanup. No schema or contract changes. Deadline expiry is covered against response, but expiry-versus-cancel and PostgreSQL/LibSQL HTTP projection plus durable notification-delivery integrations remain open.

**G5 assignment-specific responses, history pages, and read watermarks (2026-10-15):** Extended real PostgreSQL/LibSQL HTTP lifecycle tests. A response excluded from an assignment's persisted `allowed_responses` returns `PERMISSION_DENIED` and leaves request revision/status, decision, history, and outbox unchanged; the allowed response then commits. History pagination returns stable sequence cursors across pages. `MarkRead` advances a per-principal watermark to the request revision while leaving pending status and history/decision/outbox completion facts untouched. Both dialect tests passed under `-race` against disposable PostgreSQL 16 and actual LibSQL HTTP services; containers were removed. `go test ./requests/sqlstore -count=1` and integration-tag compile passed before DB execution; the earlier scoped race suite passed, and real-dialect command was `go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./requests/sqlstore -run '^Test(Postgres|LibSQLRemote)StaleRequestReconciliationIsIdempotent$' -count=1 -timeout=180s`. No schema or wire changes. Full history/read API projection parity and monotonic watermark conflict races remain unverified.

**G5 deadline expiry precedence over cancellation (2026-10-15):** Added a PostgreSQL and LibSQL HTTP race with an already-due pending task. `ExpireDue` wins the terminal state at revision 2; a simultaneous requester cancellation is rejected as `CONFLICT`; history records one expiry and no cancellation. Both real-dialect lifecycle tests passed under `-race` against disposable PostgreSQL 16 and actual LibSQL HTTP services. Prior scoped `go test -race ./requests/sqlstore -count=1`, integration-tag compile, and `git diff --check` passed; the integration run removed both services. No schema/wire change. Expiry now has tested deterministic ordering against response and cancellation on those dialects.

**G5 notification delivery failure and recovery (2026-10-15):** Extended the Engine outbox→notification→inbox event integration. A provider failure leaves the delivery durably pending with one attempt and an error, while the underlying request stays pending. After its retry time becomes due, the same delivery is claimed and accepted by the in-app sender. Verified by `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/engine -run '^TestMySQLRequestOutboxSchedulesAndDeliversRecipientScopedInboxEvent$' -count=1 -timeout=240s` against fresh loopback-only tmpfs MySQL 8.4.11; disposable container removed. Integration-tag compile, focused Engine sender unit test, and `git diff --check` passed. No request contract/schema change. Cross-dialect HTTP/API notification delivery remains open.

**G5 direct-task participant projection (2026-10-15):** Review found `DirectTaskProjector` limited visible assignments on the request view but allowed any assigned participant to fetch another participant's response detail and unredacted history, including reassignment identities and comments. Response details are now limited to the requester's view or the viewer's own assignment. Other participants' history keeps sequence, kind and time for progression visibility while redacting actor, assignment, transfer, response and comment fields; requester and involved-assignee views retain the relevant details. Added regression tests for own/other response and history, and reassignment participants. Verified: `go test -race -p 1 ./requests -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./requests`; `git diff --check`. No contract or persistence shape changed. This closes the identified direct-task projection leak at the domain projector; the MySQL API scenario is recorded next and PostgreSQL/LibSQL API projection parity remains open.

**G5 participant privacy through HTTP (2026-10-15):** Expanded `TestMySQLDirectTaskHTTPFlow` to use an `all` quorum with two assignees. After Bob responds, Charlie receives only Charlie's assignment, sees the redacted response history, and receives 404 for Bob's response detail; the requester Alice can still read that response and comment. This verifies the real API→service→SQL→projector path. Passed on fresh tmpfs MySQL 8.4.11 under `go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./api -run '^TestMySQLDirectTaskHTTPFlow$' -count=1 -timeout=240s`; disposable database/container removed. Also passed `go test -p 1 ./api ./requests ./requests/sqlstore -count=1`, same with `-race`, scoped vet and `git diff --check`. PostgreSQL/LibSQL route-level versions remain untested.

**G5 Purchase Request approval policy and runtime composition (2026-10-15):** Added the domain-owned `purchase-request-approval-v1` policy. It rejects caller-selected assignments, binds to the current submitted subject revision and digest, resolves reviewers from active tenant memberships and the configured Purchase Request `Approve` roles, and rechecks active membership, read/submit permission, workflow condition, assignment eligibility, and subject digest before response. Engine tenant composition now installs the policy and a request/kernel transaction coordinator for approved local workflow changes. Finance Manager receives the minimum Purchase Request read/submit permission needed by the existing workflow. Fixed comparison result/handled ordering in the shared workflow condition evaluator; without this, false numeric comparisons fell through to Lisp symbol evaluation and could incorrectly pass. Verified: `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache2 GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp2 go test ./internal/application/purchasing/... ./doctype ./internal/engine ./kernel -count=1` and the same scope under `-race -p 1`; both passed. No public contract or schema changed. This is not yet a complete approval verification: real tenant SQL policy lookup and approved Purchase Request transition still need dialect-backed integration coverage, including race/rollback behavior.

**G5 purchasing SQL policy and concurrent read-watermark verification (2026-10-15):** Added a MySQL integration test for live authorization revision lookup, active user roles, bounded role-based reviewer expansion, and the ORM-backed Purchase Request/owner read; `go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/application/purchasing/sqlstore -run '^TestMySQLPurchaseApprovalStoreReadsLivePolicyInputs$' -count=1 -timeout=180s` passed against a fresh MySQL 8.4.11 tmpfs container, which was removed. Added concurrent `MarkRead` calls to the real PostgreSQL/LibSQL lifecycle. PostgreSQL 16 passed; the actual LibSQL HTTP test first reproduced SQLite lock errors, so `MarkRead` now retries only LibSQL busy/locked failures with a bounded delay and context cancellation. The rerun passed: `go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./requests/sqlstore -run '^TestPostgresStaleRequestReconciliationIsIdempotent$' -count=1 -timeout=240s` and matching `TestLibSQLRemoteStaleRequestReconciliationIsIdempotent`; each used a fresh tmpfs-only database service, removed after the run. Also passed the request-store and purchasing package race tests plus integration-tag compile. No contract/schema change. The protected workflow action itself is still not combined with this real SQL policy in a single database integration test.

**G5 production Purchase Request approval integration (2026-10-15):** Added a disposable MySQL integration through the production `purchasing.RequestAuthorizer`, SQL policy store, request service, participant projector, transaction opener, coordinator, and Kernel workflow command. It proves active role-derived reviewer assignment, role-specific approve/reject choices, current subject binding, rollback of a decision when the registered action fails, and atomic successful commit of both approved and rejected request outcomes with their Purchase Request state transitions, operation audit, and domain outbox. Exact command: `KORA_PROCESS_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='<loopback disposable MySQL root DSN>' go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/application/process -run '^TestPurchaseApprovalPolicyAndDomainTransitionCommitTogether$' -count=1 -timeout=240s`; fresh MySQL 8.4.11 container and generated database were removed. The actual submission uncovered Engine's use of the direct-task-only projector for governed approvals; composition now uses `requests.ParticipantProjector`, and a regression verifies requester/assignee views plus peer response/history redaction. The scoped request, Engine, purchasing, doctype and kernel race tests and integration-tag compile passed. No contract/schema change. Equivalent end-to-end workflow approval integration on PostgreSQL/LibSQL remains open.

**Engine-wide verification after G5 projection hardening (2026-10-15):** Full Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full race suite `go test -race -p 1 ./... -count=1`, integration-tag compile `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' ./...`, `go vet -p 1 ./...`, and `git diff --check` passed after the direct-task projection change. New PostgreSQL and LibSQL stale lifecycle tests were separately run against disposable services, since the compile-only command does not execute integration tests.

**G5 cross-dialect HTTP and durable notification recovery (2026-10-15):** The direct-task HTTP flow now passes against disposable PostgreSQL 16 and the actual LibSQL HTTP transport: submit, authorized assignee inbox, response, and requester terminal view. Durable request outbox consumption and inbox notification scheduling/delivery now also pass on PostgreSQL and LibSQL HTTP, including provider failure retaining a retryable delivery, successful retry, recipient-scoped invalidation, and duplicate event suppression. PostgreSQL notification receipt/claim fencing and idempotent reclaim passed under race; LibSQL claim timestamps now decode supported remote timestamp values. Tests used fresh schemas/databases and disposable loopback services, which were removed. Exact tests: `TestPostgresDirectTaskRequestHTTPFlow`, `TestLibSQLRemoteDirectTaskRequestHTTPFlow`, `TestPostgresRequestOutboxSchedulesAndRecoversInboxDelivery`, `TestLibSQLRemoteRequestOutboxSchedulesAndRecoversInboxDelivery`, and `TestPostgresNotificationReceiptAndClaimFence`; all run with `-race -tags=integration -p 1` and the matching disposable DSN environment variable. No public contract or request schema change.

**G5 PostgreSQL boolean binding correction and three-dialect approval verification (2026-10-15):** The new PostgreSQL approval integration exposed a production SQL mismatch: bootstrapped `_kora_user.enabled` is `SMALLINT`, but purchasing reviewer expansion bound a Go boolean. Added `db.BoolParam` at the purchasing, direct-task membership, and direct-task notification persistence boundaries; PostgreSQL binds numeric `0`/`1`, while MySQL and LibSQL retain native bool parameters. Integration fixtures now use the Engine-compatible small-integer column. Refactored the Purchase Request integration to run the real policy store, SQL request service, participant projector, coordinator, Kernel workflow action, audit and outbox against each real dialect. All three disposable integration runs pass under `-race`: `TestPurchaseApprovalPolicyAndDomainTransitionCommitTogether` on MySQL 8.4.11, `TestPostgresPurchaseApprovalPolicyAndDomainTransitionCommitTogether` on PostgreSQL 16, and `TestLibSQLRemotePurchaseApprovalPolicyAndDomainTransitionCommitTogether` on the actual LibSQL HTTP transport. Each proves role-derived assignments, role-specific approve/reject, atomic domain transition and request outcome, rollback after invalid registered action, and authorization denial/no decision after membership revocation plus revision advance. Services were tmpfs-backed and removed. This also validates condition comparison and SQL owner/read checks for the Purchase Request policy. No wire contract or request schema change. `target-state-spec.md` records the PostgreSQL type correction.

**G5 complete and verified (2026-10-15):** The required request/approval behavior is implemented and exercised: direct user tasks and the registered Purchase Request approval policy; any/all/sequential assignment persistence and quorum; authorized reassign/cancel; role-specific responses; request outcome distinct from the local domain transition; subject revision/digest binding; decision/history/read receipts; paginated inbox/history stores; durable notification scheduling, delivery failure/retry and duplicate suppression; and response-time authorization. Disposable MySQL 8.4.11, PostgreSQL 16 and actual LibSQL HTTP integrations verify approval policy and atomic request/domain outcomes, invalid-action rollback, changed-subject denial without a decision, and revocation after membership revision advancement. PostgreSQL and LibSQL HTTP API flows exercise submit, inbox, respond, response detail, history, read receipt, reassign, reassignee inbox and cancel. Real PostgreSQL/LibSQL request-store lifecycle tests cover response/response, response/cancel, response/expiry, expiry/cancel, quorum, stale reconciliation and concurrent read watermarks; MySQL decision races and API participant-privacy flow pass. Notification provider failure/recovery and outbox duplicate delivery are verified on PostgreSQL and actual LibSQL HTTP. Exact final checks passed: `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./api ./requests/... ./notifications/... ./internal/application/purchasing/... ./internal/application/process ./internal/engine ./doctype ./kernel -count=1`; scoped `go vet` on the same packages; integration-tag compile `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1` on those packages; and `git diff --check`. Focused real-dialect race tests passed for Purchase Request approval on all three database paths; PostgreSQL direct-task/API, notification and request lifecycle integrations passed; PostgreSQL and actual LibSQL HTTP API lifecycle integrations passed. All disposable test databases and services were removed. No request wire schema or persistence schema change was made in this final G5 verification; the target specification records the PostgreSQL smallint binding correction. G8 consumer compatibility and G9 assignment exports are later gates and do not block G5 Engine completion. Next group: G6 durable workflow orchestration.

**DigiTax integration retirement follow-up (2026-10-15):** Rechecked the current Engine source after the requested retirement. The handler, callback route, callback tests, ERP submission script, and orphaned retry registration are absent. Updated `KERNEL-MUTATION-MIGRATION.md` so removed callback behavior and test results are explicitly historical and not represented as supported coverage. No DB schema or API contract changed.


**DigiTax template retirement completed (2026-10-15):** Removed the last active DigiTax-specific field definitions (`digitax_item_id`, `digitax_item_code`) from `config/v0/erp_kenya/doctypes/item.yaml`. A repo-wide scan across Engine, Cloud, standalone UI and TypeScript SDK now finds DigiTax names only in dated migration evidence and this ledger; the obsolete callback route, handler, tests, ERP submission script and retry job were already removed. Existing tenant databases and records were not modified, and historical migration evidence remains. Verified YAML parses, `rg -ni 'digitax|digi_tax|digi-tax'` over source/config (excluding historical docs and this status ledger) returns no active references, and `git diff --check` passes. This removes the starter-template fields only; no destructive tenant schema migration was run.


**G7 management executor recovery gate revalidated (2026-10-15):** The current operation worker calls `OperationExecutor.Execute` before `Store.Finish` checks the lease owner/fence. Therefore every executor side effect must be operation-ID idempotent or reconcile actual state after a crash/expired claim; writing the durable journal alone does not make a mutation safe. No Engine executor is currently composed and no mutation route is registered. Existing `site.CreateSite` uses canonical tenant identity and durable `provisioning`→`active` directory states, but has no context-aware bootstrap, package, invitation delivery, or complete cross-store recovery adapter; deletion has a canonical-ID fence and retry worker but still needs Cloud checkpoint integration. This recheck confirms the next action is executor/use-case implementation with operation-identity recovery and per-command tests before route registration; do not expose the dormant routes until then. Evidence: inspected `management/worker.go`, `management/sqlstore/store.go`, `site/create.go`, `site/deletion_recovery.go`, `internal/engine/platform_routes.go` and process composition. No code or schema changed in this inspection.


**G7 stale operation claim rejected before execution (2026-10-15):** `management.Worker.process` now immediately renews/fence-validates a durable claim before invoking the operation executor. Previously it waited up to one-third of the lease before the first validation, allowing a claim already stale on arrival to begin an effect. A rejected initial renewal exits without calling the executor or finishing the stale claim; the durable row remains eligible for its current owner/recovery. Added `TestWorkerDoesNotExecuteClaimRejectedByInitialRenewal`. Passed `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache go test -race -p 1 ./management ./management/sqlstore -count=1`, corresponding scoped `go vet`, integration-tag compile `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./management/sqlstore`, and `git diff --check`. No schema or public contract changed. This closes stale-before-start only; executing side effects still require stable operation-ID idempotency or reconciliation, and an executor is not yet composed.

**G7 Engine-owned tenant suspend/resume operation (2026-10-15):** Implemented one durable lifecycle slice end to end. `SetTenantState` now requires `expected_config_revision`; Engine's SQL directory transition checks that revision under a transaction/row lock, commits the status/revision and directory outbox row together, and makes replay of the same target idempotent. Engine composes a bounded, process-owned operation worker only when `KORA_ENGINE_MANAGEMENT_CALLER_ID` is configured; that configuration enables only state mutation and operation-read capabilities, and the worker is cancelled/joined before the platform DB closes. Cloud suspend/resume now uses its typed management client, derives an idempotency key from tenant/target/revision/reason, waits for the durable Engine operation, then converges Cloud's deployment runtime and persists degraded status on either failure. Updated the executable specification, self-host instructions, and Cloud boundary docs. Changed repositories/files: Engine `contract/management.go`, `site/directory_registry.go`, `internal/engine/management_runtime.go`, `management_state_executor.go`, `http_process.go`, `resources.go`, and tests; Cloud `internal/platform/kora_site.go`, `internal/provisioning/manager.go`, and tests; Engine `deploy/selfhost/README.md`, `docs/architecture/target-state-spec.md`, and this ledger; Cloud `docs/cloud-boundary.md`. Contract schema changed; no tenant/business DB schema changed. Passed Engine `go test -p 1 ./contract ./api ./management ./management/sqlstore ./site ./internal/engine -count=1`, Cloud `go test -p 1 ./internal/platform ./internal/provisioning -count=1`, nested SDK `cd sdk && go test -p 1 ./management -count=1`, and focused Cloud race tests over platform/provisioning. Subsequent disposable live lifecycle runs passed under `-race` on MySQL 8.4.10, PostgreSQL 16, and the actual LibSQL HTTP transport; these are focused state-directory tests, not the whole persistence matrix. The Cloud management client was tested over a fake Engine HTTP server, not against a composed live Engine management server. G7 remains in progress: provision, package apply, deletion and interruption recovery are not composed; Cloud direct Engine imports and database paths remain; no deployment release has been performed.

**G7 lifecycle slice broader validation (2026-10-15):** Follow-up checks passed: full Cloud `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; Engine `go test -race -p 1 ./contract ./api ./management ./management/sqlstore ./site ./internal/engine -count=1`; the dedicated `TestProcessResourcesJoinsManagementWorkerBeforeClosingPlatformDB` race test; scoped Engine and Cloud `go vet -p 1` for the changed packages; nested SDK `go test -p 1 ./management -count=1`; integration-tag compile `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./site`; focused real-database `-race` tests for `TestLiveMySQLSiteStateTransitionIsRevisionFencedAndIdempotent` on MySQL 8.4.10, `TestLivePostgresSiteDirectoryLifecycle` on PostgreSQL 16, and `TestLiveLibSQLSiteDirectoryLifecycle` through the actual LibSQL HTTP driver; `make check-contracts`; `make check-cross-repo-contracts` after regenerating the complete import graph to 532 edges; `gofmt -d` on changed Go files; and `git diff --check`. All three database services were fresh, loopback-only and tmpfs-backed, then stopped and removed; no existing database or container was used. An earlier simultaneous cold-cache verification exhausted `/dev/shm` and caused linker errors; after clearing only task-generated `/dev/shm/kora-gocache`, `/dev/shm/kora-gotmp`, and `/dev/shm/kora-testtmp`, serial reruns passed. Root filesystem remains nearly full (about 613 MiB available at the time checked), so broad parallel builds are unsuitable. These results close the focused SQL state-transition and process-join evidence, not the complete persistence matrix or deployed Cloud release gates listed above.

**G7 Cloud lifecycle failure visibility (2026-10-15):** Cloud now joins the Engine or deployment-runtime failure with any error while persisting the degraded Cloud site state; it no longer silently drops that persistence failure on suspend/resume. Added a regression proving both failures remain discoverable through `errors.Is`. `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./internal/platform ./internal/provisioning -count=1`, full Cloud `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, and `go vet -p 1 ./...` all passed after this change. This does not replace a durable Cloud management-operation journal for all provisioning/package/deletion work.

**G7 provisioning contract correction (2026-10-15):** Added required `ProvisionTenant.primary_alias`; `aliases` now explicitly means additional hostnames. The previous aliases-only shape had no ordering/canonical-host semantics, so Engine could not safely determine which hostname to pass as site creation identity. Updated the target spec and Go validation/tests, regenerated the Engine schema/TypeScript bundle and synchronized standalone UI/SDK artifacts, and updated the SDK fixture. Exact checks passed: Engine `go test -p 1 ./contract ./api ./management ./sdk/management -count=1` (the nested module package was then rerun correctly from `sdk/`: `go test -p 1 ./management -count=1`); SDK `bun test ./src/generated/engine-v2.test.ts` (8 tests) and `bun run check:contracts`; UI `bun run check:contracts`; `make check-contracts`; `make check-cross-repo-contracts`; and `git diff --check`. At that checkpoint, the full UI suite did fail under Bun because Vitest-only globals and one alias import were unavailable, followed by a Bun segfault. That is no longer a current blocker: the later G7 verification below passed the complete Studio suite (204 tests) and build. The Engine provisioning replay executor and its separate recovery route are also recorded in the later G7 entries; provisioning remains incomplete until Cloud migration and package activation gates pass.

**G7 alias propagation in Cloud provisioning (2026-10-15):** Cloud's onboarding request now carries `extra_domains` through to the Engine site-creation call. Previously the lifecycle adapter supported extra domains, but the provisioning manager silently dropped the request field, leaving aliases out of Engine's authoritative directory. Added a regression on the provisioning flow asserting both requested domains reach the lifecycle boundary. Changed Cloud `internal/provisioning/manager.go` and its test; no Engine schema/persisted format changed. Passed `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./internal/provisioning ./internal/platform -run 'TestStartProvisioningReusesLifecycleOutputForRuntimeDeploy|TestKoraSiteLifecycle|TestProvisioning' -count=1`, scoped `go vet -p 1`, and `git diff --check`. This fixes the legacy Cloud path's alias loss but does not complete migration to Engine's durable provision operation API.

**G7 provisioning failure checkpoint visibility (2026-10-15):** Cloud provisioning no longer discards errors when persisting the failed site/job/lead state after Engine creation, template application, runtime deployment, managed-channel setup, or runtime reload fails. It now joins the originating side-effect error with the Cloud checkpoint persistence error, so callers can distinguish the uncertain external outcome while retaining both causes. Added a regression that injects an Engine creation failure and a failed Cloud checkpoint write and verifies both via `errors.Is`. Changed Cloud `internal/provisioning/manager.go` and `manager_test.go`; no wire or persisted schema changed. Passed `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./internal/provisioning -run 'TestStartProvisioning' -count=1`, scoped `go vet -p 1 ./internal/provisioning ./internal/platform`, and `git diff --check`. This improves recovery observability but does not implement the Engine-owned provision operation executor or Cloud management migration.

**G7 atomic Cloud provisioning intent (2026-10-15):** The initial Cloud lead, account, subscription, site, and provisioning-job intent now persists atomically before Engine/runtime side effects. Added `Repository.PersistProvisioningIntent`, with one-transaction MySQL implementation and one-lock in-memory implementation; `StartProvisioning` no longer performs five separately committed writes for acceptance. A sqlmock regression injects a record-write failure after the lead insert and verifies the transaction rolls back. Changed Cloud `internal/controlplane/repository.go`, `memory.go`, `mysql.go`, new `provisioning_intent_test.go`, and `internal/provisioning/manager.go`. No wire or persisted schema changed. Passed Cloud `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./internal/controlplane ./internal/provisioning -count=1`, full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 1 ./...`, and `git diff --check`. This guarantees a coherent Cloud request record but does not replace the Engine operation journal; typed provisioning execution, package/delete migration, and remaining direct Engine imports remain open.

**G7 management operation retry resilience (2026-10-15):** Cloud now retries transient network, HTTP 408/429, and 5xx failures when submitting a revision-fenced state operation or polling its durable operation, within the caller deadline or a two-minute default bound. Repeated state submissions reuse the exact deterministic idempotency key; transient operation reads preserve the last known operation identity/state. A regression simulates a 503 on submission and on polling, verifies the retry uses the same key, and completes successfully. Changed Cloud `internal/platform/kora_site.go` and `kora_site_management_test.go`. Passed `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./internal/platform -run 'TestSetTenantState' -count=1`, full Cloud `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 1 ./...`, scoped vet, and `git diff --check`. This hardens the existing state-management slice; it does not enable the uncomposed provision/package/delete operations.

**G7 current boundary audit and delete-contract correction (2026-10-15):** Rechecked the current Engine and Cloud source before continuing. Engine composes its durable operation store, service, bounded worker and HTTP route for `set_tenant_state` and `operation.read`; it does not compose provision, package, or delete executors. Cloud `KoraSiteLifecycle` still calls `site.CreateSite`, reads the Engine registry directly when management inspection is unavailable, and calls `site.DeleteRegisteredSite`; `KoraTemplateApplier` still opens tenant databases and imports configuration directly. The package-reference/artifact-resolution and database-allocation adapters are not implemented. A further safety mismatch was found: `DeleteTenant.RecoveryCheckpointRef` is syntax-checked as an opaque string but no Engine or Cloud path verifies that it identifies a tenant-matched, restorable checkpoint. The target specification now requires trusted checkpoint verification before the Engine advertises or accepts deletion and requires Cloud's durable deletion workflow to hold verified backup and object-store evidence. This correction preserves the intended deletion boundary while avoiding a false safety guarantee from a field name alone. Changed Engine `docs/architecture/target-state-spec.md` and this ledger; no wire contract or schema changed. Passed Engine `make check-contracts` (generator and local fixture drift checks) and `git diff --check`. G7 remains in progress. Next concrete action: implement the configured checkpoint-verifier boundary and Cloud durable deletion evidence flow, then add and run disposable-database deletion recovery tests before exposing `tenant.delete`; provision and package executors and their Cloud migrations remain separate G7 work.

**G7 deletion checkpoint identity model (2026-10-15):** Cloud's existing deletion shape had only `ManifestRef`, often a URI, while Engine's `DeleteTenant.RecoveryCheckpointRef` accepts an opaque provider reference. Added separate optional `TenantID` and `RecoveryCheckpointRef` fields to Cloud's external and internal workflow models, retained `ManifestRef` for audit, and added `DeletionCheckpointReady` to require the exact stable tenant, `deleting` phase, operation identity, both references, and verified backup plus object-store flags. `DeletionEvidenceComplete` now requires the tenant and recovery reference as well. Added rejection tests for mismatched tenant, wrong lifecycle phase and each missing evidence item. Updated the target spec and Cloud boundary documentation to clarify that Engine must independently verify the opaque reference and that deletion stays disabled without the verifier. Changed Cloud `cloud/deletion.go`, `cloud/deletion_test.go`, `internal/domain/deletion.go`, `internal/domain/deletion_test.go`, and `docs/cloud-boundary.md`; Engine target spec and this ledger also changed. No wire/schema or DB migration occurred. Passed full Cloud `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, Cloud focused race `go test -race -p 1 ./cloud ./internal/domain -run 'Deletion' -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./cloud ./internal/domain`, Engine `make check-contracts`, formatting check `gofmt -d cloud/deletion.go cloud/deletion_test.go internal/domain/deletion.go internal/domain/deletion_test.go`, and whitespace checks in both repositories. This adds the domain predicate only: no Cloud persistence or delete request currently consumes it, no provider issues checkpoint refs, and Engine does not yet verify one. G7 remains in progress. Next concrete action: persist deletion workflows and connect a configured checkpoint provider to Cloud and Engine before migrating the destructive call.

**G7 durable Cloud deletion persistence and recovery coordinator (2026-10-15):** Added the Cloud `cloud_deletion_workflows` migration and MySQL/memory repositories with tenant-serialized creation, immutable identity/evidence references, CAS transitions, and active/latest workflow lookup. `DeleteSite` now resumes persisted checkpointed deletion through the typed Engine management client, removes Cloud runtime state, persists archival and completion, and treats completed retries idempotently. The Engine lifecycle adapter inspects canonical tenant identity, submits a stable-idempotency delete command, polls the durable operation with bounded transient retries, and confirms the tenant is absent. Added coordinator, storage, concurrency, checkpoint-gating, recovery-operation and HTTP adapter tests. Updated Cloud boundary documentation. Changed Cloud `internal/controlplane/{repository.go,memory.go,migrations.go,deletion_workflows.go,deletion_workflows_test.go}`, `internal/domain/deletion.go`, `internal/provisioning/{manager.go,manager_test.go}`, `internal/platform/{kora_site.go,kora_site_management_test.go}`, and `docs/cloud-boundary.md`. Persisted schema change: new `cloud_deletion_workflows` table (migration 26); no public wire schema change. Exact checks passed: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, focused race `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/provisioning ./internal/controlplane ./internal/platform -run 'Deletion|DeleteSiteUsesPersisted|DeleteWithCheckpoint' -count=1`, and `go vet -p 1 ./...`. This recovery slice has no workflow producer or verified evidence-provider integration; Engine has no checkpoint verifier and does not compose the delete operation. Sites without a workflow still use the legacy direct deletion path for compatibility. Next: implement/compose Engine checkpoint verification and deletion operation, then add the Cloud deletion request/evidence producer and test interrupted operations against disposable databases before retiring that fallback.

**G7 Engine checkpoint verification boundary and allocation-bound delete operation (2026-10-15):** Engine now derives `TenantDescriptorV2.data_plane_ref` from current Engine directory database allocation metadata without exposing database credentials or raw coordinates. The reference is a length-prefixed SHA-256 digest over DB type/host/port/name and file-storage kind/object bucket; credential rotation does not alter it. Added `CheckpointVerifier`/`VerifiedCheckpoint` injection and a delete executor that compares the provider-resolved checkpoint reference, tenant, current allocation, completeness, immutability, and restorability before invoking canonical Engine deletion. Management runtime advertises/registers `tenant.delete` only when a verifier is supplied; no provider is configured here, so the destructive route remains unavailable by default. The target spec now defines `data_plane_ref` derivation and keeps it distinct from checkpoint proof. Changed Engine `internal/engine/{management_delete_executor.go,management_delete_executor_test.go,management_runtime.go,platform_routes.go,http_process.go}`, `site/{directory.go,directory_registry.go,directory_alias_descriptor_test.go,registry_test.go}`, `api/{management_inspect.go,management_inspect_test.go}`, and `docs/architecture/target-state-spec.md`; Cloud `docs/cloud-boundary.md` updated to reflect the verifier boundary. No DB schema or generated wire schema changed. Focused checks passed: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./internal/engine ./site ./api -count=1`. Remaining G7 deletion gates: supply/configure a trusted checkpoint provider, verify data-allocation fencing through destructive execution, implement the Cloud workflow producer/evidence provider, run disposable-database interruption/recovery tests, and keep legacy deletion until compatible rollout is proven. Next: validate whole Engine package/race/vet/contracts and harden allocation fencing at the deletion boundary.

**G7 allocation-fenced deletion hardening and verification refresh (2026-10-15):** Added `SQLSiteRegistry.SetDeletingAtAllocation`, which locks the current tenant row and checks both `ConfigRevision` and the allocation digest before atomically setting `deleting` and publishing the directory event. Tenant registration now refuses rows already `deleting` or `deleted`, and the checkpoint-bound teardown path rechecks the same allocation before database removal. Updated the target spec with this race-prevention transaction. Expanded SQL-mock tests and added live MySQL 8.4.11 tests for stale-allocation rejection, allocation immutability after the delete fence, and actual deletion of an isolated disposable tenant DB plus directory row. Ran those tests against fresh loopback-only MySQL on port 3312 with `/var/lib/mysql` on tmpfs; the test-owned container and databases were stopped/removed, and no pre-existing service was modified. Full Engine verification passed: `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; `go test -race -p 1 ./site ./api ./internal/engine -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./...`; `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' ./site ./api ./internal/engine`; live MySQL `KORA_SITE_DIRECTORY_LIVE_MYSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' go test -p 1 ./site -run '^TestLiveMySQL(DeletionFenceBindsCheckpointToCurrentAllocation|CheckpointBoundDeletionDropsOnlyTheVerifiedAllocation)$' -count=1 -timeout=180s`; `make check-contracts`; `make check-cross-repo-contracts`; and `git diff --check`. The first full suite run exposed an existing stale LibSQL identifier-quote expectation in `analytics/semantic_query_test.go`; it now matches the supported dialect's double-quoted identifier and the complete suite passes. Updated Engine files: `site/{registry.go,registry_test.go,deletion_recovery.go,deletion_recovery_test.go,registry_mysql_integration_test.go}`, Engine executor/composition and descriptor files listed above, plus the LibSQL expectation test. Import graph regenerated and checked at 533 internal edges. PostgreSQL and LibSQL destructive integration were compiled but not live-run in this slice. Remaining: no trusted verifier implementation/configuration or Cloud evidence/workflow producer exists; do not enable deletion until those are supplied and recovery-after-interruption is verified. The legacy direct Cloud path remains only for compatibility during migration.

**G7 route gating regression (2026-10-15):** API regression now verifies a state-only management deployment returns 404 for `tenant.delete`; the Engine runtime grants that capability only with an injected checkpoint verifier. Focused validation after this test and final formatting passed: `go test -p 1 ./api ./internal/engine ./site -count=1`, `go test -race -p 1 ./api ./internal/engine -run 'Management(Operation|Delete)|ManagementInspect' -count=1`, `go build -o /dev/null .`, `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check`.

**G7 Cloud deletion request producer and resumable evidence stages (2026-10-15):** Added `RequestSiteDeletion` behind explicit organization, credential-revocation, tenant-drain, and checkpoint-provider interfaces. It persists a stable workflow and operation ID before side effects, stores the requesting operator, advances stages with CAS, passes the same idempotency key to providers on retries, records stage errors without losing resumability, validates checkpoint tenant/evidence before the `deleting` transition, and resumes interrupted `revoking`/`draining` work. `DeleteSite` enters this producer only when every provider is configured; otherwise sites without a persisted workflow keep the existing compatibility behavior. Same-state CAS updates are limited to provider references/error reporting and cannot mutate identity or checkpoint evidence. Cloud model payload change: optional persisted `requested_by` field; no new table or migration beyond migration 26 already recorded above. Changed Cloud `internal/provisioning/{manager.go,manager_test.go}`, `internal/controlplane/{repository.go,memory.go,deletion_workflows.go,deletion_workflows_test.go}`, and `internal/domain/deletion.go`. Added success, provider-retry/stable-ID, evidence-tenant mismatch, and no-advance-on-invalid-evidence tests. Passed full Cloud `TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-cloud-testtmp GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-cloud-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-cloud-gocache go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; focused `go test ./internal/provisioning ./internal/controlplane`; focused race on those packages; focused vet on those packages; and `git diff --check`. Initial race/vet attempt hit `/tmp` exhaustion; rerun with `/dev/shm` scratch completed successfully. G7 remains in progress: deployment has no configured organization resolver, credential revoker, drain provider, checkpoint issuer, or Engine verifier adapter, so this producer is intentionally unavailable in current composition. Need interruption tests against disposable Cloud DB plus Engine end-to-end management operation, provider composition/configuration, and Cloud direct-DB/import migration before G7 can close.

**G7 Engine registry-read compatibility gate (2026-10-15):** Cloud's `KoraSiteLifecycle.Inspect` now prefers the typed Engine management API and refuses a direct `_kora_site_alias`/`_kora_site_registry` read unless `KORA_CLOUD_ALLOW_LEGACY_ENGINE_REGISTRY_READS=true` is explicitly set. The environment default is false, the lifecycle dependency receives the setting from Cloud configuration, and `.env.example`, README and Cloud boundary docs describe the temporary migration bridge. Updated the previous SQL-mock tests to opt in and added regressions proving the default path issues no registry SQL and the setting parser is opt-in. No public contract or database schema changed. Passed focused config/platform/provisioning unit and race tests; full Cloud `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; full `go vet -p 1 ./...`; `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./...`; and `git diff --check`. Cloud read-side migration now fails closed by default, but the explicit compatibility option remains until legacy deployment consumers migrate. Cloud still directly imports Engine implementation packages for creation/deletion/template paths; G7 is not complete.

**G7 lifecycle-wide Engine database gate supersedes registry-read-only toggle (2026-10-15):** Replaced the narrower `KORA_CLOUD_ALLOW_LEGACY_ENGINE_REGISTRY_READS` switch with `KORA_CLOUD_ALLOW_LEGACY_ENGINE_LIFECYCLE`. With the default false, Cloud does not open or bootstrap Engine's platform registry in `platform.Build`, legacy create/inspect/delete lifecycle methods fail closed, and shared-mode configuration requires either the typed management client or explicit temporary legacy mode. Legacy mode requires the Engine platform DSN. Added config validation tests for management-only startup without Engine DB credentials, explicit legacy-mode parsing, and default-off direct operations. Updated Cloud `.env.example`, README, go-live and boundary documentation. No public contract or database schema changed. Passed focused config/platform/app unit and race tests; full Cloud `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; full `go vet -p 1 ./...`; integration-tag compile `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./...`; `make check-cross-repo-contracts`; and `git diff --check`. The earlier read-only toggle entry above is historical and superseded by this setting. Legacy create/delete remain available only in explicit compatibility mode; typed Engine provision/package executors are still not composed, so Cloud cannot yet safely replace those operations or remove the old path.

**G7 alias ownership enforcement (2026-10-15):** Engine enforces one normalized-alias owner with a verified unique index; startup rejects legacy conflicts and fails closed when projection state is unavailable. Registry, alias rows, metadata, and directory-outbox changes commit atomically; conflicts roll back registration. Removed post-commit alias sync. The target spec records stop/inspect/fix/rebuild migration steps. Changed Engine alias/registry code and tests. Persisted schema adds the unique index; no wire change. Verified race-enabled `site` tests, `go vet`, API/Engine/CLI tests, and live MySQL 8.4, PostgreSQL 16, and LibSQL HTTP directory-lifecycle tests; MySQL conflict rollback was confirmed. After restoring and formatting the workspace source, verified the full Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1` and `go test -race -p 1 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./...`, `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check`, all passed. Disposable loopback DB containers were tmpfs-backed and removed. G7 remains open; resolve existing conflicts with Engine stopped before rollout.

**G7 Engine-owned marketplace package validation (2026-10-15):** Added authenticated `POST /api/management/v2/packages/validate`, which applies Engine's existing bounded parser to a maximum 3 MiB request with at most 256 files and rejects responses requiring more than the v2 limit of 100 findings. The nested typed Go management client exposes `ValidatePack`; Cloud's marketplace release store now uses that client for module/integration draft validation, validation-state transitions, and resolved compositions. Cloud fails closed when Engine validation is unavailable and no longer imports Engine `site` for pack parsing. Existing manifest/file/validation DTOs are reused, so no schema, persisted format, or generated wire artifact changed; the target spec and Cloud boundary docs record the new route and bounds. Changed Engine `api/management_inspect.go` and tests, `sdk/management/client.go` and tests; Cloud `cloud/marketplace_release.go` and tests, marketplace fixture test, `internal/platform/dependencies.go`, `internal/app/marketplace_release_test.go`, and `internal/platform/kora_site_test.go`; and target/boundary docs plus route/import inventories. The alias-enforcement recheck caught two legacy Cloud SQL tests that did not seed the alias-index readiness row; added the required readiness expectation. Passed Engine API race tests for management validation/inspection, nested SDK management-client race tests, Cloud `go test -race -p 1 ./cloud ./internal/app ./internal/platform -count=1`, matching scoped Cloud vet, Engine API vet, `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts` (complete graph at 534 internal edges), and Engine/Cloud `git diff --check`. Full Cloud unit tests and full Cloud vet also passed after the source migration. An initial Cloud compile caught one remaining `site.ValidatePack` in composition resolution; it now uses the management client. G7 remains in progress: Engine provisioning and package-application executors, trusted allocation/artifact resolution for execution, interruption recovery, and Cloud migration off direct tenant DB/config imports are still required; this validation route does not advertise package application.

**G7 provisioning consumes Engine validation result (2026-10-15):** Removed the redundant Cloud `site.ValidatePack` call from provisioning template verification. The marketplace resolver now has an explicit contract to return packages only after Engine's authenticated validator accepts them; the provisioning manager still recomputes and verifies the immutable bundle digest before execution. Legacy CMS templates without marketplace manifests retain their prior compatibility path. This removes Cloud's direct `site` import from `internal/provisioning/manager.go`; package application still directly opens tenant databases through `template_applier.go` and remains a separate, uncompleted migration. Passed Cloud `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/provisioning ./internal/platform ./cloud -count=1`, matching scoped vet, regenerated and checked the complete cross-repository import graph (533 internal edges), `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and Engine/Cloud diff checks. No API or persistence schema change. G7 remains in progress; the Cloud template applier, provisioning lifecycle, trusted allocation/artifact execution resolvers, and durable Engine provision/apply executors remain open.

**G7 Cloud deletion workflow SQL recovery test (2026-10-15):** Added integration-tagged `TestMySQLDeletionWorkflowRecoversAcrossRepositoryRestart`. On a fresh tmpfs-backed MySQL 8.4.11 database it persists the workflow before side effects, records revocation progress, advances to draining with tenant-bound checkpoint evidence, reconstructs a repository after the simulated restart, resumes under the same operation ID, rejects a stale phase transition, and completes with durable evidence intact. Ran `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./internal/controlplane` and the live test under `-race -tags=integration -p 1`; both passed. The live command used the disposable `KORA_CLOUD_TEST_MYSQL_*` environment and a fresh database created/dropped by the existing test helper. The MySQL container was removed after the run. No production data, public contract, or DB schema changed. G7 remains open: this proves Cloud journal recovery only; a configured Engine checkpoint verifier and end-to-end provider→Engine delete operation remain unimplemented/unconfigured, as do provisioning/apply executors and Cloud removal of direct tenant DB import paths.

**G7 management package-validation response bound (2026-10-15):** Hardened the read-only Engine validator response because a 3 MiB request can include long invalid file paths whose echoed findings would exceed the supported client's 1 MiB response limit. Engine now rejects more than 100 findings and truncates finding code/path/message to 80/256/512 UTF-8 bytes, bounding the successful response independently of attacker-controlled path length. Added a multibyte-path regression that confirms response size and UTF-8 validity. Passed Engine API race test and vet, `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and both repository diff checks. The executable spec records the response bounds; no public type/schema changed. G7 remains in progress.

This compiles the registered integration tests but does not execute the full database matrix. No schema or wire changes.

**G7 removed unused Cloud Engine script dependency (2026-10-15):** Removed `Dependencies.EmbeddedScripts`, an unused field typed as Engine `script.Runner`, and its implementation-package import. The field had no readers or writers in Cloud source, so this narrows the boundary without changing runtime behavior or a public contract. Regenerated the complete import graph (532 internal edges); production import recheck now finds Engine `site`, `db`, and `doctype` but no `script` or `natsprovider` imports in Cloud. Passed Cloud `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-cloud-gocache GOTMPDIR=/tmp TMPDIR=/tmp go test -race -p 1 ./internal/platform ./internal/app -count=1`, full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 1 ./...`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, graph `--check`, and Engine/Cloud `git diff --check`. G7 remains in progress: Cloud still has gated legacy lifecycle and platform-DB wiring, and its template applier still opens tenant databases directly; Engine provision/apply execution and resolvers are not composed.

**G7 operation recovery audit (2026-10-15):** Current source confirms `management/sqlstore.ClaimDue` reclaims only `requested`/expired `running` operations; `recovery_required` is terminal, and the authenticated operation projection exposes only state/error, with no reconciliation action. A generic retry would be unsafe because operation kinds can include external effects, so it must not be enabled until recovery is command-specific and its proof/evidence is durable. This is an implementation gate for Cloud's interrupted operation recovery, not a completed capability. No code or contract changed during this inspection. Next step: implement audited, kind-scoped reconciliation/retry semantics (starting with revision-fenced tenant state, whose directory write is idempotent), then test the same operation identity through recovery and restart before wiring other command kinds.

**G7 audited tenant-state operation reconciliation (2026-10-15):** Added v2 `ReconcileTenantStateOperation` and authenticated `POST /api/management/v2/operations/{id}/reconcile-tenant-state`. It accepts only a caller-owned `set_tenant_state` operation in `recovery_required`, preserves its operation ID/command, requires a bounded reason and idempotency key, appends an audit row in `_kora_managed_operation_recovery`, atomically returns the operation to `requested`, clears the previous uncertain outcome, and increments the worker fence. Same-key/same-content retries return the same operation; different content conflicts. The route is advertised only with the existing state mutation grant and operation-read capability. No provisioning, package or deletion operation can use it. Engine's typed Go management SDK exposes the method, and the v2 schema/runtime-validator bundle is synchronized to the embedded UI, Studio and TypeScript SDK. The target spec documents the explicit supported kind and exclusions. Changed Engine `contract/management.go`, tests and generated v2 schema; `api/management_operations.go` and tests; `management/operation.go` and tests; `management/sqlstore/{schema.go,recovery.go,store.go,store_test.go,recovery_live_test.go,store_mysql_integration_test.go}`; `internal/engine/management_runtime.go`; nested `sdk/management/client.go` and tests; route inventory; and target spec. Cloud boundary docs now document this limited retry path. Database schema adds only the separate recovery-audit table; other public consumers receive the new v2 DTO.

**G7 reconciliation verification:** Passed full Engine `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; focused race `go test -race -p 1 ./contract ./management ./management/sqlstore ./api ./internal/engine -count=1`; `go vet -p 1 ./...`; repository-wide integration-tag compile `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./...`; `go build -o /dev/null .`; `make check-contracts`; `make check-cross-repo-contracts`; route inventory generation/check; and Engine diff check. Exact focused database tests were `KORA_TEST_DSN='[REDACTED]' go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./management/sqlstore -run '^TestMySQLManagementAcceptanceDurabilityAndIdempotency$' -count=1 -timeout=180s`, `KORA_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN='[REDACTED]' go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./management/sqlstore -run '^TestLivePostgresTenantStateOperationRecovery$' -count=1 -timeout=180s`, and `KORA_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./management/sqlstore -run '^TestLiveLibSQLTenantStateOperationRecovery$' -count=1 -timeout=180s`; all passed and all three containers were removed. Cloud full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1` and `go vet -p 1 ./...` passed. Nested SDK management race tests passed. SDK `bun run check:contracts`, `bun run test` (25), and `bun run build` passed; standalone UI contract check, tests (204), and build passed with the existing >500 kB chunk advisory; embedded UI contract check and tests (166) passed. A first test compile caught a redeclared local; the rerun passed. **G7 Engine base provisioning operation (2026-10-15):** Added an Engine-owned provisioning executor that is composed only when `KORA_ENGINE_MANAGEMENT_DATABASE_ALLOCATION_REF` is explicitly configured. It accepts only that exact opaque allocation reference and the configured deployment ID, uses Engine's own database settings, creates the canonical tenant ID and passwordless bootstrap administrator, admits work once for directory/create work, then activates the tenant runtime after releasing that permit. A replay that finds an active tenant verifies canonical ID, aliases and Engine-derived allocation identity before reactivating the runtime without repeating bootstrap writes. Mismatched commands fail before effects; ambiguous create/activation errors remain `recovery_required`. The provision route rejects `package_digest` before accepting an operation while package activation is disabled. Updated Engine config/CLI composition, operation dispatcher, API route adapter/tests, self-host instructions and target spec. No wire or persisted schema change. Exact verification passed: full Engine `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; Engine race `go test -race -p 1 ./api ./internal/engine -count=1`; scoped `go vet -p 1 ./api ./internal/engine`; `make check-contracts`; `make check-cross-repo-contracts` (532 import edges); and `git diff --check`. The live Engine provisioning/replay tests are now recorded in the following recovery entry. Cloud still provisions through its legacy direct Engine path; package artifact resolution/transaction-safe activation, Cloud operation migration, and release/deployment evidence remain open, so G7 is still in progress. Next concrete action: implement package artifact resolution and activation before migrating Cloud provisioning/template consumers.

**G7 package-free provisioning recovery (2026-10-15):** Added `ReconcileTenantProvisionOperation` and `POST /api/management/v2/operations/{id}/reconcile-tenant-provision`, separate from the tenant-state reconciliation route. SQL recovery requires the same caller-owned `provision_tenant` operation in `recovery_required`, validates the stored command/tenant identity, rejects any package digest, and atomically appends the existing recovery audit record, clears the outcome, requeues the same operation ID and advances the lease fence. The Engine rechecks configured deployment/allocation and stable tenant identity before replay; only a package-free base provision can use this retry. Added the typed Go SDK method and synchronized the v2 schema/runtime validator to Engine embedded UI, Studio and TypeScript SDK. The executable specification and Cloud boundary docs now distinguish this route from state recovery and exclude package/deletion effects. Provisioning capability is restricted to one explicitly configured allocation and matching MySQL/PostgreSQL platform/tenant dialects; LibSQL provisioning stays disabled until a trusted provider can create separate tenant databases. Changed Engine `contract/management.go`, `api/management_operations.go`, `management/operation.go`, `management/sqlstore/recovery.go`, Engine management runtime/config/CLI composition and tests; nested `sdk/management`; generated v2 schema/manifests in Engine UI, standalone UI and TS SDK; Engine target/self-host docs and Cloud boundary docs. Public v2 DTO/schema and route inventory changed; the existing audit table is reused, with no new persisted schema. Final tests passed: Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, `go test -race -p 1 ./contract ./management ./management/sqlstore ./api ./internal/engine -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./...`, `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./...`, and `go build -o /dev/null .`; nested SDK `go test -race -p 1 ./management -count=1`; full Cloud `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1` and `go vet -p 1 ./...`; Engine `make check-contracts` and `make check-cross-repo-contracts` (532 import edges, 11 fixture artifacts); embedded UI contract check, tests (166) and build; Studio contract check, tests (204) and build; and TS SDK contract check, tests (25) and build. Build warnings: embedded UI reports an existing ineffective dynamic import; Studio reports an existing chunk above 500 kB. Live tests passed under race using disposable tmpfs containers: `go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./management/sqlstore -run '^TestLive(MySQL|Postgres|LibSQL)TenantProvisionOperationRecovery$' -count=1 -timeout=180s` against MySQL 8.4.11, PostgreSQL 16 and LibSQL 0.24.33 HTTP; and `go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/engine -run '^TestLiveManagementProvisionReplay$' -count=1 -timeout=180s` against MySQL and PostgreSQL. Containers were stopped and removed. The first MySQL attempt omitted the selected platform database and bootstrap could not identify `DATABASE()`; the rerun selected a newly created DB inside the disposable container and passed. The three-dialect tests prove audited recovery-store behavior; the full provision executor create/replay test covers MySQL/PostgreSQL only. G7 remains in progress: Cloud still uses the legacy create/template path, package activation/artifact resolution and delete recovery are unimplemented, and no Cloud-to-composed-Engine live test or release gate has passed. Next: package activation with allowlisted artifact resolution and transactional config/runtime reconciliation, then Cloud cutover tests before retiring direct tenant DB paths.

**G7 package parsing boundary (2026-10-15):** Extracted `site.ParseConfigPack`, which validates and parses bounded pack files into a `doctype.ConfigSnapshot` plus script bodies without tenant database writes or process-local registry mutation. The existing `ImportConfigFromPack` API remains compatible and now consumes this pure parser. The new tests exposed and fixed an existing inconsistency where the importer advertised `script` content but rejected `.js` before reaching that case. The parser now permits `.js` only for script files and keeps YAML extensions for other content types, with existing path and size checks preserved. Updated the executable spec to require pure parsing before the durable activation path and documented the extension split. No public wire or persisted schema change. Passed exact checks: `go test ./site -count=1`; `go test ./contract ./management ./management/sqlstore ./api ./internal/engine -count=1`; and `go test -race -p 1 ./site ./api ./internal/engine -count=1`. G7 remains in progress: immutable artifact resolution, transaction-safe version/journal/DDL activation, runtime reconciliation, Cloud migration from direct DB/template import, deletion recovery/provider composition and release gates remain open. Next: design the executor around Engine's existing activation journal and tenant runtime replacement semantics before enabling package capability.

**G7 parser follow-up verification:** After the package parser change, `go vet ./site ./api ./internal/engine`, `git diff --check`, Cloud `go test -p 1 ./internal/platform ./internal/provisioning ./internal/app -count=1`, and Cloud `go vet ./internal/platform ./internal/provisioning ./internal/app` all passed. No disposable service was needed and no production data or deployment was touched.

**G7 Cloud template compatibility gate (2026-10-15):** Cloud now wires `KoraTemplateApplier` only when `KORA_CLOUD_ALLOW_LEGACY_ENGINE_LIFECYCLE=true`; the applier independently rejects direct tenant DB imports when that flag is absent and respects caller cancellation. `StartProvisioning` now rejects a selected template before persistence or tenant creation if no application provider is configured, instead of recording `apply_template_config` as successful by omission. Added regressions for both gates and gave the existing package-provisioning happy-path test an explicit fake applier. Updated the Cloud boundary and target specification. No contract/schema changes. Passed Cloud `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/platform ./internal/provisioning -count=1`, scoped vet, full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 1 ./...`, Engine `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts` (11 cross-repo artifacts; import graph current), and `git diff --check`. **Superseded G7 provisioning migration dependency finding (originally 2026-10-15):** The original finding correctly captured then-missing management settings, stable-tenant wiring, and the Cloud `Provision` call, but its claim that shared runtime deployment required tenant DB coordinates was incorrect: `SharedCloudManager` uses the stable Engine tenant route and ignores per-tenant database settings. Subsequent Engine provisioning/package executors, Cloud stable-tenant commands, and the G9 ingress/runtime route have since been implemented. The correction and a real shared-runtime Cloud→Engine saga verification are recorded in the 2026-10-16 G7 entry below. Do not treat this historical finding as an open dependency; only the listed current G7 compatibility, release, and trusted checkpoint-provider gates remain.

**G7 audited tenant-deletion recovery (2026-10-15):** Added `ReconcileTenantDeleteOperation` and `POST /api/management/v2/operations/{id}/reconcile-tenant-delete`, enabled only when the Engine delete executor is composed with a trusted checkpoint verifier. The service and API use the verified management caller. SQL recovery requires that caller's same `delete_tenant` operation to be `recovery_required`, validates its stored command and tenant identity, persists an idempotent recovery audit, requeues the same operation ID and advances the lease fence atomically. The Engine executor re-verifies checkpoint identity, immutability, completeness, restorability, and current allocation on every attempt. The Go management SDK exposes the typed method. Cloud now attempts one durable reconciliation per deletion lifecycle call, keeps the checkpoint reference and operation ID, retries transient submission with the same reconciliation key, and leaves a second uncertain result visible. Updated Engine executable route spec and Cloud boundary docs; synchronized Engine schema/runtime validator/fixture manifests to embedded UI, standalone UI, and `@kora/extension-sdk`. No new persisted schema; the existing management recovery audit table is reused. Changed Engine `contract/management.go`, `management/{operation.go,operation_test.go,sqlstore/recovery.go,sqlstore/store_test.go}`, `api/management_operations{,_test}.go`, `internal/engine/{management_runtime.go,management_delete_executor_test.go}`, nested `sdk/management/{client.go,client_test.go}`, generated consumer contracts; Cloud `internal/platform/{kora_site.go,kora_site_management_test.go}` and `docs/cloud-boundary.md`; Engine `docs/architecture/target-state-spec.md`. Cloud has no production checkpoint provider configured, so Engine keeps delete/recovery routes unavailable by default. No live SQL-backed deletion-recovery integration or composed Cloud-to-Engine deployment test ran in this slice. G7 remains in progress: package activation/artifact resolution, production provider composition, Cloud provisioning migration and legacy direct DB/template retirement, and deployment/release gates remain open. Next: implement package activation from allowlisted immutable artifacts through Engine's config activation journal and runtime reconciliation; keep G9 ingress work as a required dependency before Cloud can stop receiving tenant database coordinates.

**G7 Cloud marketplace artifact integrity (2026-10-15):** Made immutable artifact writes retry-safe when the same bytes already exist and reject content collisions; canonicalized file ordering before serialization/composition; and made database-backed release reads revalidate manifest and artifact digests rather than trusting a process cache. Metadata persistence failure now retains the immutable artifact so a retry or existing release cannot lose it; safe orphan collection must verify no release row references the key. Added regressions for byte collisions, ordering stability, stored artifact mutation, and metadata-failure retention. Updated Cloud boundary documentation. No API/schema change. Cloud full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, race tests `go test -race -p 1 ./cloud ./internal/app ./internal/platform ./internal/provisioning -count=1`, full `go vet -p 1 ./...`, focused marketplace race tests, and `git diff --check` passed after the final changes.
