Engine package parsing is already pure (`site.ParseConfigPack`) and per-tenant config activation already has a durable journal (`configstore.ActivationJournal`), but no management executor composes them yet. Package activation, Engine-owned artifact resolution, and Cloud provisioning cutover remain open. Next: design an executor that resolves only allowlisted immutable bytes, parses before writes, fences the tenant's expected config revision, then uses the existing recovery journal and tenant generation swap.

**G7 package-activation fence recheck (2026-10-15):** Source review shows `ManagementInspectHandler.writeDescriptor` currently fills contract `ConfigRevision` from the platform `_kora_site_registry.config_revision`; `site/directory_registry.go` increments that value for lifecycle and directory mutations, while config-version activation in `api/system.go` commits tenant config without advancing the platform revision. Thus the existing `ApplyPackage.ExpectedConfigRevision` value cannot yet prove that a package activation is based on the current tenant configuration, and a config change between inspection and package execution would not be fenced by the existing directory revision. Also confirmed `internal/engine/management_runtime.go` does not compose an apply-package executor or advertise `tenant.package.apply`, so the route is currently absent by default. Keep that capability disabled. Before enabling package application, add a distinct monotonic tenant configuration revision with a compatibility-safe contract representation, update it through all config activation paths and the durable package recovery journal, and test stale concurrent activation. This is a boundary correction required by the target's separate config/runtime/directory/fencing revisions; do not reinterpret the existing directory CAS field or weaken the fence. No code or contract changed in this review slice.

**G7 tenant configuration revision fencing (2026-10-15):** Added a tenant-local monotonic `_kora_config_revision` with idempotent bootstrap seeding and transaction-bound increments across snapshot activation, document types, workflows, analytics configuration, views, scripts, and workflow/doc-type deletion paths. The management descriptor now exposes additive `tenant_config_revision` separately from the existing platform lifecycle/directory `config_revision`; the management route resolves it from the active tenant runtime and fails closed if unavailable. The target spec now documents this distinction and requires Cloud to use the tenant-local value before package activation. This closes the stale-revision source-data gap; package apply remains unadvertised because no safe Engine executor is composed yet.

Changed Engine files include `configstore/config_revision.go`, config store and script/view mutation paths, `site/bootstrap.go`, management descriptor/composition, system deletion transactions, and corresponding tests. Generated contract copies were synchronized for Engine embedded UI, standalone UI and SDK. No existing package names or persisted identifier prefixes changed.

**G7 tenant activation claim fence (2026-10-15):** Added the tenant-local `_kora_management_activation_fence` table and immutable operation identity/high-water claim recording. `Store.ActivateSnapshotAtManagementClaim` checks the current fence inside the same tenant transaction as the config revision CAS and config writes, so stale leases cannot commit activation after a takeover. Added SQLMock coverage and a real disposable-database test that bootstraps system tables, claims fence 4 then 5, proves fence 4 is rejected without advancing config revision, and proves fence 5 commits the revision. Verified by integration-tag compile for `./configstore ./site` and race-enabled live integration `go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./configstore -run '^TestLiveManagementActivationFenceAcrossDialects$' -count=1 -timeout=240s` against MySQL 8.0, PostgreSQL 16, and LibSQL sqld 0.24.33 HTTP; all three passed. The first run correctly refused the reused LibSQL test service because it retained prior disposable test state; reran against a fresh tmpfs-backed sqld instance. **G7 atomic management activation journal advance (2026-10-15):** Added `Store.ActivateSnapshotAtManagementClaimWithJournal`, which validates the current tenant-local management fence, performs config writes and config/auth revision CAS, and advances the existing activation journal from its expected phase to `activated` in one tenant transaction. The executor still must own DDL planning, config-version/history persistence, post-commit runtime replacement, and recovery before this helper is used to advertise package application. The live takeover test now persists one immutable accepted snapshot and journal, proves a stale fence cannot change either the config revision or journal phase, and proves the current fence commits both together. **G7 shared recoverable DDL planner (2026-10-15):** Extracted the existing full-snapshot DDL planning from the config-version HTTP handler into `schema.PlanSnapshotActivation` and pure `GenerateSnapshotActivationDDL`. The compatibility route now uses the shared planner, which diffs against the live schema, retains explicit historical field-type changes, and quarantines only field removals whose source columns still exist. This is the DDL-plan seam required by the management executor; it does not execute DDL or itself provide recovery. Added tests for missing-table creation and revision-scoped removal/type-change plans. Exact checks passed: `go test -p 1 ./schema ./api -run 'TestGenerateSnapshotActivationDDL|TestLivePostgresConfigVersionRoutes' -count=1`; live race integration `KORA_CONFIG_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='<loopback disposable MySQL DSN>' go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./api -run '^TestMySQLConfigActivationJournalRetry$' -count=1 -timeout=180s`; live race integration `KORA_API_LIVE_POSTGRES_DSN='<loopback disposable PostgreSQL DSN>' go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./api -run '^TestLivePostgresConfigVersionRoutes$' -count=1 -timeout=180s`; full Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; scoped race and vet `./schema ./api ./internal/engine`; scoped integration-tag compile for those packages; and `git diff --check`. MySQL activation-journal retry and PostgreSQL config-version routes both passed using disposable tmpfs-backed MySQL 8.0 and PostgreSQL 16; containers were removed. **G7 shared journal-compatible DDL application (2026-10-15):** Added `schema.ApplySnapshotActivationDDL` and routed the existing API activation path through it. MySQL/PostgreSQL execute statements sequentially with request-context cancellation between statements; LibSQL uses its actual dialect batch transport and existing idempotent fallback after a partial/already-applied batch. The activation handler still records the planned statement list before application and replans from live schema on a retry. Added sqlmock tests for ordering, stop-on-error, cancellation-before-write, and LibSQL batch invocation, plus an integration test over real LibSQL HTTP that executes and repeats a multi-statement DDL plan idempotently. Live race checks passed: MySQL `TestMySQLConfigActivationJournalRetry`, PostgreSQL `TestLivePostgresConfigVersionRoutes`, and LibSQL `TestApplySnapshotActivationDDLUsesLibSQLHTTPBatch` against disposable tmpfs-backed MySQL 8.0, PostgreSQL 16, and sqld 0.24.33. The first LibSQL test expectation incorrectly assumed repeated CREATE should fail; the actual supported dialect correctly fell back and recovered idempotently, and the corrected test passed. Also passed Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, race `go test -race -p 1 ./schema ./api ./internal/engine -count=1`, vet for the same packages, integration-tag compile for `./schema ./api ./internal/engine`, and `git diff --check`. Test containers were removed. Changed Engine `schema/config_activation.go`, `schema/config_activation_test.go`, new `schema/config_activation_libsql_integration_test.go`, `api/system.go`, and this ledger. No public contract or persisted schema changed. This supplies common planning/application primitives but not the management package executor, claim-bound DDL journal transitions, config-version/history transaction, post-commit runtime replacement, or package-operation reconciliation; package capability remains disabled. Next: implement those orchestration steps in Engine and test recovery after a deliberately interrupted DDL plan.

**G7 atomic management config-version commit (2026-10-15):** Added `configstore.Store.CommitManagementActivation`. After DDL is journaled and validated, it checks the tenant claim fence, applies the complete config snapshot and config/auth revision CAS, inserts a new Active config version while superseding the previous Active version, and advances the activation journal to `activated` inside the caller-owned transaction. Added a rollback-path SQLMock test showing a config-version history insertion failure returns an error before journal advancement and requires transaction rollback. Expanded the live takeover test across MySQL 8.0, PostgreSQL 16, and LibSQL HTTP to prove a stale claim changes neither config revision, active version nor journal, while the current claim atomically commits a version and activated phase. **G7 verified package script persistence fix (2026-10-15):** Rechecked package parsing against config activation and found that `ParseConfigPack` returns script bodies separately while `SaveScriptsTx` consumes `ScriptSnapshot.Source`. The management artifact verifier now copies each body into its matching verified-hash snapshot before it can be journaled, and rejects a snapshot script with no non-empty verified body. Added a regression proving the returned snapshot and hash map carry the same package script source. Checks passed: `gofmt -w internal/engine/management_package_artifact.go internal/engine/management_package_artifact_test.go`; `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./internal/engine ./configstore ./schema -count=1`; and `git diff --check`. This fixes a persistence mismatch but does not compose package execution or recovery; package application remains disabled. Next: implement the fenced executor/reconciliation flow with additive snapshot merge and runtime replacement tests.

**G7 durable worker claim propagation (2026-10-15):** Changed the management `OperationExecutor` boundary to receive the full validated `Claim` (operation, owner, fence, lease) after the worker's initial renewal. Engine state, provision, delete, and dispatcher executors now reject missing owner/fence data and consume the claim's operation identity. Added a worker race test proving the executor receives the validated owner/fence/lease fields. Verified: `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./management ./management/sqlstore ./internal/engine -count=1`; same scoped `go vet`; and `git diff --check` (passed). Changed Engine files: `management/worker.go`, `management/worker_test.go`, `internal/engine/management_{state,provision,delete}_executor.go` and corresponding executor tests. This makes fencing identity available at the effect boundary but does not itself atomically fence writes across the management platform store and tenant databases; package activation must persist/check a tenant-side operation fence and config revision in its transaction. No wire or database schema change. Management executors are internal and there is no released external SDK contract for this Go interface. Next: finish the tenant activation transaction/journal so a stale lease cannot commit package config, then wire an allowlisted resolver and test claim takeover/recovery against real supported databases.

**G7 transaction-bound tenant config compare-and-set (2026-10-15):** Added `BumpConfigRevisionAtTx` and `ActivateSnapshotAtConfigRevision`. The expected tenant-local revision is checked with one conditional SQL update in the same transaction as the policy epoch and configuration writes; stale revisions return `ErrConfigRevisionConflict` and the caller rolls the transaction back. Existing `ActivateSnapshot` preserves its prior behavior and still advances once. Added sqlmock coverage for current/stale CAS and rollback. Extended real API activation tests for MySQL and PostgreSQL and added a fresh-database LibSQL HTTP integration test; all prove current activation commits, stale activation conflicts, revision remains unchanged by the stale attempt, and its role row is not persisted. Passed disposable MySQL 8.0 `TestMySQLConfigActivationJournalRetry`, PostgreSQL 16 `TestLivePostgresConfigVersionRoutes`, and actual sqld 0.24.33 HTTP `TestLibSQLPackageActivationRevisionCAS`; temporary loopback containers used tmpfs and were removed. Full Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full race `go test -race -p 1 ./... -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./...`, integration-tag compile `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./...`, and `git diff --check` passed. Changed files: `configstore/config_revision.go`, `configstore/auth_revision.go`, `configstore/store.go`, config revision/store tests, `api/config_activation_mysql_integration_test.go`, `api/config_versions_postgres_integration_test.go`, and `api/config_revision_libsql_integration_test.go`. No public wire or persisted schema change. This proves the tenant revision CAS, not the complete durable package activation: package snapshot merging, DDL planning/recovery, operation-fence binding to the activation journal, immutable version/history writes, generation replacement, and Cloud artifact resolver/cutover remain open; `tenant.package.apply` stays disabled.

**G7 package-operation reconciliation contract (2026-10-15):** Added `ReconcileTenantPackageOperation`, a separate caller-scoped Engine route and SDK method, and SQL-store recovery that accepts only the same caller's `apply_package` operation in `recovery_required`. It validates the stored command and tenant identity, writes an idempotent audit record, preserves command and operation ID, clears the old outcome, and increments the worker lease fence in one transaction. Added SQL tests for allowed recovery, rejection of other kinds, same-key replay and changed-reason idempotency conflict, plus API caller/capability tests. Package reconciliation can be advertised only alongside package apply; both remain disabled until the executor and journal resume path are composed. Updated Engine contract schema and synchronized generated Engine/UI/SDK artifacts. The specification now documents why this must be a distinct path: retries must verify the original digest and resume through the tenant activation journal, not use base provisioning replay. Checks passed: full affected package tests `go test -p 1 ./contract ./management ./management/sqlstore ./api ./internal/engine -count=1`; nested Go SDK `cd sdk && go test -p 1 ./management -count=1`; scoped race tests for affected Engine packages and nested SDK `go test -race -p 1 ./contract ./management ./management/sqlstore ./api ./internal/engine -count=1` and `cd sdk && go test -race -p 1 ./management -count=1`; scoped `go vet -p 1 ./contract ./management ./management/sqlstore ./api ./internal/engine`; `make check-contracts`; `make check-cross-repo-contracts`; and `git diff --check`. Changed Engine contract/API/management SQL-store and runtime adapter, nested SDK client/tests, generated contract consumer artifacts, target spec, and this ledger. No database schema change; the existing recovery audit table and operation table are reused. G7 remains in progress: no package executor, tenant-side recovery orchestration, Cloud cutover, or package capability has been enabled.

**G7 package recovery cross-dialect and consumer verification (2026-10-15):** Added integration-tag tests for durable package-operation reconciliation and ran them with race detection against fresh tmpfs-backed MySQL 8.0, PostgreSQL 16, and LibSQL sqld HTTP containers; all three passed, including restart replay, one audit record and advanced claim fencing. Containers were removed after the run. Cloud compatibility passed full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1` and `go vet -p 1 ./...`. Standalone UI passed its configured `bun run test` (Vitest: 48 files/204 tests) and `bun run build`; TypeScript SDK passed `bun test` (25 tests) and `bun run build`. Both consumer contract checks passed. A separate plain `bun test` attempt in the Studio repository invoked Bun's built-in runner instead of the configured Vitest script, producing runner-API failures and a Bun crash; the authoritative configured `bun run test` passed. `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, `git diff --check`, and the no-leftover-container check passed. This verifies the new wire/API path across existing consumers; Cloud still has no package operation migration or recovery orchestration and package apply remains disabled.

**Latest G7 package activation boundary (2026-10-15):** Added the authenticated Cloud immutable artifact endpoint at `/internal/engine/marketplace/releases/{releaseID}` and the Engine HTTPS-only (loopback exception for tests) resolver, wired through CLI configuration. Cloud serves only digest-matching Published/Deprecated releases, disables caching, authenticates with a dedicated constant-time bearer check, and caps both pack size and encoded response size. Engine bounds response bytes, rejects redirects, validates the exact release ID/digest and strict JSON shape, then passes the artifact through existing digest and Engine pack validation before activation. Config is opt-in: Engine sets `KORA_ENGINE_MANAGEMENT_ARTIFACT_URL` plus `KORA_ENGINE_MANAGEMENT_ARTIFACT_TOKEN`; Cloud package management requires `KORA_CLOUD_ENGINE_MANAGEMENT_PACKAGES=true` and matching `KORA_CLOUD_ENGINE_ARTIFACT_TOKEN`. These credentials are separate from the Cloud→Engine management bearer and managed-channel credentials. Updated Engine self-host deployment docs and Cloud boundary/go-live/operator docs.

The package executor is now composed only when that trusted resolver is configured. Its live test exercised actual tenant bootstrap, config journal/version commit, runtime replacement failure and recovery without duplicate effects across fresh tmpfs-backed MySQL 8.4.11, PostgreSQL 16, and LibSQL sqld HTTP services. Exact verification: Engine focused unit/race `go test -p 1 ./internal/engine ./cli -count=1` and `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/engine ./cli -count=1`; Engine `go vet -p 1 ./internal/engine ./cli`; `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./internal/engine`; live race `KORA_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' KORA_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN='[REDACTED]' KORA_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/engine -run '^TestLiveManagementPackageExecutorAcrossDialects$' -count=1 -timeout=240s`; Engine full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; Cloud focused unit/race for app/config/store; Cloud full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; scoped `go vet`; `make check-cross-repo-contracts` (11 artifacts, route inventory and complete import graph current); and `git diff --check`. The three disposable DB containers were removed; existing services were untouched. A first route-level Cloud test attempt panicked because its App fixture lacked the normal logging dependency; after supplying a discard logger, unit/race tests passed. This verifies the executor and each HTTP boundary in isolation, not one end-to-end Cloud lifecycle → artifact fetch → Engine worker/runtime harness. G7 remains in progress: package activation's post-commit uncertain outcomes and restart/takeover need broader fault-injection tests; Cloud still uses direct tenant database/template/import paths and provisioning compatibility paths; deletion checkpoint provider, production settings, cross-service runtime composition, and consumer/deployment release gates remain open. `tenant.package.apply` is advertised only when the operator explicitly configures the artifact resolver, but no release-readiness claim is made until those remaining gates pass.

**G7 package activation after interrupted DDL (2026-10-15):** Found that LibSQL activation re-planning inspected tables/columns/indexes but not the `_modified_on_update` trigger. A crash after the table statement could therefore leave a re-planned activation without its required trigger. Updated `schema.PlanSnapshotActivation` to query LibSQL `sqlite_master` for missing triggers and append an idempotent `CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS`; documented this dialect-specific recovery requirement in `target-state-spec.md`. Extended the package executor live test to persist an `applying` journal, run only the first DDL statement, then instantiate and run the executor from durable state. The retry now completes the remaining schema and config effects; the LibSQL assertion confirms the trigger exists. This is the smallest correction to the target's live-schema replan rule because SQLite table introspection alone does not establish trigger completeness.

Verification passed: `go test -p 1 ./db ./schema ./internal/engine -count=1`; full Engine `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; `go test -race -p 1 ./db ./schema ./internal/engine -count=1`; live integration race `KORA_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN=[REDACTED] loopback MySQL 8.4.11> KORA_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN=[REDACTED] loopback PostgreSQL 16> KORA_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN=[REDACTED] loopback LibSQL HTTP> go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/engine -run '^TestLiveManagementPackageExecutorAcrossDialects$' -count=1 -timeout=240s`; scoped `go vet -p 1 ./db ./schema ./internal/engine`; integration-tag compile for `./schema ./internal/engine`; Cloud `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/app ./internal/config ./cloud -count=1`; `make check-cross-repo-contracts`; and `git diff --check`. The LibSQL HTTP service used image digest `ghcr.io/tursodatabase/libsql-server@sha256:[REDACTED_HASH_OR_TOKEN]`; all three containers were tmpfs-backed, loopback-only, and removed. An initial test assertion used an incorrect table prefix and failed; a retry against the same fresh LibSQL service then correctly refused its now-nonempty test database. After recreating only that disposable service, the corrected cross-dialect test passed. G7 remains in progress for a full Cloud→artifact→Engine worker/runtime composition, package operation takeover/restart through the HTTP reconciliation route, Cloud's remaining direct tenant-database/template paths, deletion verifier provider, and production compatibility/release gates.

**G7 Cloud provisioning capability preflight (2026-10-15):** Cloud intentionally supports a management-only shared runtime for inspection and existing Cloud operations, so a management URL without provisioning must not fail startup. However, `StartProvisioning` previously persisted a Cloud site/job before `KoraSiteLifecycle.Create` rejected the absence of both typed Engine provisioning and the explicit legacy bridge. Added the optional `SiteProvisioningCapability` check before template resolution or any Cloud provisioning write. `KoraSiteLifecycle.ProvisioningAvailable` validates the selected managed/legacy path, and `Create` applies the same guard when invoked directly. A regression proves unsupported tenant creation returns before writing sites, jobs, or leads; platform tests cover disabled, explicit-legacy, and missing typed-client cases. Documented this behavior in the Cloud README, go-live guide, and boundary guide. No public contract or schema changed. Passed Cloud full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 1 ./...`, scoped race `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/config ./internal/platform ./internal/provisioning -count=1`, and `git diff --check`. G7 remains in progress: Cloud's legacy tenant database/template/import paths, combined Cloud-to-Engine worker/runtime composition, deletion checkpoint provider, and release gates remain open.

**G7 HTTP artifact fetch through live Engine package activation (2026-10-15):** Replaced the static artifact resolver in `TestLiveManagementPackageExecutorAcrossDialects` with the actual Engine `HTTPManagementPackageArtifactResolver` against an authenticated loopback HTTP artifact provider implementing Cloud's internal route envelope. The test now covers release-ID path binding, accepted digest query/header/body, bearer delivery, Engine validation/parsing, interrupted DDL journal replan (including LibSQL trigger recovery), durable config commit, runtime replacement failure, and same-operation retry against fresh MySQL 8.4.11, PostgreSQL 16, and LibSQL HTTP databases. This is an Engine HTTP→executor integration; Cloud's real registered artifact route is tested separately in `internal/app`, so a single process-level Cloud+Engine composition remains open. The first run exposed an invalid fixture using `object:inventory-1` where the actual `ApplyPackage` consumer sends the immutable release ID; corrected the fixture to `inventory-1` and the full three-dialect race test passed. Exact run: `KORA_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN=[REDACTED] loopback MySQL> KORA_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN=[REDACTED] loopback PostgreSQL> KORA_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN=[REDACTED] loopback LibSQL HTTP> go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/engine -run '^TestLiveManagementPackageExecutorAcrossDialects$' -count=1 -timeout=240s`. Also passed Engine `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/engine -count=1`, integration-tag compile, normal and integration-tag scoped vet, and `git diff --check`; only the three containers created for the test were removed. G7 remains in progress for actual Cloud-route-to-Engine-resolver process composition, Cloud legacy database/template retirement, checkpoint provider configuration, invitation outcome proof, and migration/release gates.

**G7 package worker restart and service reconciliation (2026-10-15):** Extended the cross-dialect Engine package activation test to submit through `management.Service`, persist in `management/sqlstore`, and execute through the real bounded `management.Worker`. An injected runtime replacement failure now proves the queue records a recoverable uncertain result after tenant config commits once; `Service.ReconcileTenantPackage` audits and requeues the same operation; reopening the SQL store/service and starting a new worker finishes activation without repeating the config revision or active config version. The live test uses the actual authenticated HTTP artifact resolver and interrupted-DDL journal path across fresh tmpfs-backed MySQL 8.4.11, PostgreSQL 16, and LibSQL HTTP. Exact live race command passed: `KORA_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' KORA_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN='[REDACTED]' KORA_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/engine -run '^TestLiveManagementPackageExecutorAcrossDialects$' -count=1 -timeout=240s`; it passed all three dialects. Also passed `go test -p 1 ./management ./management/sqlstore ./internal/engine -count=1`, the same scoped race test, normal and integration-tag `go vet`, `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./internal/engine`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check`. The readiness harness initially used a LibSQL `/health` path that returns 404 and did not reach the test; the probe was corrected and the successful test run used new loopback-only tmpfs containers, which were removed. No contract or schema changed. The operation and tenant database share one disposable test database here, so this proves worker/service restart semantics on supported dialects but not production platform/tenant database separation or one-process Cloud-to-Engine composition. G7 remains in progress for those boundaries, Cloud's legacy tenant DB/template paths, deletion checkpoint provider configuration, invitation outcome proof, and release gates.

**G7 authenticated capability discovery and Cloud preflight (2026-10-15):** Added the v2 `ManagementCapability` enum and `ManagementCapabilitiesV2` contract plus authenticated `GET /api/management/v2/capabilities`, which returns the sorted operations enabled by the running Engine worker composition. The supported nested Go HTTP client exposes discovery through optional `CapabilitiesClient`, preserving source compatibility for existing `management.Client` implementations. Cloud now checks live Engine capabilities before persisting provisioning intent and requires provision, provision-reconcile, and operation-read. When a marketplace package template is selected, Cloud also requires package-apply and package-reconcile and asks Engine's existing pack validator to accept the immutable release before tenant creation. Added Engine route/auth/schema tests, SDK transport coverage, and Cloud regressions proving missing recovery/validation capabilities do not reach Engine provision or persist Cloud provisioning state. Updated the target spec, Cloud README, go-live and boundary guides, route inventory, and generated Engine/embedded UI/Studio/SDK v2 contract copies. No database migration. Passed Engine full `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full integration-tag compile `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./...`, scoped `go test -race -p 1 ./contract ./api -count=1`, scoped `go vet -p 1 ./contract ./api`, and `make check-contracts`; nested SDK `go test -race -p 1 ./management -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./management`, `bun run check:contracts`, `bun run test` (25 passed), and `bun run build`; Cloud full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 1 ./...`, scoped `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/platform ./internal/provisioning ./internal/config -count=1`; embedded UI contract check, 35 test files/166 tests, and production build; standalone Studio contract check, 48 test files/204 tests, and production build; `go run ./cmd/routeinventory -check`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and Engine `git diff --check`. The frontends built with existing chunk/dynamic-import advisories. Capability checks use HTTP test servers; a single Cloud process connected to a live Engine capability route and runtime is not yet composed. G7 remains in progress: Cloud's direct tenant-database and legacy template/lifecycle paths, the configured deletion checkpoint provider and Engine verifier, durable invitation delivery proof, and deployment/release compatibility gates remain open.

**G7 legacy template fallback gate (2026-10-15):** Cloud's marketplace resolver now attaches the legacy `ContentCatalog` fallback only when `KORA_CLOUD_ALLOW_LEGACY_ENGINE_LIFECYCLE` is enabled. Added a regression proving the fallback is not invoked while the flag is off and remains available under the explicit compatibility flag. No contract or schema changed. Passed Cloud `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/platform ./internal/provisioning -count=1`, full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 1 ./...`, and `git diff --check`. The direct legacy template importer and lifecycle remain in place for deployments that explicitly enable the compatibility gate; their final retirement still requires migration and release-window evidence. G7 remains in progress.

**G7 invitation outcome safety (2026-10-15):** Engine's public magic-link request now returns failure on account-lookup infrastructure errors and on mailer errors; when provider delivery is uncertain it attempts to revoke the issued single-use token and does not return it. Missing mail configuration is an error and no longer logs a raw sign-in link. `auth.RegisterAuthRoutes` now accepts the exported `MagicLinkMailer` interface, which remains source-compatible for existing `*email.Sender` callers and allows deterministic send-failure tests. The success response remains non-enumerating, so it still cannot prove whether a user existed or email was accepted. Cloud now requires a durable `delivery_status: accepted` plus stable `delivery_id` receipt before marking its welcome/invitation step successful; the existing generic public response is persisted as an unknown/failure result instead. Added Engine tests for provider failure/revocation, missing sender, database lookup failure, and non-enumerating unknown accounts, plus a Cloud provisioning regression that proves the generic 200 is not marked successful. Updated the target spec and Cloud boundary/go-live/README documentation with the required management-receipt contract. No database schema or current wire contract changed; the typed management invitation operation and durable receipt are still not implemented, so notifications remain visibly unresolved/manual. Passed Engine `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./auth -count=1`, full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 1 ./...`, integration-tag compile `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./...`, `make check-contracts`, and `make check-cross-repo-contracts`; Cloud targeted race test `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/provisioning -run '^TestProvisioningDoesNotClaimUnverifiedMagicLinkDelivery$' -count=1`, full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 1 ./...`, and `git diff --check`. The import graph was regenerated from complete module lists after the new auth test dependency (535 internal edges); drift check passes. G7 remains in progress for the typed durable invitation operation, combined Cloud/Engine runtime composition, configured deletion checkpoint provider/verifier, remaining legacy paths, and deployment/release gates.

**G7 legacy catalog composition gate (2026-10-15):** Cloud now constructs and wires its old content-catalog adapter only when `KORA_CLOUD_ALLOW_LEGACY_ENGINE_LIFECYCLE` is true. The disabled path no longer carries a dormant resolver client or its configured catalog credentials. Added a composition helper test alongside the resolver fallback tests. Passed Cloud race tests for `./internal/platform ./internal/provisioning`, full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 1 ./...`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check`. The compatibility importer remains available only behind the explicit flag until the typed replacement and release migration gates pass; G7 remains in progress.

**G7 durable tenant invitation operation (2026-10-15):** Added contract `RequestTenantInvitation` and the SMTP-backed `tenant.invitation.deliver` management capability and tenant-scoped route. The Engine executor activates and pins the tenant runtime, uses that runtime's shared work admission and context-aware administrator lookup, creates a single-use token but stores only its hash, and persists an intent journal before SMTP. It reports success only after the configured provider accepts the message and the receipt is committed. A reclaimed in-flight attempt is fenced, its token revoked, and its outcome left `unknown`/`recovery_required`; it is never resent automatically. The operation ID is the stable delivery ID. Cloud now requires the invitation capability before persisting managed-provisioning intent, submits the typed command with a stable SHA-256 idempotency key, saves the operation ID before polling, and resumes that same ID after restart. Removed the old Cloud call to the non-enumerating public auth route. The tenant journal DDL is present in MySQL/PostgreSQL/LibSQL; bumped `systemBootstrapVersion` to 23 so existing tenants receive the additive table. Generated v2 contract copies were synchronized. Changed Engine contract/API/auth/runtime/DDL plus `internal/engine/management_invitation_executor.go` and tests; Cloud typed client/lifecycle/provisioning/control-plane checkpoint and tests; Engine/Cloud contract docs and this ledger.

**G7 invitation verification (2026-10-15):** Passed full Engine `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; Engine race `go test -race -p 1 ./auth ./internal/engine ./api ./contract ./db ./management/... -count=1`; full Engine `go vet -p 1 ./...`; integration-tag compile `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./...`; nested Engine SDK `go test -race -p 1 ./management -count=1` and `go vet ./management`; Cloud full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, focused race `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/platform ./internal/provisioning ./internal/controlplane -count=1`, and full `go vet -p 1 ./...`; TS SDK `bun run check:contracts && bun run test && bun run build` (25 tests); embedded UI same pipeline (35 files/166 tests); standalone Studio same pipeline (48 files/204 tests). Engine full API route, journal transaction/reclaim, auth cancellation and all-dialect DDL unit tests passed. Fresh-tenant DDL was verified under race against disposable tmpfs MySQL 8.4.11 and PostgreSQL 16 via `TestLiveManagementProvisionRouteAndWorker`, and actual LibSQL HTTP migration via `TestBootstrapMigratesExistingLibSQLV17OutboxWithoutLosingRows`; each disposable container was removed. `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, import graph regeneration/check (535 internal edges), and Engine `git diff --check` passed. Frontend builds passed with existing Vite dynamic-import/chunk-size advisories. No live SMTP provider delivery was tested; the executor journal and provider-result branches are unit-tested. G7 remains in progress: there is no operator reconciliation command for an `unknown` invitation, no single harness composing Cloud lifecycle with the real Engine invitation executor/runtime supervisor/SMTP, and Cloud's direct tenant DB/template imports, deletion checkpoint provider, deployment migration, and release-window gates remain open.

**G7 invitation outcome reconciliation (2026-10-15):** Completed the previously missing operator recovery path for an ambiguous SMTP result. Added generated v2 `InvitationDeliveryOutcome` and `ReconcileTenantInvitationOperation` contracts, the `tenant.invitation.reconcile` capability, and `POST /api/management/v2/operations/:id/reconcile-tenant-invitation`. Engine checks the original operation, tenant journal, email, current runtime and claim fence; a verified `delivered` finding preserves the single-use token, while `not_delivered` revokes the old token and moves the receipt to retryable before the same management operation is requeued. An already-used token cannot be classified as not delivered. Same-key replay is audited/idempotent. Cloud now requires the recovery capability for managed provisioning and provides admin/support-gated `POST /api/cloud/provisioning-jobs/invitation/reconcile`; it records the provider evidence reason, polls the same Engine operation, and marks its invitation step successful only after Engine confirms success. Cloud's `requested_by` is documented as an operator-reported audit label; authority remains the authenticated Cloud administrator and verified Engine management principal. The target spec was corrected in this change to define the route and outcome semantics. No table or module-path change. Changed Engine contract/API/runtime/executor/SQL recovery and tests, nested SDK typed route/tests, Cloud lifecycle/provisioning/app route/tests, generated embedded UI/Studio/SDK v2 bundles, Cloud boundary/go-live docs, and this ledger.

Verified: Engine `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`; Engine race `go test -race -p 1 ./api ./contract ./management/... ./internal/engine -count=1`; Engine full `go vet -p 1 ./...`; integration-tag compile `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./management/sqlstore ./internal/engine`; live cross-dialect race `KORA_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' KORA_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN='[REDACTED]' KORA_MANAGEMENT_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./management/sqlstore -run '^TestLive(MySQL|Postgres|LibSQL)TenantInvitationOperationRecovery$' -count=1 -timeout=240s` (MySQL 8.4.11, PostgreSQL 16 and actual LibSQL HTTP all passed; tmpfs-backed loopback containers were removed); Cloud `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, race `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/app ./internal/platform ./internal/provisioning ./internal/controlplane -count=1`, and `go vet -p 1 ./...`; nested SDK `go test -race -p 1 ./management -count=1` and `go vet ./management`; TypeScript SDK contract check, 26 tests/81 assertions, and build; embedded UI contract check, 35 files/166 tests, and build; standalone Studio contract check, 48 files/204 tests, and build; `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, route/import graph drift checks, and `git diff --check`. Frontend builds retain the existing dynamic-import and >500 kB chunk warnings. No live SMTP provider was used.

**Compatibility/deployment gates:** G7 remains in progress. Cloud's legacy tenant database, config/site import, template/lifecycle adapters and direct implementation DTO paths remain until their durable replacements and release windows pass; deletion checkpoint provider verification and a process-level Cloud→Engine invitation/runtime integration test are still open. No production assignment export, external provider outcome or production release was exercised. Do not treat invitation reconciliation as proof of inbox delivery or exactly-once SMTP.

**Next concrete action:** Continue G7 with the configured deletion checkpoint verifier and remaining Cloud tenant-management/direct-database migration; keep `tenant.delete` disabled until the provider verifies immutable, tenant/allocation-bound restorable evidence. Then integrate the Cloud route and Engine worker/runtime in one disposable process-level harness before compatibility-path retirement.

**G7 checkpoint verifier composition (2026-10-15):** Reinspection found the verifier field existed on `PlatformRouteConfig` and reached operation assembly, but the public Engine `Config` did not expose the injection point used by `New`. Added `Config.ManagementCheckpointVerifier` and threaded it into the HTTP platform route composition. Extended `TestNewUsesPlatformDatabaseForApplicationFallbacks` to assert the same injected verifier reaches route assembly. Updated the target specification to identify this supported programmatic injection point and clarify that the CLI still has no concrete backup adapter/configuration and therefore advertises no tenant-delete capability by default. This is a composition fix only: no provider or deletion capability was added, and Cloud deletion remains fail-closed until its credential revocation, draining, checkpoint, and organization providers are configured.

**G7 Cloud lifecycle against a real Engine process (2026-10-15):** Added `internal/platform/engine_process_management_integration_test.go`. **G7 deletion identity recovery (2026-10-16):** Rechecked the deletion saga and found Cloud passed its persisted provider operation ID to revocation, drain and backup, but generated a separate Engine idempotency key from tenant plus checkpoint. Cloud now carries tenant, hostname, persisted operation ID and checkpoint together in `SiteDeleteRequest`; the Engine idempotency key is derived from tenant plus that stable operation ID. If Engine has already removed the tenant after Cloud lost the response, Cloud accepts the expected management 404 during identity preflight, replays the same idempotent delete command, and confirms the durable receipt/removal before advancing its workflow. Other inspection failures and hostname mismatches still fail closed. No contract, schema or deployment assignment changed. **G7 authoritative proposal validation (2026-10-16):** Cloud's `ProposalWorker` now calls the typed Engine configuration dry-run with its tenant-scoped site route and configured Engine credential before marking generated proposals ready; absence of the client/token/site fails closed, and Engine rejection details feed the bounded model-repair loop. One bounded Engine decoder and one candidate-rule validator are now used by both dry-run and direct draft creation, so callers cannot bypass validation by skipping preflight. Engine checks Link/Table target relationships, role-as-Link rejection, duplicate roles, view route uniqueness, view DocType bindings, workflow references/transitions, scripts, and disallowed public permission grants; it accepts legacy `doctype` view input as an alias for canonical `source_doctype`. Both config-check and draft request bodies are capped at 3 MiB, returning 413 when exceeded. Changed Engine `api/config_validation.go`, new `api/config_validation_test.go`, `api/config_proposal.go`, and Cloud `internal/platform/proposal_worker.go` plus its tests and `docs/cloud-boundary.md`. No database schema or API route changed. **G7 shared configuration-draft contract (2026-10-16):** Engine now publishes generated v2 request/result DTOs for creating an inactive configuration draft, and the API returns the shared typed result. Cloud's proposal worker uses the shared request and `tenantclient.CreateConfigurationDraft` instead of hand-building the request, auth/Host headers, and response decoder. It omits legacy `created_by`; Engine attributes the draft from verified execution identity, and the Cloud request test asserts the actor field is absent. The client preserves tenant hostname routing and enforces the draft request's 30-second timeout on a private copy of the shared `http.Client`; tests prove both shorter and longer existing client timeouts are overridden without mutating shared state. Contract artifacts and copies were regenerated. Passed Engine `go test -race -p 1 ./api ./contract -count=1`, scoped vet, `make check-contracts`, and `make check-cross-repo-contracts`; Cloud `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/tenantclient ./internal/platform -count=1`, scoped vet, and 10 race repetitions of `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/tenantclient -run '^TestDoWithTimeoutAppliesExactBoundWithoutMutatingSharedClient$' -count=10`. `git diff --check` passed in both repositories. No release was published. This closes only the onboarding draft DTO duplication; package application/validation, Cloud's tenant DB/template compatibility path, deletion provider composition, three allowlisted Engine implementation imports, and coordinated release gates remain open. Next: continue G7 compatibility-path inventory and remove direct tenant DB access only after the typed management replacement and migration gate pass.

### G9 Deployment assignment cutover

**Status:** in progress.

**G9 aggregate assignment inventory command (2026-10-16):** Added `kora site-directory summarize-assignments`, which reads the canonical v1 directory in 500-entry pages, normalizes blank assignment to `default`, groups only by placement and status, caps in-memory buckets at 4,096, and reports the starting directory revision only when the ending revision still matches. It emits no tenant IDs, aliases, DB settings or credentials; malformed placements/status, unordered/oversized pages, excess bucket cardinality, and concurrent directory changes fail the scan. Updated the Cloud deployment-ingress runbook, Engine target specification and `kora-local-operations` reference to use the aggregate report first and reserve private `site-directory list` output for tenant-specific plans. Tests cover counts/normalization/privacy, changing revisions and bucket cardinality. A real SQL-backed command acceptance created 502 directory rows in a fresh tmpfs-backed MySQL 8.4.11 container, exercised two actual pages plus revision reads, and verified exact counts and that tenant IDs/aliases/DB metadata were absent from output. The first acceptance attempt pinged MySQL before its host listener accepted connections and failed before schema or row writes; the test now waits for a successful authenticated host-side ping. Passed `go test ./cli -run '^(TestSummarizeDirectoryAssignments.*|TestSiteDirectoryMigrationCommand_Exists)$' -count=1`, `go test -race -p 1 ./cli -run '^TestSummarizeDirectoryAssignments' -count=1`, `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./cli`, `go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./cli -run '^TestLiveMySQLSiteDirectoryAssignmentSummary$' -count=1 -timeout=240s -v`, the matching command under `go test -race -tags=integration -p 1`, full `go test ./cli -count=1`, `go vet ./cli`, `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check`. MySQL startup probing emitted transient `unexpected EOF` messages before host readiness; both final disposable runs passed and removed their test containers. No production directory was queried and no assignment was changed. Production assignment inventory and cutover remain open.

**Changes and contract:** Added `contract.SiteDirectoryEntryV2` with validated canonical decimal-string revisions, stable tenant identity, aliases, status, deployment assignment, and optional opaque data-plane reference. Added a projection from the existing v1 descriptor: blank `runtime_cell_id` maps to `default`, while nonblank assignments pass the existing assignment validator. This preserves a single writable assignment source and now feeds Engine gateway routing decisions; it does not migrate persisted storage. Engine validates the complete projection during startup and resync and validates feed events before mutating routing state, rejecting malformed IDs, assignments, and duplicate tenant IDs without partial publication. The gateway stores an immutable v2 snapshot, routes by `deployment_id`, and propagates publication errors. Tenant activation and durable-work discovery use a shared strict assignment comparison; a cold-work probe rechecks the latest canonical assignment after reading a potentially stale page and before opening the tenant database. Added `contract-fixtures/site-directory-v2.json`, manifest entries for Engine, Cloud, Studio, and SDK, and deterministic fixture synchronization/checking. Managed provisioning now stamps the command deployment into the existing v1 `runtime_cell_id` field inside the same registration transaction and outbox event. A retry may fill a blank assignment only while status is `provisioning`; active assignments are never silently moved, and explicit mismatches conflict. Legacy blank active rows remain compatible during the migration window. The target specification documents the projection, assignment write, and recovery rule.

**Files:** Engine `contract/site_directory.go`, `contract/site_directory_test.go`, `site/directory.go`, `site/directory_alias_descriptor_test.go`, `site/create.go`, `site/registry.go`, MySQL/PostgreSQL/LibSQL assignment integration tests, `ingress/gateway.go`, `ingress/gateway_test.go`, `cli/serve.go`, `cli/placement_config_test.go`, `internal/engine/platform_directory.go`, `internal/engine/process_startup.go`, `internal/engine/tenant_directory.go`, `internal/engine/management_provision_executor.go` and its tests, `contract-fixtures/site-directory-v2.json`, `docs/architecture/contract-fixture-manifest.json`, `scripts/sync-contract-consumers.py`, `docs/architecture/target-state-spec.md`, `deploy/selfhost/README.md`; synchronized fixture copies and manifests in Cloud, Studio, and SDK.

**G9 ingress loop guard (2026-10-16):** The compatibility gateway now marks a forwarded request with `X-Kora-Ingress-Hop`. The destination Engine can serve its locally assigned site, strips the marker before application handling, and rejects a second forwarding attempt with HTTP 508. This prevents accidental Engine-to-Engine routing cycles while preserving one ingress-to-owner hop; Cloud ingress composition and deployment-specific process configuration remain open. Updated `ingress/gateway.go`, `ingress/gateway_test.go`, and spec section 11.5. Passed `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./ingress -count=1`, matching `go vet -p 1 ./ingress`, and `git diff --check`. Regression tests exercise two live in-process HTTP gateways to prove second-hop rejection and verify marker stripping for local handlers.

**G9 v2 routing snapshot (2026-10-16):** The retained compatibility gateway now projects incoming v1 descriptors into `SiteDirectoryEntryV2` at its adapter boundary and makes dispatch decisions from `deployment_id`. Full snapshot replacement validates every entry and duplicate tenant identity before atomic publication; invalid feed updates return errors without mutating the current snapshot. Engine startup and directory resync now propagate publication failures instead of silently continuing. The previously added one-hop marker allows the owning Engine to serve the request and rejects any further forwarding. Updated `ingress/gateway.go`, `ingress/gateway_test.go`, `internal/engine/process_startup.go`, `internal/engine/tenant_directory.go`, their directory test stub, and spec section 11.5. Passed `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./ingress ./internal/engine -count=1`, matching `go vet -p 1 ./ingress ./internal/engine`, and `git diff --check`. The test verifies the v2 route assignment and that invalid replacement/feed data leaves the previous snapshot intact. Cloud ingress composition and deployment-ID environment migration remain open.

**G9 durable-work assignment recheck (2026-10-16):** Added `site.SiteBelongsToDeployment` with strict ID validation and explicit blank-to-default behavior, retaining `SiteBelongsToRuntimeCell` as a source-compatible adapter. Runtime directory and initial tenant filtering now call the deployment-named policy. `DurableWorkScanner` checks both the paginated descriptor assignment and the latest row returned by its current directory lookup; if assignment changed, it skips WAL and tenant DB probes. **G9 cell/ingress retirement audit (2026-10-16):** No cell purge has occurred, and the active gateway is still Engine-to-Engine forwarding. This is intentionally retained compatibility code under the target-spec gate, not the final ingress architecture. Cloud `internal/app/app.go` registers the control-panel/API routes and has no deployment-aware reverse proxy or ingress router; Cloud site `EngineURL` values are used for tenant-specific outbound operations and links, not as an authoritative routing projection. The source-only assignment inventory found no deployment router in Studio or SDK, but production assignments, stored ignored local site data, real deployment traffic, and external consumers have not been queried. `assign-cell` remains the only assignment mutation command; the specified `assign-deployment` replacement has not been implemented. One harness defect was found: `TestLiveEngineDeploymentsMoveAndRecover` was guarded by the legacy `KORA_SITE_DIRECTORY_LIVE_ENGINE_CELLS` flag. Its opt-in is now `KORA_SITE_DIRECTORY_LIVE_ENGINE_DEPLOYMENTS`; the separate legacy cell acceptance retains the old flag. The exact attempt `KORA_SITE_DIRECTORY_LIVE_ENGINE_CELLS=1 GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -v -tags=integration -p 1 ./cli -run '^TestLiveEngineDeploymentsMoveAndRecover$' -count=1 -timeout=6m` failed after 240.61s at “destination serves after source shutdown”; therefore nondefault assignment movement/recovery is not verified and must be debugged before cutover claims. After renaming the deployment opt-in, `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./cli` passed as a compile check. A diagnostic rerun with `KORA_SITE_DIRECTORY_LIVE_ENGINE_DEPLOYMENTS=1 GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -v -tags=integration -p 1 ./cli -run '^TestLiveEngineDeploymentsMoveAndRecover$' -count=1 -timeout=6m` also failed after 71.70s: the destination returned HTTP 404 `site_not_found` for the stable tenant ID after source shutdown. While the disposable service was live, SQL showed the tenant assigned to `deployment-a` at config revision 2 and the destination consumer cursor at directory revision 2; the destination startup log had zero local tenants, as expected before reassignment. Added a 60s phase deadline with HTTP status/body and bounded process logs in the failure report, and added `TestTenantDirectoryAssignmentEventRegistersColdTenantForLazyActivation`; the focused race test and `go vet -p 1 ./internal/engine ./net` passed. The unit test proves policy-level event application and lazy activation, but the process-level router still fails and remains unresolved. A third diagnostic attempt using the same deployment test command and environment failed after 70.17s with the same 404. New structured Engine logs prove the destination applied the nondefault assignment with `local_owner=true` at directory revision 2, so SQL feed delivery and assignment comparison are correct; the remaining fault is between lazy descriptor publication, the process HTTP SiteRouter, and first-request activation. The disposable MySQL container and schemas were removed by test cleanup. After these changes, `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./cli ./ingress ./site ./internal/engine -count=1`, matching `go vet -p 1 ./cli ./ingress ./site ./internal/engine`, and `git diff --check` all passed.


**G9 embedded workspace routing regression (2026-10-16):** Resolved the earlier multi-process movement failures recorded in the preceding diagnostic entry. The directory feed had applied `deployment-a` correctly, but `workspace.RegisterSPARoutes` served embedded `/s/{tenant}/*` requests through `SiteRegistry.SiteByName`, bypassing lazy descriptors and `SiteRouter` generation pinning. The fallback router already used lazy activation, which is why the process-level routing behavior differed. `workspace/spa.go` now calls `EnsureName`, maps capacity/resident failures to retryable 503, acquires and pins the selected runtime handle, injects the same tenant context as the net router, and releases the handle after the request. Added `knet.InjectSiteRuntimeContext`, `TestPathSiteRouteActivatesColdTenantBeforeServingEmbeddedWorkspace`, and a focused directory-assignment regression. Changed Engine files: `workspace/spa.go`, `workspace/spa_test.go`, `net/siterouter.go`, `internal/engine/tenant_directory.go`, `internal/engine/tenant_directory_test.go`, and `cli/serve_cells_integration_test.go`. No public wire contract or schema changed. The deployment live-test opt-in now uses `KORA_SITE_DIRECTORY_LIVE_ENGINE_DEPLOYMENTS`; `KORA_SITE_DIRECTORY_LIVE_ENGINE_CELLS` remains the legacy compatibility acceptance flag. Passed both real two-process disposable MySQL tests: `KORA_SITE_DIRECTORY_LIVE_ENGINE_DEPLOYMENTS=1 GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -v -tags=integration -p 1 ./cli -run '^TestLiveEngineDeploymentsMoveAndRecover$' -count=1 -timeout=6m` and the analogous command with `KORA_SITE_DIRECTORY_LIVE_ENGINE_CELLS=1` and `-run '^TestLiveEngineCellsMoveAndRecover$'`; both passed and removed their disposable databases. The live test is a local ownership/routing acceptance, not deployment ingress cutover proof. Also passed `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./cli ./ingress ./site ./internal/engine ./net ./workspace -count=1` and matching `go vet -p 1` for those packages. Regenerated the complete import graph (548 edges); `make check-contracts` passed. The first cross-repo check correctly detected the stale graph, then passed after regeneration; `make check-cross-repo-contracts` verifies 15 shared artifacts, route inventory, full graph, and the three-edge Cloud allowlist. `git diff --check` passed. Cell/forwarding deletion is still blocked on Cloud or operator deployment ingress, authoritative production assignments/consumer inventory, an `assign-deployment` compatibility path, traffic cutover evidence, and the consumer release window. Therefore do not purge `ingress` or `assign-cell` yet.

**G9 deployment assignment CLI compatibility (2026-10-16):** Added `kora site-directory assign-deployment <site-id> <deployment-id> [--apply]`, using `KORA_ENGINE_DEPLOYMENT_URLS` and deployment-named JSON output, backed by the same revisioned assignment storage and direct-origin validation as the existing command. The `assign-cell` command and `KORA_ENGINE_CELL_URLS` remain available as compatibility adapters; neither command performs ingress traffic cutover. Corrected the legacy command help text, which still incorrectly said tenants were eagerly loaded despite the lazy activation behavior. Updated `cli/site_directory_assign_cell.go`, `cli/site_directory.go`, its CLI tests, and specification section 11.5. No schema or wire contract changed. Passed `go test -race -p 1 ./cli -run 'Test(ApplySiteCellAssignment|WriteSiteDeploymentAssignmentReport)' -count=1`, `go run . site-directory assign-deployment --help`, full scoped race tests across `./cli ./ingress ./site ./internal/engine ./net ./workspace`, matching `go vet -p 1` checks, `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts` (15 shared artifacts, current route inventory, 548-edge import graph, three reviewed Cloud boundary edges), and `git diff --check`. The earlier live two-process deployment/cell movement and recovery harnesses also pass, as recorded above.

**G9 placement-diagnostic consistency (2026-10-16):** Rechecked the deployment-named CLI path after adding it and found its shared assignment helper still emitted cell-specific errors, including `KORA_ENGINE_CELL_URLS`, for a missing target origin. The helper now formats refusal and persistence errors using the selected placement name, and the deployment regression proves a missing target reports “target deployment” without mutating the directory. Updated the Engine ingress package and directory-validation comments to describe deployment assignment and the retained forwarding compatibility role. No schema or wire contract changed. Passed full `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp go test -race -p 1 ./cli ./ingress -count=1`, matching `go vet -p 1 ./cli ./ingress`, and scoped `git diff --check`. This is a diagnostic correction only; it does not make Engine forwarding the target or satisfy ingress cutover gates.

**G9 Engine directory resolver for ingress (2026-10-16):** Added authenticated `GET /api/management/v2/directory/resolve` with exactly one `site_id` or `alias` selector. It returns the validated `SiteDirectoryEntryV2` from Engine's canonical assignment source, brackets descriptor resolution with directory-revision reads, and returns retryable 503 if a concurrent update makes the result inconsistent. The endpoint uses the existing bounded management work admission. The nested Go management SDK exposes this through optional `DeploymentDirectoryClient` methods, retaining compatibility for older management clients. Updated Engine `api/management_inspect.go`, tests, nested `sdk/management/client.go` and tests, route inventory, and spec section 11.5; no generated contract/schema change because the response reuses the existing v2 directory entry. Passed full Engine API race tests (`go test -race -p 1 ./api -count=1`), nested SDK management race tests (`cd sdk && go test -race -p 1 ./management -count=1`), matching scoped vet commands, `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts` (15 shared artifacts, current routes, complete import graph and three-edge Cloud allowlist), and `git diff --check`. An initial combined vet command failed because the nested SDK module is outside the root Go module; both module-scoped vet commands then passed. This is an authenticated source API for the upcoming Cloud/operator routing adapter; it does not itself provide a gateway or close ingress cutover.

**G9 separate deployment-ingress process (2026-10-16):** Cloud now has a standalone `kora-cloud ingress` mode, independently configured and runnable without constructing the Cloud app or opening its control-plane DB/broker. It calls Engine's authenticated directory resolver through the public Go management client, routes to direct Engine origins, preserves tenant host/path/query, rebuilds forwarded headers from the inbound request, overwrites the hop marker, bounds active/waiting requests, directory lookups, direct-origin count, upstream connections, and TTL/LRU route-cache size, fails closed for cold unavailable/misassigned tenants, and keeps warmed routes only for their configured TTL during directory outage. Admission counts/deadlines, cache bounds and TTL have explicit env settings with validation. Compose exposes it under an opt-in `ingress` profile without a dependency on the `control-plane` service; Cloud and Engine docs describe the deployment-placement direction and keep Engine forwarding as a migration adapter. Changed Cloud `cmd/kora-cloud/main.go`, `cmd/kora-cloud/main_test.go`, `internal/config/config.go` and tests, new `internal/ingress/gateway.go` and tests, `docker-compose.yml`, `Dockerfile`, `.env.example`, `README.md`, `docs/kora-cloud-architecture.md`; updated this Engine spec and ledger. The typed Engine directory response is reused; no wire/schema change. Passed `go test -race -p 1 ./cmd/kora-cloud ./internal/ingress ./internal/config`, `go test -race -p 1 ./cmd/kora-cloud -run '^TestIngressCLIProcess' -count=1 -v`, Cloud full `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full `go vet -p 1 ./...`, and `go build -o /tmp/kora-cloud-g9 ./cmd/kora-cloud`. Tests cover authenticated directory lookup, direct upstream proxy, host/path/query preservation and spoofed forwarding-header replacement, bounded/expiring route cache, warm-cache operation during directory outage, admission overflow, not-found/inactive/unmapped tenants, endpoint-map cap, path-selector injection rejection, and CLI child-process routing plus SIGTERM shutdown without Cloud DB/broker configuration. `docker compose --profile ingress config --quiet` passed using a temporary `.env.example` copy, then the copy was removed. Engine `make check-contracts` and `make check-cross-repo-contracts` passed; the latter verifies 15 fixture artifacts, route inventory, complete 556-edge Go import graph, and the three reviewed Cloud compatibility imports. Final Engine and Cloud `git diff --check` passed. Production assignment and traffic cutover remain unproven.

**G9 live Cloud-ingress deployment move (2026-10-16):** Added opt-in Cloud integration test `internal/platform/deployment_ingress_integration_test.go`. It starts two real Engine processes and the built Cloud ingress binary against one fresh tmpfs-backed MySQL 8.4.11 service, uses the authenticated Engine directory resolver, verifies a live tenant request reaches the source, stops the source and observes 502, revises the canonical assignment to the destination, then verifies that the ingress cache expires and the destination Engine serves the same tenant. Engine direct-origin compatibility maps are configured in both processes; the Cloud ingress itself maps both Engine origins. The test removes its generated tenant directory rows and database and never drops the disposable platform schema. Two initial acceptance attempts found missing peer-origin configuration in the Engine compatibility gateway and a collation issue in test cleanup; both were corrected. The final exact command `KORA_CLOUD_INGRESS_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/platform -run '^TestCloudDeploymentIngressMovesTenantBetweenEngineProcesses$' -count=1 -timeout=6m -v` passed: source-to-destination move at directory revision 3. It ran against a fresh Docker MySQL 8.4.11 container with `/var/lib/mysql` on tmpfs; the container was removed afterward. The integration package compile check `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./internal/platform` also passed. This proves local Engine/Cloud ingress movement and recovery behavior; it is not production assignment inventory or traffic-cutover evidence.

**G9 self-hosted operator ingress runbook (2026-10-16):** Added `kora-cloud/docs/deployment-ingress.md` with compatible Engine/Cloud release pinning, privileged management credential handling, standalone ingress environment and Docker invocation, direct-origin constraints, TLS-fronted listener guidance, health/readiness semantics, multi-deployment tenant-path smoke checks, revisioned assignment update guidance, and routing rollback. Linked it from Cloud README and Engine `deploy/selfhost/README.md`. Compose now forwards `KORA_REPO_REF` to both Cloud services, and `.env.example` labels its alpha fallback as local-build-only so operators can pin the compatible Engine release. It explicitly preserves the no-ingress single-Engine mode and states that readiness does not prove directory/origin health. Documentation-only change; no API, schema, or deployment assignment format changed. Checks: reviewed command/config names against current `Dockerfile`, `docker-compose.yml`, `internal/config`, and Engine `assign-deployment` command; Compose profile resolution and assertions for both Cloud build contexts, the Engine source ref argument, and readiness command passed; `docker build --check -f Dockerfile .` reports no warnings; `git diff --check`, `make check-contracts`, and `make check-cross-repo-contracts` passed.

**G9 distroless ingress healthcheck execution (2026-10-16):** Exercised the configured Compose healthcheck executable in the actual `gcr.io/distroless/base-debian12` runtime, mounting the locally built current Cloud binary read-only and using host networking to probe an ephemeral localhost ingress listener. `docker run --rm --network host --mount type=bind,src=/tmp/kora-cloud-g9-health,dst=/kora-cloud,readonly --env KORA_CLOUD_INGRESS_HTTP_ADDR=127.0.0.1:28741 --entrypoint /kora-cloud gcr.io/distroless/base-debian12 ingress-healthcheck` exited successfully while the ingress process was serving `/readyz`; the temporary process was stopped and the one-shot container removed itself. This validates the runtime command and base image execution, but does not replace a full Cloud image/Compose stack build. Full image build remains deferred because only 2.6 GB was available on the 99%-used shared root filesystem and other Docker workloads are active.

**G9 Cloud Compose build-context correction (2026-10-16):** The opt-in ingress service initially copied the existing control-plane build stanza, whose `context: ..` resolved to the parent of both repositories while `Dockerfile` requires Cloud-root `go.mod`, web assets, and `.dockerignore`. The ordinary `docker build -t kora-cloud .` documentation already establishes the Cloud repository as the intended context. Updated both `control-plane` and `ingress` to `context: .` / `dockerfile: Dockerfile`, so both use the same buildable image source and new readiness binary. Verified Compose resolves both contexts to `/home/asena/projects/kora-cloud`, ingress retains its `ingress-healthcheck` command, and `docker build --check -f Dockerfile .` reports no warnings. Compose profile configuration also passes with a temporary `.env.example` copy that was removed. A full Docker image build has not been run because the shared root filesystem is at 99% usage (2.6 GB available); do not count the Dockerfile check as image-build evidence. No application contract or database schema changed.

**G9 ingress readiness probe (2026-10-16):** Added a Cloud binary health-check mode used by the optional Compose ingress service. The check derives the loopback probe target from `KORA_CLOUD_INGRESS_HTTP_ADDR`, bypasses environment proxies, requests `/readyz`, and exits nonzero for unavailable or non-ready listeners. Gateway tests cover GET/HEAD readiness and reject other health-check methods. This lets Compose/load balancers distinguish a running-ready ingress process from startup failure without requiring curl/wget in the distroless image. Cloud `docs/kora-cloud-architecture.md` now documents `/healthz`, `/readyz`, and the health-check command. Tests cover a successful configured listener, HTTP 503, and connection failure. Passed `go test -race -p 1 ./cmd/kora-cloud ./internal/ingress ./internal/config -count=1`, matching scoped and full `go vet -p 1 ./...`, full Cloud `go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, `docker compose --profile ingress config --quiet` with a temporary `.env.example` copy that was removed, `go build -o /tmp/kora-cloud-g9-health ./cmd/kora-cloud`, and `git diff --check`. No public contract or DB schema changed.
