Keep Engine forwarding, legacy cell flags, and `assign-cell` until these compatibility gates pass. Broader local ownership proof for inbound/replay/background workers also remains open. G10 retirement remains gated on production cutover, the consumer release window, and operational evidence.
### G10 Purge and documentation completion

**Status:** in progress; final removals are gated by G7–G9 compatibility and deployment evidence.

**Existing deployment and recovery artifacts:** Engine `deploy/selfhost/compose.yaml`, `.env.example`, `nginx.conf`, optional PgBouncer/ProxySQL examples, `backup.md`, `restore.md`, and the operator README are present. The Cloud repository now has a separate deployment-ingress runbook linked from both repository READMEs. Active documentation search found cell terminology only in explicit compatibility instructions and historical/audit evidence; no active document directs operators to build a new cell-based architecture.

**Changes this checkpoint:** Engine `deploy/selfhost/compose.yaml` (65-second Engine stop grace), `deploy/selfhost/README.md` (safe shutdown order), `configstore/workflow_action_inventory.go`, `cli/workflow_legacy_inventory.go`, corresponding tests, target spec and this implementation ledger. No API or database schema change.

**Checks executed:** `make check-selfhost-backup` passed (16 tests plus Python compilation); the self-host Compose model parsed and resolved Engine `stop_grace_period` to `1m5s`; the focused Engine shutdown/drain race tests passed; `git diff --check` passed. The live SQL restore/account inventory drill and Engine-plus-UI runtime smoke are recorded at the ledger top. These do not constitute a full Docker image/Compose build, tenant workflow UAT, or production restore/cutover.

**Open removal and deployment gates:** The Engine cell-forwarding path, `assign-cell`, and legacy cell environment aliases remain until G9 ingress traffic and production assignments are migrated. The three Cloud Engine implementation import edges and direct-DB/template compatibility path remain until the G7/G8 release window and consumer inventory pass. Any old persisted workflow executor or other purge-register item still requires a source-by-source compatibility audit; this ledger does not assert that the full purge register has been exhausted. Complete isolated deployment/restore validation and operational runbooks before marking G10 verified.

**Purge source checks so far (2026-10-16):** The former process-global `map[*sql.DB]` session-manager pattern has no current source match. A fresh four-repository source scan found no Cloud, standalone UI, or TypeScript SDK references to `SiteRuntimeServices`, `UpdateSiteConfig`, or `Manager.UpdateConfig`. Engine production route assembly passes no mutable registry; tenant requests use the immutable service snapshot pinned by `SiteRouter`. The exported `api.SiteRuntimeServices` type and compatibility registration functions remain reachable in Engine for downstream Go callers and tests, so their removal still needs a consumer release window or usage evidence; the repository scan cannot establish external package usage. The console storage-update defect has been corrected through `TenantReloadController.Reload`, with durable directory/outbox commit, request/admission release, coordinator replacement, and an honest refresh-pending response on activation failure. `SetStorage` remains only in create/onboarding fallback branches when the Engine runtime installer is absent. `Manager.UpdateConfig`/`SiteRouter.UpdateSiteConfig` remain as compatibility methods but are no longer used by the console update path; their exported surface and any downstream callers still need a compatibility gate. `api.Handler`'s legacy static service maps also remain exported compatibility surfaces and are consulted only when no pinned runtime snapshot or registry entry exists. The durable actor/timer workflow tables remain persisted and are not classified as obsolete by this source scan.

**Business inventory versus migration inventory audit (2026-10-16):** Current Engine source has no Go package implementing stock/inventory business rules. `config/v0/inventory` contains 34 tenant template/configuration files, and `tests/inventory_package_contract_test.go` validates that package. The similarly named `api/ai/legacy_inventory.go`, `cli/workflow_legacy_inventory.go`, `script/inventory.go`, and `workflow/sqlstore/legacy_inventory.go` perform bounded read-only aggregate inspections of persisted AI, script, workflow-action and legacy actor/timer state; they do not implement stock behavior and are required for the specified safe migration inventory. Separately, Engine's default tenant composition imports the Purchase Request domain adapter, `api/mpesa_handlers.go` implements a payment-provider callback, and both UIs contain POS/payment/scanning components. Those are business features rather than migration scanners; no deletion was made pending the requested scope decision and compatibility review. This is a source inventory, not evidence that template packs or those features are unused in deployed tenants.

**Persisted document-workflow action inventory (2026-10-16):** Added a bounded, read-only extension to `kora workflow legacy-inventory` that scans live `_kora_workflow` definitions and all `_kora_config_version` snapshots, including old JSON and canonical s-expression forms. Output is aggregate only (source, normalized snapshot status, action phase/type, async flag and count); it excludes workflow/script names, URLs, conditions, tenant config bodies and IDs. Each source is capped at 10,001 rows and SQL filters config payloads above 1 MiB before returning them; action rows are aggregated into a fixed-size bucket set. Dialect-aware metadata distinguishes absent tables from partial/unknown schemas. Oversized, truncated, malformed and unrecognized-schema input marks the scan incomplete. The target spec names this as a prerequisite for retiring document transition-action compatibility and distinguishes it from durable process instances. No API or schema changed. Passed normal and race tests for focused `configstore`/`cli` inventory cases, disposable MySQL 8.4.11 live integration (`TestLiveWorkflowActionInventoryAcrossDialects/mysql`), integration-tag compile, `go vet -p 1 ./configstore ./cli`, and `git diff --check`. PostgreSQL and actual LibSQL HTTP integration were not run because no disposable endpoints were configured; they remain unverified. Operators must run this inventory against every persisted tenant DB before any action-family removal.

**G10 exported runtime-service API recheck (2026-10-16):** Rechecked `api.SiteRuntimeServices`, runtime registration helpers, and `SiteRouter.UpdateSiteConfig` against current production and test call sites. The production handler uses the pinned tenant snapshot, but compatibility route registration and admission middleware still accept the exported service map, and tests/downstream Go consumers can compile against these APIs. The repository cannot prove an external-consumer release window, so no API was deleted. Keep this compatibility gate explicit; revisit after consumer inventory/release evidence.

**G10 pre-migration workflow inventory procedure (2026-10-16):** Updated `deploy/selfhost/README.md` to require running the candidate Engine's read-only `workflow legacy-inventory --all-sites` before schema migrations/restart. The example writes into an operator-designated private directory (mode 0700) because report rows contain site IDs/hostnames; it requires a successful exit and every tenant's `workflow_actions.complete=true`, and stops on missing/incomplete results. Updated migration guidance to keep legacy workflow/cell compatibility until their independent gates pass. This is an operator procedure, not a production inventory run or migration. Passed the full relevant `go test ./configstore ./cli -count=1`, self-host Compose `config --quiet` using disposable placeholder values, extracted runbook-shell `bash -n`, and an explicit trailing-whitespace scan across all eight changed files. Deployment behavior and report capture were not exercised against existing tenant data.

**G10 legacy actor executor removal recheck (2026-10-16):** The prior actor/timer executor is not wired into current Engine composition, but `_kora_workflow_actor`, legacy `_kora_workflow_timer`, and audit rows can contain pending persisted work with no generic semantic mapping into process instances. The target spec requires owner-specific reconciliation and forbids mechanical conversion; the new inventory reports only aggregate counts and is not a migration. Retain its tables/readers and do not claim automatic recovery; removal remains blocked on tenant inventory plus per-family owner mapping or explicit operator reconciliation.

**G10 unused graph/plugin/reconciliation reachability audit (2026-10-16):** `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-workflow-inventory-cache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-workflow-inventory-tmp go list -deps -f '{{.ImportPath}}' .` found no `graph`, `plugin`, `reconcile`, or `recon` package in the Engine server binary dependency graph. First-party non-test Go import search found only `recon/failure.go -> graph`; there are no imports from Cloud, standalone UI or TS SDK. This establishes current in-process unreachability, not downstream compatibility: these are exported Engine Go packages and no complete external consumer/release inventory exists. No package was removed; keep the purge gate open until consumers are migrated or the release boundary explicitly retires those APIs.

**G10 storage reload defect follow-up (2026-10-16):** Production console composition now injects the existing `TenantReloadController.Reload` operation into `api.ConsoleHandler`. The update handler commits the canonical directory record/outbox event, releases its request-pinned runtime handle and platform-work admission permit, then asks the coordinator to build and atomically publish the current revision; it no longer mutates `SiteRouter.UpdateSiteConfig` or calls `SiteRuntimeServices.SetStorage` for an update. Releasing both permits before replacement avoids self-drain and shared-pool admission deadlocks. A reload error is reported as HTTP 503 `site.runtime_refresh_pending`, making the durable config change explicit while leaving the old prepared generation in place for feed/recovery. Storage configuration is prevalidated and any closable validation backend is closed. Added handler regressions for publication after durable commit, safe replacement/drain after releasing the current request pin and platform permit, and honest failure behavior with the old generation retained. Passed focused update tests under race, full `go test -race -p 1 ./api ./internal/engine -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./api ./internal/engine`, `make check-contracts`, `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts` (15 shared fixture artifacts, route inventory and three-edge Cloud boundary allowlist), and scoped `git diff --check`. This fixes the single-process console update path and retains the feed as recovery for durable updates when immediate local activation fails; it does not remove `SiteRuntimeServices`, whose other active consumers and shared analytics-cache role are documented above.

**G10 process analytics-cache ownership split (2026-10-16):** Added a dedicated `api.AnalyticsQueryCache` process owner and wired one Engine-owned instance through both API route groups and Engine resource metrics. `SiteRuntimeServices` no longer owns cache state; it holds only a compatibility reference and preserves its snapshot method for existing adapters. Existing route-registration signatures still work through the default compatibility cache, while Engine uses the explicit owner path. Engine’s production `BuildApplication` passes no mutable service registry into route handlers: tenant middleware supplies the pinned service snapshot. Removed the now-unused `Application.RuntimeServices` and `ApplicationRouteConfig.RuntimeServices` fields and their registry allocation; the compatibility type remains in `api` for non-Engine callers until release gates pass. `BuildApplication` returns the owned cache, and its composition test requires it. After the final composition-field removal, passed `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp go test -race -p 1 ./api ./internal/engine -count=1`, full `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -p 1 ./... -count=1`, full `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp go vet -p 1 ./...`, `make check-contracts`, `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts` (15 artifacts, route inventory and Cloud import allowlist), and scoped `git diff --check`. This splits process cache ownership from tenant service state and removes the Engine composition's mutable registry without removing compatibility APIs; those remain gated on external consumer migration/release compatibility.

**G10 compatibility service fallback precedence (2026-10-16):** Fixed `Handler.runtimeService` so an existing registry entry remains authoritative, including an explicitly empty service bundle; legacy per-site maps are consulted only when the registry has no entry. This avoids both the former bug where an empty registry object hid valid legacy configuration and the unsafe alternative of reviving a retired service from a stale fallback map. Added race-tested coverage for absent, populated and explicitly empty registry entries. **G10 legacy workflow migration contract correction (2026-10-16):** Reconciled the current schemas before proposing any actor-to-process migration. `_kora_workflow_actor` has only route/workflow/instance identity, lease/version, status, JSON snapshot and timestamps; it lacks stable tenant identity, immutable process definition/version, subject revision, domain action key and process idempotency receipt. `api/ai.RunActor` uses the generic `ai_run` key, while canonical AI state lives in `_kora_ai_run`, `_kora_ai_step`, `_kora_ai_task`, `_kora_ai_message`, approval/audit and usage records. Source searches and schema/code inspection only; no tenant data, production database, or data values were queried. Documentation diff passes `git diff --check`; no API or schema change.

**G10 best-effort hook durability audit (2026-10-16):** `orm.runHooks` routes every `after_*` lifecycle script to `internal/engine.AsyncHookWorker` when configured. That worker uses a bounded in-memory channel; queue overflow increments a process metric and logs, but accepted work has no persisted retry/receipt across process loss. Configured document `WorkflowTransition.OnSuccess` scripts also run after the document transition commits and log failures without durable replay. Shipped `config/` packs contain no lifecycle or transition hook declarations in the scanned YAML, but tenant-defined scripts may exist outside this checkout. These hooks can invoke authorized nested mutations, so the source search does not establish that their effects are nonessential. Preserve the general hook API; required effects must move to a transaction-bound domain operation or durable workflow/outbox with migration and replay coverage before the old semantics can be retired. Passed current focused race tests: `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp go test -race -p 1 ./orm ./internal/engine ./api -run 'Test(AsyncAfterHookCarries|HookEnqueueFailedCount|AsyncHookQueue|AsyncHookAdmission|StartAsyncHookWorker|ProcessResources.*AsyncHook|ProcessResourcesStopsProducersBeforeDrainingAsyncHooks|HealthPayloadIncludesAsyncHookOverflow)' -count=1`. These tests cover queue bounds, worker admission and shutdown, not process-crash recovery. No implementation change; durable-hook replacement and tenant hook inventory remain open.

**G10 durable event source comparison (2026-10-16):** Compared async lifecycle hook payloads with the transactional outbox envelope. Document mutations already append a durable outbox event, but `ChangeEventToEnvelope` currently omits the verified actor/role context and the selected lifecycle-script version; replaying scripts from that event would therefore change authorization and configuration semantics. Do not replace the queue with a naive outbox subscriber or claim hooks durable. A correct migration needs a versioned, transactionally recorded hook invocation (including stable script/config identity and verified execution context), an authorized execution adapter, receipt/effect transaction semantics, and reconciliation for external effects. Updated target-spec section 15's purge gate and section 16's implementation-correction notes with these constraints. This exact mismatch explains why the queue is not yet removed. No runtime code change; source inspection only.

**G7 durable tenant drain provider (2026-10-16):** Added Cloud `EngineDeletionDrainProvider` in `internal/platform/engine_deletion_drain.go`, composed it from `internal/platform/dependencies.go` only when a typed Engine management client is configured, and covered it in `internal/platform/engine_deletion_drain_test.go`. The provider verifies tenant and alias through typed Engine inspection, requires state-set/reconcile/read capabilities, submits a suspended-state command with a deterministic key derived from tenant plus the persisted Cloud deletion operation, and reconciles/polls the same Engine operation through completion. It does not revoke credentials or attest backups, and cannot enable deletion without the still-required organization, credential-revocation, checkpoint-provider and Engine-verifier composition. Tests cover repeated execution, stable command identities, recovery-required reconciliation and fail-closed tenant/capability mismatches. Passed Cloud `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./internal/platform ./internal/provisioning -count=1`, matching `go vet`, and integration-tag compile `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./internal/platform`. Engine `make check-contracts` passed. Cross-repository check initially detected the complete import graph was stale after the new Cloud→Engine management SDK edge; regenerated it with `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py` (561 internal edges), after which `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, `python3 scripts/architecture-import-graph.py --check`, and scoped `git diff --check` passed. No wire/schema change or deletion capability was enabled.

**G7 partial-provider fail-closed regression (2026-10-16):** Added Cloud `internal/provisioning/manager_test.go` coverage proving that configuring the new Engine drain provider alone does not create a Cloud deletion workflow or invoke the provider. The `RequestSiteDeletion` guard requires organization resolution, credential revocation, draining, and a trusted checkpoint provider before persisting any deletion intent. Passed Cloud `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/provisioning ./internal/platform -count=1`, matching `go vet`, integration-tag compile for both packages, and scoped `git diff --check`. No contract/schema change; production deletion remains unavailable until all providers and Engine verifier are composed.

**G7 fingerprint-bound managed credential and session revocation (2026-10-16):** Added Engine v2 `RevokeManagedChannelClientV2`/result contracts and `POST /api/internal/channel/managed-client/revoke`, restricted to the exact Engine provisioning principal route. Engine compares a lowercase SHA-256 fingerprint before disabling the managed extension using an access-token compare-and-set, and revokes all outstanding sessions for that managed client inside the same tenant-database transaction. Same-token retries succeed; stale fingerprints conflict, so delayed deletion retries cannot disable a newer token or revoke its sessions. Cloud now has a typed tenant client call and `EngineDeletionCredentialRevoker`; it requires its separate Engine provisioning credential and Cloud-held managed tenant token, derives a stable key from tenant plus persisted deletion operation ID, sends only the fingerprint, and returns a redacted credential reference. The provider is composed only when the provisioning token exists. Updated Engine target spec and Cloud boundary/architecture docs. Regenerated Engine schema/TypeScript contract artifacts and synced embedded UI, standalone UI, and TypeScript SDK copies; updated route inventory. Passed Engine `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp go test -race -p 1 ./contract ./api ./auth`, Cloud `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-testtmp go test -race -p 1 ./internal/tenantclient ./internal/platform ./internal/provisioning`, matching `go vet` commands for those package sets, and integration-tag compile `go test -race -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./api`. Live Engine handler checks passed on disposable MySQL 8.4.11 (`TestLiveMySQLManagedCredentialIdempotency`), PostgreSQL 16 (`TestLivePostgresExtensionRoutes`), and the actual LibSQL HTTP transport (`TestLiveLibSQLManagedCredentialLifecycle`); each test created its own disposable schema/database and verified the managed token and session were revoked, and each container was tmpfs-backed and removed. Cloud's real-process saga `TestCloudLifecycleAgainstEngineProcess` also passed under `-race -tags=integration` with a disposable MySQL 8.4.11 container: it issued a channel session, verified it authenticated, revoked through the Cloud provider, verified Engine rejected that session, then closed Cloud's management proxy and successfully performed an authenticated tenant business mutation. The first Cloud attempt using an ephemeral mapped port timed out before the test; rerunning with a fixed loopback port and explicit disposable `kora_test` database passed. `make sync-contracts`, `make check-contracts`, and `make check-cross-repo-contracts` passed after route inventory regeneration. No production tenant or credentials were touched. Deletion remains gated on organization identity, backup/checkpoint providers, and Engine's trusted checkpoint verifier.

**G7 deletion drain completion fence (2026-10-16):** Rechecked the Cloud deletion sequence and found that Engine's management `Retire` returned immediately after removing a tenant snapshot while its admitted requests and workers were still draining asynchronously. Cloud could therefore revoke credentials and begin checkpointing before a previously admitted write had finished. Engine `TenantRuntimeCoordinator.Retire` now joins the retired generation's drain before reporting success. If the caller deadline expires, the Engine operation stays recoverable while the owned drain continues; a retry joins that same drain, and never treats an absent routing snapshot alone as proof of completion. Cloud now suspends/drains before credential revocation, then repeats the idempotent drain at its durable draining stage before checkpointing, covering workflows already persisted in that stage. Added Engine tests for successful join and deadline/retry join, plus Cloud stage-order/recovery assertions. No contract or schema change. Passed `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp go test -race -p 1 ./contract ./api ./auth ./internal/engine -count=1`, focused Engine retirement retry tests under `-race`, Cloud focused provisioning/platform/tenantclient deletion and revocation tests under `-race`, and Cloud's real Engine-process lifecycle/revocation/tenant-operation integration under `-race -tags=integration` against a fresh tmpfs-backed MySQL 8.4.11 container. The first integration-container launch used a MySQL flag removed in 8.4 and failed before the test connected; the container was removed and the rerun with the default MySQL 8.4 configuration passed. Scoped `git diff --check` passed and the disposable container was removed. Cloud deletion remains gated on organization identity, checkpoint-provider and trusted-verifier deployment; final purge/cutover gates remain open.

The real Cloud→Engine provisioning saga now uses `runtime.NewSharedCloudManager` (not a fake runtime), asserts the management descriptor exposes no DB type/host/port/name/user/password, verifies the runtime URL uses the stable Engine tenant ID, then completes managed-channel session issue/revocation and a tenant business mutation through Engine's tenant route after closing the management proxy. `runtimeEnv` now omits DB variables when the management-created result has no DB type; the explicitly retained legacy lifecycle path still includes its DB settings. This corrects the stale G7 dependency note above; that earlier note should not be treated as an open blocker. **G8 onboarding tenant binding correction (2026-10-16):** The public onboarding page previously trusted Cloud’s returned `site.hostname` as the expected workspace identity. A stale but internally matching UAT hostname and resume URL could therefore pass the URL validator. The page now binds both response hostname and tenant-scoped resume URL to the slug submitted by the user; a mismatched site response is rejected before showing a handoff link. Added website regression cases. `bun test src/lib/onboarding-handoff.test.ts` passed (6 tests); `npm run build` passed contract validation, TypeScript, and Vite production build (existing >500 kB chunk warning). Still requires a real Cloud-backed onboarding/UAT and deployed website release to verify the full external handoff.

**G10 async invocation boundary revalidation (2026-10-16):** Rechecked current source after the G8 handoff fix. Kernel-owned document lifecycle callbacks are intentionally invoked after commit (`kernel/command_exec.go`, `kernel/pipeline.go`), so the current `orm.AsyncHookSink` handoff has a commit-to-enqueue crash window. Workflow `OnSuccess` scripts are also submitted after `runKernelWorkflowTransition` returns committed state (`api/router.go`); `OnFailure` scripts run after a rejected command, so neither can be made atomic with the attempted state change by simply moving the existing queue call. The current worker does receive the selected `ScriptRecord`, but `policy.Execution` is not serializable by design and the script provider permits outbound HTTP as well as nested local mutations. A correct replacement therefore needs (1) a versioned, tenant-persisted invocation with frozen script and verified identity, (2) a transaction-bound local mutation executor and completion receipt, and (3) explicit uncertain/reconciliation handling for HTTP or other external effects; failure hooks require their own durable trigger identity. The spec’s §16.1 retirement gate remains accurate; no compatibility hook was removed and no durability claim is made. Next action: design the persistence/store transaction contract and real-dialect migration together before changing dispatch behavior.

**G10 workflow success-script snapshot correction (2026-10-16):** `HandleWorkflowAction` previously loaded the `OnSuccess` `ScriptRecord` only after the document transition committed, so an edit between commit and dispatch could cause the transition to run code that was not selected when it was requested. The handler now prepares an immutable in-memory action plan before the transition and dispatches that exact record after commit; queue ownership and best-effort behavior remain unchanged. Extended `TestWorkflowActionAsyncScriptUsesBoundedSinkWithoutProvider`: a post-commit notification mutates the stored script, and the queued action must still contain the pre-transition source while the store contains the replacement. This narrows a version-selection race; the invocation remains process-local and is not crash-durable. Verified: `GOTMPDIR=/tmp/kora-g10-workflow-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-g10-workflow-gocache TMPDIR=/tmp/kora-g10-workflow-gotmp go test -vet=off -p 1 ./api -count=1`; focused MySQL integration `go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./api -run '^TestWorkflowActionAsyncScriptUsesBoundedSinkWithoutProvider$' -count=1 -timeout=240s -v` against the task-owned MySQL 8.4.11 tmpfs container (the DSN password was read inside a Python test runner and not printed); `go vet -p 1 ./api`; `gofmt`; and scoped `git diff --check` all passed. An initial direct DSN attempt was skipped by the fixture after MySQL rejected the guessed password; the credential-aware rerun passed. A concurrent pair of initial compile jobs exhausted `/dev/shm` and failed with `no space left on device`; I stopped the duplicate job and did not clear unrelated caches. Race testing was not run because shared build storage remained constrained. Changed Engine files: `api/router.go`, `api/kernel_resource_integration_test.go`, and this ledger. No contract/schema change. Durable source-transaction capture, verified replay identity and local/external effect separation remain open G10 work.
