Returned HTTP 200 with both `site.hostname: uat-164a3be1` and `resume_url: http://localhost:5173/s/uat-164a3be1/workspace/onboarding` (the exact stale-response shape that the former caller would accept). The rendered page stayed on the form, showed `workspace response tenant mismatch`, and exposed no success link. This verifies the caller binds the server response to the submitted slug, in addition to the six helper tests and production build recorded above. No provisioning or email occurred. Browser process closed; no browser profile or existing user process was modified.

**G9 ingress Compose credential isolation (2026-10-16):** The optional Cloud ingress service previously used the full control-plane `.env` as an `env_file`, injecting the Cloud database, SMTP, encryption and admin credentials into a separate routing process that does not need them. Removed that inheritance; Compose still interpolates `.env` values into the explicit ingress environment allowlist, including only its Engine directory credential, direct-origin map, listener, bounds and log format. Added `scripts/check-ingress-compose-isolation.py` to render the actual Compose model with synthetic sentinel values and assert the ingress allowlist, required Engine settings, and control-plane credential wiring; CI runs this regression. Documented the boundary in Cloud `docs/deployment-ingress.md`. Passed `python3 scripts/check-ingress-compose-isolation.py`, `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-ingress-compose-test TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-ingress-compose-test GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-ingress-compose-cache go test ./cmd/kora-cloud -run 'Test(IngressConfigCanBeValidatedWithoutCloudControlPlane|IngressHealthcheckUsesConfiguredListenerAndRequiresReady)$' -count=1`, and scoped `git diff --check`. The first Go-test invocation could not start because the specified temporary directory did not exist; after creating the task-owned directory, the same test passed (2 tests), then the task-owned temp/cache directories were removed. The Compose test created a mode-0600 temporary `.env` only because none existed and removed it afterward; no services were started and no live credentials were used. No API/schema change. Production ingress deployment and assignment cutover remain open G9 gates.

**G10 workflow-action config-revision fence (2026-10-16):** The workflow handler now captures the tenant configuration revision before resolving either `OnTransition` or `OnSuccess` script actions and passes it through the internal Kernel operation context. In the transition transaction, Engine takes a lock/reservation on the revision row and verifies that it is still current before committing domain state; a concurrent script/config update therefore causes a conflict instead of committing a transition based on stale behavior. Added sqlmock coverage and real-MySQL regressions that (1) edit the only synchronous `OnTransition` action during execution, with no `OnSuccess` action, and assert conflict, unchanged document state, and no queued effect, and (2) prove a competing config writer blocks until a transition releases the revision fence. This ensures the fence is not accidentally conditional on post-commit scripts. This supplements the in-memory script snapshot correction above; it does not make hooks crash-durable. Passed `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-g10-revision-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-g10-revision-gotmp go test -p 1 ./api ./configstore ./kernel -count=1`; focused task-owned MySQL 8.4.11 integration `go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./api -run '^(TestWorkflowActionAsyncScriptUsesBoundedSinkWithoutProvider|TestWorkflowActionRejectsWhenSelectedConfigRevisionChangesBeforeTransition|TestConfigRevisionFenceSerializesConcurrentTenantConfigWrite)$' -count=1 -timeout=240s`; `GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-g10-revision-gotmp GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache TMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-g10-revision-gotmp go vet -p 1 ./api ./configstore ./kernel`; `gofmt`; and scoped `git diff --check`. Race testing was not run because shared temporary storage remained constrained (about 600 MiB available). The fence serialization test also passed on actual PostgreSQL 16 in a task-created tmpfs-only container and through actual LibSQL HTTP using a task-created sqld container with `/var/lib/sqld` on a 64 MiB tmpfs. Both containers were stopped and removed. The LibSQL integration test probes `SELECT 1` over HTTP before changing state. The opt-in tests are `KORA_CONFIG_REVISION_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN=[REDACTED] DSN> go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./configstore -run '^TestPostgresConfigRevisionFenceSerializesConcurrentWrite$' -count=1 -timeout=90s -v` and `KORA_CONFIG_REVISION_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN=[REDACTED] DSN> go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./configstore -run '^TestLibSQLConfigRevisionFenceSerializesConcurrentWrite$' -count=1 -timeout=90s -v`; both passed. `go test -tags=integration -run '^$' -p 1 ./configstore` and `go vet -tags=integration -p 1 ./configstore` passed. The MySQL API regression separately proves the same writer-blocking behavior on MySQL 8.4.11. No public JSON contract changed; the fence is an internal operation-context field. Durable invocation capture, recovery, and local/external effect transaction semantics remain open.

**G10 self-host restore drill extension (2026-10-16):** Rechecked the live restore script against its runbook claims and found that it did not actually test partial-dump rejection or file archive/extract despite the surrounding documentation describing truncated-restore rejection. Extended `scripts/selfhost-restore-drill.py` to import a half-dump into a separate randomly named, network-isolated MySQL project and require the source-sequence manifest validator to reject it, leaving the complete target restore untouched. Added a synthetic Engine file-volume tar create/extract/compare using tmpfs source/target mounts and the pinned Alpine image; the archive stays in the private `/dev/shm` drill directory. Updated `deploy/selfhost/backup.md` to state exactly what the file check proves and what remains untested. Passed `make check-selfhost-backup` (16 unit tests plus Python compilation), `python3 scripts/selfhost-restore-drill.py --run` against MySQL 8.4.11 (full dump/restore, partial restore rejection, stale-sequence rejection, restart/auth checks, and synthetic file archive/extract all passed; dump 3,765,462 bytes), and scoped `git diff --check`. The three random Compose projects used network-isolated services and tmpfs database data and were cleaned up; all scratch files were in `/dev/shm`. No public API/schema change. Also passed `docker build --check -f Dockerfile .` (no warnings) and `docker compose -f deploy/selfhost/compose.yaml --env-file deploy/selfhost/.env.example config --quiet`. The synthetic file test is not a restore of an operator's real Engine volume; full Engine image build/boot, real file-volume cutover, off-host encryption, recovery objectives, and production rehearsal remain open. The image build was not attempted because root filesystem headroom remains below 1 GB.

**G10 durable async workflow-success scripts (2026-10-16):** Async `OnSuccess` workflow scripts now enter `_kora_workflow_async_script` in the same SQL transaction as the workflow transition. The stored row freezes the script record, config revision, verified actor and committed document; retry identity is `(tenant, source operation, action index)`. A tenant-owned worker claims with monotonically fenced leases, has a six-minute lease for scripts capped at one minute, retries failures up to five attempts, and runs with no script provider so retries cannot repeat local mutations or HTTP effects. The process durable-work scanner now detects due invocations and wakes a cold tenant. The post-commit process-local enqueue is skipped for these success actions; synchronous transition hooks and process-local async failure hooks retain existing behavior. Updated Engine `workflow/service.go`, `workflow/sqlstore/{execution_schema.go,async_scripts.go}`, `kernel/{kernel.go,pipeline.go}`, `api/{kernel_handler.go,router.go}`, `internal/tenantruntime/services.go`, `internal/engine/{tenant_services.go,workflow_async_scripts.go,durable_work_scanner.go}`, API/Engine tests, and §16.1 of the target specification. No public JSON API changed; additive per-tenant SQL table added. Passed focused package tests `go test -p 1 ./workflow ./workflow/sqlstore ./kernel ./api ./internal/engine ./internal/tenantruntime`; passed `go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./api -run '^TestWorkflowActionAsyncScriptUsesBoundedSinkWithoutProvider$' -count=1 -timeout=240s -v` against the local disposable MySQL integration fixture, proving the transition persists the original selected script despite a post-commit script edit, captures the committed document/verified actor/config revision, recovers an expired lease under a new worker, rejects the stale worker's completion and accepts the current fence. Added unit tests proving provider access is absent and tenant mismatch fails closed. Race tests, full PostgreSQL/LibSQL invocation-store integration and a full Engine worker-restart process test remain to run. Scope remains narrower than the G10 gate: lifecycle after-hooks and async workflow `OnFailure` scripts remain process-local; webhooks remain unsupported; no exactly-once guarantee is claimed. Also retain the source-transaction local-effect and external-effect reconciliation work before replacing provider-capable lifecycle hooks.

**G10 durable document-hook store live transaction/recovery acceptance (2026-10-16):** Added `script/async_invocation_sql_integration_test.go` and ran its opt-in `TestAsyncHookStoreTransactionsAndRecoveryAcrossDialects` under the race detector against fresh tmpfs-only MySQL 8.0, PostgreSQL 16, and the actual LibSQL HTTP transport. Exact test command: `go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./script -run '^TestAsyncHookStoreTransactionsAndRecoveryAcrossDialects$' -count=1 -timeout=180s -v`, with `KORA_ASYNC_HOOK_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN`, `KORA_ASYNC_HOOK_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN`, and `KORA_ASYNC_HOOK_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN` set to task-created loopback databases; `GOCACHE`, `GOTMPDIR`, and `TMPDIR` used the existing task-owned `/dev/shm` locations. All three subtests passed. They verify that a source-transaction rollback removes its invocation, a committed local effect and success receipt roll back together, a replacement lease fence rejects stale claims, retryable failures can be reclaimed, and an expired invocation with a persisted external-effect marker becomes `uncertain` rather than being retried. The first run exposed two fixture issues (the MySQL DSN lacked a selected database, then its test effect table used an unindexable TEXT primary key); both were corrected and the complete three-dialect run passed. The current LibSQL wrapper also requires explicitly invoking `/bin/sqld`; the initial wrapper-form container exited before the test and was removed. Every task-created database used tmpfs and was stopped/removed; no existing container or user database was accessed. No production schema or public contract changed in this test addition. This verifies SQL-store behavior over real transports, not full Engine script execution/domain authorization, process crash recovery, or G10 completion.

**G10 durable Engine hook local-domain effect acceptance (2026-10-16):** Added `internal/engine/durable_lifecycle_hooks_integration_test.go`, which drives `processOneDurableLifecycleHook` through the real API script provider, verified actor/grant, nested kernel mutation, SQL outbox and invocation store. After committing the source invocation, the test closes the source DB pool, reopens the database and constructs a new store/worker owner before processing; the invocation is recovered from SQL rather than process memory. The injected script first creates a `Task` and then fails; the task and its outbox event roll back while the invocation remains retryable. A second worker claim runs the successful retry and commits the task, its outbox event and the fenced success receipt together. Passed under `-race` with the actual MySQL 8.0, PostgreSQL 16 and LibSQL HTTP transports: `go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/engine -run '^TestDurableLifecycleHookWorkerCommitsAuthorizedLocalMutationAcrossDialects$' -count=1 -timeout=240s -v`, with `KORA_ENGINE_HOOK_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN`, `KORA_ENGINE_HOOK_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN`, and `KORA_ENGINE_HOOK_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN` set to fresh task-created tmpfs databases. The first fixture run revealed that generated record names require `_kora_naming_series`; the disposable fixture now creates that dialect's existing bootstrap DDL. This was a test-fixture omission, not a production bootstrap change. The worker now invokes nested post-commit callbacks only after commit and suppresses async after-hooks already captured as durable invocations; this fixture has no active lifecycle scripts, so duplicate suppression is not directly asserted. All disposable services were stopped and removed; no existing DB or user data was touched. Follow-up focused race tests and `go vet` passed for `./api ./orm ./kernel ./script ./internal/engine`; `git diff --check` passed. This verifies local nested domain effects, retry after tenant pool/store recreation and outbox atomicity; it does not simulate an Engine process restart or cold-tenant manager activation, and does not verify external HTTP reconciliation on a live endpoint or overall G10 completion.

**G10 Engine process restart and cold-tenant lifecycle-hook recovery (2026-10-16):** Extended `cli/serve_workflow_restart_integration_test.go` so `TestEngineProcessRestartResumesTimedWorkflow` creates a minimal disposable `Task` tenant configuration, runs Engine process one, stops it, commits a valid pending after-save hook invocation into the tenant SQL store, and starts a separate Engine process. The replacement process loads the tenant only when the acceptance calls its tenant route, then the test waits for both the durable workflow timeout and the lifecycle-hook receipt to reach terminal success. This is a full Engine process restart and cold tenant runtime activation check, beyond the earlier worker/pool recreation integration. Passed against a task-created MySQL 8.4.11 container with a tmpfs datadir using `TMPDIR=/dev/shm KORA_ENGINE_WORKFLOW_PROCESS_RESTART=1 go test -p 1 -tags=integration -run '^TestEngineProcessRestartResumesTimedWorkflow$' -count=1 -v ./cli` (24.96s), and under `-race` with `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm KORA_ENGINE_WORKFLOW_PROCESS_RESTART=1 go test -race -p 1 -tags=integration -run '^TestEngineProcessRestartResumesTimedWorkflow$' -count=1 -timeout=240s -v ./cli` (45.472s). Both runs emitted MySQL driver's expected `unexpected EOF` messages while readiness polling probed the starting container; DB bootstrap, Engine startup, workflow completion and lifecycle-hook completion all passed. The task-created containers were removed by test cleanup. `TMPDIR=/dev/shm go test -p 1 -tags=integration -run '^$' ./cli ./internal/engine` compiled both integration test packages successfully; scoped `git diff --check` passed. The fixture uses a valid committed invocation inserted after process one stops; atomic source-operation capture and local domain effect/receipt behavior are covered separately by the cross-dialect Engine worker test above. External HTTP uncertainty/reconciliation remains open.

**G10 external HTTP endpoint fixture attempt (2026-10-16):** A first attempt to extend the Engine worker integration with an `httptest` loopback endpoint was correctly rejected by the production script provider's SSRF guard (`requests to private/internal hosts are not allowed`) before any network dispatch. The test assertion expecting a request was therefore invalid; the attempted code was removed, the disposable MySQL test container was removed, and the production allowlist/private-host protection was left unchanged. The existing store-level three-dialect test still proves an expired, durably marked external effect becomes visible as `uncertain` and is not automatically retried; existing API provider tests prove the marker is required before dispatch. Full worker-to-controlled-endpoint reconciliation remains unverified and requires a safe transport-injection/controlled public test endpoint design.

**G10 durable async workflow `OnFailure` scripts (2026-10-16):** Extended the existing `_kora_workflow_async_script` payload contract with optional `phase` values `on_success` and `on_failure` (old rows without a phase remain readable as before); successful transitions set `on_success`, while `OnFailure` snapshots are persisted in a separate tenant transaction only after the failure outcome is known. If the kernel transition ran, its mutation transaction has already rolled back; a pre-transition script rejection opens no transition transaction. The handler pins the script/config revision before running `OnTransition`, captures only the verified execution actor and pre-transition document, sends the record through the existing fenced provider-disabled workflow worker, and returns HTTP 503 if an async failure action cannot be durably recorded rather than falling back to volatile enqueue. Synchronous failure actions retain their existing best-effort execution. Updated Engine `workflow/service.go`, `workflow/sqlstore/async_scripts.go`, `workflow/sqlstore/async_scripts_dialects_integration_test.go`, `kernel/pipeline.go`, `api/router.go`, and the API integration fixture, plus the authoritative spec §16.1 and implementation ledger. No table/schema or public HTTP contract change. `TestWorkflowFailureAsyncScriptIsPersistedDurably` passed under `-race` against a new tmpfs-backed MySQL 8.0 container using `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm KORA_TEST_DSN='[REDACTED disposable DSN]' go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./api -run '^TestWorkflowFailureAsyncScriptIsPersistedDurably$' -count=1 -timeout=180s -v`; it verifies successful persistence, no process-queue fallback, HTTP 503 when the durable store fails, and unchanged pre-transition data. The first attempt exposed an incorrect test expectation that a missing state field was stored as `Draft`, while runtime correctly interprets missing status as `Draft`; the assertion was corrected to verify the snapshot remained pre-transition. The existing durable `OnSuccess` API integration and new `OnFailure` test both passed under `-race` in a fresh MySQL run using `KORA_TEST_DSN='[REDACTED]' GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./api -run '^TestWorkflow(ActionAsyncScriptUsesBoundedSinkWithoutProvider|FailureAsyncScriptIsPersistedDurably)$' -count=1 -timeout=180s -v`. `TestAsyncScriptStoreFencedRecoveryAcrossDialects` round-tripped `on_failure` under `-race` on each supported transport with these two runs: `KORA_ASYNC_SCRIPT_DISPOSABLE_MYSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]' GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./workflow/sqlstore -run '^TestAsyncScriptStoreFencedRecoveryAcrossDialects$' -count=1 -timeout=180s -v`; and the same command with `KORA_ASYNC_SCRIPT_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_DSN='[REDACTED]' KORA_ASYNC_SCRIPT_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN='[REDACTED]'` instead. All three containers used tmpfs datadirs, loopback-only ports, and were removed. Focused `go test -p 1 ./workflow ./workflow/sqlstore ./kernel ./api`, `go test -race -p 1 ./workflow ./workflow/sqlstore ./kernel ./api`, scoped `go vet`, integration-tag compilation, `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and scoped `git diff --check` passed. A repeated client request is a separate failure attempt and no exactly-once guarantee is claimed. Webhook actions remain unsupported.


**G10 external workflow response-loss transport test (2026-10-16):** Added `TestExternalActionResponseLostAfterProviderEffectStaysUncertain`. A test-only registered external action POSTs through an actual `httptest.Server`; the server records the effect and stable idempotency key, then closes the connection before sending a response. The executor produces a durable-attempt transition with `InstanceUncertain` and `kora.workflow.outcome_unknown`, never an automatic retry. Passed `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm go test -race -p 1 ./workflow -run 'TestExternalAction(ResponseLostAfterProviderEffectStaysUncertain|ErrorBecomesRecoverableUnknownOutcome|OnlyRetriesWhenAdapterProvesNoEffect)$' -count=1 -v`. Existing SQL-store integration covers fenced uncertainty and operator reconciliation separately. This test proves executor behavior at the HTTP transport boundary; it does not add a production HTTP action adapter or a single end-to-end external action/reconciliation acceptance. No runtime, contract or schema change.


**G10 LibSQL uncertain-workflow reconciliation and timestamp correction (2026-10-16):** Extended the actual LibSQL HTTP workflow integration to read a fenced `uncertain` instance after crash recovery, reconcile a provider-confirmed success, replay the same reconciliation idempotency key, and verify exactly one immutable history entry and outbox event. The first run exposed that LibSQL returns timezone-bearing SQLite timestamp strings such as `2026-10-16 14:08:19+00:00`, while the workflow instance reader only accepted RFC3339 or offset-free SQLite strings. Extended the persistence parser to accept both fractional and whole-second space-separated timestamps with `Z`/numeric offsets, normalize to UTC, and added unit coverage. Passed focused parser/schema tests under `-race`, then `KORA_WORKFLOW_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN='http://127.0.0.1:19047' GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./workflow/sqlstore -run '^TestLibSQLWorkflowStartAndClaim$' -count=1 -timeout=180s -v` against task-created LibSQL sqld with `/var/lib/sqld` on a 128 MiB tmpfs and loopback-only port; the container was removed after the pass. Also passed `go test -race -p 1 ./workflow ./workflow/sqlstore -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./workflow ./workflow/sqlstore`, integration-tag compilation, `gofmt`, and scoped `git diff --check`. No schema/public contract changed. This closes live LibSQL uncertain-state reconciliation coverage; no production HTTP workflow action adapter is introduced, and webhook compatibility remains open.


**G10 combined external HTTP action and durable reconciliation acceptance (2026-10-16):** Extended `TestLibSQLWorkflowStartAndClaim` with an end-to-end case on the actual LibSQL HTTP transport. It claims a real durable workflow step using a verified service identity and registered external action, POSTs to a controlled loopback `httptest.Server` that records one provider effect and stable idempotency key then drops the response, persists the fenced `outcome_unknown` transition and event, reads the uncertain state, applies an operator reconciliation, and replays the same reconciliation idempotency key. Assertions require exactly one observed provider effect, persisted uncertainty before resolution, one reconciliation history/event, and the resolved continuation. The first run correctly surfaced a fixture-order error because a prior reconciled test instance remained `ready` at its `done` continuation; the fixture now commits that continuation to completed before claiming the HTTP case. Test cleanup removes all tenant-scoped workflow receipts/history/definitions and outbox events, including the new fixture. Passed `KORA_WORKFLOW_DISPOSABLE_LIBSQL_DSN='http://127.0.0.1:19047' GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./workflow/sqlstore -run '^TestLibSQLWorkflowStartAndClaim$' -count=1 -timeout=180s -v` using a task-created LibSQL `sqld` server on loopback with a 128 MiB tmpfs database; container removed afterward. Also passed `go test -race -p 1 ./workflow ./workflow/sqlstore -count=1`, `go vet -p 1 ./workflow ./workflow/sqlstore`, integration-tag compilation, formatting, and `git diff --check`. No schema or public contract changed. This closes the combined external action/reconciliation test gate for LibSQL, not the absence of a production HTTP workflow action registration or the legacy webhook action compatibility gate.


**G10 isolated self-host Compose runtime boot (2026-10-16):** Inspected the Engine and Studio worktrees before building (558 and 103 existing status entries); all source/artifact work occurred in `/dev/shm/kora-g10-compose-test` and no repository build output was overwritten. Built the current Engine embedded UI with `bun run build`, copied it only into the temporary Engine source copy, and built a static current Engine binary using `CGO_ENABLED=0 GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm go build -ldflags='-s -w -X github.com/asenawritescode/kora/cli.Version=g10-compose-local' -o /dev/shm/kora-g10-compose-test/kora .`. Built the standalone Studio source copy with `bun run build --mode production`; it completed with the existing large-chunk warning. Created task-only runtime images from these outputs using `docker build -t codex-g10-engine-runtime:local /dev/shm/kora-g10-compose-test/engine-runtime` and `docker build -t codex-g10-workspace-runtime:local /dev/shm/kora-g10-compose-test/workspace-runtime`, then supplied those tags through a `/dev/shm` Compose override so the checked-in `deploy/selfhost/compose.yaml` could run without pulling large builder images or invoking an unverified build stage. `docker compose -p codex-g10-selfhost -f deploy/selfhost/compose.yaml -f /dev/shm/kora-g10-compose-test/compose.override.yaml --env-file /dev/shm/kora-g10-compose-test/compose.env config --quiet` and `up -d --no-build --wait --wait-timeout 180` passed. Fresh disposable MySQL, Engine and workspace containers all became healthy. Engine `/readyz` returned `{"status":"ready"}`; Engine `/api/ping` and the same path through the workspace proxy returned `{"message":"pong","version":"g10-compose-local"}`; workspace `/healthz` returned `ok`, root returned standalone SPA HTML, and Engine `/console/login` returned embedded SPA HTML. Engine logs showed absent NATS fell back to SQL polling. `docker compose ... stop --timeout 65 engine` exited promptly and logged graceful SIGTERM shutdown while MySQL remained up. Compose `down --volumes --remove-orphans` removed only the task project; exact task images and the tmpfs build directory were also removed. An initial probe used nonexistent `/api/v1/ping` and returned 404; the current registered route is `/api/ping`, which passed. A host-routed Engine `/workspace/` probe against an empty directory returned the expected `site_not_found`; embedded `/console/login` verified embedded asset serving, and standalone workspace remains path-routed. No tenant onboarding was attempted. Updated the local-operations and E2E skills to use the registered Engine liveness route `/api/ping` (their prior `/api/v1/ping` probe returned 404) and documented the isolation/runtime-image limits for self-host Compose boot checks. This verifies a disposable runtime topology from current artifacts, not the checked-in multi-stage Dockerfile build, tenant onboarding UAT, or a real application file-volume backup/restore.

**G10 local application file-volume restart and backup/restore test (2026-10-16):** Added `api/local_file_volume_test.go` with `TestLocalFileVolumeSurvivesEngineRestartAndBackupRestore`. The test uploads a file through the actual API upload handler into a real local storage backend, downloads and checks it, reconstructs the backend/API composition over the same root as after process restart, then uses the operator tar archive/extract sequence into a fresh root and verifies download bytes, checksum, size, and filename metadata. Passed `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm go test -p 1 ./api -run '^TestLocalFileVolumeSurvivesEngineRestartAndBackupRestore$' -count=1 -v` and the same command with `-race`; `GOCACHE=/dev/shm/kora-gocache GOTMPDIR=/dev/shm/kora-gotmp TMPDIR=/dev/shm go vet -p 1 ./api` and scoped `git diff --check` passed. `docker compose -f deploy/selfhost/compose.yaml --env-file deploy/selfhost/.env.example config --format json` was parsed without printing its generated sample secrets; an assertion confirmed local backend, `KORA_STORAGE_LOCAL_PATH=/sites`, and named volume `engine_sites` mounted at `/sites`. The self-host Compose file now sets that storage root explicitly and its README documents the volume mapping. No schema or public contract changed. The API and archive behavior plus mount wiring are verified; a container-level API upload using the actual Engine process and mounted Compose volume remains open.

**G10 checked-in Engine and workspace multi-stage Dockerfile builds (2026-10-16):** Built the actual Engine Dockerfile with `docker buildx build --progress plain --output type=oci,dest=/dev/shm/kora-engine-multistage.oci.tar --build-arg VERSION=architecture-check -f Dockerfile .`; embedded Bun UI build, Go dependency download/compile, Alpine runtime package install, and OCI export all passed. Built the actual standalone workspace Dockerfile from `/home/asena/projects/kora-studio-mvp` using `docker buildx build --progress plain --output type=oci,dest=/dev/shm/kora-ui-multistage.oci.tar -f Dockerfile .`; Bun frozen install, TypeScript/Vite production build, nginx runtime stage, and OCI export all passed. Vite emitted the known large-chunk warning. Both temporary OCI archives were removed, and unused BuildKit cache was pruned (no Docker volumes/images or repository build artifacts were removed) to restore root filesystem space. These are image-build checks, not a runtime upload/restore acceptance using the built images; that gate remains open. No source or contract change resulted from the builds.

**G8 live local onboarding-to-workspace UAT (2026-10-16):** Inspected task-owned service status and repository worktree counts before browser use: Engine `git status` had 559 entries, Cloud 170, standalone Studio 103, and public website 72; no files were reset or overwritten. Local Engine `/api/ping` and `/readyz`, Cloud `/healthz` and `/readyz`, Studio tenant routes, and public website `/onboard` returned HTTP 200. Engine and Cloud process environments contained the expected app-base/reload/config credential variable names (values were not printed). Using the isolated local Chrome CDP profile from `kora-e2e-testing`, submitted a new synthetic onboarding request on the local public website. Cloud completed it; the returned “Open Kigae” URL was on the Studio origin and contained the requested tenant path. Inspected only the synthetic recipient's Mailpit search result and message; its magic link used the same Studio origin and tenant path. Followed the one-time link once; Studio authenticated, rendered the expected new tenant workspace, and `/s/<tenant>/api/auth/me` returned 200. The workspace root retained the same `/s/<tenant>` prefix. Browser console errors were empty. Initial unauthenticated requests returned expected 401s; the magic-link exchange resulted in an authenticated session. A browser request to the external public template catalog was blocked by the isolated Chrome network guard; local onboarding still completed through the task-local Cloud API. The test stopped at the onboarding conversation before generating, reviewing, or activating a config, and does not prove the previously reported deployed-site issue globally fixed. No existing Mailpit messages were deleted or inspected. Tokens, conversation IDs, and test email are omitted. The isolated Chrome profile and local redirect helper were closed; local app services were left running.

**G8 magic-link replay finding and fix in progress (2026-10-16):** A direct CDP network observer on a fresh local sign-in found two POSTs to the tenant-scoped `magic-link/verify` endpoint for one link: the first returned 200 and the replay returned 401. The login route now synchronously removes `magic_token` with `history.replaceState` before beginning verification, preserving the tenant path and unrelated return-state query. Added pure URL tests for token removal and return-state preservation; both pass. `bun run build` also passes. A follow-up browser reached the expected tenant workspace, but its capped aggregate log did not isolate verification statuses, so a clean direct-network capture proving exactly one verification POST remains required. No schema or public contract change.

**G8 clean-profile magic-link regression verification (2026-10-16):** Repeated with a fresh isolated Chrome profile and a newly issued link for the synthetic tenant. An independent CDP Network observer recorded exactly one `POST` to `/s/architecture-check-20261016/api/auth/magic-link/verify`, with HTTP 200 and no second verification request. The browser rendered the tenant workspace at `/s/architecture-check-20261016/workspace/onboarding`; a page-side check confirmed `magic_token` was absent from the address bar and no query keys remained. The link, email, and opaque IDs are omitted. This supersedes the prior paragraph's outstanding browser-capture gate. No schema or public contract change. Conversation review and configuration activation remain untested.

**G8 AI conversation isolation and authoring usability (2026-10-16):** Workspace `ChatWidget` now exposes a New conversation control that assigns and stores an opaque thread ID for the tab; Engine hashes that ID with the verified user and page context into a bounded `subject_key`, so existing context-based history remains backward compatible and separate threads load separate transcripts. `useChat` drops stale async results after context switches. Explicitly requested record creates and updates execute through the shared Kernel policy, validation, idempotency receipt, audit, outbox, and lifecycle hooks; ordinary updates no longer create an unresumable second approval. The kernel still rejects direct updates to an active workflow state field; transitions use the registered domain operation. AI JavaScript authoring requires a verified Administrator and recent authentication, validates source, records creator attribution, and saves an inactive draft; only an Administrator can activate it in Admin → Scripts. Added test coverage for per-thread/per-user keys, create/update tool contract policy, non-admin script denial, inactive-draft fields, and Administrator authorization routing. Updated the target spec and active kernel migration guide to describe explicit create/update intent and the protected-transition boundary. No database schema migration; the optional `threadId` chat context and create/update tool safety flags are public behavior changes. Passed `go test -race ./api/ai ./kernel`, `go vet ./api/ai ./kernel`, `make check-contracts`, `make check-cross-repo-contracts`, and `git diff --check`. The integration command `go test -race -tags=integration -p 1 ./api/ai -run '^TestAIRecordMutationUsesKernelReceiptAndAudit$' -count=1 -timeout=240s -v` passed against a task-owned disposable MySQL 8.0 database; its DSN was injected in memory and no credential was printed. It verifies create/update replay, no unnecessary update approval rows, audit/outbox/receipt counts, lifecycle hooks, and denial for a principal without create permission. Standalone Studio `bun run test` passed (59 files/234 tests), `bun run build` passed with the existing large-chunk warning, and targeted ESLint on both modified chat components passed. The authenticated synthetic Cloud provider-gateway round-trip returned HTTP 200. The updated Engine built to `/tmp/kora-engine-ai-update` and restarted; Engine `/api/ping` and `/readyz`, Cloud `/healthz`, and Studio returned HTTP 200, with Cloud AI and Engine gateway readiness true. An authenticated workspace browser chat and visible thread reset still need manual confirmation. The local Engine is left running with the shared gateway configuration.

**G8 full browser AI write and script-draft verification (2026-10-16):** Follow-up to the previous entry's browser gate. A fresh synthetic tenant `ai-chat-uat.local` was installed in a separately named database on the task-owned local MySQL host. Rebuilt Engine at port 8002 was configured for the already-running local Cloud provider gateway; Studio Vite at port 5179 proxied only this test session to it. In the authenticated browser, starting a new conversation removed the earlier transcript and tool context; asking AI to create a Task yielded `TASK-0001`; asking it to update that record changed the title on the same row while the workspace count remained one; requesting a before-save JavaScript script yielded draft `trim_task_title`. A read-only SQL query confirmed `_kora_script` contains that row for `Task`, event `before_save`, with `is_active=0` and 128 characters of source. Browser console errors: none. The first isolated attempt without a provider gateway correctly returned the provider-key UI state; rerun with the authenticated shared gateway succeeded. Added regression coverage for the previously missing durable lifecycle-hook verified tenant and config-revision metadata in `api/siteTx`: `go test -race ./api ./api/ai ./kernel -count=1` passed; `go vet ./api ./api/ai ./kernel` passed. The existing AI create/update/replay MySQL integration passed under race using a temporary database on the local disposable test DB: `KORA_AI_MUTATION_MYSQL_DSN='root:[redacted]@tcp(127.0.0.1:3308)/?parseTime=true&multiStatements=true' go test -tags=integration -race ./api/ai -run '^TestAIRecordMutationUsesKernelReceiptAndAudit$' -count=1`. `git diff --check` passed. The named synthetic tenant database was removed after UAT. No production or other tenant data was modified. No public contract/schema change. SDK coordinated release, remaining G8 compatibility gates, and broader G0–G10 scope remain open.

**G2 shutdown ownership for hijacked realtime WebSockets (2026-10-16):** During G8 browser UAT, an isolated Engine did not complete runtime retirement while a realtime WebSocket remained open; closing the browser first allowed shutdown to complete. `net/http.Server.Shutdown` does not own hijacked connections, so added `api.RealtimeConnectionOwner` to track/cancel/close them, wired it through Engine application assembly to `ProcessResources`, and close streams before worker joins and tenant retirement. A real websocket unit test confirms the active handler exits and client connection closes; a late-registration case confirms streams are immediately closed after shutdown begins; assembly and resource-order tests confirm process ownership/wiring. Passed `go test -race ./api ./internal/engine`, `go vet ./api ./internal/engine`, `go build -o /tmp/kora-engine-ws-shutdown .`, and `git diff --check`. The original isolated UAT process was terminated with SIGQUIT after exceeding its shutdown deadline; this was a disposable Engine and its separate UAT databases were dropped. The code-level regression suite now passes; a rebuilt full Engine process shutdown with a live browser websocket is the remaining direct runtime acceptance.

**G2 full Engine shutdown with active realtime WebSocket (2026-10-16):** Added `TestEngineShutdownClosesRealtimeWebSocketBeforeJoiningResources`. It starts an actual `knet.Server`, opens a real coder/websocket connection through the API realtime handler, calls `Engine.Shutdown` with a one-second deadline, and asserts the handler and HTTP serve goroutine both exit cleanly. This verifies the end-to-end Engine→ProcessResources→RealtimeConnectionOwner shutdown order, complementing the application-assembly wiring test. Passed under `go test -race ./internal/engine -run '^TestEngineShutdownClosesRealtimeWebSocketBeforeJoiningResources$' -count=1 -v` and again in `go test -race ./api ./internal/engine`; package vet, Engine build, and `git diff --check` also passed. This resolves the prior paragraph's remaining direct WebSocket runtime acceptance.

**G9 disposable database and ingress recheck (2026-10-16):** An aggregate-only query of the task-owned test MySQL `kora-live-test-mysql` found directory revision 2 and 11 active default tenants. This is a disposable integration/test directory, not the DB used by the running Engine, and must not be used as the deployment assignment inventory. Cloud control-plane listener 8081 is present; no separate `kora-cloud ingress` process/listener is running and `.env.local` supplies no ingress configuration. G9 race suites passed: Engine `go test -race ./site ./ingress`; Cloud `go test -race ./internal/ingress ./cmd/kora-cloud ./internal/platform`. Production assignment/consumer inventory, direct deployment origins, and cutover remain gated. No assignment was changed.

**G9 currently running Engine directory recheck (2026-10-16):** Rebuilt Engine CLI ran the specified read-only `site-directory summarize-assignments` using the running Engine process database configuration; output was revision 201, one active entry on `default`. The same DB outbox has 95 distinct site IDs, whose latest retained event states are aggregate-only 94 `deleted` and one `active`, all default. Reconstructing retained states as of revision 14 on this DB yields one active default plus four deleted, so the older report of seven active `deploy-local-uat` entries came from a different DB/environment and cannot be merged with this result. No tenant identifiers or aliases were read into the report. Do not infer authorization for restoration or deletion from this inventory; reconcile expected environment and retained tombstones with the operator before a cutover. G9 production placement, consumer/origin inventory, and ingress traffic migration remain open.

**G8 local magic-link SMTP delivery correction (2026-10-16):** The running local Engine had no `KORA_SMTP_*` variables. Its generic sender logged mail and returned success when SMTP was absent, so the public magic-link route could report success without placing mail in a mailbox. Updated the local restart script allowlist and health wait, added SMTP environment wiring and operator guidance to self-host Compose, and made Engine reject an unconfigured sender before persisting a token. Added `TestIssueMagicLinkRejectsUnconfiguredEmailBeforePersistingToken`. Local `.env.local` now points to the already-running Mailpit on `127.0.0.1:1025` using unauthenticated `plain` transport only for loopback testing, and sets Studio's public origin. `./restart_server.sh` first returned a PID without a healthy service; the process then died silently. Started the same newly built Engine with its configured environment, confirmed `/api/ping` 200, then corrected the restart script to wait for health and report early exit. The updated script subsequently restarted Engine and confirmed health. A synthetic sign-in template sent by Engine's actual `email.Sender` was found as a new Mailpit message for only the synthetic recipient; its body included the expected `http://localhost:5173/s/clientele/workspace/auth/login?...` link. Existing mailbox content was preserved. Passed `go test ./auth ./email -count=1`, `go test -race ./auth ./email -count=1`, `go vet ./auth ./email`, `sh -n restart_server.sh`, self-host Compose `config --quiet` with disposable interpolation values, and scoped `git diff --check`. Changed Engine `auth/session.go`, `auth/session_test.go`, `restart_server.sh`, self-host Compose/env/docs, this ledger, and local-operations skill. No contract or schema change. This verifies local SMTP acceptance and template origin, not inbox delivery by an external provider or an actual existing user's endpoint request; the latter requires a known account in the intended tenant. Production SMTP configuration remains operator-specific.

**G7 Cloud provisioning 404 diagnosis and actionable error (2026-10-16):** The reported Cloud preflight error occurs while requesting `GET /api/management/v2/capabilities`. Engine only registers that route with durable management operation routes when `KORA_ENGINE_MANAGEMENT_CALLER_ID` is set; the provisioning capability additionally requires `KORA_ENGINE_DEPLOYMENT_ID` and `KORA_ENGINE_MANAGEMENT_DATABASE_ALLOCATION_REF`, while the inspection surface requires the dedicated `KORA_ENGINE_MANAGEMENT_TOKEN`. An isolated disposable MySQL + Engine container reproduced the omission: startup without these management settings had no capability route; restarting that Engine with a management token, stable caller ID, `KORA_ENGINE_DEPLOYMENT_ID=default`, and `KORA_ENGINE_MANAGEMENT_DATABASE_ALLOCATION_REF=mysql-selfhost` returned HTTP 200 and advertised `tenant.provision`. This proved a local configuration fix without changing the reported external deployment. `deploy/selfhost/compose.yaml` now forwards all four optional settings; `.env.example` and README explain matching Cloud values, direct Engine-origin URL, assignment alignment, and the preflight request. Cloud now turns a management 404 into a specific URL/release/caller-ID diagnostic instead of opaque `INTERNAL_ERROR`; regression passed with `go test -race -p 1 ./internal/platform -run '^(TestProvisioningPreflightExplainsEngineManagementCapabilities404|TestProvisioningPreflightRequiresInvitationCapability|TestProvisioningPreflightRequiresInvitationReconciliation)$' -count=1`. Engine capability-route suites passed with `go test -race -p 1 ./api -run '^(TestManagementCapabilitiesOnlyAdvertiseEnabledOperations|TestManagementOperationRoutesRegisterOnlyReadyCapabilitiesAndBoundBodies)$' -count=1`; Engine Compose rendering passed with settings absent and present. **The external Engine/Cloud deployment was not reconfigured or restarted**; its operator must apply matching values, validate that the capability response advertises the required operations, and only then retry the same durable provisioning operation.

**G10 container-mounted Engine file backup/restore acceptance (2026-10-16):** Using the already-built local Engine image `kora-g10-volume-uat-engine:latest`, a new Docker network, tmpfs-only MySQL 8.4.11 container, and two uniquely named disposable file volumes, the test created a synthetic tenant and logged in over HTTP. The test bootstrapped CSRF with an authenticated tenant navigation GET, uploaded a synthetic text file through `POST /s/<tenant>/api/upload`, and verified its bytes through `GET /s/<tenant>/api/v1/files/<path>`. Engine was stopped before archiving the mounted source volume; the archive was extracted into a fresh named volume, and a replacement Engine container was started against the same disposable DB with only the restored volume mounted at `/sites`. After a fresh tenant login and CSRF bootstrap, the replacement Engine served the same file bytes and checksum. The test-created network, MySQL/Engine containers, both file volumes, archive, credentials, file content, and metadata scratch files were removed after the test. No existing containers, user DBs, or production data were used. This closes the previously open container-level file-volume proof; it does not rehearse off-host encryption, full DB-plus-file production cutover, or recovery objectives.

**G7 local managed-channel 401 diagnosis (2026-10-16):** Cloud's running process had `KORA_CLOUD_ENGINE_PROVISIONING_TOKEN`; the local Engine process had no `KORA_ENGINE_PROVISIONING_TOKEN`, so Cloud's dedicated bearer credential was rejected with HTTP 401. Copied the matching value directly from Cloud's process environment into the ignored local Engine `.env.local` without displaying it, added the token to `restart_server.sh`'s explicit allowlist/launch environment, and documented the distinct credential in self-host Compose, `.env.example`, and README. Restarted the local Engine and confirmed the values match in memory; a read-only `GET /s/<registered-tenant>/api/internal/site/identity` using Cloud's token returned HTTP 200 and `healthy=true`. Ran `go test ./api ./auth -run 'ManagedChannel|Provisioning' -count=1`, `go test ./internal/tenantclient ./internal/platform -run 'ManagedChannel|Provisioning' -count=1`, `docker compose -f deploy/selfhost/compose.yaml --env-file deploy/selfhost/.env.example config --quiet`, and `sh -n restart_server.sh`; all passed. The previously reported `clientele` Cloud record remains a separate directory mismatch: its Engine URL resolves to a tenant ID not present in the active Engine directory, and its read-only identity check returns `site_not_found`. No retry or tenant/database mutation was made because the Cloud job is marked succeeded and the correct existing tenant database has not been identified. No production or hosted service was changed.

**G7 demo2 provisioning recovery (2026-10-16):** Inspected only job `job-1792169085921697541`; the persisted record showed template/database/bootstrap/config/runtime steps complete, with `configure_channel_client` failed on the old 401. After confirming Engine's read-only site identity returned HTTP 200 with the now-matching Cloud managed-channel token, used Cloud's existing guarded retry endpoint once. That persisted channel credential successfully but encountered a separate 401 on the Cloud→Engine reload call. Compared the local process values without displaying them, copied Cloud's configured reload token into the ignored Engine `.env.local`, restarted Engine, and verified the pair now matches. The retry had left exactly this job in `running` with a failed reload checkpoint; its channel step was already succeeded, and it was the only running provisioning job. Called the existing resume-pending endpoint once; recovery reused the persisted checkpoints and finished with job `succeeded`, site `active`, and channel/reload/welcome-email checkpoints `succeeded`. The Engine SMTP target was local Mailpit. Final read-only Engine identity returned HTTP 200 and healthy. No tenant was recreated, no production service was changed, and no mailbox was cleared or inspected. The unrelated `clientele` missing-directory issue remains open.

**G7 demo3 provisioning recovery (2026-10-16):** Inspected only job `job-1792169787505278221`. Its persisted checkpoints showed channel credential already succeeded and only Engine reload had failed with HTTP 401; the existing Engine identity check was healthy and both Cloud/Engine provisioning and reload credential pairs matched after the local configuration repair. Used the guarded retry for that same failed job, which completed with HTTP 200. Read-only verification shows Cloud job `succeeded`, site `active`, channel/reload/welcome checkpoints `succeeded`, Engine identity HTTP 200/healthy, and Studio route `/s/demo3/workspace` HTTP 200. No new tenant was created, no credential was printed, and no mailbox was inspected or cleared.

**G7/G8 local onboarding validation recovery and simplified ready handoff (2026-10-17):** Found the running local Cloud/Engine configuration credentials differed; copied Cloud's credential directly into the ignored Engine `.env.local` without printing it, restarted Engine, and verified the in-memory values match. Both services returned `/readyz` 200; a no-write tenant dry-run returned HTTP 200 with the expected invalid result for an empty candidate, proving authentication only. Cloud's proposal worker now distinguishes Engine candidate rejection from validator transport/auth failure; non-candidate failures emit a retryable event and stop without repeatedly regenerating the draft. `go test ./internal/app ./internal/platform -count=1` and a targeted race test passed. Studio now pulses only the newest active timeline item, closes `configuration.validated`, and gives the user one concise next action: “Your draft passed its checks. Click ‘Prepare pending version’ below to review it. It won’t go live until you approve it.” If a pending version already exists, the UI opens Config Versions without re-composing. Studio's activity test and 60-file/237-test suite passed before the new activity test; the new test passed separately, and the latest full suite/build passed (61 files/238 tests; Vite emitted its pre-existing >500 kB chunk warning). Cloud was rebuilt into tmpfs and restarted after verifying the credentials still match; `/readyz` returned 200. No Lead was created, no pending version was created, and no activation or candidate mutation occurred.
